# Ask Cozy — Inline Workspace Product Requirements Document

**Version:** 1.377
**Date:** October 13, 2026
**Status:** Approved product direction; implementation is partial and tracked by requirement
**Scope:** Ask Cozy interaction across homeowner-facing domains on desktop and mobile, including lightweight capability discovery and Ask-native completion

**Revision 1.377 — Projects: the dispute package is a Home Record, with storage cleanup outside the transaction (slice 2 of `docs/architecture/PROJECT_TRACKER_DOCUMENT_STORE_DESIGN.md`, owner ruling D-P4; 2026-10-13):** The generator created a legacy `Document` row for every package, and its comment said the document and the package were written "in the same transaction" even though the PDF was uploaded to object storage first. **Sequence now:** render the PDF; stage the object (`stageGeneratedDocument`: pre-allocated record and version ids, canonical storage key, checksum); then **one** database transaction creates the Home Record (new type `PROJECT_DISPUTE_PACKAGE`, sensitivity `LEGAL`, household-visible, verified by the system at generation, immutable first version), the `ProjectDisputePackage` row (`documentId` is the record id) and the link to the issue (`PROJECT_ISSUE`, `SOURCE`, bound to the version). No transaction is open while the document is rendered or uploaded. **Failure:** if the transaction fails the staged object is deleted; if deletion fails its key is recorded in the new `OrphanedStorageObject` table and the purge worker's tick retries it until it is gone; if even recording fails the key is logged. **Schema (edited, not pushed):** `PropertyRecordType.PROJECT_DISPUTE_PACKAGE` and `OrphanedStorageObject`. The type is not in the Home Records upload route, so no person can create one; the Home Records page labels it. **Legacy:** the generator no longer creates a legacy `Document`; existing packages keep their legacy id (no rewrite, D-P6) and the link audit skips them as legacy and checks new ones; each package version is its own record with its own link, so earlier versions' links are not replaced. **Not covered:** evidence documents cited by a package are not linked (they are in the immutable snapshot with their version ids); a purged package record leaves `documentId` dangling, which the deletion-impact link discloses beforehand. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend (one enum value, one table) and `prisma generate` in apps/workers (the purge worker reads the new table); redeploy the backend and the purge worker. **Tests:** `tests/unit/projectDisputePackageRecord.test.js` (5: staging happens outside any transaction and the record, version, package and link are created together with no legacy `Document`; transaction failure deletes the object; a failed deletion is recorded; the helpers record and retry without throwing; the worker hook and the upload route's type list); backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; frontend `next build` run; the registry set at its 10-failure baseline. Nothing is browser, database or storage verified.

**Revision 1.376 — Projects: document references have a declared source and a link, written together (slice 1 of `docs/architecture/PROJECT_TRACKER_DOCUMENT_STORE_DESIGN.md`, owner ruling D-P1; 2026-10-13):** **Canonical source:** the Home Record id stored on the project's own row (with, for evidence, the exact version in a stored map) is the durable reference; a `PropertyRecordLink` is its deletion-impact projection; every writer changes both in one database transaction, so a write fails if the link cannot be written. **Schema (edited, not pushed):** five link entity types (`PROJECT_PAYMENT`, `PROJECT_CHANGE_ORDER`, `PROJECT_PROGRESS_LOG`, `PROJECT_ISSUE`, `PROJECT_ISSUE_NOTE`); id columns `receiptDocumentId`, `supportingDocumentId`, `photoDocumentIds` (the three that had only a copied key); version maps `complianceEvidenceVersionIds`, `evidenceVersionIds` (issue and note). **Link uniqueness, decided per use** (the existing key has no `versionId` and other domains upsert on it, so it is unchanged): a contract, receipt, supporting document or photo is `REPLACE` (another record removes the old link; the same record moves to the version chosen); issue, note and compliance evidence is `KEEP_BOUND_VERSION` (a record already cited keeps its bound version, only new records bind to the version chosen, unlisted records lose their link). **Writers:** contract documents on create, edit and handoff; the payment receipt on mark-paid; change-order supporting document on create and edit, and compliance evidence on review (cleared with the review when the structured impact changes); progress photos; issue evidence on create and edit; issue-note evidence. **Ask** gets its receipt, supporting-document and photo fields back (slice 0 removed them) writing the id columns; no key column is written from Ask; `deletionImpact` for a record now lists the payment, change order, log, issue or note that cites it (`entityExists` checks all five). **A defect avoided:** the milestone photo gate and the photo counts read only `photoKeys`, so a photo chosen in Ask (now `photoDocumentIds`) would never satisfy "requires photo evidence"; both queries, the desktop log page and Ask's log list count both. The gate does not check that a Home Record photo still exists after a purge. **Repair check:** `findProjectDocumentLinkProblems` (read only: missing link, version mismatch, orphan link, evidence version unbound; legacy ids are not problems) and `scripts/repair-project-document-links.ts` (dry run by default; `--apply` creates, corrects and removes links and binds earlier evidence to the version its link has, or the current one, then re-checks and exits non-zero if anything remains). This also closes the interim state slice 0 left: ids written before links existed have none until it is run. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend (five enum values, three id columns, three JSON columns) and `prisma generate` in apps/workers, then run the repair script's dry run and, if it lists anything, `--apply`. **Not here:** the dispute package as a `PROJECT_DISPUTE_PACKAGE` Home Record (slice 2), desktop pickers and Home Record uploads from Ask (slice 3), retiring the transitional path (slice 4). **Tests:** `tests/unit/projectDocumentLinks.test.js` (7: replace vs keep-bound, transactional note, receipt, issue, repair and idempotence, link types and photo gate); the slice-0 Ask test updated; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; frontend `next build` run; the registry set at its 10-failure baseline. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.375 — Projects: documents are Home Records, and Ask's project completion works (slice 0 of `docs/architecture/PROJECT_TRACKER_DOCUMENT_STORE_DESIGN.md`, owner rulings D-P3 to D-P8; 2026-10-13):** **The defect:** Ask listed, validated and uploaded project documents against the legacy `Document` table, while `confirmCompletion` looks a proof id up in Home Records and refuses anything else, so every proof chosen in Ask was refused. A regression test (`tests/ask/projectTrackerDocuments.test.js`) failed 4 of 4 before the change. **The rule (D-P3):** `projectDocuments.service.ts` is the one resolver: same property, active, visible to the household, clean current version, any type; the sensitivity is shown and is not a filter; a legacy id is accepted only when named as already stored on that exact record (never listed, never newly offered). **Ask:** options and validation read Home Records and show type and sensitivity; the warranty document is a `warranty` proof; the contract document is stored as `contractDocumentIds` on create and edit; issue attachments are stored as `evidenceDocumentIds`; the upload control is removed from project document fields (the shared Ask upload route stores a legacy Document; Home Record uploads are slice 3). **Disclosed regression:** payment receipt, change-order supporting document and progress photos are no longer offered in Ask, because their only stored shape is a copied storage key and there is no id column to hold a reference; they return with slice 1. **Services:** issue notes, issue create and edit, change-order compliance review (stored whatever was typed until now: it can no longer be completed with ids that resolve to nothing in this property), the dispute package's evidence, the renovation handoff's `contractDocumentIds` and (for eligible Home Records) Material Specs' document check use the resolver; both completion-proof loops require an active, household-visible, clean Home Record of the property. **Dispute package:** snapshot `project-dispute-package-v2` names the contract document and records each evidence item's `versionId`; the PDF prints a title, "Contract document unavailable" or "Contract document removed", never a storage key. **Inventory:** `prisma/project-document-reference-inventory.pgadmin.sql` (read only) classifies every id and key reference and the `uploadedBy` kinds. **Finding for the owner:** `Document.uploadedBy` carries a user id in one writer and a homeowner-profile id in the routes and the uploader check; changing the writer as ruled would break `documentAuth`'s uploader recognition, so it was not changed (design §7.2). **Not here:** links, the five child entity types and the dual-write transaction (slice 1), the dispute package as a `PROJECT_DISPUTE_PACKAGE` Home Record with storage cleanup (slice 2), desktop pickers and Home Record uploads from Ask (slice 3). **Tests:** `projectDocumentsService.test.js` (6), `projectTrackerDocuments.test.js` (5), the completion-capture test updated for no upload; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; the standard registry set at its 10-failure baseline plus two failures that predate this change. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.374 — Renovation: create the execution project from a ready case, in Ask (slice 4 of `docs/architecture/RENOVATION_PROJECT_HANDOFF_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner rulings D-H1 to D-H8; 2026-10-13):** The desktop's "Create and link Project" had no Ask path. `RENOVATION_READINESS_COMMAND` gains the kind `HANDOFF` (no new operation; action `renovation-readiness-handoff`, allowlisted for `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS`), offered as **Create the execution project** on the readiness answer only to a household **owner**, for a case with no Project whose readiness is `READY` or `READY_WITH_ACKNOWLEDGED_OPEN_ITEMS`. A contributor or viewer is told before any form. Refused with a reason, not a form: a Project already exists, readiness is stale or not evaluated (refresh it first), or blockers are open. **Planned start only (D-H2):** the already-started and historical modes, which skip the readiness gate, are not accepted by the form (they stay on the desktop), and there is no document step (D-H4). **Form:** project type, contractor (required for provider-led work; prefilled with the selected quote's vendor and licence), contract amount (prefilled from the approved scope, else the selected quote, and said so), funding (default **Not decided yet**; Self-paid, Covered and Mixed are choices, never assumed: D-H7), start date and optional end date. The fulfillment mode is the one readiness evaluated and is shown, not chosen. **Review:** every value, the resolved contract amount and its source ("From the approved scope", "From the selected quote", "Entered"), **"Not set (recorded as unknown, not $0)"** when there is none and a typed 0 as a known zero (D-H5), the funding, the number of open conditions that become blocked milestones and of open dependencies carried over, and that no contractor is contacted, Home Record scope documents stay on the case, and nothing you entered is applied if a project appears first. **Confirmation binding:** `loadHandoffPrefill` returns a version over the case id and last update, the scope version, the readiness evaluation (each item's status, acknowledgement and update), the fulfillment mode, the selected quote id and last update, and the absence of a Project; any change since the review makes the confirmation stale. **Write:** `handoffToProject` (the one transaction of v1.371) with a request keyed to the execution (`ask:<execution>:handoff`), so a repeat of the same confirmation is an idempotent replay. **Receipt (D-H8):** "Project created and linked", "Project already created by this request" (replay), or "A project already existed" with a statement that nothing entered was applied; and when the work item could not be synchronized the receipt says so and that repeating the request retries it, instead of claiming it exists. **Owner steps:** those of v1.372 (`prisma db push`, workers `prisma generate`); none new. **Tests:** `tests/ask/renovationReadinessHandoffCommand.test.js` (8), `tests/unit/renovationHandoffPrefillVersion.test.js` (2: each bound input changes the version and nothing else does); backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; the standard registry set at the 10-failure baseline. Not browser or database verified; the repair command of v1.373 and the desktop's already-started and historical modes are unchanged.

**Revision 1.373 — Renovation: governed repair of partial project handoffs (slice 3 of `docs/architecture/RENOVATION_PROJECT_HANDOFF_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner ruling D-H6; 2026-10-13):** Handoffs created before v1.371 may have left a Project with no case link, no lifecycle change, no handoff events and a `DRAFT` status. **One implementation of "what makes a Project a handoff":** the steps after creation moved out of `handoffToProject` into `completeHandoffInTx(tx, …)` (status, blocking of upcoming milestones, case link, lifecycle, readiness event, case event). Each step checks what exists and does only what is missing, writes only on the given transaction client, and returns the steps it applied, so a new handoff and the repair run the same code and a second run changes nothing. **Detection (read only):** `detectIncompleteHandoffs` lists Projects created from a case that lack the case link, the `PROJECT_HANDOFF` readiness event or the `PROJECT_HANDOFF_STARTED` case event and names exactly what is missing; the milestone blocking is listed and applied only for a Project still in `DRAFT`, so real later work is never re-blocked. `prisma/renovation-partial-handoffs-detection.pgadmin.sql` is the same detection as a read-only query. **Repair:** `repairIncompleteHandoff` completes one Project in one serializable transaction, then retries the work item synchronization. The intake mode (planned, already started, historical) was never stored and decides the Project's status and the case lifecycle, so it is stated by the person and never guessed; a non-planned mode needs a reason of at least 20 characters. The repaired events carry `repaired: true`, the original key from the Project's `sourceActionId` (or `repair:<projectId>`), readiness state `REPAIRED` and no payload hash (there is no request to hash); the actor is the case's recorded owner. **Command:** `scripts/repair-renovation-handoffs.ts` is a dry run by default, lists every candidate with the status and lifecycle it would set, refuses `--apply` without `--intake-mode` (executed: both argument errors), and after applying re-runs detection and exits non-zero if any partial handoff remains. If detection returns nothing there is nothing to run. **Not here:** re-evaluating readiness at repair time (the original state was not recorded), and the Ask handoff action (slice 4). **Owner steps:** none unless detection finds candidates. **Tests:** `tests/unit/renovationHandoffRepair.test.js` (5: classification, plan per mode, reason required, apply then idempotent re-run with verification, and the completion step alone); the atomicity tests now assert the shared completion writes only on the transaction client; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Nothing is database verified.

**Revision 1.372 — Projects: an unknown contract amount is stored as unknown, and funding is undecided unless chosen (slice 2 of `docs/architecture/RENOVATION_PROJECT_HANDOFF_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner rulings D-H5 and D-H7; 2026-10-13):** Not an Ask change: a Project created with no contract amount was stored with 0, indistinguishable from a real zero-dollar project, and funding was recorded as `SELF_PAID` for a person who had not chosen it. **Schema (edited, not pushed):** `ProjectRecord.contractAmountCents` and `currentContractAmountCents` are nullable (null is unknown, 0 is a known zero); `ProjectFundingMode` gains `UNDECIDED`, which is the column default. Existing rows are not rewritten (a historical zero cannot be told from a real one). **Consumers (found by regenerating the client and reading every hit; each is null-safe now):** creation stores `null` for an amount that was not supplied; `recalcProjectTotals` leaves the current total unknown while the base is unknown and keeps the approved change-order delta on its own field (unknown, known zero, known amount, and known amount plus approved change orders are distinct); the provider price variance on completion and in analytics is `null` for an unknown amount; the ownership-cost source uses a project's contract amount only when it is known and positive; the home-action governance already read the amount as nullable. The renovation handoff resolves the amount as request, then scope, then selected quote, else unknown (a typed 0 and a scope's recorded 0 are kept). The inspection-finding repair project is created with an unknown amount and undecided funding. **Validators:** the project and handoff schemas accept an omitted or null amount and default funding to `UNDECIDED`. **Ask (reads):** a project with no amount shows "Contract amount not set" in the list and "Not set" with a remaining balance of "Unknown until the contract amount is set" in the detail, never a balance derived from the amount paid. **Desktop:** the handoff form asks for funding (default "Not decided yet") and says a blank amount is recorded as not set, not as $0; the new-project form offers "Not decided yet" and defaults to it; the project list, detail, completion prefill and funding label handle an unknown amount and an undecided funding mode. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend (two nullable columns, an enum value, a default change) and `prisma generate` in apps/workers; `prisma/project-contract-amount-detection.pgadmin.sql` is read only and lists projects whose zero or `SELF_PAID` may have been an assumption, for review. **Not here:** the detection-and-repair command for partial handoffs (slice 3) and the Ask handoff action (slice 4). **Tests:** `tests/unit/projectContractAmountUnknown.test.js` (4), a handoff test that an unsupplied amount is stored as null and a typed zero as 0, and an Ask read test for the not-set rendering; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `next build` run. Two pre-existing failures in `homeActionProjectSafetyTier` and `phase3MajorMoment` occur with and without this change. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.371 — Renovation: the project handoff is one transaction, and a repeat is told apart (slice 1 of `docs/architecture/RENOVATION_PROJECT_HANDOFF_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner rulings D-H1 and D-H8; 2026-10-13):** Not an Ask change: the canonical handoff (`handoffToProject`) created the Project in one transaction and then did the status change, milestone blocking, case link, lifecycle change and both events as separate statements; a failure after the create left a Project with none of them, and the next call returned that Project from its first statement without completing anything, so the case never moved and the desktop showed "Project linked". **Fix:** `createProjectInTx` and `linkProjectToCaseInTx` write on a given transaction client; the handoff now commits the Project and milestones, the final status, blocked milestones, the contributor link, the case lifecycle and both events together, with no helper that opens its own transaction. The work item synchronization stays post-commit and best effort, is always retried when the Project already exists, and its result is reported in the receipt (`workItemSynced`) so a work item is never claimed that does not exist; failures are logged. **Replays:** the case is scoped to the property before an existing Project is considered; the key and a hash of the normalized values are recorded in the handoff event. Same key and values: `IDEMPOTENT_REPLAY`; same key, different values: 409 `RENOVATION_HANDOFF_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; different key: `ALREADY_CREATED`, with `appliedSubmittedValues: false`; the route is 201 only for a creation. A Project with no recorded handoff event (a partial handoff from before this change) returns 409 `RENOVATION_HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` rather than success. **Checked:** the workers have no handoff reconciliation. **Not here:** a nullable contract amount and an undecided funding mode (D-H5, D-H7), the detection query and governed repair command (D-H6), and the Ask action; a missing amount still persists as zero and funding still defaults to `SELF_PAID` until the next slice. **Owner steps:** none. **Tests:** `tests/unit/renovationHandoffAtomicity.test.js` (8: hash stability, the three replay outcomes, the incomplete case, every write on the transaction client, no later step after a failed link, and a source check that no second transaction is opened); the existing renovation tests pass; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.370 — Property Tax: confirmed tax facts from a tax document already in Home Records, in one transaction (slice 4 of `docs/architecture/PROPERTY_TAX_EVIDENCE_AND_INTAKE_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner rulings D-T3, D-T4, D-T6; 2026-10-13):** The only way to create a tax document intake was to upload a new file in the Property Tax tool, which also made a second copy of a notice already on file; Ask could not upload, so it had no path. **Service:** `confirmFromRecord` takes an existing Home Record and, in **one transaction**, finds the primary account holder's property, requires the record to be an active `TAX_DOCUMENT` with a clean current scan (D-T3: no other type), refuses one already confirmed or rejected, creates the intake (or reuses the unconfirmed one) and its record link, stages the typed values, confirms them, creates the canonical assessment, bill and field evidence with their links, and marks the record verified. `stageAndConfirm` does the same for an intake that came from an upload, replacing the desktop's two requests; both refuse when the intake changed after the values were reviewed. A failure anywhere rolls the whole action back (D-T6); `stageManualFields` and `confirm` keep their behaviour and now share the same transaction bodies. **Affirmation (D-T4):** the intake is created under its own version `property-tax-existing-record-v1`, not `property-tax-vault-v1`, because the record was already stored; the statement shown is "Use this Home Record for manual Property Tax review, create the confirmed tax facts shown below, and mark the record verified. ContractToCozy has not automatically read or verified the document." and the route requires `affirmed: true`. **Ask:** `PROPERTY_TAX_COMPARABLE_COMMAND` gains one more declared kind, **Confirm facts from a tax notice or bill** (action `property-tax-document-facts`, no new operation). One form: the document (only active, clean TAX_DOCUMENT records not already confirmed, 50 at most), its kind, tax year, parcel, valuation date, classification, assessed and taxable value, assessment ratio, bill amount, effective rate and exemptions, and the affirmation; at least one parcel, assessment or bill value is required. The review lists every value and the affirmation; the confirmation is bound to the record, its current version and the intake's state and calls `confirmFromRecord`. With none waiting the answer says so and that Ask cannot upload. **Desktop:** the facts form gains the valuation date, assessment ratio and effective tax rate readiness needs (the assessed-value ground's canonical `valuationDate` could not be supplied from a document before), confirmation is one call, and a "use a tax document already in your home records" choice with the same affirmation sits beside the upload. **Owner steps:** those of v1.369 (`prisma db push`, `prisma generate` in apps/workers, the links backfill SQL, redeploy backend and purge worker); this slice adds none. **Not here:** AI extraction of tax documents, rejecting an intake, other record types as intakes, and removing the upload's separate storage consent. **Tests:** `tests/ask/propertyTaxDocumentFactsCommands.test.js` (8, including the service's single transaction, the distinct affirmation version, and every refusal); the readiness no-desktop-links test expects the new action; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `next build` run, the registry set at the 10-failure baseline. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.369 — Property Tax: record links and purge behaviour (slice 3 of `docs/architecture/PROPERTY_TAX_EVIDENCE_AND_INTAKE_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner ruling D-T5; 2026-10-13):** Nothing in the property tax code registered a Home Record link, so a tax document that backed an intake, a confirmed assessment or bill, or appeal evidence showed no active links in its deletion impact; deleting it cascaded the intake and document links away and left confirmed values at `HIGH` confidence with no document. **Schema (edited, not pushed):** four `PropertyRecordLinkEntityType` values (`PROPERTY_TAX_INTAKE`, `PROPERTY_TAX_ASSESSMENT`, `PROPERTY_TAX_BILL`, `PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_EVIDENCE`) and a nullable `sourceRemovedAt` on field evidence, assessment records, bill records and appeal evidence. **Links:** creating an intake links the record to it (same transaction); confirming it links the assessment and bill it created; saving evidence with a supporting document links it to that exact version in the same transaction as the evidence row and removes the link of a source it replaced. `homeRecords.entityExists` knows the four types, so broken-link detection works. **Purge (the worker's delete transaction, before the delete):** `removePropertyTaxSourceContent` marks field evidence, assessments, bills and appeal evidence that depended on the record `sourceRemovedAt` and deletes nothing; the intake, its staged unconfirmed values and the document links go with the record by cascade, as before. A confirmed value is not changed. **Presentation:** a document-sourced value with `sourceRemovedAt` is reconciled as the new canonical state `HOMEOWNER_CONFIRMED_SOURCE_REMOVED` ("Homeowner confirmed; source document removed") and its confidence is shown no higher than `MEDIUM`; the stored confidence is not rewritten, so confidence at confirmation and source availability stay separate. The record list carries `sourceRemovedAt` and the same capped confidence; appeal evidence carries `sourceRemovedAt`, and a new source clears it. An official source is unaffected. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend, `prisma generate` in apps/workers, run `apps/backend/prisma/property-tax-record-links-backfill.pgadmin.sql` (idempotent; adds links for intakes, assessments, bills and evidence that predate this change; it has a dry run and a verify query), and redeploy the backend and the purge worker. **Not here:** confirmed values are not re-labelled for purges that happened before this change (their references are already null and no marker exists); the Home Records deletion-impact screen shows the links but has no tax-specific wording. **Tests:** `tests/unit/propertyTaxSourcePurge.test.js` (3), the intake workflow tests assert the links, the evidence tests assert the link and the replaced-source cleanup; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; frontend `next build` run. Nothing is browser or database verified, and the worker was not type-checked against a regenerated workers Prisma client (an owner step).

**Revision 1.368 — Property Tax: a Home Record as appeal evidence (slice 2 of `docs/architecture/PROPERTY_TAX_EVIDENCE_AND_INTAKE_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`, owner ruling D-T2; 2026-10-13):** Evidence could cite only a link. The canonical service (`upsertEvidence`) now resolves `supportingDocumentId` through `assertEvidenceRecordEligible` (`propertyTaxEvidenceSource.ts`), replacing the earlier "exists in the inventory" check that accepted any visible record or legacy document. **Eligibility is the record's, not its label:** the same property, active (not archived or trashed), visible to the whole household (an owner-only or recipient-selected record is never offered, because the appeal workspace is shared), a current version with a clean scan, and a record type that can support an appeal (tax document, deed, survey, inspection report, photograph, permit, disclosure, closing document, contract, invoice, receipt, utility, or `OTHER`, which is labelled "Other supporting evidence"). A `LEGAL`, `PERSONAL` or any other sensitivity label does not make a record ineligible; the label is shown in the picker and the confirmation. **Ask:** the evidence form gains an optional "Or a document in your home records" select beside the link; exactly one source is required (neither or both is refused); the review shows the document with its kind and sensitivity, and says how many owner-only records were left out when a record is refused; the confirmation saves the record as the supporting document and no link. **Desktop:** the evidence form gains the same select (the page filters with the same rule from the records list, which now includes each record's current-version scan status; the service remains the authority and refuses the rest) and saves either a link or a document, not both. The route's existing `supportingDocumentId` field is the path; no schema change and no new owner step. **Not here:** a Home Record as a comparable's source (the comparable still uses the earlier inventory check), tax document intakes, record links and purge behaviour (slices 3 and 4). **Tests:** `propertyTaxEvidenceCommands.test.js` now 11 (the service's where-clause is asserted, including that no sensitivity filter exists), `lib/propertyTax/__tests__/appealEvidenceRecords.test.ts` (3); backend `tsc --noEmit` and frontend `next build` run; the registry set is at the 10-failure baseline. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.367 — Property Tax: appeal evidence from a link, and web-only source links (parity register P2 "Property Tax", slice 1 of `docs/architecture/PROPERTY_TAX_EVIDENCE_AND_INTAKE_IN_ASK_DESIGN.md`; 2026-10-13):** Ask could add comparable sales but not the evidence the TAX_CLASS and EXEMPTION grounds read. `PROPERTY_TAX_COMPARABLE_COMMAND` gains three declared kinds (no new operation, no schema change; action ids `property-tax-evidence-class`, `-exemption`, `-condition` allowlisted for `PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_READINESS`), offered on the appeal readiness answer by ground: **Add class evidence** (a `FACTUAL_ERROR` row: the class you say is correct and an explicit yes/no that the official record shows a different class; a "no" is refused because nothing then supports a factual error), **Add the exemption decision** (program, a decision from the reviewed rule's accepted list only, a notice date not in the future) and, on any ground, **Add supporting condition evidence (optional)**. The assessed-value ground needs qualified comparables, not an evidence row, so its normal action is the existing comparable; a condition statement is labelled optional and the review says it does not by itself satisfy that ground. A source is required and must be an `http` or `https` address. The primary account holder only (the evidence service and rules are theirs), told before a form. The review lists every value, the source and, when it replaces the earlier row, what that row said; the confirmation is bound to the reviewed rule, the row and its last update and calls `upsertEvidence`, the route's own call. Keys match the desktop (`tax_class-primary`, `exemption-primary`, `assessed_value-primary`) so each surface replaces what the other saved; a condition on another ground uses `<ground>-condition`. **Source links:** `isHttpUrl` (URL parsing, protocol allowlist, no credentials) is applied in the evidence and comparable route schemas and inside `upsertEvidence` and `upsertComparable`; stored rows are not re-validated. **Desktop:** the TAX_CLASS evidence form asked nothing about the mismatch and saved `officialRecordMismatch: true`; it now requires the person to tick that the official record shows a different class and sends that answer. **Not here:** a Home Record as the evidence source, tax document intakes, record links and purge behaviour (slices 2 to 4). **Tests:** `tests/ask/propertyTaxEvidenceCommands.test.js` (7); the readiness no-desktop-links test now expects the third summary action; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; the standard registry set at the 10-failure baseline; frontend `next build` run after the desktop change. The certification check was not re-run: no operation or certified answer changed beyond a description. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.366 — Coverage comparison: add a quote from a document on file (parity register P2 "Coverage Intelligence"; 2026-10-13):** The comparison answer said adding a quote was "not currently available in Ask". `COVERAGE_COMMAND` gains **Add a quote** (kind `ADD_QUOTE`; owner-only like the rest of the command, declared-action only, no new operation and no schema change; the action id `coverage-add-quote` is allowlisted for `COVERAGE_COMPARISON_STATUS`). It mirrors the desktop panel's form: a Home Record chosen from a list (active records, newest first, 50 at most, legacy documents not offered; the canonical service checks the inventory as the primary owner, so owner-only records are valid here), a name, an optional carrier, five amounts in dollars (all-peril deductible, dwelling, personal property and liability limits, annual premium), policy form, valuation basis, a comma-separated endorsements list, an optional page and one statement that the values were checked against the document. At least one value is required. The review lists every value entered and says Ask did not read the document, contacts no insurer, changes no policy and recommends no carrier. **As on the desktop, values not checked stay pending and do not count toward equivalence** (the review states the checked/not-checked choice). The confirmation is bound to the comparison's version (its options and last update), refuses a comparison that is decided or changed since the review, re-validates the stored quote and re-checks the chosen record is still on file, then calls `addCoverageComparisonOption`, the route's own call. With no document on file the answer says so and that Ask cannot upload a file. **Not here:** adding another verified policy term (the route supports it; the desktop panel does not offer it either), document extraction of the quote's values (they are typed, as on the desktop), and the property-level analysis run. **Tests:** `tests/ask/coverageCommands.test.js` now 12 (4 new); the standard registry set is at the 10-failure baseline; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. The certification check was not re-run: no operation or certified answer changed. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.365 — Renovation readiness: the desktop form picks a Home Record (follow-up to v1.357-1.358; 2026-10-13):** The desktop "Verify satisfied" control took a typed document id, so a person had to know a record's identifier and could paste a legacy `Document` id. It is now a select over the property's Home Records, narrowed by `readinessEvidenceOptions` to the same rule Ask uses (v1.358): active, visible to the whole household (an owner-only record is never listed, even to an owner, because the case is shared) and of a standard, financial, insurance or claim sensitivity; newest first. The helper text says how many owner-only records were left out, that the file is not read or verified, and that files are added in Home Records first. The backend resolver is unchanged and remains the authority. **Effect on the legacy branch:** the desktop no longer sends a legacy document id, so the readiness form is no longer a reason to keep the legacy branch in `updateReadinessItem`; it is not removed here, because other callers of the route were not audited. The records list type gains the optional `visibility`, `sensitivity`, `lifecycleStatus` and `createdAt` fields the route already returns. **Tests:** `lib/renovation/__tests__/readinessEvidenceRecords.test.ts` (4). The page itself has no component test. Nothing is browser verified.

**Revision 1.364 — Ask import-graph guardrails restored (2026-10-13):** `askImportGraphGuardrails` had two failing tests that the program's usual verification set did not include. (1) `support/commandInputs.ts` had grown to 495 lines against a 400-line ceiling; the Project Tracker input schemas moved unchanged to `support/projectTrackerInputs.ts`, and a two-line `inputPrimitives.ts` holds the one helper both need so neither module imports the other (the graph stays acyclic). `commandInputs.ts` re-exports the new module, so no importer changed. (2) The facade test required `askHandlerSupport.ts` to re-export every support module. That rule dates from the original split (about ten shared modules); since then about sixty domain command modules (pure forms and validation for one command) are imported directly by their handlers, and re-exporting them through one `export *` facade would only create name collisions (several define `hasValue`, `parse…Answer`, and similar). The rule is now: the facade holds no code and re-exports only modules that exist, and a support module that is not re-exported must be imported by some other file, so dead modules are still caught. This is a deliberate change to the guardrail, made because the rule no longer matched the architecture, not to make a failing test pass; the layering and cycle checks are unchanged. Verification: the guardrail file passes 6 of 6, `tsc` is clean, and the registry suites are at the same 10-failure baseline.

**Revision 1.363 — Quote Comparison: add a quote from a document on file, on the desktop and in Ask (slice 5 of the import design; 2026-10-13):** The two surfaces now use the Home Record import of v1.362. **Desktop:** "Choose quote document" adds the file to Home Records as a quote (household-visible, financial) and imports from that record, instead of the legacy analyzer; a file whose scan has not finished is retried for about 15 seconds; a newer version of a document already in the comparison opens a choice (replace the earlier proposal, or keep both); a document that does not show a provider and a price is refused with what is missing; the imported proposal is unconfirmed and is confirmed through the review dialog of v1.360. The legacy import route and function remain for callers that still send a legacy document id. **Ask:** `QUOTE_COMPARISON_COMMAND` gains an **Add one from a document** summary action (contributor floor, declared-action only, no new operation). It lists only quote, contract and other records that are active and shared with the whole household (an owner-only record is never offered, even to an owner), asks for the replace-or-keep-both choice when an earlier version is already in the comparison, refuses a document whose current version is already imported, and reviews before anything is read ("nothing is confirmed: you review every value afterwards"); the confirmation calls `createQuoteProposalFromRecord` with the household role. The receipt says how many line items and terms were read and that nothing is confirmed, and points at Confirm the facts. With no quote on file the answer says so and that Ask cannot upload a file. The service loads the import lazily because it depends on the document analyzer's model key. **Owner steps:** those of v1.361 and v1.362 (`prisma db push`, `prisma generate` in apps/workers, redeploy backend and purge worker). **Not verified:** the desktop flow in a browser, extraction quality on real quotes, and the scan-retry timing; no component test covers the desktop upload because the workspace client is a single large component. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.362 — Quote Comparison: import a quote from a Home Record (slice 4 of the import design; 2026-10-13):** New `POST …/quote-comparison/workspaces/:workspaceId/quotes/from-record` (contributor floor) and `createQuoteProposalFromRecord`, with no desktop or Ask surface yet (slice 5). It resolves the record for the acting role and requires it to be active, shared with the household and of type `QUOTE`, `CONTRACT` or `OTHER` (an `INVOICE` is refused with its own message); it validates the version belongs to the record and has a clean scan; it runs the quote extraction once if there are no candidates; it assembles the proposal from reviewed values (a Home Records correction arrives as the person's correction, a Home Records confirmation does not count as the affirmation); and it creates the quote **unconfirmed**. **Provenance (P2, D-Q5):** new `QuoteHomeRecordSource` (one per quote, the exact record and version, extractor and schema versions, `supersededByQuoteId`, `sourceRemovedAt`) with a unique index on `(workspaceId, propertyRecordVersionId)`; the legacy `QuoteComparisonExtraction` and its required document relation are untouched. A `PropertyRecordLink` of the new `SERVICE_QUOTE` type (source record, exact version, quote id, purpose `SOURCE`) makes deleting the source show up in its deletion impact. **Journey:** new `HOME_RECORD` entity type, validated inside the same transaction (a record of another property or a trashed one is refused); the quote, provenance, record link, journey context and the advance to scope review are one transaction, and analytics are emitted after it commits. **Launch-blocking requirements:** the same version into the same workspace returns the existing quote (`alreadyImported`); a missing provider, or a missing total and priced line items, refuses with `QUOTE_SOURCE_INCOMPLETE` naming what is missing and creates nothing (a total is taken from line items only when every line item has its own total, and the quote says so); a newer version of a record already in the comparison returns `QUOTE_SOURCE_VERSION_CHOICE_REQUIRED` until the caller chooses `REPLACE` (the earlier quote is rejected and keeps its provenance, marked superseded) or `RETAIN_BOTH`. **Purge (D-Q7):** `removeQuoteSourceContent` runs in the purge worker inside the same transaction that deletes the record: it deletes line items, terms and facts that were only extracted, scrubs source text, page and the raw extracted value from what a person confirmed or corrected, recomputes readiness from what remains, and stamps `sourceRemovedAt`; a purge blocked by a legal hold removes nothing. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend (new table, `HOME_RECORD` and `SERVICE_QUOTE` enum values) and `prisma generate` in apps/workers, then redeploy the backend and the purge worker. **Not done:** the desktop upload and the Ask import (slice 5); the unconfirmed headline values on a quote whose source is purged remain on the (unconfirmed, therefore not comparable) quote row because those columns are required. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.361 — Home Records: quote extraction (slice 3 of the Quote Comparison Home Record import design; 2026-10-13):** Home Records could extract only warranties, receipts and insurance policies, and the general analyzer has no line items, terms, scope or dates. This slice adds the quote extraction contract and creates no quote. Changes: (1) New `QUOTE` value on `PropertyRecordType` and `SERVICE_QUOTE` on `ExtractedFactTargetDomain` (the record type is accepted by the Home Records upload route and labelled "Quote or estimate" in the Home Records page; the AI-review panel there is unchanged). (2) `serviceQuoteExtraction.ts`: a quote-specific prompt, a typed response schema, validation of the model's reply (a reply that is empty, not JSON, or off-schema is a failed parse; individual unreadable line items and terms are dropped and counted, with caps of 100 line items and 50 terms), the minimum-evidence rule (a confident classification as a quote plus a provider and either a total or a priced line item), and the mapping into reviewable candidates: the headline facts, one candidate per line item and per term (a JSON value), each carrying its page and source text. (3) `documentIntelligenceService.analyzeServiceQuote` makes the model call with the prompt and returns the validated parse. (4) `runExtraction` extracts a `QUOTE` record, and an existing `CONTRACT` or `OTHER` record, with the quote contract; it refuses and stores nothing when the file does not read as a quote (`PROPERTY_RECORD_EXTRACTION_NOT_A_QUOTE`) or cannot be read (`…_UNREADABLE`), so the type can be corrected and tried again; it refuses an `INVOICE` without a model call (`…_INVOICE_NOT_QUOTE`); a quote that is missing a required fact is stored with what was found, and the import will refuse to build from it. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend (two enum values) and `prisma generate` in apps/workers. **Not verified:** extraction quality on real quotes (multi-page line items, totals, terms, page attribution) needs a live model and sample documents; the tests cover the contract, not the model. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.360 — Quote Comparison: confirming a quote affirms exactly what was shown (slice 2 of the Home Record import design; 2026-10-13):** `confirmQuoteProposal` marked every unconfirmed line item, term and fact confirmed in one transaction whether or not the page had shown it, and the desktop's "Review and confirm" button confirmed immediately with no review. Changes: (1) `quoteReview.ts` defines the set a confirmation covers (headline facts, every line item, every term, every extracted fact, each with its state: as extracted, corrected by you, already confirmed) and a fingerprint of it. (2) A new `GET …/quotes/:quoteId/review` returns that set. (3) `POST …/confirm` now requires a body naming the fingerprint and the identifiers shown, plus any line items or terms the person removed. The service re-reads the proposal inside the transaction and refuses with `QUOTE_REVIEW_STALE` (409) if the stored set differs from the request in any way (a value changed, was added, or is missing); it deletes removed values before confirming and computes readiness from what remains. A confirm with no body is rejected. (4) Editing a proposal now keeps the difference between extracted and corrected: a headline fact the person changed is stored as their correction with the extracted value beside it, an unchanged one waits for affirmation, and a line item or term that is new or changed is marked corrected (previously every fact was reset to "extracted"). (5) Desktop: "Review and confirm" opens a dialog listing every value with its state and a Remove control per line item and term; the button states the counts it affirms. (6) Ask: the Confirm-the-facts review lists every headline fact, line item and term in a list above the confirmation card (the confirmation card itself is limited to 12 fields) and the confirmation carries the same request; a proposal with more than 100 line items or terms is refused with the reason, because Ask cannot show every value. **Known limits:** Ask cannot remove or correct a line item or term (it says so on the review; the desktop dialog can remove, and correcting a headline fact is Edit on either surface); the shell imports are unchanged, so the richer extractor is still not shipped (design slice 4 depends on this contract). Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.359 — Renovation scope documents accept Home Records (domain slice 2 of the Home Records evidence migration; 2026-10-13):** Scope drawing and specification documents were validated only against the legacy `Document` table (`assertDocumentsInProperty`), so a household with only Home Records could not attach one. Changes: (1) Creating a case with an initial scope and creating a scope version now resolve every document id through the shared property-document resolver with the acting member's role: a Home Record the member can see passes (record-level visibility applies), and a legacy Document is still accepted until none is sent; an id that resolves in neither is refused with the same `RENOVATION_SCOPE_DOCUMENT_MISMATCH`. (2) The scope ids are bare strings with no store marker, so the contract readiness item now looks the first specification id up (Home Record first) instead of assuming legacy, and records the result as its evidence source. (3) **Project handoff finding:** the project's `contractDocumentIds` are read from the legacy store (the Project Tracker reads `Document` rows for name, file and size), so a Home Record id carried into it would dangle. The handoff therefore carries over only ids that are legacy Documents and names the rest in the project description ("N scope documents are Home Records and stay on the renovation case") instead of dropping them silently. Moving the project's own documents to Home Records is a separate, larger change and is not done. No schema change and no owner steps beyond v1.357's. Not covered by a test: the readiness derivation lookup and the handoff filter (the derivation and handoff need the full case fixture); the scope validation is covered. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.358 — Renovation readiness: mark an item satisfied with a document (parity register P1 "Renovation Risk Advisor"; 2026-10-13):** Built on v1.357, which moved the canonical service to Home Records. `RENOVATION_READINESS_COMMAND` gains a **Mark satisfied with a document** row action (contributor floor, declared-action only, no new operation). It is offered on an open item whose rules allow manual satisfaction (source-controlled items are not offered it). The form is a picker over the Home Records the whole household can already see (active, household visibility, an allowed sensitivity; an owner-only record is never offered, even to an owner, because the case is shared; legacy documents are not offered) plus what the member checked. The review shows the item, the record and its kind and says the file is not read or verified and that readiness organizes records and does not establish compliance. The confirmation re-checks that the record is still offered and calls `updateReadinessItem`, which resolves it again with the member's role and stores its source. With no eligible record the answer says so, and how many owner-only records were left out, instead of leaving a dead end; uploading is not in Ask. Nothing is browser or database verified; the v1.357 owner steps (`prisma db push`, `prisma generate` in workers, the backfill SQL) apply before this is used against the real database.

**Revision 1.357 — Renovation readiness evidence moves to Home Records (domain slice 1 of the Home Records evidence migration; 2026-10-13):** Not an Ask change: Ask's "mark satisfied" command (next step) needs the canonical service to accept Home Records, so the service moved first. Findings: a readiness item's `evidenceDocumentId` is a plain string with no foreign key and was already mixed. Requirement and compliance-condition sources are Home Records (`resolvePropertyDocuments`, a `PropertyRecord` relation), while the manual "mark satisfied" check and the scope documents (`assertDocumentsInProperty`) read only the legacy `Document` table. Changes: (1) New nullable `RenovationStartReadinessItem.evidenceSource` (`HOME_RECORD` | `LEGACY_DOCUMENT`, new enum `RenovationEvidenceSource`); derivation sets it per source (requirement and condition: Home Record; scope specification document: legacy) and a manually satisfied item keeps its own source across re-derivation. (2) `updateReadinessItem` resolves the evidence through the shared property-document resolver with the acting member's role, so record-level visibility applies and an id the member cannot see is refused; a legacy Document id is still accepted, because the desktop form takes a free-text id, and is stored as `LEGACY_DOCUMENT`; the readiness event payload records the id and its source. Source-controlled items remain unchangeable. (3) `prisma/renovation-evidence-source-backfill.pgadmin.sql` classifies existing rows (dry run, apply, list of the unclassified), idempotent. **Owner steps:** `prisma db push` in apps/backend and `prisma generate` in apps/workers, then run the backfill SQL in pgAdmin. **Left for later:** scope drawing and specification documents still validate against the legacy store, so a household with only Home Records cannot attach one there; the desktop form is still a free-text id and should become a record picker; the legacy branch is removable once no caller sends a legacy id. Quote Comparison import is separate (its extraction table has a non-null foreign key to `Document`). Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.356 — UI review pass for the buyer and tax tool cards (2026-10-13):** The cards added in v1.351-v1.355 use the shared renderer, so the review checked what that renderer does with them. Findings and fixes: (1) A workflow form's helper line read "…nothing is saved until you confirm after you continue." because the renderer appended "after you continue." to every destination label. For `WORKFLOW_INPUT` captures (every command form, since a review always follows) the label is now shown as written, ending in a full stop; other classifications keep the suffix. (2) The longest forms were one tall column: the Closing Disclosure figures (12 fields), a lender offer or Loan Estimate draft (19 fields with the lender), the inspection plan (11) and the funds readiness form (7). They now declare `groups`, which the renderer already steps through with a progress bar and "Next: <step>", so each step is three to seven fields; every field belongs to a step (asserted). Short forms (one to three fields) stay single or conversational as before. (3) Checked and left alone: summary actions are `START_WORKFLOW` (the only summary action type the renderer draws, which is why they are not `MUTATE_RECORD`), row actions are `MUTATE_RECORD` and drawn as buttons, the per-figure confirmation is a multi-select that is not asked one at a time, and the confirmation card shows the description (where the wire-safety text lives) above the field list. Not done: the shared form still has no per-field helper text under individual inputs, and no live browser check was run. Frontend: the four pre-existing failing suites are unchanged (askWorkspaceSplitGuardrails, capitalTimelineTrack, displayPatterns, maintenanceShelves).

**Revision 1.355 — Property Tax: add a comparable sale (parity register P2 "Property Tax"; 2026-10-13):** Comparable sales could be entered on the Property Tax page but not from Ask. New `PROPERTY_TAX_COMPARABLE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered as **Add a comparable sale** on the appeal readiness answer. It asks the address, sale date (never in the future), price, property class and, optionally, living area, lot size and condition, then calls `propertyTaxAppealReadinessService.upsertComparable`, the comparable route's own call. The key is derived from the address and sale date, so the same sale replaces its earlier entry rather than adding a copy. The review says Cozy does not verify the sale or compute an adjustment for it and nothing is filed; an uncovered jurisdiction is explained rather than erroring. Not here: adjustments, a source link or document, the appeal evidence entries and the document intakes (they need a record picker and the reviewed rule's evidence shape). Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Not built, with the reason (2026-10-13):** Renovation "mark a readiness item satisfied" needs an evidence document, and `updateReadinessItem` validates it against the legacy `Document` table, which Home Records are not in (the same constraint that blocks Quote Comparison import); Permits "record an official status" is deliberately never set from Ask (v1.315); Renovation project handoff is owner-only and creates a project record, which needs its own design.

**Revision 1.354 — Inspection Hub: archive a report (parity register P2 "Inspection Hub"; 2026-10-13):** The reports answer could confirm and compare a report but not archive it. `INSPECTION_REPORT_COMMAND` gains an **Archive this report** action (contributor floor, declared-action only, `MUTATE_RECORD` row action on a report that has been read: waiting for review or confirmed; a report still being read, or already archived, is refused). The review says archiving removes the report from the inspection reports and from the counts that include it, that its open findings stay on file, that nothing is deleted and nobody is contacted, and that Ask cannot restore it (the Inspection Hub has no restore). The confirmation is bound to the report's status, last update and open findings and calls `archiveReport`, the route's own call. No new operation, so no registry change beyond the allowlisted action id. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.353 — Buyer Closing: payment path, inspection plan and title or escrow contact (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** Three small buyer records had a desktop form and no Ask path. New `BUYER_PLAN_SETUP_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered from the answers each belongs to: **Set how I am paying** (cash or financed; on the lender offers answer; `BuyerAcquisitionService.updatePurchaseFinancingPlan`; switching to cash says it clears the selected lender offer and intent to proceed, and the offers stay on file), **Update my inspection plan** (on the inspection review answer; `updateInspectionPlan`: inspection, completion, report-due and contingency dates, access and scope notes, specialist inspections, an added question, reinspection needed and its dates; the review lists only real changes and refuses a change equal to what is recorded) and **Update my title or escrow contact** (on the title and escrow readiness answer; `BuyerTitleEscrowService.update`, contact only; the review says it is shared with the household, that Ask does not contact the person, and notes that look like account or routing numbers are refused). Ask records dates, not times of day. Not here: inspection attendees, the reinspection proof document, removing a contact and the other title workspace fields. The buyer status/document/inspection no-desktop-link test now asserts actions are Ask commands rather than empty. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.352 — Buyer Closing: purchase Loan Estimates (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** Ask had no view of the buyer's lender offers. New read `BUYER_LOAN_ESTIMATES` (viewer floor; "Show my lender offers", "Compare my loan estimates"; refinance wording is excluded) reading `BuyerPurchaseLoanEstimateService.list`: each offer's draft and confirmed revisions, the selected offer and the lowest confirmed APR with a note that a lower APR is not always the better offer. New `BUYER_LOAN_ESTIMATE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change) offered as actions on that answer when the purchase is financed: **Add a lender offer** (`createOffer`), **Start a new revision** (`addRevision`, copying the latest confirmed figures and leaving the issued date blank), **Edit the draft** (`updateDraft`), **Confirm the figures** (`confirm`) and **Select this offer** (`selectOffer`). Confirmation is per figure: the buyer ticks each of the nine required figures against the document, each shown with its value, and there is no blanket confirm. Selecting an offer is a planning record: intent to proceed is a separate explicit yes/no that defaults to no; the review says Ask does not apply for a loan, lock a rate or contact a lender, that a different selection resets appraisal tracking, that an expired recorded rate lock is expired, and that confirming a new revision of the selected offer clears the selection. Not here: document extraction, the source document link and the five-year figures. Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.351 — Buyer Closing: Closing Disclosure revisions and funds readiness (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** The disclosure and funds readiness answer was read-only. New `BUYER_CLOSING_DISCLOSURE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered as actions on `BUYER_DISCLOSURE_FUNDS_READINESS`: **Start a revision** (`BuyerClosingDisclosureService.createRevision`, copying the confirmed figures but leaving the issued date blank to re-enter), **Edit the draft** (`updateDraft`: issued date, the nine money and rate figures, cash-to-close direction, change explanation), **Confirm the draft figures** (`confirm`) and **Update funds readiness** (`updateFundsReadiness`: method, expected date, ready, verification channel, instructions verified, questions, questions resolved). Wire-fraud safeguards: confirmation is per figure (the buyer ticks each of the ten recorded figures against the document, each shown with its value and its difference from the selected Loan Estimate; there is no blanket confirm and all ten are required because the service requires all ten); funds readiness needs a Closing Disclosure workspace first; instructions cannot be marked verified without a named trusted channel; free text that looks like an account or routing number is refused; every review and the confirmation card say Ask never tells anyone to close and never asks the buyer to trust emailed wire instructions, and that no account, routing or wire credentials are stored. A missing selected Loan Estimate is explained, not an error. Not here: source document extraction and the professional close (held). Nothing is browser or database verified.

**Revision 1.350 — Documents and Home Records: record state (parity register P2 "Documents and Home Records"; 2026-10-13):** The document answer listed Home Records and offered no controls on them. New `HOME_RECORD_STATE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered as row actions on a Home Record to contributors and owners: **Archive** (`HomeRecordsService.archive`; it is kept with its versions and links, not deleted), **Record verification** (`setVerification`: not verified, pending, verified, rejected, with notes; the review says Ask did not read or verify the record and that Verified is the buyer's own mark) and **Set when it is in effect** (`setEffectivePeriod`, end not before start). The service applies record-level visibility; the command re-reads the record and refuses a trashed one, and the confirmation is bound to its lifecycle, verification, period and last update. **Not built, deliberately:** trash (it needs a decision about the links that evidence depends on and starts a purge clock, so it wants its own design), retention and legal hold (owner-only), versions, uploads (the analyzer boundary), links, saved searches, and extraction candidates other than the existing promotion flow. Legacy vault rows carry no controls. **Tests:** `tests/ask/homeRecordStateCommands.test.js` (4). Not browser- or database-verified.\n\n**Revision 1.349 — Buyer Closing: contract revisions (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** The contract timeline answer was read-only. New `BUYER_CONTRACT_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered on that answer: **Start a revision** (a draft copied from the confirmed revision, or from the property address when none), **Edit the draft** (acceptance, target closing and possession dates, earnest money and who holds it, seller credits, special conditions; a blank keeps the value), **Update a contingency** in the draft (status and deadline; the service replaces the draft's contingency list, so Ask sends the list with that one change) and **Confirm the draft**. Writes are `BuyerContractService.createRevision / updateDraft / confirm`, the routes' own calls. Confirmation keeps the desktop's per-field attestation, not one blanket confirmation: the buyer chooses which of the draft's held fields they checked against the signed contract, the property address is always required, and only the chosen fields move the plan (the closing date and the deadlines counted from it, the accepted and possession milestones, contingency deadlines); the review names what moves and says ContractToCozy does not review contracts and this waives nothing. A contingency marked satisfied or waived in the draft is the buyer's own note and does not touch the seller. The timeline answer now shows the draft and its contingencies, and offers the controls to contributors. **Not built:** source documents and extraction, buyer and seller names, included and excluded items, agreed repairs, and adding or removing contingencies. **Tests:** `tests/ask/buyerContractCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.\n\n**Revision 1.348 — Buyer Closing: closing insurance (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** Ask had no view of the buyer's closing insurance. Two new operations (registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). `BUYER_INSURANCE_READINESS` (read, viewer floor, typed: "show my insurance quotes for closing", "insurance binder", "insurance requirements before closing") reads `BuyerInsuranceService.get`: the requirements, the quotes, the chosen quote, the bound policy and the lender and closing proof status. `BUYER_INSURANCE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only): **Add a requirement** and **Update where it stands**, **Record a quote** (insurer, annual premium, deductible, valid until, what it excludes; dollars become cents), **Choose this quote**, **Record that it was bound** (policy number, effective and expiration dates) and **Update proof** (for the lender and for closing). Every write is `BuyerInsuranceService` (`createRequirement`, `updateRequirement`, `createQuote`, `selectQuote`, `bind`, `update`), the routes' own calls, behind a review and a confirmation bound to the workspace, its quotes and requirements, the chosen quote and the bound policy. The binding is the material one: its review says it records a policy the buyer already bound with their insurer and creates the policy record on the home, and that Ask did not buy or bind it and cannot cancel it; the service's own refusals (a chosen quote, an annual premium, an unexpired quote, dates in order) are checked at review so nothing fails after the confirmation. The answer's boundary says Ask records what the buyer did with their insurer and the lender decides whether proof is enough. **Not built:** the insurance contact, binder and quote documents, and quote coverage limits and endorsements notes. **Tests:** `tests/ask/buyerInsuranceCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.\n\n**Revision 1.347 — Buyer Closing: appraisal and underwriting status (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** `BUYER_CLOSING_ITEM_COMMAND` gains two kinds, offered as **Update appraisal status** and **Update underwriting status** on the financing readiness answer. Appraisal: status (not ordered, ordered, scheduled, completed, a problem reported, resolved), the ordered, appointment and completion dates, and what the problem was; underwriting: not started, in review, conditional, or clear to close as the buyer recorded it. Both call `BuyerPurchaseLenderReadinessService.updateReadiness`, the route's own call. The service's rules are checked at review so nothing fails after the confirmation: an appointment date is required to mark it scheduled, a completion date to mark it completed, a description to report a problem, and the dates must be in order. Recording clear to close is the buyer's own note; the review says the lender issues it, not Ask, and that Ask does not tell anyone whether to close. Like the lender conditions it needs a financed purchase with a selected loan estimate. **Not built:** the title workspace's contact and requirements, documents on items, insurance, contract revisions, Closing Disclosure revisions and funds readiness. **Tests:** `buyerClosingItemCommands.test.js` now 6. Not browser- or database-verified.\n\n**Revision 1.346 — Buyer Closing: lender conditions and title or escrow issues (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** The financing and title readiness answers listed blocking items and offered nothing. New `BUYER_CLOSING_ITEM_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change): **Add a lender condition** and **Add a title or escrow issue** (kind, a few words, details, due date, whether it holds up closing) and **Update where it stands** on an open item (lender conditions: open, submitted, satisfied, set aside; title issues: open, with a professional, resolved, set aside). Every write is `BuyerPurchaseLenderReadinessService.createCondition / updateCondition` or `BuyerTitleEscrowService.createIssue / updateIssue`, the routes' own calls, behind a review with before and after and a confirmation bound to the tracker's items; the services enforce the contributor floor, and a lender condition needs a financed purchase with a selected loan estimate, which Ask says rather than failing after the confirmation. A settled item is left as recorded. The answers now list every open item (blocking first, marked "Holds up closing") instead of only the blocking ones, so an item can be updated. Setting an item aside or resolving it records the buyer's own reading; the lender or title professional decides, and the review and receipt say that Ask contacts no one and does not tell anyone whether to close. **Not built:** appraisal and underwriting status, the title workspace's contact and requirements, documents on items, and insurance requirements and quotes (own slice). **Tests:** `tests/ask/buyerClosingItemCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.345 — Buyer Closing: the final walkthrough (parity register P2 "Buyer Closing"; 2026-10-13):** The walkthrough readiness answer listed unresolved issues and offered nothing. New `BUYER_WALKTHROUGH_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change): **Update the plan** (date, access confirmed, utilities on), **Record an observation** (where, what kind of check, what you found, notes), **Record the result** of an observation, **Record an issue** from a problem observation (kind, a few words, details, whether it holds up closing; tied to that observation as the service requires), **Record what is being done** about an open issue (sent to your agent, attorney, title or escrow, resolved, or accepted as is) and **Mark complete**. Every write is `BuyerWalkthroughService` (`update`, `createObservation`, `updateObservation`, `createIssue`, `updateIssue`, `complete`), the routes' own calls, behind a review with before and after and a confirmation bound to the record's status and last update; the service enforces its contributor floor and its rules, and **Mark complete** is offered only when its rules would accept and otherwise says what stands in the way. The answer now lists the observations and issues with these controls, and Ask never tells anyone to close, delay or withhold funds (the boundary is unchanged). **Fixed in passing:** the readiness answer counted a routed or accepted-as-is issue as unresolved (it filtered for statuses the service does not use); an issue is open only while its status is open, which is the service's own completion rule. **Not built:** evidence documents on observations and issues, and attendees. **Verification note (Guidance Overview, no change):** the plan editing endpoints the desktop client defines but no page calls (block a step, change a journey's issue type) are not parity gaps; the page's remaining guidance controls (repair-or-replace branch, model shortlist, vendor suggestions) are either already in Ask or are Gemini advisors, which stay outside Ask under the LLM-last-resort rule. **Tests:** `tests/ask/buyerWalkthroughCommands.test.js` (6; the readiness answer reads the buyer plan context and is covered by its rules, not end to end). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.344 — DIY: the person's own skill levels and a note on a project (parity register P2 "DIY"; 2026-10-13):** Two new operations (registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). `DIY_SKILL_PROFILE_COMMAND` is a **caller-scoped** command (the third; viewer floor by construction): the DIY skill profile is one row per user and the route has no property or role, so a viewer may set their own levels and nothing changes for anyone else. A nine-area form (beginner, intermediate or advanced) seeded from the person's own profile, a review of each change (before and after), then `DiyService.upsertSkillProfile`, the route's own call; tools owned and the quiz answers are left as they are, and an unchanged form is refused. It is offered as **Set my skill levels** on the DIY projects answer to every member. `DIY_PROJECT_NOTE_COMMAND` (contributor floor) is **Add a note** on a project in planning or in progress (three to 1,000 characters, at most 100 notes), through `DiyService.updateProject`, the route's own call; because the route replaces the whole notes list, Ask sends the list it just read with the new note appended, and a change in between stales the confirmation. `callerScopedCommandClass`'s pin of the caller-scoped set now lists the new command (a deliberate act). **Not built:** the DIY decision tool and AI guide generation (the AI guide is the LLM path), step bulk editing and planning visuals (page presentation), project photos, and the retry endpoints (completion recovery already exists). **Tests:** `tests/ask/diyCommands.test.js` (5); `diyProjectsCapabilitySlice` updated for the summary action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.343 — Seasonal checklist task controls (parity register P2 "Seasonal Home Care"; 2026-10-13):** The seasonal maintenance answer listed tasks but offered nothing on them. New `SEASONAL_ITEM_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), offered as row actions on each seasonal task: **Add to maintenance** (`addSeasonalTaskToMaintenance`: needs an existing-home setup and a task not already linked), **Snooze** (one, two or four weeks; `SeasonalChecklistService.snoozeTask`, which also snoozes the linked work item), **Set aside** (`dismissTask`; never a high-priority task; it cancels the linked maintenance task) and **Bring back** (`restoreTask`; it reactivates a cancelled linked task). The review shows the effect on the linked maintenance task. **Disclosed difference:** the snooze, set-aside and bring-back services filter on the home's primary account holder, although the route's ownership middleware lets any member through; Ask offers those three only to the primary account holder and says so, rather than letting the service refuse after the confirmation. Completing a seasonal task is completing its linked maintenance task, which Ask already does from the maintenance list, so it is not repeated. **Not built:** add all critical tasks, remove from maintenance, dismiss a whole checklist. **Tests:** `tests/ask/seasonalItemCommands.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.342 — Home Operations work items: list, snooze, reschedule and routine bulk-accept (parity register P2 "Maintenance and Home Operations"; 2026-10-13):** The Home Operations work-item API had no Ask surface beyond the ranked Home Actions feed. Two new operations (registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). `HOME_WORK_ITEMS` (read, viewer floor, message-routable: "show my home operations work items") lists open work (not closed, verified or replaced), soonest due first, 40 at most, with state, due date, snooze and whether routine. `HOME_WORK_ITEM_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only): **Snooze** (one week, two weeks, one month, or stop; `snoozeWorkItem`, which touches only the reminder date), **Change due date** (one exact date; `rescheduleWorkItem`, which replaces the date and records the previous one) and **Accept routine suggestions** (a summary action when low-consequence candidates exist; `batchTransitionWorkItems`, the batch route's own usecase, 50 at most, listing each item in the review). The review shows the item and the before and after, and says nothing is scheduled or contacted. Each change is bound to the item's state, due fields and snooze; the batch to the exact eligible set and each item's state, and the confirmation refuses if that set changed. **Not built:** assign, watchers, duplicate, evidence, material approval and completion (each a separate flow with its own evidence or role rules), clearing a due date, and a due date on the batch. **Process note:** the previous commit (`a6dd3bab`) shipped with `askTrustArchitecture`'s direct-answer certification failing, because the new negotiation-package operation's certified answer shared too little wording with its question; this change fixes the wording. **Tests:** `tests/ask/homeWorkItemCommands.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.341 — Inspection Hub: open-items filters and the negotiation package (parity register P2 "Inspection Hub"; 2026-10-13):** (1) The open inspection findings answer now reads the page's open-items filters from the message: a severity (safety, major, minor, monitor, informational) and a home system (roof, plumbing, electrical, HVAC, foundation and the rest), alone or together ("show my major roof inspection findings"); the title and the empty state name the filter. The list stays all confirmed reports, open or accepted as is, 50 at most, so it is the page's open items plus accepted-as-is. (2) New `INSPECTION_NEGOTIATION_PACKAGE` (read class, contributor floor as the route has; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change), reached only by **Build a negotiation package** on a confirmed pre-purchase report row. A form with one choice per open finding (ask for a credit, request a repair, accept as is; most serious 25) then `InspectionHubService.generateNegotiationPackage`, the page's own pure calculation, shown as the credit total (the middle of each estimate) and the three lists. The route persists nothing, so Ask saves and sends nothing and has no confirmation; the answer says so and that an estimate is not a quote.
