The form is bound to the report's open findings and their last updates; a changed list or an incomplete answer re-asks. **Not built:** a document or an email to the seller; archive of a report. **Tests:** `tests/ask/inspectionFindingDecisions.test.js` now 8 (filters, package form and calculation, answer shape); report-list test updated for the new row action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.340 — Inspection Hub: one report's findings, keep an eye on a finding, and the seller's fix-or-disclose plan (parity register P2 "Inspection Hub"; 2026-10-13):** Two new operations (registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). `INSPECTION_REPORT_FINDINGS` (read, viewer floor, launched from a confirmed report row, which now offers **See its findings**) lists one report's findings, most serious first, in still-open and resolved-or-dismissed sections, with severity, estimated cost, work disposition and, for a pre-listing report, the seller's recorded plan. `INSPECTION_FINDING_DECISION_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only) offers on each finding the decisions its state allows: **Keep an eye on it** (the page's Monitor disposition, through `InspectionHubService.dispositionFindingAsWork`, the route's own call: the work item is accepted and deferred, to come back for review in about 90 days; it is not offered once a finding is monitored or accepted) and, on a pre-listing report only, **Plan to fix**, **Disclose with a price adjustment** and **Disclose with a credit** (through `saveFixDisclosureDecisions`, the route's own call; an earlier cost estimate and seller note are kept). The review shows the finding and the before and after; nothing is sent to a buyer, agent or contractor, and it says so. **Disclosed difference:** the page's plan save replaces a finding's resolution-notes field, so a finding that already has notes of its own (for example how it was resolved) is not offered a plan in Ask; the page would overwrite them. **Not built:** duplicate (needs a work-item picker), correction requested, the negotiation package (a pure calculation with a per-finding decision form), archive, open items across reports with filters. **Tests:** `tests/ask/inspectionFindingDecisions.test.js` (5); the report-list test updated for the new row action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.339 — Inspection Hub: the reports, the comparison and review-and-confirm (parity register P2 "Inspection Hub"; 2026-10-13):** Ask worked on findings but had no view of the reports themselves, no comparison and no way to confirm a report. Three new operations (registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). `INSPECTION_REPORTS` (read, viewer floor, message-routable: "show my inspection reports") lists the home's reports (not archived) newest first with type, date, inspector, state (being read, waiting for review, confirmed) and finding counts. `INSPECTION_REPORT_COMPARE` (read, viewer floor, launched from a report row only) compares a confirmed report with the confirmed one before it using the page's own `compareReports`: resolved, still open, new, worse. `INSPECTION_REPORT_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only) is **Review and confirm** on a report waiting for review: the review shows the findings that will be trusted by severity and the page's own write-back preview (home condition records updated, permit flags added, coverage gaps flagged); the confirmation runs `applyWriteBacks`, the confirm route's own call. Confirming makes the findings trusted and turns actionable findings into candidate work; it accepts no work and sends nothing, and the review says so. The confirmation is bound to the report's status and last update and to each open finding's status and last update. **Disclosed differences:** Ask offers confirm only on a report waiting for review (the route also accepts one still being read); the comparison pairs a report with the one before it rather than any two. **Not built:** report upload and reading (analyzer boundary), archive, the negotiation package, the fix-or-disclose decisions, open items across reports with filters, and the work disposition of a finding; each is its own slice. Caught by the startup-registry test before it could reach boot: the command first shipped with one positive example. **Tests:** `tests/ask/inspectionReportCommands.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.338 — Seller Prep: the sale case itself (parity register P2 "Seller Prep"; 2026-10-13):** Ask decided checklist items but could not start the sale case or change its dates, budget range or stage, and the no-case answer said starting one was "not currently available in Ask". One new operation, `SELLER_PREP_CASE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change): **Start a sale case** (only when the property is marked for sale and no case exists), **Set target dates** (exact dates, closing not before listing, a blank keeps a date), **Set prep budget range** (the four ranges or none) and **Update sale stage** (only the legal next step: one forward in preparing, listed, under contract, closed, or cancel while the sale is not over, the service's own order mirrored so the form offers nothing it would refuse). Each goes through `PropertySaleCaseService.createCase / updateTargetDates / updateBudgetRange / transitionStatus`, the routes' own calls, behind a review that shows the exact before and after and a confirmation bound to the case's status, dates, budget and last update. A stage change records where the household's sale stands; it lists, contracts or closes nothing with anyone, and the review and receipt say so. The actions sit on the checklist's summary (and **Start a sale case** on the no-case answer), to contributors and owners only. **Not built:** the buyer-package hand-off and the notable-upgrades confirmation. **Tests:** `tests/ask/sellerPrepCaseCommands.test.js` (5); `sellerPrepCapabilitySlice` updated for the new no-case copy and action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.337 — Claims: the checklist and notes (parity register P2 "Claims"; 2026-10-13):** Ask could file a claim and move its status; the page also works through the claim's checklist and keeps a timeline. Two new operations. `CLAIM_CHECKLIST_VIEW` (a read, viewer floor) shows a claim's checklist in order with each item's status and whether it is required, launched only from a claim row. `CLAIM_ACTIVITY_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change) is **Mark done**, **Not applicable** and **Reopen** on an item (no form; the step an item allows is one function, `claimChecklistKinds`) and **Add a note** on the claim (one text field, three to 2,000 characters). They go through `ClaimsService.updateChecklistItem` and `addTimelineEvent`, the routes' own calls, behind a review and a confirmation; the checklist step also recomputes the claim's completion percentage and writes its timeline line, as the route does. Nothing is sent to an insurer or provider, and the review and the receipt say so; the receipt repeats no note text. Every claim row now offers **Checklist** (to any member; a viewer sees the list without steps), after the status transitions for a contributor. The change is bound to the item's status and completion time, or to the claim's last activity. **Not built:** uploading and attaching documents (binary files and a record picker, so they stay where documents are uploaded) and regenerating a checklist (it deletes the items and their progress); the CSV export is a download, not a conversation. **Tests:** `tests/ask/claimActivityCommands.test.js` (5); two claims tests updated for the extra row action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.336 — Do-Nothing Simulator: save, change and delete scenarios (parity register P2 "Do-Nothing Simulator"; 2026-10-13):** Ask could run the simulator and read scenarios; the page also creates, edits and deletes them. New confirmation-gated `DO_NOTHING_SCENARIO_COMMAND` (contributor floor; declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The simulator answer gains **Add a scenario** (summary action) and **Change** and **Delete** on each saved scenario, for a contributor or owner. One form (name; 6, 12, 24 or 36 months; skip routine maintenance; let warranties lapse; deductible strategy; cash buffer in dollars; risk tolerance) → a review listing the name, horizon and each assumption (or "the simulator's defaults") → `DoNothingSimulatorService.createScenario`, `updateScenario` and `deleteScenario`, the routes' own calls. **The service replaces a scenario's overrides wholesale**, so an edit sends the current overrides merged with the changed ones and a blank keeps the recorded value (otherwise changing one assumption would silently drop the rest). A scenario is a set of assumptions: the review and the receipt say that saving, changing or deleting one **runs no simulation** and that runs already computed are kept on a delete. The service keeps scenarios for the home's primary account holder, so for anyone else Ask says so. The change is bound to the scenario's name, horizon, overrides and last update. **Role floors:** the three writing scenario routes gain `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` (the run route is unchanged). **Not built:** the "ignore these risks" list, which the page edits by risk id. **Tests:** `tests/ask/doNothingScenarioCommands.test.js` (5); the simulator slice test updated for the second summary action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.335 — Service Price Radar: the register's gap does not exist on the desktop; the save route gains its floor (parity register P2 "Service Price Radar"; 2026-10-13):** the register listed "saved-check correction and deletion and richer history". Traced against the desktop router: it has exactly four routes (list checks, read one check, track an interaction event, save a check). There is **no edit or delete of a saved check** and no history beyond the list and the single read, both of which Ask shows (`SERVICE_PRICE_CHECKS`), so there is nothing to bring to parity and building correction or deletion in Ask would be new behavior, not parity; it is recorded as closed. **One real finding fixed:** `POST .../service-price-radar/checks` persists a record on the household's property with only property membership, so a viewer could save checks; it now requires a contributor, matching the Ask command that runs a check (`SERVICE_PRICE_CHECK_RUN`, contributor floor). The interaction-event route tracks the caller's own use and stays open to any member. **Tests:** `tests/unit/servicePriceRadarRoleFloor.test.js` (2). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.334 — Home Habit Coach: the property-wide settings (parity register P2 "Home Habit Coach"; 2026-10-13):** the register's one remaining gap (verified 2026-10-12) was the coach's property-wide preferences. New confirmation-gated `HOME_HABIT_PREFERENCES_COMMAND` (contributor floor, as the `PATCH /home-habits/preferences` route is; declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The habits answer gains **Change coach settings** for a contributor or owner. One form (the coach on or off; how many habits to show at once, 1 to 20 or blank for the coach's own default; which of the ten kinds of habit to hide; seeded with what is recorded) → a before-and-after review showing only what changes → `HomeHabitCoachService.updatePreferences`, the route's own call. The settings are the household's, shared by every member, which the review says; habits already adopted into the routine are not removed. The read side never creates the settings row (`getPreferences` returns defaults), so the first save is the first write. The change is bound to the settings row's last update. **Not built:** snooze defaults, quiet hours and personalization (the route accepts them; the page does not expose them as a household decision worth a conversational form). **Tests:** `tests/ask/habitCoachPreferencesCommands.test.js` (5); two habits tests updated for the second summary action. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.333 — Financing profile edit in Ask (owner ruling 2026-10-13; step 3 of the agreed order; 2026-10-13):** completes the Financing slice left open by 1.314 and 1.332. New confirmation-gated `FINANCING_PROFILE_COMMAND` (**owner** floor, PROPERTY_ROLE class; declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list). The Financing answer gains **Update my mortgage and purchase details** for an owner (a contributor or viewer is offered nothing, and the copy that said editing was unavailable is gone). One form (mortgage status and type, purchase price and date, original and current balance, balance as-of date, interest rate in percent, remaining term in years, monthly payment, second mortgage and its balance, PMI; seeded with what is recorded; a blank keeps the recorded value; no user id is a field) → a confirmation that shows **the exact before and after of every field that would change**, including each loan fact a "no mortgage" answer would clear in the same save → `updateFinancingProfile`, the one canonical writer. The review is computed by the writer's own planner (`planFinancingProfileChange`), so what is shown is what the write applies, and an impossible combination (loan facts together with no mortgage, or loan facts over a recorded no-mortgage) is explained instead of confirmed. **Safeguards:** the form's context version and the write's `expectedUpdatedAt` both bind to the profile the owner was shown, so a profile that moved is refused (`ASK_CONTEXT_VERSION_CONFLICT`); a current balance above the original is stated on the confirmation, whose consent text says it is on purpose, and only then is it acknowledged to the writer (never inferred); every changed field is written to the profile's change log with surface ASK. **The conversation receipt is redacted:** it names the fields that changed and points to the owner-only history, and repeats no amount, rate or date. The refinance capture and the purchase-date command (1.332) are unchanged and use the same writer. **Tests:** `tests/ask/financingProfileCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified; `prisma db push` for the 1.332 schema addition must be applied before this command can write its log.

**Revision 1.332 — Financing profile: one canonical, owner-only, logged writer (owner ruling 2026-10-13; builds steps 1–2 of the agreed order; 2026-10-13):** the owner approved the direction with adjustments: the **entire persisted financing profile is owner-only** (lender and account facts, balances, rate, payment, term, second mortgage, **PMI**, **and the purchase price and date**); contributor-editable what-if assumptions stay outside it (the ephemeral Break-Even and Sell/Hold/Rent assumptions already do); the same rule applies on **every** surface, not Ask alone. **Built:** `financingProfileWriter.updateFinancingProfile` is now the only writer of `PropertyFinancingProfile` (a test asserts no other module calls `upsert`, `update` or `create` on it). (1) *Authorization is inside the writer:* an owner, or an explicit trusted `SYSTEM` actor that must state a reason, else 403 `FINANCING_PROFILE_OWNER_REQUIRED`. (2) *Stale writes are refused* by binding the write to the profile's `updatedAt` inside the write itself (409 `FINANCING_PROFILE_STALE`). (3) *NO_MORTGAGE clearing is atomic:* the status and every loan fact it contradicts change in one write; loan facts alongside it, or written over NO_MORTGAGE, are refused (the stronger guard the rate and status captures already had, preserved); loan facts elsewhere still promote a profile to MORTGAGED, as the page always did. (4) *A current balance above the original is a caution, not a rejection:* 409 `BALANCE_EXCEEDS_ORIGINAL` until explicitly acknowledged (capitalization and negative amortization can make it right). (5) *Purchase price and date have one authoritative record:* this profile (Property carries no copy); property create and edit now write them through the writer. (6) *Field-level log:* new model `PropertyFinancingProfileChange` (one row per changed field: batch, field, before and after values, actor kind and user, system reason, source surface, time), written in the same transaction; an unchanged write logs nothing; `GET /properties/:propertyId/financing/profile/history` is owner-only. **Surfaces moved onto it:** the desktop `PUT` (floor raised from contributor to owner; the body may carry `expectedUpdatedAt` and `acknowledgeBalanceExceedsOriginal`), property create and edit (the primary owner), refinance completion (inside the decision's transaction; the household completing a refinance acknowledges that the new balance may exceed the old original balance), the Ask refinance capture and the Ask purchase-date command (whose operation floor is now owner, in the definition, the command registry and the matrix together), the Home Action "no mortgage" command, and the two Property Context captures, which keep their conditional single-statement SQL unchanged and now require the owner and write the field log in their transaction. **Behavior changes to expect:** contributors can no longer edit the financing profile on the page, capture the mortgage rate or status in Property Context, set the purchase date in Ask, or complete a refinance that writes loan facts; the page shows a viewer or contributor a short role note and, for the owner, confirms a balance above the original before saving. **Schema:** `PropertyFinancingProfileChange` is added to `prisma/schema.prisma`; no migration was created and no `db push` was run, so apply `prisma db push` in `apps/backend` (and `prisma generate` in `apps/workers`) before deploying. **Not built yet (step 3):** the Ask profile-edit command with a redacted receipt and exact before-and-after confirmation. **Tests:** `tests/unit/financingProfileWriter.test.js` (7); the capture, route-floor, refinance, purchase-date and policy tests were updated to the owner rule. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.331 — Home Briefing: owner-only public sharing of selected updates (owner ruling 2026-10-13, built; 2026-10-13):** implements ruling (8) of 1.329. New confirmation-gated `HOME_BRIEFING_SHARE_COMMAND` with the **owner** floor and the PROPERTY_ROLE class (a public link discloses household information to anyone who has it, so it is deliberately not part of the caller-scoped briefing command). Summary action **Share selected updates** (owners, when the latest briefing has share-eligible updates) and, on a new **Links you are sharing** list, **Stop sharing** per live link. One form (which of the latest briefing's share-eligible updates, one to ten; how long: 1, 7, 14 or 30 days) → a review whose body lists **each selected update's title and summary exactly as the recipient will read them**, the expiry, and that anyone with the link can read them together with the property's name, city and state; "updates added or changed later are not included" is stated because the share is a selection of fixed updates. Creation goes through `createSelectedHomeBriefingShare`, the route's own call. **Access history** is the live-links list's *Opened N times* and *Last opened*; **revocation** is `revokeHomeBriefingShare` and immediate. Cozy keeps only a hash of the link, so the receipt shows it once as an action and says it cannot be shown again; the receipt repeats no recipient detail (no recipient is captured at all). The selection is re-checked at execution against the delivery's current share-eligible updates, so an ineligible or foreign id is refused. **Role floors:** the share-creation and share-revocation routes gain `requireHouseholdRole('OWNER')`; the public read route is unchanged. **Tests:** `tests/ask/homeBriefingShareCommands.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.330 — Caller-scoped authorization class for Ask commands (owner ruling 2026-10-13, built; 2026-10-13):** implements ruling (9) of 1.329. `AskDomainCommandDefinition` gains `authorization: 'PROPERTY_ROLE' | 'CALLER_SCOPED'`. `command(...)` can no longer produce a VIEWER floor (its floor parameter is contributor-or-owner); the only way to a VIEWER floor is the new `callerScopedCommand(...)`, which sets floor VIEWER and authorization CALLER_SCOPED together, so the contributor floor of every existing command is untouched. A caller-scoped operation may change only state scoped exclusively to the authenticated account: the user id comes from the session (the confirm context), never from the request or the stored parameters; no property-shared row changes; no other member's notifications, preferences or reading state changes. **Applied to the two operations built under 1.326 and 1.327:** `NEIGHBORHOOD_INTERACTION_COMMAND` (a reading of a local change, stored per user and match) and `HOME_BRIEFING_COMMAND` (a member's briefing, settings and item readings, all stored per user and property). Their operation definitions, command definitions and matrix entries move to the VIEWER floor together, their answers offer the steps to any member who can see them, and the handlers and capture branch no longer refuse a viewer (property membership is still enforced). The page and Ask now agree for these two. **Tests:** `tests/ask/callerScopedCommandClass.test.js` (3) asserts that every VIEWER-floor command is declared CALLER_SCOPED and the reverse, that the operation floor agrees with the command floor for every domain command, and that exactly these two exist (adding one is a deliberate act); hostile-id tests in both commands' files prove a foreign `userId` in the parameters or an item id that belongs to another member is ignored or not found. **Recorded:** a future caller-scoped operation must add its own hostile-id test, and must not take an acting user from anywhere but the session. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.329 — Owner rulings on the pending parity decisions; Reserve balance-to-target; Status Board viewer controls (2026-10-13):** the owner reviewed the nine pending decisions and ruled as below. **Rulings recorded:** (1) *Professional close* is not closing a project: it records completion of a property purchase and advances the Buyer Plan into the homeowner lifecycle. It stays open as its own design slice and may be built only on the complete Closing Day Companion safeguard set (owner or authorized buyer role; identification, funds, wire-fraud, access and professional-confirmation checks; evidence naming the closing professional and the effective date; review and confirmation; execution-time revalidation; never inferred from task completion). (2) *Continuity Plan capture* is split by sensitivity: ordinary contacts may be suitable with owner authorization and redacted receipts; shutoff locations and instructions may be suitable as structured records without credentials; access secrets (gate codes, key locations, alarm information) stay out of Ask until a secret-specific capture and storage design prevents transcript persistence and repetition. (3) *Emergency Help* is a previously decided boundary (FRD v1.64, option A: no model-backed troubleshooter in Ask; incident and claim continuation instead) and is retained; reopen only deliberately. (4) *Negotiation Shield archive* is new behavior, not parity, and is deferred until reversibility, searchability, retention of drafts and evidence, who may archive and restore, and buyer-linked cases are defined. (5) *Reserve balance-to-target* is approved with the semantics below. (6) The *"Correct home information" redirect* is a verified prior decision (revision 1.288), kept temporarily for facts without an Ask correction operation; an in-Ask correction is preferred wherever a governed path exists. (7) *Savings sensitive facts* and *partner handoff* are two decisions, both kept off: facts need a field-level privacy design (transcript exposure, redacted receipts, retention, role restrictions, correction and deletion); a handoff needs owner-only initiation, named recipient and purpose, an exact data preview, explicit consent, delivery status and revocation. (8) *Home Briefing public share links* are approved as owner-only (owner creates and revokes; only explicitly selected share-eligible items; expiring token; confirmation of exactly what becomes public; no silent inclusion of later changes; access history and immediate revocation; no recipient-sensitive detail repeated in the receipt). (9) *Viewer-capable Ask commands* are approved as a **caller-scoped authorization class** (an operation declares CALLER_SCOPED; the authenticated user id comes from the session and never the body; no property-shared row, no other member's notifications, preferences or reading state changes; tests prove hostile ids in input are ignored), not a weakening of the contributor floor. *Financing profile edit*: the financing profile is the canonical owner of mortgage and loan facts and refinance capture must call the same writer; field-level permissions first (owner-only for lender identity, balance, rate, lien and account facts; contributor only for lesser planning assumptions), with review, confirmation, provenance and execution-time freshness. **Built in this revision:** (a) *Reserve balance-to-target.* "Set my reserve fund to $5,000" (also "my reserve fund balance is $5k" and a form) re-reads the canonical balance, shows the balance on record, the stated balance and the signed correction, explains it is an increase or a decrease and not a deposit or withdrawal, and requires confirmation. At execution it re-reads the balance and rejects if it changed, then logs one `BALANCE_CORRECTION` of the difference through the same `addContribution` the ledger uses; when the balance already equals the target it logs nothing. A zero balance is a legitimate target. A "change my reserve plan" now offers four choices. Tests: `tests/ask/reserveFundSetBalance.test.js` (4); `reserveFundDirectRouting` updated for the new route and the fourth choice. (b) *Status Board page.* The Refresh, pin, hide and override controls are disabled for a viewer with a short role note instead of returning a 403; the server rejection is unchanged. **Not built (rulings above gate them):** professional close, Continuity access secrets, Emergency Help, Negotiation Shield archive, Savings facts and handoff, Home Briefing share links, the caller-scoped command class and the Financing profile edit are queued as dedicated slices. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.328 — HOA Compliance: association, approval requests and violation notices (parity register P2 "HOA Compliance"; 2026-10-13):** the HOA answer was a read and said adding the association was not available in Ask. New confirmation-gated `HOA_COMPLIANCE_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). Summary actions: **Set up my HOA** or **Update HOA details**, **Add an approval request** (once an association exists) and **Report a violation**; row actions on each approval: **Change** and **Remove**. **Shared effects (D6 pattern):** the property-context gate, the project-compliance applicability checks, analytics and tool-lifecycle completion that the create-approval route runs moved out of the controller into the new `hoaComplianceOperations` module (`createHoaApprovalRecordWithEffects`, `upsertHoaAssociationWithEffects`, `reportHoaViolationWithEffects`), which the controller and Ask both call; `propertyContextJustInTimeSlice4Hoa` is re-pointed to it and asserts the controller no longer creates a record around the gate. Forms mirror the route schemas: the association (name; management company; contact; dues in dollars stored as cents; frequency; next due; notes), an approval request (the work, what is asked, where it stands, submitted and expiry dates, notes) and a violation (summary, related work, details, fix-by date, fine in dollars, urgency). **What the household may say is its reported status** (not submitted, submitted, under review, correction requested, resubmitted, withdrawn): an association *decision* with its truth layer, source and evidence, approval conditions and a denial reason are not form fields (the service refuses conditions without a decision), so an "approved" status stays the association's to record on the page. A blank in a change form keeps the recorded value, and the review shows before and after for each changed field. A removal is the service's soft delete and withdraws nothing with the association; a violation report opens a violation record and guidance and does not respond to, dispute or pay anything. Which approvals may be added is also subject to the existing property-responsibility gate, whose refusal message reaches the person. **Role floors:** the five writing routes under `/properties/:propertyId/hoa/...` gain `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')`. **Not built:** recording an association decision with evidence, attaching documents, and the renovation-advisor session link. **Tests:** `tests/ask/hoaComplianceCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.327 — Home Briefing: read, prepare, settings and update readings (parity register P1 "Home Briefing / Home Gazette"; 2026-10-13):** Ask had no Home Briefing surface (its capability card was bound to the home change summary). New read operation `HOME_BRIEFING_VIEW` (viewer floor) and confirmation-gated `HOME_BRIEFING_COMMAND` (declared-action only; both registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The view reads `getHomeBriefingView` with a new `readOnly` option: the route's GET creates the member's preference row on every look, the Ask read shows the defaults as they would be created and writes nothing. It shows the member's settings (on or off, cadence, topics, channels), the latest briefing's updates with their reading state, and the earlier-briefing count. Actions for a contributor or owner: **Prepare my briefing** (`generateHomeBriefing`; the review says it may notify through the chosen channels), **Change settings** (one form: on or off, how often, topics, where to send it; mirrors the route schema, at least one channel) and per-update **Mark seen**, **Dismiss**, **Not useful** (`recordHomeBriefingItemOutcome`). Everything is the caller's own (settings, deliveries and readings are stored per user), so nothing changes for another member. Phrases like "show my home briefing" route to the view; "what changed recently" still goes to the home change summary. **Recorded limitation:** as with 1.326, the Ask command framework's floor type is contributor-or-owner, so viewers set their own briefing on the page only. **Not built:** sharing selected updates (a public link: an owner decision) and marking an update acted on (needs a separate next step). The Home Gazette is retired (HTTP 410) and has no Ask surface. **Tests:** `tests/ask/homeBriefingCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.326 — Neighborhood Change Radar / Around Your Home: follow, dismiss, mark not relevant (parity register P1 "Neighborhood Change Radar"; 2026-10-12):** the local-change answer listed matched changes (and counted the ones a person had set aside) but offered no way to follow or set one aside. New confirmation-gated `NEIGHBORHOOD_INTERACTION_COMMAND` (declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). Each listed change gains **Follow**, **Dismiss** and **Not relevant** for a contributor or owner; it goes through `setAroundYourHomeInteraction`, the route's own call, behind a review card and a confirmation. What a change's current reading allows is one function (`neighborhoodKindsFor`: a followed change is not followed again; a set-aside one is not listed in Ask, as before). The reading is **stored per user and match**, so it is the caller's own view: it changes nothing for anyone else and nothing in the source record, and the review says so. The radar's recompute and ingest routes are retired (HTTP 410), so there is nothing else here to write. **Recorded limitation:** the page route has no role floor and, being caller-scoped, a viewer may set their own reading there (the owner rule for caller-scoped state); the Ask command framework's floor type is contributor-or-owner, so in Ask the step is offered to contributors and owners only. A viewer-capable Ask command would need framework support (the floor type and the confirm check) and is left as a follow-up. **Not built:** the page's filters (sort, type, effect) and the per-event detail, which are read presentation. **Tests:** `tests/ask/neighborhoodInteractionCommands.test.js` (4). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.325 — Home Risk Replay: record what you observed (parity register P1 "Home Risk Replay"; 2026-10-12):** the past-hazard answer listed matched hazard records and any effect the household had recorded, but a household could not record or update one. New confirmation-gated `PAST_HAZARD_OUTCOME_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). Each hazard record gains **Record what I observed** (or **Update what I observed** once one exists) for a contributor or owner. It is one form (no effect, an effect, or not sure; when you noticed; what you saw) → review card → `recordPropertyHazardOutcome`, the route's own call, behind the route's reviewed-source-coverage gate (the same `HOME_RISK_REPLAY` check; unavailable coverage is said, never an all-clear). The route's rules are mirrored: a confirmed effect needs a description of at least 10 characters and the date is not in the future; a cause is not a form field. The review says that a confirmed effect **also adds an entry to the home's timeline** (what the service does), and that it is the household's observation, not a finding of damage or of a cause, and that a "no effect" statement is not a finding that the event had no effect. Saving again replaces the current statement and keeps the earlier one in the assertion history. The legacy replay runs and events remain retired (HTTP 410) and are not offered. **Not built:** linking evidence (a claim, an inspection or repair record, a document) to an outcome, which needs a record picker; the date and hazard-type filters on the view. **Tests:** `tests/ask/pastHazardOutcomeCommands.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.324 — Renovation readiness: refresh, acknowledge, withdraw and reopen (parity register P1 "Renovation Risk Advisor"; 2026-10-12):** the renovation readiness answer listed what blocks starting the work but offered no control over it. New confirmation-gated `RENOVATION_READINESS_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The answer now carries **Refresh readiness** (a summary action; *Run the readiness check* when it has not been evaluated) and row actions on each item: **Acknowledge the risk** on an open item the rules allow to be overridden, **Withdraw the acknowledgement** on one already acknowledged (both **owner only**, as the route is, with the row action absent for a contributor and a refusal if one is dispatched anyway), and **Reopen** on an item a person marked satisfied by hand (an item satisfied by its source record is not reopened by hand). Each goes through `renovationReadiness.service` (`evaluateReadiness`, `governOverride`, `updateReadinessItem`), the routes' own calls, behind a review card and a confirmation. The forms mirror the route schemas: an acknowledgement needs a reason of at least 20 characters and an explicit "I accept the risk", a withdrawal 10 characters, a reopen a note. Which step an item allows now is one function (`readinessItemKinds`) shared by the row actions and the confirmation's re-check; the service's own rules (an authority, safety or source-controlled blocker cannot be overridden) reach the person in its own words. A refresh keeps the case's work mode (provider or DIY) and says so; every review says readiness organizes the household's records and does not establish that the work is compliant or permitted. **Not built:** marking an item satisfied (it needs an evidence document from the property record, so it stays on the page), the project handoff, compliance records and conditions, and HOA document reviews. **Tests:** `tests/ask/renovationReadinessCommands.test.js` (8). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.323 — Savings & Benefits: save, dismiss, stop pursuing and record the outcome (parity register P1 "Savings & Benefits"; 2026-10-12):** the savings answer listed opportunities, work in progress and realized value but offered no control over any of them. New confirmation-gated `SAVINGS_BENEFIT_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). Row actions on the savings answer: **Save for later** and **Dismiss** on an available opportunity (a recurring-cost saving that is new or viewed; a benefit that is detected, with dismiss also once viewed), **Record what happened** and **Stop pursuing** on one under way (an open canonical action). Each goes through `savingsBenefitsCanonical.service` (`createCanonicalAction`, `updateCanonicalAction`, `recordCanonicalActionOutcome`), the routes' own calls, behind a review card and a confirmation; save and dismiss use a stable idempotency key per opportunity so repeating returns the same action. The outcome is one form (what happened; the amount received or the monthly or yearly saving seen; what backs it; why it was denied or closed; the observed period for a recurring saving) that **mirrors the service's own stage rules** so a gap shows before the review: a received saving needs an amount and what backs it, a recurring one an observed window of at least 28 days that is not in the future, a denial and a closure a reason. The review says a received saving is counted as reported and not verified against the provider. A service refusal (a governance rule, a state transition) reaches the person in its own words. **Primary owner only**, as the service is: the row actions are absent for anyone else and a change is refused with that disclosure. **Not built, by decision:** recording a sensitive eligibility fact (income, disability, age, veteran status, immigration status and the like), a partner handoff, a quote request, and a partner complaint or handoff revocation: personal data leaves the household in those, so they need their own owner decision and stay on the page. Also not built: the follow-up date and the checklist-progress update. **Role floors:** the seven writing routes under `/properties/:propertyId/savings-benefits/...` and the two under `/properties/:propertyId/home-savings/...` gain `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')`; the three id-addressed `/home-savings/opportunities/...` writes are unchanged (the service verifies the primary owner). **Tests:** `tests/ask/savingsBenefitCommands.test.js` (9). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.322 — Property Tax: the appeal lifecycle (parity register P1 "Property Tax"; 2026-10-12):** the appeal readiness answer said where the household stood; it could not start, prepare, record or close an appeal. New confirmation-gated `PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The readiness answer now carries *Start an appeal case* (a summary action) and **Your appeal cases**: each case with its status, ground, tax year, filing reference, hearing, decision and open reminders, and the one step it can take now (*Prepare the packet*, *Record that I filed*, *Record a response or hearing*, *Record the decision*, *Add a reminder*), and the open reminders with *Mark done* / *Dismiss*. Each step is one form → review card → `propertyTaxAppealCaseService`, the route's own call (the controllers add only validation, mirrored in the forms): start (`createOrResume`), packet (`updatePacket`: narrative of at least 50 characters, the packet's own five-step checklist, four placeholders, and "I have reviewed this"; the review says whether it will be marked ready to file or saved as in progress), filing (`confirmExternalFiling`, date not in the future, reference of at least 3 characters), response or hearing (`recordTrackingEvent`), reminder (`upsertReminder`, keyed from the title so asking again updates it), decision (`recordDetermination`; a blank amount is omitted, never zero; "close the case" is its own answer). **Starting re-runs the readiness check** and, when not ready, says what is still needed and offers no confirmation. Which step a case offers is one function (`appealCaseAvailableKinds`), shared by the row actions and the confirmation's re-check, so the order of an appeal (packet, then filing, then responses and the decision) is decided in one place; a step the case does not allow now is refused and a case changed while a confirmation was open is refused. **Cozy prepares and records and never files:** every review says so, and a decision is recorded as entered, not checked against the authority. **Primary owner only**, as the service's lookup is (it throws otherwise): the section is absent and a change is refused with that disclosure. **Role floors:** the fifteen writing routes under `/properties/:propertyId/property-tax/...` gain `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` (they had `restrictToHomeowner` and property membership only). **Not built:** evidence and comparables management (`PUT .../appeal/evidence`, `.../comparables`), intakes and document upload, the record correction, the action list and its refresh, filing-confirmation document attachment. **Tests:** `tests/ask/propertyTaxAppealCommands.test.js` (12); `propertyTaxReadinessNoDesktopLinks` now asserts the start action is the only action and has no href. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.321 — Sell / Hold / Rent: what-if assumptions (parity register P1 "Sell / Hold / Rent"; 2026-10-12):** same gap and same shape as 1.320. The page lets a household replace the appreciation, selling-cost, inflation, tax, insurance and maintenance assumptions, the home value and the rental assumptions (monthly rent, rent growth, vacancy, management fee); the Ask answer said adjusting them was not available. `SellHoldRentInput` gains `ephemeral` (the overrides apply to that estimate only; no reusable assumption set is saved, and the analysis-history row was already skipped for any override request). A hypothetical ("what if I rent it for $3,200 and vacancy is 8%", "suppose management fees are 10%") is parsed by `support/sellHoldRentWhatIf.ts`: rent as dollars a month (a `k` suffix is thousands, $100–$100,000), vacancy and management bounded 0–50%, rent growth 0–20%, and the shared terms read through the Break-Even parser so one phrase means one thing (its general "cost growth" has no counterpart here and is dropped; a rent figure is never read as the home value). The answer is its own: *What if: the N-year comparison*, the assumptions changed, the what-if against the standard estimate (sell net proceeds, hold net change, rent net change, the model's indicator), the scenario strip, sources and the boundary. It does not run the property-context capture, the plan thread or the progress block: the household's recorded context and plan are not touched. The routing pattern gains one narrow alternative so "what if I rent / sell it…" reaches this operation; the routing-calibration, specialist-routing, hierarchical-routing and correction-routing files fail identically before and after. **Not built:** saved scenarios, an existing assumption set, and the debt overrides (balance, rate, term, payment). **Tests:** `tests/ask/sellHoldRentWhatIf.test.js` (5). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.320 — Break-Even: what-if assumptions (parity register P1 "Break-Even"; 2026-10-12):** the page lets a household replace the appreciation, cost-growth, inflation, property-tax, insurance, maintenance and selling-cost assumptions and the home value; Ask offered the standard five- or ten-year projection only. The Break-Even route is read-only apart from one thing, and that shaped this: every request that carries assumption overrides makes `FinancialAssumptionService.resolveForTool` save an `AssumptionSet` row, so a viewer's page load writes. Ask must not, so `resolveForTool` gains `persistOverrides` (default true: the route is unchanged) and `BreakEvenInput` gains `ephemeral`, which applies the overrides to that projection only; its note says nothing was saved. A message with an explicit hypothetical framing ("what if", "suppose", "assume", "if appreciation…") and a recognised assumption is parsed deterministically (`support/breakEvenWhatIf.ts`; percentages to decimals, rates bounded −10% to 25%, selling costs 0–20%, a home value $10,000 to $100,000,000; a specific term such as "selling costs" or "maintenance" consumes its number so it never becomes the general cost-growth rate). The answer computes the standard and the what-if projection and shows *The assumptions you changed*, *Your what-if against the standard projection* (when it breaks even, net, cumulative costs and appreciation) and the what-if's own table, drivers and sources; the title is prefixed "What if:" and the standard projection is unchanged. The standard answer now says how to try one. No confirmation: nothing is written. **Not built:** saved scenarios and picking an existing assumption set (the page's `assumptionSetId` and mortgage-balance, rate, term and payment overrides), and a form for the assumptions. **Tests:** `tests/ask/breakEvenWhatIf.test.js` (3) and three in `breakEvenCapabilitySlice`. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.319 — Status Board: correct an item and refresh the board; reads no longer write (parity register P1 "Status Board"; 2026-10-12):** the Ask board could only be viewed, and viewing it silently created board rows and recomputed statuses. New confirmation-gated `STATUS_BOARD_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The status board answer now offers *Refresh the board* (a summary action, for a contributor or owner) and *Correct this item* (a row action on every item that does not need its install date; an item that does keeps the existing *Add install date* capture, which dispatches with the inventory id). *Correct* is one form (condition, recommendation, installed-on, purchased-on, a note up to 2000 characters, pin or unpin, hide or show; "Use the estimate" clears an override) that sends only what was filled in, through the same `patchItemStatus` the page route calls, so the correction sits beside the computed estimate as a user override and never replaces it. *Refresh* is the route's `ensureHomeItems` + `computeStatuses`. A correction is bound to the item's last status update, so a change made while the confirmation was open is refused. **Reads are read-only:** `listBoard` takes `{ readOnly: true }` and the Ask read uses it, so it no longer creates rows or recomputes; an empty board offers the refresh instead. **Role floors:** `POST /status-board/recompute` and `PATCH /status-board/:homeItemId` gain `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')`; the page's recompute is only ever user-triggered, so no automatic viewer flow breaks (its Refresh and override buttons still show for a viewer and now get a 403: hiding them is a follow-up). The HTTP `GET` keeps its create-and-recompute behaviour (it is the route's contract; the read-only audit of GET handlers remains a recorded follow-up). **Not built:** filtering and grouping the board. **Tests:** `tests/ask/statusBoardCommands.test.js` (8); `statusBoardCapabilitySlice` asserts the read is read-only. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.318 — Capital Timeline: adjust what the timeline assumes for an item (parity register P1 "Capital Timeline"; 2026-10-12):** the capital plan answer showed the timeline but a household could not correct it from Ask. New confirmation-gated `CAPITAL_TIMELINE_COMMAND` (contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). The capital plan answer now carries **Your adjustments to the timeline**: the existing overrides (read for any role), an *Adjust an item* action and a *Remove* row action for a contributor or owner. *Adjust* is one form (the item, what to adjust: a planned date, a planned window, a cost range, how many years are left, leave the item off the timeline, or a note, and the fields for that choice) validated per type into the canonical override payload (`{date}`, `{start,end}`, `{minCents,maxCents}`, `{remainingYears}`); a window must end on or after it starts, a cost range must not invert, every type but a note needs an item, and a raw payload is not a form field. It goes through `HomeCapitalTimelineService.createOverride` / `deleteOverride` after the same `assertFinancialContextApplicable('CAPITAL_TIMELINE', 'capitalPlanning')` the routes run (their controllers add nothing else). Saving marks the latest timeline out of date; the receipt says it is used the next time the timeline is run. An add is bound to the household's items and current adjustments. **Not built:** editing an adjustment in place (remove and add again), and running the timeline. **Tests:** `tests/ask/capitalTimelineCommands.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.317 — Coverage Intelligence: the policy review, starting a comparison, and recording the decision from Ask (parity register P0 "Coverage Intelligence"; 2026-10-12):** the comparison answer said "starting one is not currently available" and there was no review in Ask. New read-only `COVERAGE_REVIEW_STATUS` (viewer floor, message-routable: "show my coverage review", "what questions should I ask my insurer", "review my homeowners insurance"; comparison and item-gap phrasing keep their routes) reads `findCurrentCoverageReview` (strictly read-only since v1.308): the questions, the evidence each rests on, what is missing and the boundary, with an out-of-date review flagged and never generated by a read; it discloses owner-only as the service is. New confirmation-gated `COVERAGE_COMMAND` (contributor floor, owner-only, declared-action only; registered across the registries and the governance list; no schema change): *Review my coverage / Refresh the review* (`getOrCreateCoverageReview`), *Start a comparison* (`getOrCreateCoverageComparison`, offered in the comparison answer's empty state), and *Record my decision* (offered while a comparison is open: a form of keep, change, shop, defer or professional review, the option to switch to, and a reason when that option is not equivalent, as the canonical service requires; a switch with no reason is asked for before any confirmation). The decision's Home Action resolution, guided-journey completion, capability lifecycle completion and analytics are extracted from the controller into `coverageDecisionOperations.recordCoverageDecisionWithEffects` (the D6 pattern), so the route and Ask have identical effects; a replay of the same decision is idempotent and does not repeat them, and the Ask decision carries an execution-bound source id. A decision is the household's record: no policy is changed, no insurer is contacted. Three unit tests that pinned the controller's source now read the operations module; one Ask test pins the handler's role-aware registration. **Not built:** adding a quote or another policy term to a comparison (document evidence and fact verification; its own design), and the property-level coverage analysis run and simulation. **Tests:** `tests/ask/coverageCommands.test.js` (8). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.316 — Home Upgrade Planner: record a decision, rename, archive, restore, recalculate and delete a saved option from Ask (parity register P0 "Home Digital Twin / Upgrade Planner"; 2026-10-12):** the upgrade options answer was read-only ("open it to ..."). New operation `HOME_UPGRADE_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). A contributor or owner now sees a **Manage options** list under the answer: per active option *Record my decision* (a form: still deciding, selected, deferred, rejected or closed; a reason is required to defer or reject, as the canonical schema requires; the review states it starts no work and commits to no contractor), *Calculate / Recalculate* (queues the planner's own calculation job through `JobQueueService`, the call the compute route makes; the receipt says results arrive after a short wait and earlier results stay until then), *Rename* (name and note), and *Archive*; archived options are listed for *Restore* and *Delete* (the service enforces archived-only and refuses an option a project was created from). Everything goes through `HomeDigitalTwinScenarioService` (`recordDecision`, `updateScenario`, `deleteScenario`), the calls the planner's scenario routes make (their controllers add no gate or analytics, so there is nothing else to share). Confirmations are bound to the option's last change, status, decision and archive state. A viewer sees none of it; the out-of-date notice now says a contributor or owner can recalculate. **Not built:** creating a new option (a per-scenario-type input payload with component and assumptions; its own slice), the handoff to a project, and the component comparison options. **Tests:** `tests/ask/homeUpgradeCommands.test.js` (7). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.315 — Permits: add, change and remove a permit record from Ask (parity register P0 "Permits", slice PM-B; 2026-10-12):** the permit list is no longer read-only. New operation `PERMIT_TRACKER_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). **Shared effects (the Negotiation Shield D6 pattern):** the controller's property-context feature gate (`PERMITS` / `CREATE_MANUAL_PERMIT`, 409 `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_INCOMPLETE`), project-compliance applicability check, analytics and tool-lifecycle completion are extracted into `permitTrackerOperations.ts` (`createManualPermitWithEffects`, `updatePermitWithEffects`, `deletePermitWithEffects`); the three controller handlers call them, so Ask and the page have identical effects (one source-text test that pinned the gate order in the controller now pins it in the operations module). **Add a permit** (summary action on the permit list and its empty state): one form (kind, the work, permit number, description, reported status, contractor, applied / issued / expires, estimated cost, notes) -> review card -> create. **The official status is not a field**: a homeowner records the REPORTED status; the authority's status stays unconfirmed until its record is added (a separate, evidence-bearing step, not built), and the review says so. **Change details** (row action): the form shows what is recorded; the review lists only fields that differ, before -> after (a blank keeps what is recorded, because the canonical update cannot clear a date); a permit that came from public records offers only its reported status and notes, as the service enforces. **Remove** (row action): soft delete after a review that says it leaves the tracker, reopens any flag it had resolved, and cancels nothing with the authority. A viewer is offered none of these; confirmations are bound to the permit's last update; a stored review that smuggles an official status is refused. **Not built (PM-C and later):** recording an official status with evidence (document-visibility rules), inspections, flags and remediation, readiness checks (photo upload + AI), the disclosure export and the open-data fetch. **Tests:** `tests/ask/permitTrackerCommands.test.js` (11). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.314 — Financing: the read and the project-financing calculator (parity register P0 "Financing", slices FN-A and FN-B; 2026-10-12):** Ask had mortgage capture inside the refinance flow and no financing read. New read-only operation `FINANCING_OVERVIEW` (viewer floor, skill-less, message-routable; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change) answers "how much equity do I have", "can I get a HELOC", "show my financing" and "what would a $40,000 project cost to finance" (also "25k", "$1.5m"). It reads `getProfile`, `getLatestEquity` and `listScenarios`, and, when the message names a project cost, the page's own stateless `calculate`: HELOC (draw and repayment payments), home equity loan, personal loan at the low and high end of the range over three and five years, contractor financing with its promo and deferred-interest risk, and paying cash with its opportunity cost, with the rates' as-of date, the national-average disclaimer and a boundary ("illustrations, not an offer"). With nothing recorded it says so and does not dress partial facts up; a not-eligible HELOC is labelled comparison-only. Saved scenarios are listed read-only. Typed routing excludes refinancing, closing financing, lender and loan-estimate phrasing. **A read that wrote, fixed:** `getLatestEquity` (behind `GET …/financing/equity`, and behind the "stateless" calculator when the snapshot was stale) computed and PERSISTED an `EquityPosition` row when none was fresh; it now returns the freshly computed numbers in memory (the shape the existing could-not-save fallback already returned) and only the explicit `refreshEquity` saves a snapshot. History therefore grows on refresh, not on page views. **Not built, on purpose:** editing the financing profile (the mortgage fields it shares with the refinance capture need one canonical owner first; decision D-FN1 stays open), saving a calculation as a scenario, archiving a scenario, and the explicit equity refresh. **Tests:** `tests/ask/financingOverview.test.js` (8, including that a read and the calculator write nothing and the refresh does). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.313 — Permit Tracker read (parity register P0 "Permits", slice PM-A; 2026-10-12):** Ask had only permit counts inside the renovation readiness answer. New read-only operation `PERMIT_TRACKER_STATUS` (viewer floor, skill-less, message-routable; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change) answers "show my permits", "what permits do I have", "do I have an open permit for the roof", "permit inspections" and "unpermitted work". It reads what the page's list, summary and flags endpoints read (`listPermits` 30, `getPermitSummary`, `listFlags` open flags 10) with none of the route's effects. **Official versus reported is kept apart** (the tool's own rule: `status` is the authority's record and can never be changed by the household; `reportedStatus` is the household's or contractor's word): each permit shows `Official: …`, and `Reported by the household: …` only where the official status is not confirmed, so a reported "issued" is never presented as an issued permit. Permits are grouped Active / Closed out or no longer valid; open inspections, issue, finaled and expiry dates, contractor and a verified marker are shown; open flags list the work type, reason, disclosure risk and status. An empty tracker says it is not the same as nothing having been permitted. The typed pattern is narrow: "do I need a permit" and "is my renovation ready" keep the readiness route (the new pattern is placed before it and excludes need/require/ready/renovation/project phrasing). `listPermits` now also returns `reportedStatus` (additive). **Not here (PM-B and later):** add or change a permit, record an official status with evidence, inspections, flags and remediation, readiness checks (photo upload + AI) and the disclosure export (each needs its own decision or design). **Tests:** `tests/ask/permitTrackerStatus.test.js` (6). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.312 — Negotiation Shield buyer mode: start negotiating an inspection finding and record the seller's response and outcome from Ask (parity register P0 "Negotiation Shield"; 2026-10-12):** the buyer negotiation readiness answer listed findings in negotiation and stopped there. New operation `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_BUYER_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). Each finding row now offers a contributor or owner **Start negotiating** (a finding classified for seller negotiation on a confirmed inspection report, not dismissed, with no negotiation yet) or **Record the seller response** (a finding already in negotiation); a viewer is offered neither. *Start*: a form (repair, credit or either; credit requested; notes) -> review card -> `NegotiationShieldService.startBuyerNegotiation`, which creates the buyer case or reuses the one already linked. *Record*: a form prefilled with what is recorded (seller response, outcome, agreed credit, notes) -> a review card that states what the outcome does beyond saving it — a credit or a seller repair marks the finding resolved and completes its Buyer Plan task, "I will handle it after closing" reopens it as post-closing work — -> `recordBuyerNegotiationOutcome` with the caller's role (the route's own extras are the contributor floor and the role, both honored). An accepted credit needs the agreed amount, as the canonical schema requires. Confirmations are bound to the finding's last change and its negotiation link. Nothing is sent to the seller or an agent: Ask records what the household did and was told. **Not here:** attaching completion evidence, the buyer analysis and draft message, and classifying a finding for negotiation (the Buyer Plan's own disposition). **Tests:** `tests/ask/negotiationShieldBuyerCommands.test.js` (10). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.311 — Price Finalization: record, change and finalize an accepted price from Ask (parity register P0 "Price Finalization", slices PF-A/B/C; 2026-10-12):** the price records answer ended in "saving one is not currently available in Ask". New operation `PRICE_FINALIZATION_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). **Shared effects (the Negotiation Shield D6 pattern):** the controller's project-compliance applicability gate, property-context version stamp, analytics event and, on finalize, guided-journey completion are extracted into `priceFinalizationOperations.ts` (`createPriceFinalizationDraftWithEffects`, `updatePriceFinalizationDraftWithEffects`, `finalizePriceFinalizationWithEffects`); the three controller handlers now call them, so Ask and the page have identical effects. **Three starts reach one record:** *Record the accepted price* (a summary block on a vendor-price Negotiation Shield review: contractor quote review and contractor urgency pressure), *Record the final price* (a summary action on the Quote Comparison review once a proposal is selected; no form, the canonical create prefills from the selected proposal and moves the comparison to final terms), and *Record an accepted price* (the price records answer, by hand). A start form takes vendor (required), quoted and accepted price, scope, payment, warranty, timeline and notes. If a draft is already open for the same review or comparison, Ask offers to change it instead of showing a form whose values the canonical create would silently discard, and a confirmation is refused if one opened meanwhile. **Change the terms** (row action on a draft): the form shows what is recorded and the review lists each changed field before -> after; only changed fields are sent. **Finalize** (row action on a draft): asks for the accepted price (prefilled from the accepted or quoted price), says it does not book work, pay anyone or contact the vendor, and, for a record that came from a Quote Comparison, that the selected proposal is marked accepted and the others rejected (what the page does, and the cross-tool write PF4). A finalized or archived record is refused before any confirmation. **Primary owner only**, as `ensurePropertyAccess` in the service is: any other household member is told so instead of being shown a form. The records answer's own floor is OWNER, so only an owner sees its row actions. **Not built, on purpose:** archive/delete (no desktop capability, a product decision), booking attach (the booking flow), editing a finalized record. **Tests:** `tests/ask/priceFinalizationCommands.test.js` (16). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.310 — Quote Comparison: change a proposal, confirm its facts, record a question and its answer, close the decision (slice QC-B; 2026-10-12):** extends `QUOTE_COMPARISON_COMMAND` (no new operation, no schema change). Per-proposal row actions are now Select, **Confirm the facts**, **Change details**, **Ask a question**, **Record the answer** (only with an open question), Reject / Return and Remove; the summary adds **Close this decision**. *Change details*: the form shows what is recorded; the review lists each changed field as before -> after and says a save clears the confirmed facts of the proposal and the selection and rejections across the comparison (what the canonical `updateQuoteProposal` does); only changed fields are sent, and the whole terms list is rebuilt (other terms kept) only when the warranty changed. *Confirm the facts*: `confirmQuoteProposal`, refused when already confirmed, with the READINESS_ASSESSED analytics the route emits. *Ask a question* records a clarification with the proposal for the household (explicitly not sent to the provider); *Record the answer* resolves the oldest open question, which the review card quotes. *Close this decision*: declined or deferred with an optional reason, through `transitionQuoteDecision`; it cancels nothing and contacts no one. Form submissions are bound to the command that opened them (an Add form cannot be replayed as a Change). **Not built, on purpose:** import a proposal from a document. `createQuoteProposalFromDocument` reads the legacy `Document` table (not a Home Record), which the Documents-retirement arc is removing, so offering Home Records would need a canonical change first; recorded as a residual gap with that dependency. The continuation into finalization is the Price Finalization slice. **Tests:** `tests/ask/quoteComparisonCommands.test.js` (19). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.309 — Quote Comparison: add, select, reject/return and remove a proposal from Ask (parity register P0 "Quote Comparison", slice QC-A; 2026-10-12):** the review used to end in "adding proposals is not currently available in Ask". New operation `QUOTE_COMPARISON_COMMAND` (confirmation-gated, contributor floor, skill-less, declared-action only; registered across the Ask registries and the governance list; no schema change). **Add a proposal** is a summary action on the review carrying the workspace: one form (provider, total, what it covers, quote date, expiry, warranty, notes) -> review card -> `createQuoteProposal` as a manual USD proposal (the warranty becomes a WARRANTY term), after the same `assertProjectComplianceDecisionsApplicable` gate the route runs and with the same INTAKE_RECORDED analytics. **Select, Reject / Return to consideration, Remove** are per-proposal row actions in a new "Manage proposals" list (the message tells them apart; the row's entity is the proposal): a review card -> confirm, through `selectQuoteForDecision`, `setQuoteProposalDisposition` and `deleteQuoteProposal`. Select is offered only for a comparison-ready proposal in a comparable set and says why not otherwise (confirming a proposal's facts is not yet in Ask); reject/return/remove are refused once final-term review begins; a closed decision refuses everything. The confirmation is bound to the workspace's and proposal's last change and refuses a change made while it was open; the canonical service's refusal (closed, locked, not comparable) is carried to the homeowner as a readable message. A viewer is offered none of these. The create receipt no longer says adding proposals is unavailable. **Not in this slice (next, same operation):** editing a proposal, confirming a proposal's facts, import from a document, clarifications, the workspace transition, and the continuation into finalization. **Tests:** `tests/ask/quoteComparisonCommands.test.js` (13). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.308 — Coverage reads are strictly read-only; explicit start/generate mutations (security/correctness; 2026-10-12):** owner ruling: no read may create, refresh or materialize anything, for any role. `GET …/coverage-comparison` used to call `getOrCreateCoverageComparison` (create, and back-fill the guidance links of an existing DRAFT) and `GET …/coverage-review` used to call `getOrCreateCoverageReview` (retire READY reviews and create a new one). **Fixed:** new read-only `findCoverageComparison` (states `BASELINE_REQUIRED` / `NOT_STARTED` / `READY`; no back-fill) and `findCurrentCoverageReview` (the review for the present fingerprint, else the most recent one flagged `isCurrent:false`, else null; shares `loadReviewInputs` with generation). New explicit `POST …/coverage-comparison` and `POST …/coverage-review` (CONTRIBUTOR floor) call the existing get-or-create / generate functions unchanged. The Ask `COVERAGE_COMPARISON_STATUS` handler, which claimed to be read-only but called get-or-create, now uses `findCoverageComparison` and answers `NOT_APPLICABLE` / `COVERAGE_COMPARISON_NOT_STARTED` (no desktop link) when none was started. Frontend: `CoverageComparisonPanel` shows a Start comparison action (contributors and above) for `NOT_STARTED`; `CoverageReviewQuestionsPanel` reads only, shows "No coverage review yet" with a Review my coverage action, and an out-of-date banner with Refresh review for contributors; viewers see read-only copy. **Tests:** `tests/unit/coverageReadsAreReadOnly.test.js` (6; 5 fail on the previous code): real controllers and services on a recording prisma, three repeated reads in each state produce no insert, update, upsert, delete, transaction or analytics event, the POSTs do write, and the router test proves the POST floor and open GETs. Three Ask tests that stubbed the old function now stub `findCoverageComparison`. **Still open (separate batches):** the four other mutating routes on the coverage router, `risk-premium-optimizer/run`, and the other D-R1 route files; `getOrCreateCoverageReview` is still called from the loss-prevention-plan run (a POST), which is an explicit mutation and unchanged. Both coverage services authorize with `homeownerProfile.userId` (owner of record only), so household members never reached them; that narrowing is unchanged and was not part of this slice.

**Revision 1.307 — D-R1 batch 2: floors on incidents, guidance, inspectionHub, homeHabitCoach and roomPlantAdvisor (security correction; 2026-10-12):** owner rule: a viewer may change only state scoped exclusively to their own account; anything that changes the household record or another member's experience needs CONTRIBUTOR. `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` on 49 writing routes (incidents 11, guidance 11, inspectionHub 10, homeHabitCoach 8, roomPlantAdvisor 9), including every guidance step complete/skip/block, journey dismiss and signal resolve (they alter the shared journey), incident acknowledgement (it writes a household-visible suppression rule and timeline event for SNOOZED/DISMISSED and a shared timeline event for every type), and habit snooze/skip/dismiss/reopen and the property-wide habit preferences (`PropertyHabitPreference` is keyed by property, not by user). **Viewer-capable, each proven caller-scoped:** incident preferences, archive, restore and auto-resolution cancel (`incidentUserPreference` keyed by incident + caller; cancel narrowed to notifications addressed to the caller) and habit `viewed`. Two shared side effects found and fixed so those routes are safe for a viewer: pinning an incident cancelled every member's pending auto-resolution (now only CONTRIBUTOR and above cancel household-wide; a viewer's pin cancels only their own), and the habit view debounce was keyed by habit only, so one member's view suppressed another's (now per caller; a missing caller is refused). Also found and fixed: `GET …/home-habits/preferences` lazily upserted the preference row on read; it now returns the schema defaults without writing. **Tests:** `tests/unit/propertyRouteRoleFloorsBatch2.test.js` (25; the five writing-route cases fail on the previous routers: 49 routes open to a viewer) and `tests/unit/viewerCapableMutationsAreCallerScoped.test.js` (8; 4 fail on the previous code): the real controllers/service on a recording prisma with a hostile body (`userId`, `incidentId`, `propertyId`, `id`) prove every viewer write is keyed by the authenticated user and never by the body. **Not done:** UI affordances for guidance/habit/plant/inspection controls a viewer can no longer use (the server now answers 403); `POST /incident-suppression-rules` takes its scope from the body, not the route; `risk-premium-optimizer/run` and the remaining ~30 property-scoped route files are the next batch.

**Revision 1.306 — D-R1 follow-ups: buyer-task denial is 403, owner-only controls are hidden from contributors, GET-persistence audit, batch 2 enumeration (2026-10-12):** (1) `HomeBuyerTaskService.assertAccess` threw a plain `Error` for a household member below the required role, which the error handler answered as 500. It now throws `APIError` 404 `PROPERTY_NOT_FOUND` for no membership (as `propertyAuthMiddleware` does) and 403 `HOUSEHOLD_ROLE_REQUIRED` for a member below the role; a missing property or task is a 404 `APIError` with the same message, so the controllers' `message.includes('not found')` mapping is unchanged. The eight other buyer services already answered 404 (not 500) and are untouched. `buyerServiceRoleEnforcement.test.js` now also asserts status 403 for every task-plan method (fails on the previous service). (2) The share-link button on the closeout package page and the outcome-measurement consent checkbox now follow the OWNER floor: new `resolvePropertyOwnerAccess` / `canManage` on `usePropertyWriteAccess` (owner of record or member OWNER; unknown property left to the server); a contributor sees no share button and a disabled checkbox with "Only the home's owner can change this choice." (3) **GET-persistence audit (batch 1 routers, code-traced: every read-named handler/service function scanned for create/update/upsert/delete and one level of callees, plus the inline inventory handlers):** no batch-1 read creates or changes a domain record. The two non-domain writes found are by design: `getPublicCloseoutPackage` increments `viewCount`/`lastViewedAt` on the public link, and inventory coverage-summary emits an analytics event. **Found outside batch 1, confirmed by reading:** `GET …/coverage-comparison` (`getOrCreateCoverageComparison`: creates or updates a `coverageComparison`) and `GET …/coverage-review` (`getOrCreateCoverageReview`: `updateMany` of stale reviews and `create`) persist on a read, so a viewer's page view writes records; not changed (needs the read-only lookup + page change pattern of 1.305). (4) **Batch 2 enumeration** (real router stacks; classification below is a proposal pending owner confirmation, no floors added): incidents 15, guidance 11, inspectionHub 10, homeHabitCoach 9, roomPlantAdvisor 9 mutating routes with no floor; personalization (6) is already gated by `requirePersonalizationCapability` (the earlier static scan over-counted it).

**Revision 1.305 — D-R1 batch 1: verified role-floor audit of homeBuyerTask, projectTracker, quoteComparison, materialSpec, inventory and claims (security correction; 2026-10-12):** the mutating routes were enumerated from the real Express router stacks (not a text scan). **homeBuyerTask** has no `propertyAuthMiddleware`, but all 48 mutating service methods resolve the caller's access themselves (CONTRIBUTOR; pause/resume OWNER): executed with the caller resolved as a VIEWER, every method refused before touching the database (`tests/unit/buyerServiceRoleEnforcement.test.js`); no change. **The other five routers had no role handling at all** (router, controller and service; the claims controller's `householdRole` only feeds document visibility). **Fixed:** `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` on every writing route — projectTracker 28, quoteComparison 12, materialSpec 12, inventory 21, claims 9 — except: `POST …/inventory/barcode/lookup` stays open (stateless third-party UPC lookup, persists nothing). **Owner rulings:** closeout public share-link create and revoke are OWNER (public tokenized disclosure; matches the vault share-link policy); the quote outcome-measurement consent is OWNER (authorizes household data use; the model already records `outcomeMeasurementConsentedByUserId`, the policy version and the timestamp, so no schema change); revoking consent records the time but not the revoking user (no column; not added). **Quote Comparison get-or-create (option A):** `QuoteComparisonWorkspaceClient.loadQuotes` called `POST …/workspaces` on page load, so a plain floor would have broken the page for a viewer. Added the read-only `GET …/quote-comparison/workspaces/lookup` (same match rule via a shared `openWorkspaceWhere`, never creates; registered before `/workspaces/:workspaceId`), floored the POST at CONTRIBUTOR, and the page now uses `usePropertyWriteAccess`: a viewer calls the lookup, a contributor/owner calls get-or-create, and a viewer with no workspace yet sees the page with no saved quotes. **Tests:** `tests/unit/propertyRouteRoleFloorsBatch1.test.js` (26) enumerates the mutations from the router stack, so a new unclassified route is floored by default; the viewer cases failed on the previous routers (81 routes open). Typecheck clean; frontend `next build` exit 0; the four suites that read these routers fail identically before and after (1 pre-existing). Behaviour change: a viewer's write to these tools now returns 403. Not verified with a real token, database or browser. **Recorded follow-ups (not in this batch):** (1) the buyer-task service throws a plain `Error` for a denied caller, which surfaces as 500 — map it to 403; (2) audit GET handlers for implicit persistence (reads must not create or mutate); (3) the UI may still offer share-link and consent controls to a contributor who now gets 403; (4) batch 2 of the D-R1 audit (about 40 remaining route files).

**Revision 1.304 — Role floors on the Permit Tracker, Price Finalization and Financing routes (security correction; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_FINANCING_PERMITS_PRICE_FINALIZATION_DESIGN.md`, sections 4 and 5):** found at the start of register item 2.
