The three routers had `propertyAuthMiddleware` (membership only) and no `requireHouseholdRole`, and neither their controllers nor services reject a viewer (the only role use is passing it to an evidence-document visibility check), so a read-only household member could add and delete permits, finalize a price and edit the financing profile and scenarios over HTTP (executed at the router layer with stubbed controllers; the first failures were `POST …/permits/fetch`, `POST …/price-finalizations` and `PUT …/financing/profile`). **Fixed:** `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` on 14 Permit Tracker routes, 3 Price Finalization routes and 5 Financing routes (profile, equity refresh, scenario create/patch/archive). Reads are unchanged at VIEWER, and `POST …/financing/calculate` stays open because it is a stateless calculator that persists nothing. **Tests:** `tests/unit/propertyRouteRoleFloors.test.js` (9): the real routers and floor with stubbed authentication, membership lookup, rate limiters and controllers; every writing route 403 to a viewer and to no role, a contributor and an owner get past the floor, reads and the calculator stay open; the viewer cases fail on the previous routes for all three tools. Neighbouring suites' failures are identical before and after. **Wider finding, heuristic and not verified:** a static scan of non-admin route files found about 45 files (about 267 property-scoped mutating routes) with no floor and no role handling in any imported controller or service, plus 17 files where a controller or service mentions role handling; this is a reason for a verified, per-file audit (design decision D-R1), not a list of vulnerabilities. Behaviour change: a viewer's write to these three tools now returns 403. Not verified with a real token or database.

**Revision 1.303 — Start a renovation case in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; register section 3, "Start a renovation case"):** the renovation readiness answer ended, when there was no case, with "Starting a renovation case is not currently available in Ask". A contributor or owner can now start one. **Reading of "parity" (checked against the page):** the page reaches a case through an exploration (goals, spaces, budget, timing, then "create plan" from an option) or a retroactive review; the older advisor-session API is deprecated with `/renovation-cases` as its successor. The canonical `createRenovationCase` that the exploration's conversion calls also accepts a direct start and is idempotency-keyed with a contributor floor on its route, so Ask starts through that same create. The exploration (option generation) is a separate, larger slice and is not duplicated. **Entry:** a **Start a renovation case** action on the readiness answer's empty state (contributor and above; a viewer is told a contributor or owner can), or a typed "start/create/begin/set up a renovation case|plan" (narrow on purpose: "start a renovation project" stays with the Project Tracker and "can I start my renovation" stays the readiness question). **Flow:** one form (name and kind of work required; objective, who is responsible, areas, target budget, hoped-for start and finish optional; finish must not precede start) → a review card → confirm. **Write:** `createRenovationCase`: the case, its first draft scope and its owner participant in one transaction, with provenance `entryPoint ASK_COZY`, `sourceType ASK_EXECUTION`, `sourceId` the execution, and the governance tier defaulting to LOW_CONSEQUENCE exactly as the page's own conversion does (not asked; historical research is not offered because it comes from the retroactive review). **Idempotency:** the canonical create is unique per `(propertyId, idempotencyKey)` and the key is `ask:<execution id>:renovation-case-start`, so a replay, reclaimed worker or double click returns the case already made; a separate execution is a deliberate second case. **Receipt:** states nothing has been applied for or started and offers **Check readiness** for the new case by name (the readiness answer selects a case by name). **New operation `RENOVATION_CASE_START`:** command registry, audience policy, semantic packages, trust corpus, coverage matrix (CONFIRMED_MUTATION, contributor floor), mutation-impact map (refreshes the readiness answer), answer-trust ids (the empty-state action under the readiness operation, the receipt action under the new one), capture path, skill-less governance entry; `startupRegistryValidation` passes. No schema change. **Tests:** `tests/ask/renovationCaseStart.test.js` (12: routing and the readiness/project phrasings it must not take, the form's input and payload in cents, validation and the dates rule, declared and typed entry, review card writes nothing, the confirmed write with the key and provenance, tampered parameters, the canonical idempotency guarantee, the empty state for a contributor and a viewer, allowlists and the validator); a mutation check (allowlist entries removed) fails exactly the allowlist test. Two existing tests pinned the old "not currently available" copy and were updated (`renovationReadinessRing`, `zeroOperationReclassificationSlice`); the whole Ask suite's failing tests are identical to HEAD's 25. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.302 — The answer-trust validator reports what it did with list-block actions (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; found while building FRD v1.301):** `validateAskAnswerTrust` strips an un-allowlisted action from a SUMMARY block but not from the own `actions` of a list-type block (PRIORITY_LIST, CAPABILITY_LIST, GROUPED_LIST, TIMELINE, CHANGE_SUMMARY, OUTPUT_ARTIFACTS, RELATED_RECORDS). That carve-out is deliberate and old: the repair of those blocks touches only their items' links and calls to action, a comment records that a typed in-Ask action on them "was never subject to the allowlist", and only a link that leaves Ask is removed. **What was wrong was the report, not the carve-out.** `invalidAction` counted such an action, so the validator recorded `INAPPLICABLE_ACTION_REMOVED`, set `repaired`, and returned outcome `REPAIRABLE` (inflating the `repaired="yes"` label on `ask_answer_trust_total`) while the action was still in the answer. **Now:** `repaired` and `INAPPLICABLE_ACTION_REMOVED` are set only when the answer actually changed (compared before and after the repair); a typed list-block action that is kept and not allowlisted adds `UNLISTED_ACTION_RETAINED`; the check `actionApplicability` still reports FAIL (it is true: the action is not in the allowlist); and a new counter, `ask_answer_trust_unlisted_action_total{operation}`, makes the retained case observable. **Enforcement is unchanged and is an owner decision.** I measured how much depends on it by instrumenting the validator and running the entire Ask suite twice (instrumentation reverted, nothing committed): across 21 chunks only **17 list-block action evaluations in 6 operations** occurred (`DIY_PROJECTS`, `MAINTENANCE_STATUS`, `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASES`, `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE_ANALYZE`, `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`, `WARRANTY_LOOKUP`), all allowlisted when judged with a household role (without a role, three ids that match the mutation-name rule were rejected, a test artifact). That is too little coverage to show that closing the carve-out would remove nothing in production, so the recommendation is: ship this report, watch the new counter in production for a period, and close the carve-out (one early-return in `repairBlock`) only if it stays at zero or each hit is fixed by adding the id to its allowlist. **Practical rule until then:** a new action id on a list block's own actions must be added to `OPERATION_ACTION_IDS` for every operation that can emit the block, and asserted directly with `isAskActionApplicable`, because the validator will not catch a miss. **Tests:** `tests/ask/answerTrustListActions.test.js` (6; the three that pin the corrected report fail on the previous validator): allowlisted passes silently; unlisted list action kept and reported as kept; the same on a SUMMARY removed and reported as repaired; a link leaving Ask removed; both facts together; the counter. The full Ask suite's failing tests are identical before and after apart from those three. Not verified in production telemetry.

**Revision 1.301 — Negotiation Shield documents slice 3a: attach an existing Home Record in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_DOCUMENTS_DESIGN.md`):** a contributor or owner can attach a record already in the household's Home Records to a review, and every reader of a review now sees what is attached. **Review:** a "Documents on this review" list (title, the document's role, "Ready" or "Being checked"; a record the household cannot see appears as "Private document"), never a file link, storage key, file name or content, with **Attach a document** for contributors and owners on the four startable scenarios. **Flow:** one form asks what the document is (the roles that scenario's rules engine counts: quote / premium notice / settlement notice and repair estimate / contractor recommendation and estimate, plus supporting document) and which record → a review card naming the record, its kind and sensitivity and saying it is shared with everyone who can see the review, and that attaching does not read the file → confirm → `NegotiationShieldService.attachDocumentMetadata` (documentId branch). **What is offered (owner decisions D-A1/D-A2):** ACTIVE Home Records visible to the whole household (an owner-only record is never offered, even to an owner), of a STANDARD, FINANCIAL, INSURANCE or CLAIM sensitivity (PERSONAL, SECURITY, LEGAL and unclassified are not), not already attached, newest first, at most 25; never the legacy `Document` store. If nothing qualifies the answer says so, mentions that private or more sensitive records are not offered, and says adding a new file is not currently available in Ask. **Confirmation re-checks the record server-side** (same property, active, household-visible, allowed sensitivity), on top of the canonical attach's own caller and household checks from FRD v1.300. **Idempotency:** the canonical link is idempotent by (case, record), which is this operation's identity; a repeat reports "Document already attached". **Receipt:** states the file was not read and that the analysis does not count it until it is run again; offers **Review** and **Run the analysis** / **Run it again**. **Not here (by decision):** upload from Ask, detach (not a desktop capability), and parsing (3b, after live verification and an OCR measurement on the Pi). **New operation `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE_ATTACH`:** command registry, audience policy, semantic packages, trust corpus, coverage matrix (CONFIRMED_MUTATION, contributor floor), mutation-impact map, action ids (also on the analysis receipt that embeds the review), capture path, skill-less governance entry; `startupRegistryValidation` passes. No schema change. **A validator behaviour found on the way (executed):** `validateAskAnswerTrust` strips an un-allowlisted action from a SUMMARY block but leaves one on a GROUPED_LIST block's own actions, so for block-level list actions the allowlist is checked directly in the tests (`isAskActionApplicable`) rather than through the validator. **Tests:** `tests/ask/negotiationShieldAttach.test.js` (16: the offer rules and bounds, the strict per-scenario answer, the Documents block and who sees its action, the form and the refusals, the review card, the confirmed write with the caller's role and no file details, already-attached, forged and changed records, removed review, allowlist per emitting operation, and the validator). The reader's query-shape test pins that documents are selected by link id, record id, role, title, visibility and scan state only. Backend typecheck is clean and across 20 neighbouring suites the failing tests are identical before and after. Not browser-, database- or file-store-verified.

**Revision 1.300 — Negotiation Shield documents: a private record can no longer be shared through a review (security correction; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_DOCUMENTS_DESIGN.md`, G9/G10 and decision D-A3):** found while designing document attach. A review is shared with the whole household, but `attachDocumentMetadata` checked only the *caller's* view of a Home Record, so an OWNER could attach an `OWNER_ONLY` record; `getCaseDetail` then returned that record's signed file link, storage key and name to every household member who could read the case (a viewer included), and `parseCaseDocument` would copy the private document's text and fields into the shared case input (code-traced; the router-level and service-level behaviour was exercised with stubs, not a real file store). **Fixed in the service:** (1) attach also requires the record to be household-visible and not trashed (409 `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_DOCUMENT_NOT_SHARED`; another role still gets the existing not-found, so nothing hints that an owner-only record exists); (2) reading a case sends a non-household record's type and link (so the analysis still counts it) but replaces its name with "Private document" and withholds the file link, storage key, type and size, whoever reads it; (3) parse refuses such a document before any text is read (same 409). **Tests:** `tests/unit/negotiationShieldDocumentVisibility.test.js` (5; the three behaviours above fail against the previous service, the contributor-not-found and the household attach/replay cases are regression guards); the 20 neighbouring suites' failures are identical before and after. **Not changed:** the page's own uploads (household visibility) and their `STANDARD` classification of every document type (a product question, recorded in the design doc); records already attached before this change keep their link but show as a private document to the household and cannot be parsed. Not verified against a database or a real file store.

**Revision 1.299 — Negotiation Shield slice 2c: edit a review's saved details in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_SLICE2_DESIGN.md`):** a contributor or owner can change the details saved on a review without leaving the conversation. **Entry:** **Edit details** on a review (**Add details** when it has none), for the four scenarios Ask can start; a buyer inspection review is not edited here and says so; viewers are never offered it. Reached only from that action (no typed route). **Flow:** the scenario form (the same fields as starting a review, without a title) pre-filled with what is saved → a review card listing exactly what changes, field by field, before → after (unchanged fields are not listed; a submission identical to what is saved says "Nothing is different" and changes nothing) → confirm. **What is read and written:** the reader (`getNegotiationShieldEditableInputForProperty`) returns only the form's own keys with primitive values, selected without raw text and skipping parsed-document inputs; the write is a new canonical service method, `NegotiationShieldService.updateManualInputFields`, which reads the saved input, applies only the named `set` and `remove`, and saves through the existing `saveManualInput`. That merge exists because `saveManualInput` replaces a case input's whole `structuredData`: a form that knows only some keys would silently drop the rest (a field only the page saved, for instance). Raw text is left as saved. **Not editable:** the title (the page cannot change it either), parsed-document inputs (immutable by the service's own rule), raw text. **Freshness:** the form is bound to a hash of the case, the input and the input's last-saved time, so a form opened, or a card reviewed, before the details changed elsewhere is refused as out of date and nothing is overwritten. **Idempotency:** the save sets named fields to named values, so a repeat is harmless; a retry of the same confirmation finds the details already as intended and reports "Details already saved" instead of failing the freshness check its own write tripped. **After saving:** the analysis is not re-run. The receipt says so, offers **Review** and **Run it again** (or **Run the analysis** if there was none), and the review shows the "details changed after this analysis" note from 2b, which is now reachable from inside Ask. **New operation `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE_UPDATE`:** command registry, audience policy, semantic packages, trust corpus, coverage matrix (CONFIRMED_MUTATION, contributor floor), mutation-impact map, answer-trust action ids (including `negotiation-shield-edit` on the analysis receipt, which carries the review), capture path, skill-less governance entry; `startupRegistryValidation` passes. No schema change. **Tests:** `tests/ask/negotiationShieldEdit.test.js` (20: answer parsing and diff; the restricted reader; the real service merge against an in-memory model including unknown-key preservation, raw text untouched, create-when-none, parsed-input immutability and scope; the form, review card, no-change, refusals; the confirmed write, retry, stale refusal, tampered parameters, removed review; the review's Edit action; the answer-trust validator); 13 fail with the service and reader reverted. Two test-only slips were caught on the way (wrong validator evidence source for the list operation; an expectation that assumed an analysis existed) and the desktop-link guard rightly refused copy that said "on the page" (reworded). Backend typecheck is clean and across 20 neighbouring suites the failing tests are identical before and after. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.298 — Negotiation Shield slice 2b: run the analysis in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_SLICE2_DESIGN.md`):** a contributor or owner can run a review's analysis from the conversation. **Entry:** a **Run the analysis** action on a review that has saved details and no analysis, **Run it again** on a review whose saved details changed after its last analysis (with a note saying so; viewers see the note and the sentence that a contributor or owner can run it, never a button), and **Run the analysis** beside **Review** on a started review's receipt. A review with no saved details offers nothing to run and says so. It is reached only from those actions (`launchContext.entityType NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE`); typed phrasing does not route to it. **Flow:** confirmation ("This runs the rules-based review of the details saved on this review and saves its findings and a draft message. Nothing is sent to anyone." plus "Earlier analyses are kept; this adds a new one." on a re-run) → on confirm, the same project-compliance applicability gate the page's analyze controller runs → `NegotiationShieldService.analyzeCase` (rules-based, no model; appends an analysis and a draft and marks the case ANALYZED) → the guided-journey completion through the one shared function from FRD v1.296, carrying the journey the review was launched from. **Receipt:** an "Analysis saved" progress line followed by the slice 1 review itself (summary, price check, findings, leverage, next steps, the collapsed unsent draft, the boundary). **Idempotency:** `analyzeCase` has no key and appends; a retry of the same confirmation is recognised by an analysis generated at or after the execution began, shown ("Analysis already saved") and not run again, with no second journey completion (a crash between the analysis and the completion would therefore leave the step open, a known and accepted edge); a separate execution is a deliberate re-run. **Reader:** `getNegotiationShieldCaseReviewForProperty` now also returns whether the case has an input and when the newest was saved, selecting that input's `updatedAt` only (never its text or data). **New operation `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE_ANALYZE`:** command registry, audience policy, semantic packages, trust corpus, coverage matrix (CONFIRMED_MUTATION, contributor floor), mutation-impact map (refreshes the list), answer-trust action ids and boundary, skill-less governance entry; `startupRegistryValidation` passes. No schema change. **Tests:** `tests/ask/negotiationShieldAnalyze.test.js` (14: when the run is offered and to whom, propose writes nothing, scope and refusals, confirm runs gate → analysis → completion once and answers with the review, retry and re-run, nothing written when the review is gone / has no details / the gate refuses / the engine fails, and the answer-trust validator); 10 fail with the review builder and reader reverted. Slice 1 and 2a tests were updated for the new, still-restrictive reader contract and the receipt's second action. Backend typecheck is clean and across 20 neighbouring suites the failing tests are identical before and after. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.297 — Negotiation Shield slice 2a: start a review in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_SLICE2_DESIGN.md`):** a contributor or owner can start a Negotiation Shield review from the conversation. Before this the list said "Ask cannot start one yet". **Entry:** a **Start a review** action on the case list and the empty state (contributor and above; a viewer's empty state says Ask cannot start one for a view-only member), or a typed "start a negotiation review". The register used to keep that phrasing off the list read and let it fall to guidance. **Flow:** choose one of four scenarios (contractor quote, premium increase, claim settlement, urgency pressure; or the typed request names it) → one compact form of exactly the fields the page builds and the rules engines read (title, scenario figures and dates, yes/no facts, notes limited to 1000 characters; no pasted-text field, no documents, no Quote Comparison link) → review card → confirm. A review needs a name and at least one detail. **Write:** `NegotiationShieldService.createCase` with the first input, in one transaction, after the same project-compliance applicability check the page's controller runs; nothing is analyzed or sent, and the receipt offers **Review** (the slice 1 read of that case). **Idempotency (design D8, revised):** the create carries `ask:<confirming execution id>:negotiation-shield-start`, stored in a new nullable `NegotiationShieldCase.idempotencyKey` under a `(propertyId, idempotencyKey)` unique index; a replayed confirmation, a reclaimed worker or a race returns the original case, a separate execution is a deliberate second review, and the page's own creates store no key (the HTTP body validator drops the field). **New operation `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE_START`:** command registry, audience policy, semantic packages, trust corpus, coverage matrix (CONFIRMED_MUTATION, contributor floor), mutation-impact map (refreshes `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASES`), answer-trust action ids, capture path, skill-less governance entry (the negotiation-shield skill stays a READ skill, same precedent as `HOME_HABIT_UPDATE`); `startupRegistryValidation` passes. **OWNER STEP, and an ordering rule:** this adds a column. Run `npx prisma db push` in `apps/backend` (and `npx prisma generate` in `apps/workers`) **before** the new backend image serves traffic: the regenerated client selects the column on every `NegotiationShieldCase` read, so a deploy ahead of the push would break the page's own Negotiation Shield reads, not only Ask's start. **Tests:** `tests/ask/negotiationShieldStart.test.js` (22: routing, the field specs and validation, the choice/form/confirmation/blocked/refresh states, the confirmed write and its refusals, the answer-trust validator, and the idempotency of the real `createCase` against an in-memory model: retry, separate execution, property scope, race, and the page's unchanged create); with only the service reverted the retry and race tests fail, with all source reverted 13 fail. `emptyStateAddPaths` gained a contributor variant. Backend typecheck is clean, and across 20 neighbouring suites the failing tests are identical before and after the change. Not browser- or database-verified: the capture form is rendered by the shared capture renderer and was not seen.

**Revision 1.296 — The Negotiation Shield journey completion is one shared function (refactor, no behaviour change; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_SLICE2_DESIGN.md`, D6 and finding F7):** after an analysis the analyze *controller* recorded a guided-journey step completion (`guidanceJourneyService.recordToolCompletion`) inline, so a caller that used only the service would have skipped it. That block moved to `negotiationShieldJourneyCompletion.ts` (`recordNegotiationShieldAnalysisCompletion`), which the controller now calls and which Ask's analysis command (slice 2b) will call, so both surfaces keep identical side effects. The module is deliberately lightweight (it imports the guidance service and the logger, not the OCR stack) and stays best-effort: a journey that cannot be updated is logged and never fails a saved analysis. **Behaviour preserved, shown by comparison:** the old (HEAD) and new controllers were run over 60 combinations (five scenarios × six journey/step/signal query shapes × hook success and failure) with the same stubs; the input to `recordToolCompletion`, the response status and the response body are identical in all 60. **Tests:** `tests/unit/negotiationShieldJourneyCompletion.test.js` (8; environment-independent, `sharp` stubbed) covers the scenario-to-signal mapping, the default and overridden step/journey/signal, the proof payload with and without analysis and draft, failure swallowed with a warning, and the controller running the gate, the analysis, the shared completion exactly once, then answering 201 (and no completion when the analysis fails). Backend typecheck clean; the neighbouring Negotiation Shield suites and the known failures are unchanged. No manual page check was made or required. Not verified with a database.

**Revision 1.295 — Negotiation Shield traditional routes now enforce a household-role floor (security correction; 2026-10-12; `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_SLICE2_DESIGN.md`, section 8):** found while designing slice 2. `negotiationShield.routes.ts` had `propertyAuthMiddleware` (membership only) and no `requireHouseholdRole` on any route, and no controller rejects a VIEWER, so a read-only household member could create, edit, attach to, parse and analyze shared cases and start or record buyer negotiations over HTTP (executed at the router layer against the pre-change router; the controllers were stubbed). **Fixed:** `requireHouseholdRole('CONTRIBUTOR')` on the seven mutating routes; reads stay at VIEWER; `POST …/events` (tool analytics) stays at VIEWER by decision. **Verification:** `tests/unit/negotiationShieldRouteAuthorization.test.js` (5; the viewer and no-role cases fail on the previous router) mounts the real router and the real floor with stubbed authentication, membership lookup, rate limiter and controllers. Behaviour change: a VIEWER's attempt to change a case now returns 403. Ask is unaffected (it has no Negotiation Shield write yet; its own floor will be CONTRIBUTOR, design D1). Not verified with a real token or database.

**Revision 1.294 — Negotiation Shield slice 1: Review a case's analysis and draft in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Negotiation Shield"):** the first slice of the register's recommended item 1, scoped (owner direction) to a read. Before this the list said each review's analysis and draft were "not currently available in Ask". **Now:** each case row in `NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASES` has a **Review** action that opens that one case in the conversation. **Design:** no new operation. Like the Property Brief preview, the same operation handles a launch with `entityType NEGOTIATION_SHIELD_CASE` and the case id as `entityId`, so there is no new registry, skill, audience, semantic-package or coverage-matrix entry and nothing for boot validation to miss; the new action ids (`negotiation-shield-review`, `negotiation-shield-back`) are allowlisted in `OPERATION_ACTION_IDS`. **Canonical read:** `getNegotiationShieldCaseReviewForProperty` in `negotiationShieldCaseList.ts` (the module Ask already uses so it does not load the service's OCR dependencies); the service's analysis and draft serializers now delegate there, so the page and Ask share one serializer and the same ordering as `getCaseDetail`. The query is `findFirst({ id, propertyId })` including only the latest analysis and latest draft: no inputs, documents, buyer finding links, storage keys or file URLs are ever loaded. A case of another property reads as "no longer available". Viewer floor, as the canonical read. **Shown:** summary, the stored pricing assessment with its normalized figures (quote, premiums, settlement, estimate, gap, requested concession), findings, leverage, recommended next steps, the confidence explanation, the existing legal/insurance/pricing boundary, and a way back. **Draft:** its own list block titled "Draft email — nothing has been sent", saying Ask has not sent it and cannot; the body is carried only in the on-demand `detail` (split at paragraph boundaries into parts of at most 1100 characters), collapsed by default in the shared renderer (`GroupedListBlock` now reveals `detail` for `negotiation-shield-draft` as well as `project-detail-records`, with its own "Show the draft" label). **No analysis yet:** says so plainly, offers only the way back, shows no draft block and no unavailable action. **Not shown:** raw saved inputs, extracted document text, buyer findings (Buyer negotiation outcomes remain a residual gap). **Defects found by the tests on the way:** the new action ids were first added to the boundary-id map (`OPERATION_BOUNDARIES`) rather than `OPERATION_ACTION_IDS`, so the validator silently stripped the way-back action (the answer-trust test caught it); and a copy edit to the empty state broke the guard that requires "Ask cannot start one" (reverted). Tests: `tests/ask/negotiationShieldReview.test.js` (8; all fail on HEAD) and `src/components/ask/__tests__/negotiationShieldReview.test.tsx` (3). `next build` exits 0, backend typecheck is clean, registry validation and the desktop-link guard pass; the known backend failures are unchanged and 4 frontend suites (10 tests) fail identically without this change. Not browser- or database-verified: no real client ran, and the reader was exercised against a stubbed model.

**Revision 1.293 — "Add a closing task" resolves to the Buyer Plan, and never builds one (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Open items by kind", section 4):** the register recorded this as an ambiguity needing an intent decision. Reading the code showed two defects, and the decision (owner, option A) was narrower: what to do when the home has no Buyer Plan. **Defects on HEAD:** (a) `add a closing task`, `create a closing task for the title search` and `Add a closing task to schedule the final walkthrough` routed to `MAINTENANCE_TASK_CREATE` (a closing task became a maintenance task), because `buyerTaskCreatePattern` matched only "closing *plan* task" or "to my closing plan"; `show my closing tasks` and `what closing tasks are left?` read `MAINTENANCE_STATUS`; (b) `BUYER_TASK_CREATE` confirms into `HomeBuyerTaskService.createTask`, which calls `getOrCreateChecklist` and so would create a buyer checklist with its default tasks on **any** property (code read, not executed), the status read having a not-active state that create lacked. **Now:** "add/create a closing task", "buyer task", "closing plan task" and "closing checklist item" route to `BUYER_TASK_CREATE`, tested ahead of the completion pattern so "add a closing task to get the survey done" does not complete anything; `show/list/what … closing tasks | checklist` route to `BUYER_PLAN_STATUS`. **With an active Buyer Plan** the title is the work to do (`Schedule the final walkthrough`, not `Closing task to schedule the final walkthrough`; also `: …`, `called …`, `for …`), reviewed and confirmed as before. **Without one** the existing "This property has no active Buyer Plan" explanation is returned with "Nothing has changed", the plan state is read first, and no checklist or task is created; there is no fallback to a maintenance task. An unreadable plan state fails the request and writes nothing. Plain "add a task to schedule the final walkthrough" is not a closing task and still creates a maintenance task. **Not done:** the confirm handler does not re-check plan state; the guard is at the propose step only. Whether a plan can become inactive inside the 30-minute confirmation window was not investigated. Tests: `tests/ask/buyerClosingTaskRouting.test.js` (7; 6 fail on HEAD). A first draft of the no-plan test passed vacuously because `buyerPlanContextProvider` is `Object.freeze`d and the stub did nothing; the test now asserts the stubbed canonical read was reached. Existing buyer suites, registry validation, the desktop-link guard and the known failures are unchanged (`skillTaxonomyExpansion` and `askTrustArchitecture` fail identically on HEAD). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.292 — "reserve plan" phrasings reach the plan read (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12):** `capitalReservePattern` named "capital reserve plan" and "reserve fund" but not "reserve plan", so `show my reserve plan`, `how is my reserve plan doing?`, `my reserve plan` and `is my reserve plan enough?` fell to general guidance (`open my reserve plan` to a clarification), found while building 1.291. "reserve plan" is added to the pattern's alternation; the Reserve Fund command routes (recalculate, posture, change, contribution) sit earlier in the cascade and are unaffected, and a question about changing the plan (`should I change my reserve plan?`) is the read, as `should I add $500 …?` already was. One alternation word in `askOperationRegistry.ts`. Test added to `tests/ask/reserveFundDirectRouting.test.js` (15; the new one fails on HEAD). Registry validation, the desktop-link guard and the 10 known failures unchanged. Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.291 — Reserve Fund typed planning commands: recalculate, posture, and the unnamed change (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Open items by kind", section 4):** typed Reserve Fund mutations other than a contribution (1.290) now resolve to a specific governed action and enter the same confirmation-gated command the buttons use. **Two distinct failures on HEAD, both fixed:** (a) *no route to the command* — `set my reserve fund posture to conservative`, `change my reserve plan to aggressive` and `use the conservative posture` went to the read-only plan or to general guidance; (b) *command reached with no action* — `recalculate my reserve plan` reached `RESERVE_FUND_COMMAND` through the local classifier, but the handler requires a declared `actionId` and answered "Choose an action from the reserve-fund answer" (code read; routing observed by probe). **Behaviour:** `recalculate | recompute | rebuild | re-run | redo` + the reserve plan, and an explicit named posture (conservative, moderate, aggressive), go straight to the existing confirmation ("Recalculate the reserve plan?" / "Use the … planning posture?"), which re-evaluates property context at confirmation exactly as before. A change with no named change (`change my reserve plan`, `manage my reserve fund`, `update my reserve fund`) answers with an inline choice of **Log a balance change**, **Recalculate the plan** and **Change planning posture**; the last asks for Conservative, Moderate or Aggressive (the same picker answers `change my reserve planning posture` directly). Each choice is the governed action the plan answer's buttons launch, so nothing is written until the confirmation. **Implementation:** one grammar (`support/reserveFundMessage.ts`, `parseReserveFundMessage`) shared by the registry route and the handler; one new action id, `reserve-fund-choose-posture` (it writes nothing), plus the existing plan-wide ids added to the `RESERVE_FUND_COMMAND` allowlist in `askAnswerTrustPolicy.ts` (without them the choices would be stripped silently). Block-level actions launch with `action.actionId`, not `action.id`, so each choice carries both; the typecheck, not a test, first caught that a SUMMARY action must be `START_WORKFLOW`. Questions (`should I use the aggressive posture?`), reads, `recalculate my budget` and non-reserve uses of "aggressive" are not matched. A refresh never re-resolves the message; a viewer is told the role requirement and is offered no buttons. **Not done:** balance-to-target (`set my reserve fund to $5,000`, needs a delta decision); remove-contribution and accept-reconciliation by typing (they need a target entry). Tests: `tests/ask/reserveFundDirectRouting.test.js` (14; 6 fail on HEAD). `startupRegistryValidation` and the desktop-link guard pass; the 10 known failures are unchanged. Not browser- or database-verified: the launch round trip is exercised with the client's launch fields, but no real client ran.

**Revision 1.290 — Reserve Fund direct-message routing (IW-TRAD-002; 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Open items by kind", section 4):** a typed deposit or withdrawal ("add $500 to my reserve fund", "withdraw $300 from my reserve fund", "log a reserve fund withdrawal of $80", "I put $250 in the reserve fund") now reaches the existing governed `RESERVE_FUND_COMMAND`. Before this, the governed contribution command was reached only from the ledger's own button, and the typed phrasings went elsewhere: `add $500 to my reserve fund` opened the **inventory-item** form (the generic "add …" pattern took it), `Add a deposit …`, `deposit $… into …` and `withdraw … from …` returned the read-only plan, and `Record a $500 reserve contribution` fell to general guidance. **Routing:** a new deterministic route ahead of the inventory pattern, backed by one dependency-free grammar (`support/reserveFundMessage.ts`) that the registry and the handler share; questions ("should I add $500 …?"), reads, and a balance target ("set my reserve fund balance to $5,000", which needs a delta) are deliberately not matched. **Behaviour:** the handler opens the same contribution form the ledger's Add button opens, with the entry type and any single unambiguous dollar amount filled in (two different amounts leave the amount blank); the form is reviewed and then confirmed exactly as for the declared action, through the same `homeReserveFundService.addContribution` the traditional controller calls. Nothing is written by the message. A viewer is blocked before any form opens. **No new operation, action id or desktop link;** `startupRegistryValidation` passes unchanged. The coverage-matrix entry for `RESERVE_FUND_COMMAND` now records `messageRoutable: true` (it already was by definition; the matrix said `false`). **Not done, recorded as open:** a typed "recalculate my reserve plan" or "set my reserve planning posture" still reaches a handler with no declared action and answers "Choose an action from the reserve-fund answer" (code read; routing observed by probe, handler not executed). Tests: `tests/ask/reserveFundDirectRouting.test.js` (7; 3 fail on HEAD, 4 are regression guards). Not browser- or database-verified.

**Revision 1.289 — all-tools register reclassified into four kinds of open item (documentation only; 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Open items by kind"):** the first list of "pending product decisions" treated every unbuilt Ask command as one. The register now separates (1) genuine product decisions: professional close, Continuity Plan sensitive capture, Emergency Help, Negotiation Shield archive; (2) previously decided boundaries: Gemini output under v1.70, upload analyzers, the correction escape hatch (undocumented, owner to confirm); (3) residual parity backlog; (4) routing defects: Reserve Fund direct routing and closing-task disambiguation. Adds a "Decided boundary" status, defines "Needs product decision" narrowly, and marks the handler sweep row fixed. No code changed.

Starting and editing a plan stay unavailable in Ask, as the empty state already said. The retired allowlist entry was removed and the coverage-matrix notes corrected. **Open product question, unchanged:** collecting access notes, shutoff details and contacts in conversation. The read deliberately omits them because answers stay in the conversation, so a write path needs its own decision on sensitivity before it is built. *Executed:* `homeDigitalWillCapabilitySlice` 11/11 (four assertions fail on the previous handler), `emptyStateAddPaths` 7/7, `suggestedNextActionsCapabilities`, `promptHistoryExemption`, `startupRegistryValidation`, `askNoDesktopLinksGuard` 3/3 with `homeDigitalWill.handler.ts` removed from the path baseline (it failed first, as designed), backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the full `tests/ask` suite.

**Revision 1.272 — Price Finalization answers no longer send the user to a desktop page (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Batches completed"):** the third batch from the handler sweep. **Found:** the saved-prices list and its empty state carried an "Open Price Finalization" action (removed at runtime, and the operation's allowlist held only that one id), and three sentences told the user to open the page to save a record, to edit, finalize or book, or to see older records. **Built:** the answer carries no action or link. Because Ask has no command for any of those writes, the empty state, the list description and the older-records note now say that saving, editing, finalizing and continuing to booking are not currently available in Ask. The retired allowlist entry and the matrix hand-off note were removed or corrected. The read itself is unchanged: primary-owner floor, newest first, 20 records, grouped by status. **Open product question:** which Price Finalization writes to build in Ask (save from an accepted quote, finalize, link a booking). The register already notes the dependency on quote selection in Quote Comparison. *Executed:* `priceFinalizationsCapabilitySlice` 8/8 (four assertions fail on the previous handler), `zeroOperationReclassificationSlice` pass, `startupRegistryValidation` 1/1, `askNoDesktopLinksGuard` 3/3 with `priceFinalization.handler.ts` removed from the path baseline (it failed first, as designed), backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the full `tests/ask` suite.

**Revision 1.271 — Coverage answers no longer link to desktop pages (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Batches completed"):** the second batch from the handler sweep. **Found:** the coverage review's rows linked to the desktop inventory page, and the comparison answer carried two "Open coverage comparison" actions and per-option links plus the sentence "open the workspace to record a keep/shop decision". The comparison actions had never been on the operation's allowlist, so they were always removed; the copy still promised them. **Built:** coverage review rows are `INVENTORY_ITEM` entities that open the inline item record and carry no link. The comparison answer has no action or link; where it used to point at the page it now states that adding and verifying a policy, adding a quote or policy term to compare, and recording a keep/shop decision are not currently available in Ask, because Ask has no command for any of them. The stale `COVERAGE_GAPS` allowlist entry was removed, and the coverage matrix hand-off notes for both operations were corrected. **Open product question:** whether to build Ask commands for the comparison write path (add option, record decision) — until then the answer is read-only by necessity. **Not in this batch:** the "Review coverage records" link in the expiration-reminder answer (`miscHandlers`). *Executed:* `coverageNoDesktopLinks` 3/3 (all three fail on the previous handler), `coverageComparisonStatus` 12/12, `coverageComparisonStrip` 5/5, `coverageConflictResolution` 3/3, `coverageGapsAnswerContract` 1/1, `askNoDesktopLinksGuard` 3/3 with `coverage.handler.ts` removed from both baselines (it failed first, as designed), backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the full `tests/ask` suite.

**Revision 1.270 — Claims answers no longer link to desktop pages (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Batches completed"):** the first batch from the handler sweep. **Found:** the Claims handler built `/dashboard/properties/{id}/claims`, `/incidents`, per-record and Emergency Help links that the trust pass removes, and one sentence telling the user to open the page. **Built:** the incident and claim status read, the file-claim capture form, the claim status-change answers (choose a claim, not allowed, review) and the incident continuation carry no page link, row link or list-level link. Claim rows keep their declared status-change actions and inline detail; incident rows are plain rows. Where an answer would otherwise be a dead end — an empty status answer, and the incident continuation, whose own copy says "start a draft claim" — a contributor or owner is offered **Start a claim**, a `START_WORKFLOW` to `CLAIM_FILE` (the message "Start a claim" routes there; a viewer is offered nothing); a populated status answer stays calm and offers no list-level action. The continuation states that the Emergency Help AI troubleshooter is not currently available in Ask, replacing the link that FRD 1.64 kept as a labelled handoff. The continuation now reads the access the orchestrator already resolved instead of resolving it again. The retired allowlist ids (`open-incidents`, `open-claims`, `open-incidents-list`, `open-claims-list`, `open-emergency-help`) were removed and `claim-file-start` added. **Product note:** the Emergency Help AI troubleshooter, previously a handoff by product option A, now has no entry point in Ask; whether to build one remains the open Emergency Help P0 decision in the register. *Executed:* `claimsCalmAnswer` 7/7, `claimsCapabilitySlice` 6/6, `emergencyCardLaunchSlice` 4/4, `claimFileCaptureRetention` 7/7 (the new and updated assertions fail on the previous handler), `askNoDesktopLinksGuard` 3/3 with `claims.handler.ts` removed from both baselines (the guard failed first, as designed), backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the full `tests/ask` suite.

**Revision 1.269 — A guard against new desktop links in Ask handlers (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12):** the handler sweep (1.268) found that new handlers could reintroduce links the trust pass strips and copy that points at pages Ask does not offer. **Built:** `tests/ask/askNoDesktopLinksGuard.test.js` counts desktop paths (`/dashboard/...` other than `/dashboard/ask`, and the public closeout recipient page) and page-referencing copy in `services/ask`, per file, against recorded baselines (98 paths in 40 files; 26 copy matches in 12 files at the time of the sweep). It is a ratchet: it fails when a file gains any, and when a file loses any without its baseline being lowered in the same change, so the list can only shrink. Comments are not counted; the answer-trust policy and the coverage matrix are exempt as the places that name paths. Project Tracker keeps one path by design (the closeout recipient link shown as text). **Limits:** the copy check misses a sentence that names a tool without the word page, panel, planner or workspace, and a path assembled at runtime. *Executed:* the guard passes (3/3) and was mutation-checked in both directions for paths and for copy (a new file with a desktop path fails; a fixed file with an unchanged baseline fails; new page copy in a clean file fails). *Not run:* the full `tests/ask` suite. No handler was changed; fixing them in batches remains open.

**Revision 1.268 — Handler-wide desktop-link sweep and an answered trust question (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Handler sweep"):** after the Project Tracker, Negotiation Shield and Quote Comparison fixes, the rest of `services/ask` was scanned for desktop paths, link actions and copy that sends the user to a page. **Found:** about 33 handler files (plus `askBuyerPlanPresentation`, `askFocusedGuidance`, `execution/askConcierge` and `support/capture`) still build links to desktop pages; the answer-trust pass removes them, so the user sees no button where the handler meant one. About 22 user-visible strings across about 15 files still tell the user to open a page or panel (for example Service Price Radar, Coverage, Claims, Appliance Oracle, Budget Planner, Property Brief, HVAC decision, Home Event Radar, Home Upgrade Planner and the three upload-driven analyzers). About 15 more only name the page a record shares, which is harmless. **Resolved question:** FRD 1.264 left open whether confirmation receipts pass the trust validator. They do: `askConfirm` runs `validateAskConfirmedCompletion` and replaces the result with the validated one before it is stored, so a desktop link in a receipt is removed, not shown. Every `href` field in the presentation contract is covered by that pass; free text is not. **Not changed:** no handler was edited in this pass. The register lists the categories, the instructive strings, and a suggested order: a guard test with a shrinking allowlist first, then per-tool batches that either point at an Ask command or say the capability is not currently available in Ask. *Executed:* static scan only (regular-expression counts are indicative; categories were read from the code). *Not run:* any test, a browser or database.

**Revision 1.267 — "Add / record / create …" no longer turns other record types into an inventory item (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12):** following 1.266, a probe of 44 natural create phrasings found the same defect for other commands: the generic inventory create match accepts "add", "record" or "create" followed by almost anything except a short noun list, and runs before most create commands. **Found:** "Create an insurance claim", "Create a guided plan" and "Add a household member" bypassed `CLAIM_FILE`, `GUIDANCE_JOURNEY_CREATE` and `HOUSEHOLD_INVITATION`; and "Create a project", "Add a plant", "Add a material spec", "Create a property brief", "Create a seasonal checklist", "Create a document", "Add a deposit to my reserve fund" and similar produced an inventory-item form for something that is not an inventory item. **Built:** `inventoryCreatePattern` additionally excludes claim, project, plan, guidance, journey, household member, closing task, deposit, contribution, withdrawal, brief, document, checklist, habit, plant, material, negotiation, quote comparison, comparison workspace and reminder when they appear within four words of the verb, so those phrasings reach their own command where one has a message route and otherwise fall through to a guidance answer. **Unchanged:** the inventory command's own phrasings ("Add a Samsung dishwasher", "Create an inventory item for my water heater", "Record a new heat pump"). **Decisions left open:** whether "Add a closing task …" should reach `BUYER_TASK_CREATE` or the maintenance command, and whether Reserve Fund contributions should be message-routable; these are product questions and are recorded in the parity register as Needs product decision. A noun list is brittle by construction: a new record type needs adding to it or to an earlier match. *Executed:* `inventoryCreateRouting.test.js` 3/3 (five phrasings reach their own command; sixteen are never offered as inventory; eight real inventory additions still are) and fails on the previous pattern; `quoteComparisonCreateRouting` 2/2; backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; a sweep of the 94 test files that call the routing resolvers or name `INVENTORY_ITEM_CREATE` found seven failing files (`askGovernance` 2, `askInteractionCoverageMatrix` 2, `askRoutingCalibration`, `askTrustArchitecture`, `hvacDecisionRouting`, `healthGapCapture`, `skillTaxonomyExpansion`); each was re-run against the unmodified registry (or recorded before this change) and fails there as well, so none is caused by it, and the `askGovernance` golden-prompt test now passes. *Not run:* the full `tests/ask` suite, a real browser or database.

**Revision 1.266 — "Create a quote comparison" starts the workspace, not an inventory item (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12):** "Create a quote comparison" and "Create a quote comparison workspace (for …)" were claimed by the generic "create <anything>" inventory command, so a homeowner using the natural verb was offered a new inventory item; "Start …" and "Set up …" were not affected. **Built:** the quote-comparison create match now runs before the inventory create match in `resolveAskOperation` (`askOperationRegistry.ts`). Its own pattern is unchanged, so it still requires create, start, open or set up followed by "quote comparison", "comparison workspace" or "workspace for quotes, bids or proposals". *Executed:* `quoteComparisonCreateRouting.test.js` 2/2 (eight create/start/set-up phrasings route to `QUOTE_COMPARISON_CREATE`; "Add a Samsung dishwasher" and "Create a record for my water heater" still route to `INVENTORY_ITEM_CREATE`; "Compare my roofing quotes" still reviews), and the `askGovernance` golden-prompt test that was failing on this now passes. A sweep of the 40 test files that call the routing resolvers found three failing files (`hvacDecisionRouting`, `askRoutingCalibration`, `askTrustArchitecture`); each was re-run against the unmodified registry and fails identically there, so none is caused by this change. *Not run:* the full `tests/ask` suite, a real browser or database.

**Revision 1.265 — Negotiation Shield and Quote Comparison no longer send the user to a desktop page (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`):** the all-tools register's code verification found two handlers still emitting `/dashboard/properties/...` links that the answer-trust policy removes at runtime, leaving copy that told the user to open a page they could not open. **Built:** the Negotiation Shield case list has no "Open Negotiation Shield" action and no per-row case link, and says a review's analysis and draft are not currently available in Ask. The Quote Comparison review has no "Open quote comparison" action and no row links; its empty state offers **Create comparison workspace** as an in-Ask `START_WORKFLOW` to `QUOTE_COMPARISON_CREATE` (message "Start a quote comparison workspace", which routes to that command) instead of a desktop link; the rejected-quote and gap copy no longer refers to a page; and the create receipt says adding proposals is not currently available in Ask. The two dead trust-policy allowlist entries, the unused `quoteComparisonWorkspaceHref` helper and its test were removed, and the coverage-matrix handoff note for Negotiation Shield was corrected. **Found, not fixed:** "Create a quote comparison (workspace)" routes to `INVENTORY_ITEM_CREATE` rather than `QUOTE_COMPARISON_CREATE`, which fails the `askGovernance` golden-prompt test; "Start a …" and "Set up a …" route correctly. **Not changed:** about 30 other handlers contain `/dashboard/properties` strings and have not been classified; `support/capture.ts` returns desktop fallback paths for several operations. *Executed:* `negotiationShieldCasesCapabilitySlice` 7/7, `quoteReviewStrip` 7/7 (new: no desktop link, an in-Ask create action whose message routes to the command), `startupRegistryValidation` 1/1, `projectTrackerCapabilityActivation` 4/4, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Existing count-drift failures in `askInteractionCoverageMatrix`, `askTrustArchitecture` and `askGovernance` (operation and command counts, plus the routing prompt above) are not caused by this change. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the full `tests/ask` suite.

**Revision 1.264 — Project Tracker status verification: no desktop links, paid-payment and log-target fixes (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12; `docs/product/ASK_COZY_PROJECT_TRACKER_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, commit `6c06840b`):** the Project Tracker parity implementation was re-checked against the code and four defects were corrected. **Built:** the project list no longer offers an "Open Project Tracker" link, because the answer-trust policy permits only in-conversation Ask links (owner decision, 2026-10-10) and the action was being removed at runtime while the parity document still described it. The closeout share receipt now shows the full recipient link and its expiry as text, so the one-time token no longer depends on an href; the dispute-package receipt says the package was saved to Home Records instead of linking to the desktop documents page; both dead trust-policy allowlist entries were removed. `updatePayment` now rejects a paid payment in `projectTracker.service` (`PAYMENT_PAID`), not only through the Ask handler's action filter. Progress-entry delete resolves its target by id within the project rather than searching the latest 100 entries, so an older entry can be deleted. **Decisions recorded:** closeout share-link revocation remains an API-only capability and is the one documented parity exception; the raw share token is part of the stored Ask execution result as well as the receipt, because the receipt is the stored result. **Open:** whether the confirmation path runs the answer-trust validator on receipt actions was not established; the fix makes the receipt correct either way. *Executed:* `projectTrackerProjectsCapabilitySlice.test.js` 19/19 (new: share receipt text link with no href, dispute receipt with no href, delete of an entry beyond the latest 100, service-level paid-payment refusal), `projectTrackerCapabilityActivation` 4/4, `startupRegistryValidation` 1/1, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. `askInteractionCoverageMatrix` and `askTrustArchitecture` fail on operation-count drift that this change does not touch. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the Playwright baselines, the full `tests/ask` suite, a rollout of the image to the cluster.

**Revision 1.263 — All-tools parity register (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-12):** desktop-to-conversation findings and recommendations are now consolidated in [`ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md`](ASK_COZY_ALL_TOOLS_PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION.md), rather than being represented by the Project Tracker appendix alone. The register covers the implemented Material Specs, Plant Advisor, Ownership Costs, Home Timeline, Reserve Fund, Property Brief, Mortgage Refinance Radar and Project Tracker reviews; shared compact conversational UI requirements; residual bounded gaps; the updated P0/P1/P2 inventory; intentional page-only analyzers; and recommended sequencing. Tool FRDs remain authoritative, this FRD owns shared Ask behavior, and the Project Tracker, Reserve Fund and Property Brief documents remain detailed appendices. The original P0 statement that Project Tracker only listed projects is explicitly superseded by its implemented create, detail, lifecycle, execution, completion and closeout workflows.

**Revision 1.262 — Project Tracker active execution controls in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-11):** the inline project record now supports the normal day-to-day execution work that previously stopped at a static read. Contributors and owners can add a manual milestone; complete an eligible manual milestone with its date and notes; mark an eligible payment paid with date and method; log a dated progress note; log a minor, major or blocking issue; and resolve a household-controlled issue with resolution notes. Every control is a declared item or project action under `PROJECT_TRACKER_COMMAND`, collects the bounded desktop fields, requires review and explicit confirmation, re-reads the live project and target immediately before execution, and calls the canonical Project Tracker service. Existing service safeguards remain authoritative: compliance-projected milestones and issues stay read-only, disputed or already-complete milestones cannot complete, photo-required milestones still need linked photo evidence, milestone-gated payments still require the milestone, payments on hold cannot be marked paid, blocking issues hold eligible payments, and resolving the last blocking issue releases them. The receipt refreshes Project Tracker reads. Change-order proposal/review/approval and final completion remain separate because their compliance, evidence, closeout and cross-system write-back contracts require dedicated flows.

**Revision 1.261 — Project Tracker lifecycle controls in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-11):** a contributor or owner reviewing an inline project can now **Start work**, **Pause project**, **Resume project**, or **Cancel project** when the live status permits it. The declared-action-only `PROJECT_TRACKER_COMMAND` re-reads the selected project, shows an explicit confirmation, revalidates the transition immediately before execution, and calls the canonical `updateProject` or `cancelProject` service. Cancellation retains the service's journey and Operational Work reconciliation. Completed and cancelled projects are read-only, and completion is intentionally not treated as a status toggle because its evidence, verification, closeout and write-back contract remains separately governed. The receipt refreshes Project Tracker reads and continues to the in-Ask list.

**Revision 1.260 — Project Tracker execution detail is available inline (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-11):** every Project Tracker row now carries a governed **Review project** continuation rather than a traditional-page deep link. The continuation re-reads the canonical `ProjectRecord`, its ordered milestones, payments, change orders and issues, plus the latest 50 progress-log entries. It presents the project status and open/blocking issue state, the current contract/paid/remaining position, and grouped execution records with milestone schedules and photo requirements, payment due/paid state, change-order cost and compliance state, issue payment-blocking and resolution state, and progress dates/photo counts. The same recorded-data boundary remains visible. The list operation now formally permits the table and empty-state blocks used by this detail view, and the Project Tracker skill and answer-trust policy declare the continuation. This closes the read-side parity gap without claiming any write occurred. Milestone, payment, change-order, issue, progress, lifecycle and governed completion commands remain separate confirmation-gated work.

**Revision 1.261 — Compact Project Tracker workflows and complete capture controls (IW-PRES-010/IW-TRAD-002; owner review, 2026-10-11):** Project Tracker creation now follows the compact conversational form contract instead of presenting the desktop setup form as a long stack. Project type remains full width but initially bounds its large choice set behind **More** while keeping the current selection visible; delivery and provider share a row; amount and project dates share a row; permit/HOA decisions and their conditional rationales align as pairs; and the first milestone uses the remaining responsive row. Repeated introduction, question, help and save-boundary copy collapse to one setup line. `PROJECT_TRACKER_COMMAND_INPUTS` also opts into the compact two-column treatment, with long explanations and larger choice groups retaining full width. The review found and fixed a functional renderer gap: Ask's command schemas already declare `NUMBER`, `MONEY`, and `LONG_TEXT`, but the frontend capture contract/control did not recognize them, leaving blank panels such as Contract or project amount. Those schemas now render bounded numeric/money inputs and compact textareas across project create/edit, milestones, payments, change orders, progress, issues and completion, without changing server validation or confirmation. *Executed:* frontend TypeScript and changed-file lint; four focused control-rendering tests; Project Tracker compact-layout guard; `git diff --check`. *Not run:* browser or service environment, per repository validation rules.

**Project setup density follow-up (2026-10-13):** The compact grid still exposed every setup decision simultaneously and was visually congested in the conversational column. `PROJECT_TRACKER_CREATE_INPUTS` now declares four focused groups—Project, Team and budget, Schedule and milestone, and Requirements and documents. The shared grouped-workflow renderer preserves draft values across stages and submits the same complete payload only from the final stage, so validation, review and confirmation behavior are unchanged while secondary decisions remain progressively disclosed.

**Revision 1.260 — Compact conversational workflow forms (IW-PRES-010; owner review, 2026-10-11):** multi-field capture in Ask must not reproduce a desktop settings page as a vertical stack of separately framed field cards. Compact workflows use one bounded workflow surface, borderless labeled field groups, full-width controls inside their assigned grid cells, tight row gaps, and responsive grouping of short related fields. The Add Timeline Event workflow is the acceptance case: title, type and details retain full conversational width; date, amount and provider share one row on wide cards and stack safely on narrow screens; repeated workflow heading, question, help and destination copy collapse to one short instruction line because the answer immediately above already establishes the task. The primary review action follows the last field without an empty spacer. Required fields, validation, draft persistence, review and consequential-action confirmation are unchanged. The shared compact field treatment also removes the nested white-card appearance from Material Specs, Plant Advisor, maintenance, inventory and ownership-scenario workflows that already opt into compact mode. *Executed:* frontend TypeScript check; focused source-shape guard for timeline layout passed, while its pre-existing unrelated `AskWorkspace.tsx` 450-line ceiling assertion remains red at 474 lines. *Not run:* browser or service environment, per repository validation rules.

**Revision 1.259 — Project Tracker can start a real tracked project in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-11):** the Project Tracker answer now offers **Start a project** to contributors and owners in both empty and populated states. The declared-action-only `PROJECT_TRACKER_CREATE` command captures the desktop create flow's core execution contract: name and type, contractor-led or DIY fulfillment, contractor name when applicable, amount, start and expected-end dates, explicit permit and HOA applicability with a source or rationale when decided, and one required initial milestone with an optional date. The milestone is required because capability governance defines meaningful Project Tracker completion as a project with at least one milestone or verified progress; Ask does not claim a bare draft is meaningful setup. The form is bound to the current project list, then shows a material-decision confirmation before `projectTracker.service.createProject` writes the canonical `ProjectRecord` and `ProjectMilestone`; duplicate protection remains owned by that service. The receipt refreshes the still-visible Project Tracker answer and continues to the in-Ask list. Viewers keep the read-only answer and see no create action. This is the first complete parity slice; project-detail reads and milestone, payment, change-order, issue, progress, pause/cancel and governed completion commands remain follow-up work. Registered in operation, audience, domain-command, answer-trust, mutation-impact, semantic-package, certification, coverage, skill, adapter and capability-bridge contracts. *Executed:* backend typecheck and the focused Project Tracker/empty-state tests (15 passed). *Not run:* a real browser or database.

**Revision 1.258 — Home Timeline desktop parity controls in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner report, 2026-10-10):** the Ask timeline now exposes the remaining normal desktop actions without sending the homeowner to the traditional page. Its typed timeline contract can opt into keyword search, event-type and date-range filters, 25/50/80/150/200 event limits, selected-event export, current-year recap download, and story playback; `HOME_TIMELINE_EVENTS` opts into all of them and reads the canonical service's supported maximum of 200 events. Selection works in both list and track presentations, and selected export calls the existing governed Home Events endpoint, retaining its truth metadata, validation, limitations and property scoping. The recap is assembled from the same privacy-filtered Ask result rather than the traditional recap endpoint, because the traditional page deliberately shows household members' private entries while Ask's established rule hides another member's private events. Opening a real event now also offers Confirm event, Mark disputed, and Attach evidence. Confirm/dispute uses an explicit second review step in the Ask detail and the existing confirmation endpoint, which appends the canonical verification audit; document attachment reuses the existing bounded upload plus `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` review/confirmation flow. Modeled events and viewers receive no mutation controls; viewers keep the read-only filters, playback, export and recap that the canonical routes allow. Search/filter/playback are presentation state only and do not alter the home record. *Executed:* frontend and backend TypeScript checks, focused frontend lint (clean), the focused Home Timeline capability and truth suites (20/20), and `git diff --check`. The capability suite required the repository's CommonJS and JSON-module `ts-node` options under the current Node runtime. *Not run:* a browser, database, live upload, export download, or verification write, per the repository's environment rule.

**Revision 1.257 — Home Timeline event logging, review and correction in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner report, 2026-10-10):** `HOME_TIMELINE_EVENTS` is no longer a read-only history. A contributor or owner sees **Log an event** in both populated and empty results; it launches the existing declared-action `CAPTURE_EVENT_CONFIRM` form, review and confirmation path and writes through the canonical `HomeEventsService`. Each real timeline entry now exposes confirmation-gated **Correct title**, **Correct date**, **Correct details**, and **Change visibility** actions backed by the existing `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT` and `HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY` operations. Modeled projection entries remain read-only because they have no canonical `HomeEvent` to mutate. The former **Details** control is now **Review event** in both Timeline and List layouts, and the inline review carries the same correction actions without navigating to the traditional timeline. Viewers retain the timeline, facts and evidence but receive neither logging nor mutation controls. Search, date/type filters, confirmation/dispute, export and annual recap remain separate follow-up parity work.

**Revision 1.256 — Ownership Costs parity in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner report, 2026-10-10):** the ownership-cost answer is no longer a condensed current-cost report with informational dead ends. It now exposes the traditional workspace's four views as explicit conversational next actions: **Current cost**, **What changed**, **What may change**, and **Plan a buffer**. Those views read the same canonical current snapshot, observed changes, forecasts, saved scenarios, variability, notification preferences, and planning decisions as the traditional UI. Missing categories now launch their registered Ask owner (including **Review property tax**) instead of stopping at static text. A contributor or owner can refresh sources; save or recalculate a forecast; create, rename, archive, or delete a scenario; accept, save, snooze, take no action, resolve, dismiss, or reopen an observed change; change reminder preferences; and record monthly-buffer or capital-reserve decisions. Writes use a declared-action-only `OWNERSHIP_COST_COMMAND`, collect any required scenario, reason, or revisit-date input, require an explicit review and confirmation, call the canonical ownership-cost services, and reconcile the still-visible ownership-cost answer. Viewers retain all reads but receive no write controls. Scenario input uses the compact two-column conversation layout. This revision supersedes Appendix C.1's earlier decision that missing categories were informational-only rows.

**Revision 1.255 — Coverage-conflict resolution in the expiration-reminder answer (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-10):** when `HOME_DEADLINE_MONITOR` is blocked by `COVERAGE_CONFLICT_REVIEW_REQUIRED`, the answer no longer links to `/dashboard/insurance`. Code-traced: an insurance conflict is a pending extracted `InsurancePolicyFact` that disagrees with a confirmed one (`getConflictedInsurancePolicyTerms`), and `confirmPolicyFact` (reached in Ask through `DOCUMENT_PROMOTION_CONFIRM`) moves the term out of `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` once no pending fact remains, so the existing pending-document review is the canonical resolution rather than a substitute. The answer now carries one typed `START_WORKFLOW` action ("Review pending policy values", `DOCUMENT_PROMOTION_REVIEW`, id `resolve-coverage-conflict`, allowlisted for `HOME_DEADLINE_MONITOR`). A warranty conflict has no Ask resolution, so that answer says so and carries no action. Executed: `tests/ask/coverageConflictResolution.test.js` (3 tests, transpile-only because another session's uncommitted files do not type-check). Not verified: against a database or browser; the Do-Nothing and tool-run conflict states still explain without an action.

**Revision 1.254 — The three recommended tool starts are built: suggest habits, run the do-nothing simulation, check a contractor quote (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-10; `docs/architecture/ASK_EMPTY_STATE_ADD_PATH_AUDIT.md` section 5):** three declared-action, confirmation-gated commands start from the answers that came up empty. `HOME_HABITS_GENERATE` (Home Habit Coach, contributor floor, no form): a confirmation, then `HomeHabitCoachService.generateHabits` and the controller's analytics action; the coach's on/off setting is checked at propose (switched off is an explanation, not an offer), a template that already has an active or snoozed habit is not repeated, and the receipt reports how many were created and how many already existed, including none ("this does not mean nothing needs care"); the confirmation is bound to the setting and the active templates, so a change in between is refused. `DO_NOTHING_SIMULATION_RUN` (Do-Nothing Simulator): a form for one of the four horizons (6, 12, 24, 36 months) and optionally one saved scenario, a review card, then `DoNothingSimulatorService.run` and the controller's analytics action; limited to the home's primary account holder exactly as the service is (`homeownerProfile.userId`, narrower than the owner role), checked at propose and again by the service at execution; conflicting insurance records are an unavailable state at propose and a refusal at execution; the receipt shows the horizon, the added-cost range and the confidence, or says plainly that there was not enough recorded history to estimate. `SERVICE_PRICE_CHECK_RUN` (Service Price Radar): a form for one of the 29 services the radar supports (never insurance, attorney or finance), a quote amount above zero and up to $250,000, and optionally the contractor and what the quote covers; a review card; then the controller's project-compliance applicability check (its context version stored with the check), `ServicePriceRadarService.createCheck` with a manual quote and the controller's TOOL_USED analytics; primary account holder only, checked twice; the receipt shows the expected range and result as computed, or that no local benchmark qualified. The answers that start them: the empty home habits answer (a viewer is told who can ask), the do-nothing answer in its empty state and as **Run it again** beside the latest run (it reaches only the primary account holder, so a blocked or conflict state offers nothing), and the quote checks answer as **Check a quote** and **Check another quote**; the audience filter now treats those button labels and "Suggest habits" and "generate" as changes, so a viewer is never shown them. Each receipt offers the in-Ask list (`Show my home habits`, `Show my do-nothing simulation`, `Show my service price radar`), and each command refreshes a still-visible empty answer. Registered in the operation, audience, domain-command, answer-trust, mutation-impact, semantic-package, certification-corpus and coverage registries and in `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_OPERATIONS`. A retry that finds its own earlier write (a run of the same horizon, a check of the same service and amount) records nothing twice. *Executed:* 17 tests in `homeToolRuns.test.js` covering, for each command, the answer's button, the declared-action guards, viewer and non-primary-holder refusals, unavailable states (coach off, conflicting coverage, removed scenario), input validation, the declared cancellation, the exact write, and revalidation at execution (stale version, changed scenarios, permission, invalid stored input, retry), with four mutations caught (primary-holder check removed, compliance check dropped, stale-version check removed, coach-off check removed); the wording tests the sweep pinned were updated; typecheck clean. The audit's "four service categories" for Service Price Radar was wrong (the handler's label table lists four; the radar supports 29) and is corrected. *Not run:* a real browser or database; the forms and the receipts rendered; the writes against the real tables; whether a household member who is not the primary account holder is the same as a contributor in practice. *Still product decisions:* Home Continuity Plan, Project Tracker, Negotiation Shield.

**Revision 1.253 — Room-specific Plant Advisor recommendations in Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner report, 2026-10-10):** the Plant Care Outlook remains the weather-aware care view for plants already in the home, while its new **Find plants for a room** action opens the recommendation workflow that previously existed only on the desktop Plant Advisor. A contributor or owner selects a recorded room and supplies the same decision inputs as the desktop flow: required light level, optional care effort, pets, goals and notes. After review and confirmation, Ask calls the canonical `RoomPlantAdvisorService`, which stores the room profile, replaces that profile's generated recommendation set and returns the deterministic ranking inline. Each result shows the fit percentage and signals, reason, care summary, placement guidance and warnings; Save, Dismiss and Add to home are independently confirmation-gated and refresh the recommendation list. Add to home uses the canonical idempotent service path and preserves recommendation lineage. A viewer receives no recommendation-generation or mutation action. The capture form uses the compact two-column Ask workflow layout instead of the tall full-width material-form treatment.
