Registered in the operation, domain-command, audience, answer-trust, semantic-package, certification-corpus, interaction-coverage and skill-governance registries. *Executed:* backend and frontend TypeScript checks; focused Plant Advisor and Plant Care tests; registry tests after updating the current aggregate to 139 operations and 60 commands; `git diff --check`. *Not run:* a browser, database or service environment, in line with repository validation rules. *Still separate:* outdoor and garden-zone advice and the weather care forecast are not recommendation inputs.

**Revision 1.252 — Empty-state sweep across eight read answers (IW-TRAD-002; owner direction, 2026-10-10; `docs/architecture/ASK_EMPTY_STATE_ADD_PATH_AUDIT.md`):** after the desktop links were removed (1.246), eight answers still ended an empty or unavailable result with "Open it to …": Service Price Radar, Home Habit Coach, Home Continuity Plan, Around Your Home, Guidance Overview, Project Tracker, Do-Nothing Simulator and Negotiation Shield. For each, the canonical owning service and its write rules were traced and Ask offers only what it can do honestly. **Built:** Guidance Overview's empty list offers **Start a guided plan** to a contributor or owner, through the existing confirmation-gated `GUIDANCE_JOURNEY_CREATE` (it asks which recorded item or approved service the plan covers; a viewer is told who can start one); a started plan refreshes a still-visible list (`GUIDANCE_JOURNEY_CREATE` now declares `GUIDANCE_JOURNEYS_LIST` as its sibling, replacing a comment that said no such read existed). **Honest explanation, no action:** the other seven no longer invite a page; Service Price Radar, Home Habit Coach, Home Continuity Plan, Project Tracker, Do-Nothing Simulator and Negotiation Shield say plainly that Ask cannot do the thing yet, and Around Your Home (a read of reviewed sources, nothing a homeowner adds) describes coverage and promises nothing. Two stale statements were corrected (Guidance Overview's boundary sent step changes to a page; Do-Nothing called a saved scenario "ready to run"). The audit's findings that matter for what comes next: Service Price Radar and Do-Nothing run only for the home's primary account holder, narrower than the owner role, so any Ask command for them needs that check; Habit Coach generation depends on the household's own on/off preference and creates nothing for a template already active, so its receipt must be able to say "created 0"; the Home Continuity Plan holds access notes and contacts that its read slice deliberately keeps out of conversation (FRD v1.54), so collecting them is a product decision; Project Tracker's create needs a contract amount, dates and permit and HOA decisions, which is not a small form. Recommended build order: Habit Coach generate, Do-Nothing run, Service Price Radar check. *Executed:* `emptyStateAddPaths.test.js` (7 tests: all eight empty states link nowhere, invite no page and carry no action; the six "cannot yet" states say so; primary-owner-only and coverage-conflict states are blocked explanations; the Guidance start is offered to contributor and owner, applicable for those roles and refused for a viewer; starting asks what to cover and writes nothing; naming an item gives a review card; confirming writes once with that scope and the execution id; a stale scope and an invalid input are refused; the cancellation copy is declared) with three mutations caught; the Habit Coach and Do-Nothing tests that pinned the old wording updated. *Not run:* a real browser or database; the clarification card was not seen rendered; the "Ask cannot … yet" wording has not had product review; answers outside this list that may share the gap were not examined.

**Revision 1.251 — Add a material and add a plant from the answers that came up empty (IW-TRAD-002; owner report, 2026-10-10):** "Show my material specs" and "Show my plant care outlook" answered "None recorded" / "No plants or garden zones tracked yet" and, once their desktop links were removed (1.246), offered no way to add anything. Two declared-action, confirmation-gated commands now start from those answers, in the room-add shape (inline form -> review -> confirm -> receipt, bound to the home's room list so a stale form is refused). `MATERIAL_SPEC_ADD` records a material: type (the fifteen the page offers) and name are required; room (or whole home), brand, product, colour code, finish, supplier and notes are optional; it writes through `MaterialSpecService.createSpec` after the same project-compliance applicability check as the traditional POST controller, then emits the controller's analytics action and its tool-lifecycle completion event; a room makes the spec room-scoped. `HOME_PLANT_ADD` adds an INDOOR plant: name and room required, nickname and notes optional, through `PlantCarePlannerService.createPlant`; a home with no room is sent to the existing add-a-room command rather than a form that cannot be filled in. Both answers offer their button to a contributor or owner only (a viewer reads the list or outlook), in the empty state and when records exist; the plant outlook's "open Plant Advisor" copy is reworded and its rows no longer carry a page link. A retry that finds its own earlier write (same name created since the execution began) records nothing twice. Each receipt offers the in-Ask list (`Show my material specs`, `Show my plant care outlook`). Registered in the operation, audience, domain-command, answer-trust, mutation-impact, semantic-package, certification-corpus and coverage registries and in `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_OPERATIONS`; `MATERIAL_SPEC_ADD` refreshes a still-visible Material Specs list and `HOME_PLANT_ADD` a still-visible outlook. *Executed:* 11 new tests in `homeRecordAdds.test.js` (forms and their fields; declared-action, refresh, wrong-message and viewer guards; input normalisation; review cards; the controller's checks and analytics at confirm; whole-home versus room scope; indoor-only plant payload; retry and invalid-input handling; the no-room redirect; the answers' buttons and their absence for a viewer) with three mutations killed (compliance check dropped, plant added as outdoor, viewer given the button); the plant outlook tests updated for the removed row links; pinned counts 133 -> 135 operations and 54 -> 56 commands; typecheck clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the forms rendering in the capture card, and the writes against the real material and plant tables. *Not covered:* outdoor plants and garden zones (they need an outdoor-applicable home and have no Ask command), a material's photos, purchase details, lot and compliance checks, and editing or removing a material or plant.

**Revision 1.250 — "Select a home" answers without a link out (IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** a question that names no home returns `NEEDS_PROPERTY`. The choice itself was already inline: the workspace renders its own home picker (`PropertySelectionCard`, `resolveAskExecutionProperty`) under that status, so the question continues from the chosen home. What was wrong was the answer's own button, a link to the desktop properties page, which the 1.246 delivery filter already removed, and its wording for a person with no home at all, who saw "Select a home" and nothing to select. `needsPropertyResult(userId)` now builds no link and words the answer by whether the person has any home (a household membership, or a home that predates membership): **Choose a home to continue** ("Choose which one below") or **Add a home to continue** (use the home menu at the top of Ask to add the first home, then ask again). A failed lookup keeps the choose wording, so no one with homes is told to add one. *Executed:* 5 new tests in `needsProperty.test.js` (homes; a pre-membership owner; none; failed lookup or no user; an operation asked with no home selected, kept whole by the answer checker) with two mutations killed (a failed lookup treated as no homes, pre-membership owners ignored); `test:ask:chunked` fails only the 9 tests that already failed on the untouched code; typecheck clean. *Not built, on purpose:* adding a home inside Ask. A home has to exist before any Ask command can run (the confirm, capture and access paths all require a property id), so it would need a property-less command path through that machinery plus the address search and first-home rules of the properties page; the "Add property" entry in the Ask header's home menu still goes to the desktop add-a-home page, which is app navigation rather than an answer. *Not run:* a real browser; the picker was not re-verified.

**Revision 1.249 — Maintenance Setup inside Ask: recommended tasks to browse and add (IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** the desktop Maintenance Setup page was a catalogue of recurring-task templates, each with per-home applicability, that a homeowner picked from and configured. `MAINTENANCE_TEMPLATES_BROWSE` is a launch-only read (non-routable, reached only by the declared **Browse recommended tasks** action, which replaces the two "Maintenance Setup" links on the maintenance answer) over the same `MaintenanceService.getMaintenanceTemplates` call the page makes: **You can add now**, **Already on your list** (a non-cancelled task under the template's action key), **Needs a home detail first** (with the missing detail named), and a count of templates that do not apply, which are left out. A contributor or owner gets **Add to my maintenance** on each addable row; a viewer reads the list only. That action opens the ordinary add-a-task form (`MAINTENANCE_TASK_CREATE`) pre-filled from the template (title, notes, recurring, frequency), so the due date and cost can still be set; the template is re-checked as applicable when the form opens and again when the task is written. The template id travels through the form and the confirmation, and the write goes through `createUserTask` with that `templateId` and the template's service category, so the task is linked to its template under the template's own key (the service reuses or reactivates it, so adding the same template twice is one task). An ordinary add is unchanged and keeps its execution key. Registered in the operation, audience, semantic-package, certification-corpus, answer-trust (boundary id) and interaction-coverage registries (launch-only read) and in `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_OPERATIONS`; `MAINTENANCE_TASK_CREATE` now refreshes a still-visible list. *Executed:* 8 new tests in `maintenanceTemplates.test.js` (sections and left-out counts; add action only for an addable row and not for a viewer; empty and all-added states; pre-fill and carried template; unavailable, refresh and wrong-message guards; the review card keeps the template; the write is under the template with its category and no execution key; an ordinary add is unchanged and a removed template writes nothing) with three mutations killed (applicability not checked, template id dropped at the write, viewer given the add action); 1 new maintenance-answer test (the in-Ask action replaces the Maintenance Setup link and survives the answer checker); pinned counts 132 -> 133 operations; `test:ask:chunked` fails only the 9 tests that already failed on the untouched code; typecheck clean. *Not run:* a real browser or database, the list rendering and the pre-filled form in the browser, and the live `getMaintenanceTemplates` against real property context. *Not covered:* the page's mobile layout and icons; asking for the list in words (it is launch-only, so a typed "what maintenance should I set up" still answers as a status question); the explorer and discovery entries for it; the create form's own desktop links were left in place and are removed by the delivery filter.

**Revision 1.248 — Confirmation receipts follow up inside Ask instead of linking to a page (IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** the 1.246 validator already removed the "Open <page>" buttons and desktop navigation from receipts at delivery, which left a receipt with nothing to do next. The receipt producers no longer build those links (29 sites across the record, maintenance, buyer, capture, radar and workflow confirm handlers and the capture-event receipt; their now-unused href variables and imports are gone), and each receipt carries one typed follow-up action instead: **Show my home timeline / warranties / rooms / home record / documents / pending maintenance / what needs attention / purchase status / inspection decisions / open inspection findings / claims / recall matches / sale readiness checklist / guided plan / quote comparisons / home event radar**. The action is a `START_WORKFLOW` that asks the ordinary read question for that record (`support/receiptFollowUps.ts`: one table of label, message and the operation the message must resolve to, and `receiptFollowUpAction(key)`), the same shape as the Home Habit Coach's `habits-show-maintenance`. The trust policy allows exactly that action on any confirmed operation by exact match of id, label, message and operation (`isReceiptFollowUpAction`), so no per-operation list grows and a changed action is refused. The warranty-attachment receipt keeps its inventory "view item" action and offers the warranties follow-up otherwise. A first attempt used string suggestions and was refused by the zero-baseline raw-suggestion guard, which is the reason for the typed action. *Executed:* 5 new tests in `receiptFollowUps.test.js` (every follow-up question routes to the operation the table names; no receipt producer builds an `open-*` action or desktop navigation; each receipt names its record's follow-up; the action is exact, link-free and tamper-evident; the answer checker keeps it and still removes a link and a changed action); the seller-prep receipt test now expects the follow-up; `test:ask:chunked` fails only the 9 tests that already failed on the untouched code; typecheck clean. *Not run:* Playwright or a real browser; the `e2e/ask` fixtures still carry the old receipt links and were not regenerated. *Not covered:* the receipt's created record is not itself opened (the follow-up opens the list that contains it); review cards shown before confirming had their link actions removed with no replacement because the card already shows the change.

**Revision 1.247 — Two dead ends from 1.246 become Ask commands: the purchase date and the home journey (IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** `PROPERTY_PURCHASE_DATE_SET` records `PropertyFinancingProfile.purchaseDate` through `financing.service.upsertProfile` (the call the financing page makes, sending only that field); `HOME_JOURNEY_SET` sets `PropertyOnboarding.ownershipState` through `entryContext.service.updateJourneyOwnershipState` (the call the onboarding page makes, which enforces the contributor floor itself) from the same five choices and wording as the page. Both are declared-action-only (no message pattern, internal operations, not reachable by fuzzy retrieval), contributor floor, and follow the room-add shape: inline form (one exact date no later than today; one of five choices) -> review card -> confirm -> receipt. Each form is bound to the value recorded when it opened, so a form opened before the value changed is refused as out of date. The entry points changed from desktop links to workflow actions: the maintenance "since I bought the home" answer's **Add purchase date** (offered to a contributor or owner only; its boundary copy now says the answer refreshes) and the audience **Confirm home journey** step on the context-required answer and on audience-framed summaries. The source answer is refreshed after the write (`reconcileAskExecutionSideEffects`; `PROPERTY_PURCHASE_DATE_SET` refreshes `MAINTENANCE_STATUS`, `HOME_JOURNEY_SET` refreshes `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`), so the answer that needed the value re-runs with it. Registered in the operation, audience, domain-command, answer-trust, mutation-impact, semantic-package, certification-corpus and interaction-coverage registries, and in `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_OPERATIONS` (skill-less, like the other settings writes). *Executed:* 9 new tests in `homeSettingsWrites.test.js` (forms, declared-action guards, viewer block, date validation, version binding, exact write payload, entry point survives the answer checker) with three mutations killed (an extra profile field, the message guard removed, the viewer check removed); 1 new maintenance test; `startupRegistryValidation` (the boot validators) and the pinned-count tests updated 130 -> 132 operations and 52 -> 54 commands; `test:ask:chunked` fails only the 9 tests that already failed on the untouched code. *Not run:* a real browser or a real database, the form rendering in the capture card for these two field types (`APPROXIMATE_DATE` limited to an exact day is the shape the Radar task form already uses; `SINGLE_SELECT` in a group is the room form's), and the write against the real financing and onboarding tables. *Still open from 1.246:* maintenance setup, the emergency troubleshooter, quote comparison and the record-creation receipts have no Ask replacement yet.

**Revision 1.246 — Ask answers link nowhere outside Ask (IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** after the Home Timeline (1.245) and Material Specs reports, the rule is enforced once instead of per handler. `isAskHrefSafeForProperty` now treats any href other than `/dashboard/ask` as unsafe, so the answer-trust validator removes it from every block type it already walks: block actions, grouped-list and timeline item links, capability-list cards, change-summary and output-artifact and related-record navigation, and priority-list calls to action. The same pass now also drops a grouped list's or priority list's own link buttons, which the repair previously skipped; typed in-Ask actions on those blocks are untouched. Capture cards' desktop-form escape hatch (`fallbackHref`) is dropped when an execution is read, so stored conversations lose it too. The status-board summary now names the Status Board in its own sentence, because the removed "Open Status Board" label had been supplying the wording the relevance check scored. *Executed:* about 30 backend tests that pinned "the page link survives the whitelist" now assert the opposite (and the typed actions beside them still survive); `test:ask:chunked` shows only the failures that already existed on the untouched code. *Not run:* Playwright and a real browser. *Consequences to decide:* answers whose only next step was a desktop page now end without it, notably adding a purchase date, resolving a coverage conflict, the emergency troubleshooter, Maintenance Setup, onboarding's home-journey, "select a home", quote comparison and claim receipts; each needs an in-Ask equivalent or an explicit decision. Handlers still build the links (about 40 files) and the validator removes them, so the source clean-up is mechanical follow-up. Stored blocks of old conversations still carry their old links.

**Revision 1.245 — Home Timeline events open in place; the answer links nowhere (IW-PRES-005/014/017, IW-TRAD-002; owner decision, 2026-10-10):** a field report showed every timeline point, List title and **Open record** leaving Ask for the desktop Home Timeline page. The `HOME_TIMELINE_EVENTS` answer now carries no `href` and no **Open Home Timeline** action (summary and empty states included). Each `TIMELINE` item carries an optional server-built `detail` (`facts[]` and `evidence[]`, bounded) assembled from the event rows the answer already read, so the existing privacy rule (another member's PRIVATE event is never shown) also bounds the detail; no raw GET endpoint is called. `TimelineTrackBlock` opens it in place (track **Details**, List **Details**) in a new `TimelineEventDetail`; the capital windows keep their live re-read. *Executed:* backend `homeTimelineEventsCapabilitySlice` 11/11 (no `/dashboard/` or `href` anywhere in the answer; detail facts and evidence), frontend `homeTimelineTrack` + `capitalTimelineTrack` 10/10, `src/components/ask` + `src/features/ask` jest with only the 6 known unrelated failures. *Not run:* Playwright (fixture updated, spec not run), a real browser. *Open:* revisions (the list reads current rows only) and evidence/visibility changes are not available in the sheet; other operations still emit `open-timeline` actions (event correction, visibility, capture confirmation) and other domains (e.g. Material Specs) still link to desktop pages.

**Revision 1.244 — Browser back/forward continuity for the focused discovery view (IW-SHELL-016; plan §6 state-continuity; 2026-10-10):** opening a topic from Ask home pushes ONE history entry carrying `askExploreFocus: { topicId, homeKey }` on the unchanged URL (the session restore compares URLs, so it ignores the entry). **Back** closes the focused view and restores scroll and focus as **Not now** does; **Forward** reopens that topic; switching topics inside the view replaces the entry rather than stacking another; an entry recorded for a different home is never reopened. **Not now** pops the entry it pushed (`history.back()`), so no closed-view entry is left; a starter or target launch instead strips the topic from the entry in place and never calls `history.back()`, because it is about to write the conversation's own URL and Back is asynchronous. Built in `useExploreFocus` (`ExploreWithCozy.tsx`, the `useResponseContextPanel` pattern). *Executed:* 6 new component tests (one entry per open; Back; Forward; topic switch replaces; Not now pops; launch never calls back; other-home entry ignored) with two mutations killed (Not now not popping; home check removed); the Explore, workspace-wiring and neighbouring Ask jest suites pass apart from failures that do not touch these files. *Not run:* a real-browser Back/Forward pass (Playwright or manual); the interaction of this entry with the response-context panel entry when both are open.

**Revision 1.243 — Reusable target selectors built; "Add a missing detail" and "Continue a project" offered (IW-SHELL-022; owner decision, 2026-10-09):** the two ideas the original direction named are now in Explore with Cozy, enabled by a domain-owned, reusable target-selection contract instead of any discovery-only picker. **The contract:** `GET /api/ask/target-selectors/:selectorId?propertyId=` returns a selection (`OPTIONS`, `NONE_ELIGIBLE`, or `UNAVAILABLE`) whose options each carry a stable target id, a homeowner label, a short summary, availability, reason codes, and the exact launch the choice sends. Reading it writes nothing. **Two selectors:** the **area** selector reads the Property Context once for all areas and offers an area only if it has askable missing, conflicted, or stale facts (with a plain count of what to add, review, or refresh); choosing one launches the per-area detail capture for that scope. The **project** selector reads the DIY service and applies the project guide's own eligibility evaluation: only planning or in-progress projects started from a reviewed template are offered, and a project is available only against its current reviewed guide (a changed or withdrawn guide is shown disabled with the reason); choosing one launches the project guide. A viewer sees area options as unavailable with the permission reason. **The rules, as built:** even a single option waits for an explicit choice; the chooser lives inside the focused topic view and **Back to the topic** returns to its starters having sent nothing; nothing eligible is explained honestly; a source that could not be read, and a request that failed, are each shown as unavailable with a retry and are never shown as "nothing to choose"; the client refuses to launch an option that the selection did not offer, an unavailable option, or an option from a stale overview. **Revalidation on choice** is the target operation's own (code-traced, not executed end to end): the area capture re-checks access and role and re-reads the area's current askable facts, ending honestly if none remain; the project guide re-evaluates the project and refuses with a reason if it is no longer guideable. **Bound through the registries:** "Add a missing detail" is a governed workflow (the write is confirmation-gated and begins only after an explicit area choice) owned by the Property Brief capability, for which the guidance bridge gained the area-capture operation; "Continue a project" is a read owned by DIY. Topic starters may now be a governed workflow, but only behind a selector. Lifecycle attribution accepts the chosen option's message. **Recorded gaps:** selector ideas are hosted only by the focused topic view, so "More ideas" does not list them yet; the contract is reusable by routing, Suggested Next Actions, and other Ask workflows but none consumes it yet; the project selector examines the newest twelve eligible projects and says when more exist. No schema change; nothing is live-verified.

**Revision 1.242 — Phase 6 gaps closed: continuations, server-owned expiry reconciliation, "viewed" corrected, DIY parity centralized (owner direction, 2026-10-09):** (1) **Continuations:** the clarification, property-choice, and structured-capture entry points now record the later result against the original execution, so a discovery-launched turn answered after a question records OUTPUT_GENERATED, with the original attribution and execution id preserved (the recorder reads both from the persisted execution and is idempotent per stage). (2) **Untouched proposals:** a server-owned CronJob (`ask-pending-expiry`, every 15 minutes, running `reconcile:ask-pending`) finds discovery-launched interactions whose stored expiry has passed and applies the existing expiry transition, which records ABANDONED once; it also backfills a missing ABANDONED for discovery-launched executions already expired by time. It adds no discovery-specific timer and nothing runs in the browser: the authoritative expiry is the one stored with each pending interaction, which is 30 minutes for every current clarification, confirmation, and structured capture. Only discovery-attributed interactions are expired early; every other pending interaction still expires lazily, as before. (3) **Terminology:** the recorded metric is OUTPUT_GENERATED (delivered), never "viewed". An `OUTPUT_VIEWED` capability is not completed by an answer, and the completion validator rejects any table entry that says so, because true viewing needs client visibility telemetry that does not exist and stays explicitly unmeasured. (4) **Parity:** an inventory of the traditional-page controllers found 12 lifecycle emit sites in 8 controllers and exactly one write that Ask also performs through the same service, the start of a DIY project. That completion is now recorded by `diyService` where the project is created (template and guide branches, after commit, only for a project actually created), and removed from the controller, so the page and Ask share one rule and each created project records it once. **Effect on analytics:** DIY projects started from Ask now record a DIY completion; the page's behavior is unchanged. The other 11 emit sites have no Ask counterpart today (their Ask operations are reads, or do not exist), so they stay where they are; a future Ask write that performs one of those domain writes must record through the service, not a handler. **Owner action required:** rebuild and roll out the backend image, and deploy the new CronJob (it is added to the Raspberry Pi overlay and applies with the usual deploy). Nothing is live-verified.

**Revision 1.241 — Capability lifecycle telemetry built for Ask discovery (IW-SHELL-021; owner decision, 2026-10-09):** a discovery selection now carries a bounded claim (`launchContext.discovery`: the reviewed entry id, a source surface of topic, explorer, or landing prompt, and the topic) and the server, not the client, records what the chosen capability then did, in the canonical tool lifecycle. The claim is honoured only when the declared operation and the message match the reviewed entry exactly; the capability, operation, and lifecycle id are always derived from the registries. Events: **STARTED** when a discovery-attributed execution is created; **OUTPUT_GENERATED** when it delivers an answer (answered or completed) or a confirmed write finishes; **ABANDONED** when a pending proposal is cancelled or expires; **COMPLETED** only when an operation's authoritative outcome is declared in an explicit table AND the kind it satisfies equals the capability registry's declared completion kind AND any output entity type is verified, the same two checks the existing completion service applies. Results that need context or confirmation, proposals, limited answers, and every failure emit nothing. **A finding the decision could not know:** none of the ten capabilities reachable from discovery declares an output-style completion kind (they declare an action, artifact, decision, plan, or verified outcome), so under this rule no discovery-launched read can complete its capability; Ask stops at OUTPUT_GENERATED by design, and the completion table's only entry (a confirmed task completion for Maintenance) is not reachable from discovery today. The client events gained the join keys (`entryId`, `capabilityId`, and the execution id on completion). **Recorded gaps:** only the first pass, confirmation, cancellation, and time-based expiry observed at confirm time are hooked, so a discovery-launched turn that needs a clarification or capture and is answered later is not recorded as output; there is no timer, so an untouched proposal is never marked abandoned; the recorded metric is OUTPUT_GENERATED (delivered), never "viewed", because true viewing needs client visibility telemetry and stays unmeasured; and traditional-page controllers emit lifecycle events that the equivalent Ask-routed actions do not (a pre-existing parity gap, not addressed here). No schema change; nothing is live-verified.

**Revision 1.240 — Binding layer built; discovery metadata now derived from the canonical registries (IW-SHELL-020; owner decision "option 1", 2026-10-09):** the explorer registry is rebuilt as a derivation. Each explorer entry now takes its outcome group, label, and search aliases from the capability registry, its operation facts from the Ask operation registry, and only the launch message, interaction type, consequence copy, and an optional label override from a new binding file, `askCapabilityBindings.ts`, which also now holds the capability-card entry reads that used to live in `askCapabilityCardLaunch.ts`, so there is one binding source. Two startup validators guard it. Building it settled facts the 1.239 decision could not know. (1) **A registered capability is a tool-catalog commitment:** it needs a real dashboard route, a rollout key, a lifecycle id, a parity-inventory row, a Knowledge Hub product tool, and a seed SQL regeneration, and it appears in Explore Tools. (2) **Seasonal care** was defined as the capability `seasonal-maintenance` (route `/dashboard/seasonal`), replacing the Knowledge Hub's existing explicit "Seasonal Maintenance" entry under the same key and slug so nothing is duplicated. (3) **"What changed recently" was not given a new capability:** the registered `home-briefing` capability reads the same canonical property-change store, so the binding uses it; this departs from "define both" for that one entry, and is reversible. (4) **The guidance bridge already owns operation-to-capability ownership,** and it disagreed with three existing card bindings and with two of the guesses made while planning; the bridge was extended (forecast to maintenance, change summary to home-briefing, the seasonal read and checklist setup to seasonal-maintenance) and the three pre-existing card disagreements are pinned in a tested allowlist, not silently changed. (5) **Deriving groups moved two entries:** DIY now sits under Plan and monitor and coverage gaps under Compare and decide, because that is where their capabilities sit. (6) **The workflow-only rule removed one entry:** "Compare contractor quotes" is dropped because `quote-comparison` is a workflow-only capability and general discovery never offers one. (7) **Aliases must be noun phrases:** question-shaped aliases shared stopwords with generic messages and made the Explore Tools goal matcher mistake "What tools do you have to help me?" for a capability request, so the approved aliases live in one reviewed map in the capability definitions and a test rejects question forms. Registering the capability and the inventory regeneration also repaired a pre-existing drift (the Environment Report had been registered without a parity-inventory row, a lifecycle id, or updated pinned counts). **Owner action required:** the regenerated Knowledge Hub seed SQL must be run in pgAdmin. Nothing here is live-verified.

**Revision 1.239 — Discovery metadata ownership, lifecycle telemetry, and reusable target selectors decided (owner decisions, 2026-10-09; nothing built yet):** three questions left open by 1.238 are closed. (1) **Canonical sources (IW-SHELL-020):** the capability registry stays canonical for capability identity and version, outcome group, homeowner label and description, approved intent aliases, and the completion definition; the Ask operation registry stays canonical for availability, audience, authorization, safety, and launch behavior. The Ask explorer registry becomes a validated binding layer, not an inventory: it holds only Ask-specific facts (capability to operation, the reviewed launch message, the interaction type, consequence copy for governed workflows, an optional presentation override). Groups and search aliases are derived from the capability registry. **The build in 1.238 does not yet conform**: it holds groups, labels, and aliases itself. Rebuilding it is required follow-up, and obstacles are recorded in Appendix C.12 (some current entries have no capability to bind to, one capability binds several entries, and an existing capability-card launch allowlist already binds capabilities to operations). (2) **Telemetry (IW-SHELL-021):** the `ask_discovery_*` events stay and answer the exposure question; canonical capability lifecycle telemetry is added and answers whether the chosen capability started, produced output, completed, or was abandoned. The two are joined only by bounded identifiers. An answered execution is not a completed capability: a result that needs context or confirmation, a proposal, or an informational answer must not emit COMPLETED by itself, and completion follows the capability's declared completion kind and an authoritative outcome. (3) **Pickers (IW-SHELL-022):** "Add a missing detail" and "Continue a project" are enabled only by a reusable, domain-owned Ask target-selection contract (an area selector from Property Context completeness and a project selector from the DIY service), never by discovery-only client pickers. Until those selectors exist both ideas stay omitted.

**Revision 1.238 — Lightweight capability discovery built; reviewed-discovery rule, search and measurement made binding (owner decisions, 2026-10-09):** Explore with Cozy (IW-SHELL-014–016) is implemented in four slices and pushed (commits `1ad59c68`, `ff61885a`, `7a8ea1a6`, `6fb88f93`); Appendix C.12 records its status requirement by requirement, with what was executed separated from what was only traced in code, and nothing live-verified. The build settled four things this FRD now states as requirements. (1) **A reviewed inventory (IW-SHELL-017):** "More ideas" and the topic starters share one server-owned registry of reviewed entries; read entries and confirmation-gated workflow entries are admitted, and any entry that could write immediately, lacks reviewed copy, needs an entity chosen first, or would fall to the grounded-guidance model fallback is excluded and rejected at startup. Existing static prompts were reviewed, not preserved: three were removed. (2) **Search (IW-SHELL-018):** client-side, deterministic, over homeowner wording and server-approved aliases only, never operation ids. (3) **Measurement (IW-SHELL-019):** bounded identifiers, visibility only after an element is on screen, bucketed search counts, and never a search phrase, label or message; separate from Suggested Next Action events (§24 updated). (4) **Indicator copy and edges:** Home care states the full actionable count and can therefore exceed the landing preview's three-card cap; a count of zero is not rendered while a measured 0% completeness is; a count of one reads "1 needs attention". The discovery projection is also loaded for the whole page session, not only the empty landing, so the cues persist mid-conversation. **Recorded gaps, not hidden:** two ideas the original direction named, "Add a missing detail" and "Continue a project", are not offered because each needs a canonical area or project picker that does not exist yet (IW-TRAD-003's honest-state wording is met by omission rather than an explained state); the starters and aliases come from the Ask explorer registry rather than from the capability registry entries, which the Capability Discovery FRD (CAP-FR-038) states differently and which awaits an owner decision; and the discovery events are not routed through the tool lifecycle telemetry that FRD names.

**Revision 1.237 — Discovery source and indicator ownership clarified (owner decision, 2026-10-09):** the lightweight discovery projection is part of the existing property-scoped Concierge Home view rather than a new parallel shell request. **More ideas** extends the existing Ask-native capability explorer with canonical registry-backed membership and homeowner-language search; it does not open or embed the traditional Explore Tools page. The three optional indicators have fixed meanings: Home care counts current actionable Home Actions attention items, DIY & Projects counts DIY projects in planning or in progress, and My Home Record uses canonical Property Context completeness. Similar metrics are not interchangeable. A stale or unavailable indicator disappears without removing its stable topic or independently available starters.

**Revision 1.236 — Lightweight persistent capability discovery (owner decision, 2026-10-09):** Ask Cozy must not make a homeowner wait for a response-level Suggested Next Action or know what to type before discovering important capabilities. The Ask shell therefore gains a quiet, persistent **Explore with Cozy** layer with three visible topic cues — **Home care**, **DIY & Projects**, and **My Home Record** — plus **More ideas**. These are not traditional application destinations, domain modes, or another static Suggested Next Action row. Selecting a topic stays inside Ask and reveals a small set of authorized, property-aware conversational starters; the first selection does not silently submit a question. The cues remain visually subordinate to the composer and current conversation, separate from conversation history, and available through a compact disclosure on narrow screens. Stable topic identity provides learnability; contextual counts or short status labels may draw attention when they are derived from current authorized records. This revision supersedes IW-SHELL-009/011 only where their earlier wording would prohibit these bounded discovery cues, and supersedes §11.9/§18 where they require Ask to expose traditional application navigation. Direct routes may continue during transition, but Ask discovery and ordinary Ask completion must not depend on them. Response-level Suggested Next Actions retain their separate job: continuing the latest answer.

**Revision 1.235 — Fix: "I need one detail to verify this answer" on the browse question (browser pass, 2026-10-08):** the same words as the "See projects you can start" button, typed or re-sent ("Change and resend" drops the launch context), went through free-text routing and landed on `DIY_PROJECTS` (with the full stop, deterministic) or `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS` (without it, local classifier, because the "can i" in `diyProjectsOtherIntentPattern` pushed it out of the deterministic DIY rule). The semantic answer validator then failed or could not decide for the answer against that question, which produced the clarification card; the outcome flipped with punctuation, template text and role. A click on the button was never affected: a declared action skips the competing-operation check (`createAskExecution.ts`, `declaredOperationRan`). Fix: one deterministic routing branch, `diyProjectsBrowseWordingPattern` ("DIY projects I/we can start/begin/do", "can I start …", "reviewed/available/startable DIY projects", "DIY projects … available/fit/suit"), routes these phrasings to `DIY_PROJECTS`, whose card already carries the browse action (primary on the empty card). Tests: `diyProjectStart.test.js` sweeps eight phrasings (with and without punctuation and case) to `DIY_PROJECTS`, keeps the permit questions on `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS` and the start unreachable by typing; `diyAnswerRelevance.test.js` runs the real card (empty, one, two projects) through the real validator for five browse phrasings. Removing the routing branch fails the sweep. **Considered and rejected:** making `DIY_TEMPLATE_BROWSE` routable by message (it would have needed calibration fixtures and evidence, skill routing sweeps and registry-drift changes across routing for one read-only card; the DIY projects card already links to it). A typed question about startable projects therefore shows the person's own projects plus the browse button, one click short of the list. Also tried and reverted: a declared-launch exemption in the semantic validator (it already exists). **Not verified in a browser.** The "This saved response needs to be refreshed" card on the stop and hand-off options view is NOT explained yet; the backend now logs the failing schema paths ("Saved Ask response failed schema validation").

**Revision 1.234 — Fix: the finish, stop, hand-off and recovery receipts were dead ends (review of the stop receipt in the browser pass, 2026-10-08):** each of these receipts ended the journey with no action. They now carry one in-Ask action, "Show my DIY projects" (new shared `showProjectsAction`, id `diy-show-projects`, a `START_WORKFLOW` launch of `DIY_PROJECTS`), on the finished receipt, the stopped and handed-off receipts, their "already" variants and the recovery "Queued again" receipt. It is allow-listed in the trust policy for `DIY_PROJECT_COMPLETE`, `DIY_PROJECT_ABANDON` and `DIY_COMPLETION_RECOVER` (the stale `open-diy-project` entry for abandon is gone). Review findings: stop and hand-off are already confirmation-gated (the "Stop this project." bubble is the options card's canned action message, and the write happens only in `confirmDiyProjectAbandon`), and the options card itself saves and reloads cleanly under the current schema (executed with the shared fake, not Postgres), so the "This saved response needs to be refreshed" card seen in the browser is probably a response saved by older backend code, NOT yet confirmed. To make that diagnosable, `mapPersistedExecution` now logs a warning with the execution id, operation, stored schema version and the failing schema paths (never content) when it falls back. Tests: `diyProjectCommands.test.js` and `diyCompletionRecover.test.js` assert the action through the real trust allow-list for each receipt; removing the allow-list entries fails 5 tests and removing the stop receipt action fails 1. Typecheck clean; the full chunked Ask suite was not run; not verified in a browser (needs a rebuild and rollout restart). Still open: un-finish and un-stop (a product decision).

**Revision 1.233 — Fix: a step receipt was a dead end (found in the browser pass on the first published test template, 2026-10-07):** after "Mark this step done" the receipt ("Marked done by you") carried no action, and the guide card that had refreshed in place to the next step sat above the fold, so the person was left at a bare receipt mid-journey. The `DIY_STEP_UPDATE` receipt (complete, skip, reopen, and the "already" receipt) now reads the project after the write, best effort, and offers one in-Ask action that opens the guide again at the bottom of the conversation: "Continue: <next step title>", or "Review and finish" when every step is resolved, or "Continue: <step>" after a reopen. New shared `continueGuideAction` (`diy-continue-guide`, a `START_WORKFLOW` launch of `DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE` on the project), allow-listed for `DIY_STEP_UPDATE` in the trust policy. If the follow-up read fails the receipt simply has no action (it never fails the receipt). Tests: `diyStepUpdate.test.js` asserts the action for complete, skip (next step), the last step ("Review and finish"), the "already" receipt and reopen, and that it survives the trust allow-list; removing the attach line fails 4 tests. **Not verified in a browser** (needs a backend rebuild and rollout restart); the full chunked Ask suite was not run. The finish, stop, hand-off and recovery receipts end the journey and are unchanged. Noticed, NOT fixed or investigated: the step confirmation card carries an unrelated generic sentence ("For an established home, this guidance emphasizes current condition, upcoming work, and sustainable long-term costs.") above its own wording.

**Revision 1.232 — Ask is self-sufficient: DIY answers no longer link to the desktop UI (owner decision, 2026-10-07):** found in the first browser pass ("Open DIY Project Center navigates back to desktop UI"), and stated by the owner as the goal: *Ask must be self-sufficient without navigating back to the desktop UI.* **This supersedes the earlier wording that traditional navigation stays "a fully supported user choice" for Ask answers (it remains a supported choice outside Ask).** Every DIY Ask surface lost its page link and its "see the project page" copy: the DIY projects card (empty and populated; rows no longer carry a project `href`), the template browse card (a started template now offers "Guide me through this project" in Ask, not a link), the guide card, previous-step view, options card and finished view, every refusal, the not-found answer and the withdrawn banner, and the step, finish, stop, hand-off, start and recovery receipts. Copy that told people to use the page was replaced by plain statements of what Ask can do or why it cannot (a viewer: "A contributor or owner of this home can mark steps done"; a withdrawn guide: "steps cannot be changed from it"). The trust policy's allow-list no longer admits `open-diy` or `open-diy-project`, so a link action would be stripped even if one were reintroduced. Guard: `tests/ask/diyAskSelfSufficient.test.js` scans every DIY Ask source file for desktop paths, `href` and page wording, and checks the real builders' output; reintroducing an `href` fails it. 20 existing tests updated to the new rule; DIY, trust and registry tests pass; the four owner-run Postgres scripts were re-run and pass (9/9, 8/8, 10/10, 8/8). **Capability gaps this deliberately exposes (Ask is NOT yet self-sufficient here):** adding time, cost and notes to a finished project exists only on the classic page; a project that was not started from a reviewed template (an AI-guide project, or one started before revisions existed) cannot be viewed or guided in Ask at all; "more active projects than shown" has no in-Ask pagination. Not verified in a browser or production (needs a backend rebuild and rollout restart); the full chunked Ask suite was not run. Other Ask domains were not changed and still use page links.

**Revision 1.231 — Fix: the DIY projects and template-browse cards were rejected by the semantic answer validator (found in the first browser pass, 2026-10-07):** typing "show my diy projects" in production gave "I still couldn't verify this answer". The real card builders run through the real `validateAskSemanticAnswerRelevance` scored the empty DIY projects card 0.38 for its own operation against 0.57 for `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS` (`ANSWER_FAVORS_DIFFERENT_OPERATION`); the score depends on the answer text, so no rephrasing helped. Cause: the only declared `DIY_PROJECTS` answer example described the populated list, and no `DIY_TEMPLATE_BROWSE` example described its empty or populated card. Reproduced locally before the fix; the populated DIY card and every browse variant also failed without it. Fix: answer-positive examples only (empty and populated wording) in `askOperationSemanticPackages.ts`; no handler, routing or registry change. New `tests/ask/diyAnswerRelevance.test.js` (3 tests) runs the real builders through the real validator for the empty, populated, truncated, browse-empty, browse-populated, contributor and viewer variants; with the fix reverted all 3 fail. Targeted Ask/DIY tests unchanged (the 2 failures in `askRoutingCalibration` and `askGovernance` are identical with and without the change). **Not verified:** in production or a browser (needs a backend rebuild and rollout restart); the full chunked Ask suite and `npm run typecheck` were not run (string-literal data change). Other launch-only DIY cards (guide, commands, finished view) were not run through the validator and are worth the same test.

**Revision 1.230 — Owner-run Postgres scratch scripts for DIY steps 5-8 executed (2026-10-07; no product code changed):** all four scripts now pass against a throwaway local Postgres 15 built from the current schema (step 5: 9/9, step 6: 8/8, step 7: 10/10, step 8: 8/8). The first runs failed 2, 2, 3 and 1 tests respectively; every failure was a stale or wrong assumption in the SCRIPT, not a product bug (step 5: a second open project from one template is now refused by step 8, and the finished view of a COMPLETED project is a `TASK_GUIDE` with `DIY_PROJECT_FINISHED_VIEW`; step 6: the "Review last step" action added in 7A, and `resolvePropertyAccess` creating a pre-household owner's OWNER membership row on first access, which is repo-wide and not DIY-specific; step 7: the minimal maintenance task row lacked the required `source`; step 8: UNPUBLISH then PUBLISH re-publishes the SAME approved revision by design, so a new head needs an edit plus a full review round). **Step 8 lock-removal mutation run by hand:** with the `pg_advisory_xact_lock` line commented out, eight simultaneous confirmations left 8 open projects (expected 1), the page-path race 5, and the advisory-lock test failed; the file was restored byte-identical and the unmutated run is 8/8. Not covered: a browser, the worker's side of completion, Docker/Pi images, production-sized data. The share-lock, advisory-lock and in-transaction role behaviour is now shown on real Postgres, not only on the fake.

**Revision 1.229 — Start a DIY project from Ask: 8B browse, 8C start, 8D frontend, 8E owner-run script (step 8 built; nothing live-verified):** two launch-only operations. `DIY_TEMPLATE_BROWSE` (read-only, reached by the new "See projects you can start" action on the DIY projects card, first on the empty card) lists only reviewed templates through a STRICT projection (`diyService.listStartableTemplates`: governed provenance, verified hash, eligible, applicable to the home; NOT `listTemplates`), marks a template that already has an open project, and gives only a contributor or owner the row action. `DIY_PROJECT_START` (confirmation-gated, CONTRIBUTOR, correction mode STOP: "stoppable, but not undoable or deletable") is reached only by that row action with the exact canned message; the proposal records the reviewed head revision and the service refuses a different head (409 `DIY_TEMPLATE_CHANGED`); a replay or a concurrent confirmation is an "already started" receipt with no second project or analytics event; the receipt offers one click to the guide (step 1 current) rather than embedding it, because the trust policy allow-lists blocks and actions per operation. No task, incident, inventory item, verdict or AI guide can be attached from Ask. The advisory lock now uses `$executeRaw` (a `void` result breaks `$queryRaw`). The DIY template page redirects to the existing project on 409 `DIY_PROJECT_ALREADY_OPEN`. The `diy` skill, adapters, audience and trust policies, impact map, coverage matrix and semantic packages were extended (130 operations, 52 commands). Executed (fake, not Postgres): 15 Ask tests, 4 frontend component tests, mutations (dropping the revision check, requiring governed in the confirm) fail as expected; the governance and registry suites pass except askGovernance routing and skillEvaluationRegistry, which fail identically at baseline. Not executed: the Postgres script (`tests/scratch/diyProjectStart.scratch.js`, 8 tests) and its read-only queries, any browser, `next build` result (see the plan record). Plan §12, rollout `docs/operations/DIY_PROJECT_START_ROLLOUT.md`.

**Revision 1.228 — Start a DIY project: 8A, the transactional start authority (backend only; nothing reachable from Ask yet):** `diyService.startProjectFromTemplate` is the one place a project is started from a template, and the page's `createProject` template branch now uses it. In one transaction, in order: the CONTRIBUTOR role (so a revoked member learns nothing, not even that a project exists), a transaction-scoped advisory lock on the (property, template) key (there is no uniqueness constraint over open projects and the revision share lock does not serialize two creations), the duplicate check (open means planning or in progress), the share-locked published head revision, integrity, eligibility, property applicability with the property context read on the SAME transaction (four assemblers made transaction-aware; any other scope is refused with a transaction client, never silently read on the shared client), the linked-task check, and the skill profile. Behavior change on the deployed page: a second open project from the same template and property now returns 409 `DIY_PROJECT_ALREADY_OPEN` carrying only the existing project's id (Ask will render a friendly receipt instead). Ask will require a governed, hash-verified revision (`requireGoverned`); the page still accepts a legacy-backfill revision. Tests (fake, not Postgres): three overlapping starts create one project and share one winner, and removing the lock makes that test fail (executed, source mutation). The owner-run Postgres script (8E) has not been written or run. No schema change. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_START_PLAN.md`.

**Revision 1.227 — DIY project commands, 7D written (step 7 complete):** an owner-run script for a throwaway Postgres covering the previous-step view and Reopen, Finish, Stop and Hand off, and recovery (writes measured by database triggers, a linked task left untouched, the outbox row left pending, Finish racing a reopen, two recoveries at once, the share locks), ten read-only queries (where the records after completions stand, invariants such as a normally completed project with no outbox row or with an unfinished step, dead-lettered requests waiting to be queued again, and reopened steps) and a rollout note. Step 7 is now built and documented; the script and queries are written and load-checked only, none of the database assertions has been run, the worker's side of a completion is not covered, and the work stays unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.226 — DIY project commands, 7C built:** a finished project in Ask now says in words what is known about the records that follow a completion (recording, recorded, or some could not be updated) and, when the earlier request failed, a person who can edit can ask Cozy to queue it again after a confirmation that says it only asks Cozy to try again, changes nothing about the project and verifies nothing; the receipt says only that it was queued again. A linked maintenance task whose update failed is shown on the open project's guide with the same option, because that status can only exist while the project is open; a project closed from its linked task shows its explanation and no action. The whole recovery decision (role, lookups, eligibility, re-queue) is now one transaction for the project page's retry buttons too. Tests and mutation checks pass against a database-free fake and jest; not run on real Postgres or in a browser; unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.225 — DIY project commands, 7B built:** from the project guide in Ask a person who can edit can finish a project once every step is resolved (the confirmation says Cozy will queue a home-history record and, when a valid linked task exists, queue it to be marked done as DIY work, that no incident changes and that it cannot be undone; the receipt reports only that the records are being recorded), and can stop it or mark it handed off to a professional through a separate read-only options card (each cannot be undone in Cozy, leaves a linked task untouched, and a hand-off books nobody). Both use the same service calls as the project page; the page's stop endpoint now checks the person's access inside its own transaction. The `diy` skill is now declared irreversible. Analytics for finish and stop are best-effort and sent only when a change was newly applied. A design correction: the all-resolved state and the options view are guide cards, not summaries, because the calm shell shows only a summary's first action. Tests and mutation checks pass against a database-free fake and jest; not run on real Postgres or in a browser; unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.224 — DIY project commands, 7A built:** from the project guide in Ask a person can now look back at a step they finished (a read-only view of that step on the same card, with its own safety note above it and the real current step still marked) and, if they can edit the project, reopen it after a confirmation that says the guide returns to it and later finished steps stay finished. When every step is resolved the summary offers "Review last step", so reopening is still reachable before the project is finished. The step update's Ask rule is now a named policy checked inside the service's transaction (advance the current step, or reopen a finished one). Reopen is not offered to viewers or on a withdrawn guide, claims nothing about the work, and is attributed to the person. Tests and mutation checks pass against a database-free fake and jest; not run on real Postgres or in a browser; unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.223 — DIY project commands, step 7 plan approved with corrections:** reopening a finished step is now reachable even when every step is resolved (a read-only "Review last step" path from the all-resolved summary); recovery of a failed link to a maintenance task appears on an open project's guide, because that status can only exist while the project is open, while recovery of failed completion records appears on a completed project; the finish confirmation says Cozy will queue a home-history record and, when a valid linked task exists, queue it to be marked done, never that it has happened; each recovery decision (role, lookup, eligibility, re-queue) runs in one transaction; and analytics for finishing or stopping are best-effort and sent only when a change was newly applied. Contributors and above may finish, stop or hand off, as on the page. Tracing recorded how a refresh keeps a step view, that confirmations have no destructive styling, and that the `diy` skill becomes the first marked irreversible. Nothing is built.

**Revision 1.222 — DIY project commands, step 7 plan (draft):** a plan for letting a household member who can edit the property, from the project guide in Ask, look back at a finished step (read only) and reopen it, finish the project once every step is resolved, stop it or hand it off to a professional, and ask for the records that follow a completion to be recorded again when they failed. Each is confirmation-gated, uses the same service the project page uses and is the person's own report. Stopping, handing off and finishing cannot be undone anywhere in the product, so the plan puts the stop and hand-off actions behind a separate read-only view, states the irreversibility, and asks the owner to decide whether Ask should be stricter than the page about who may use them. Tracing found that the page's stop endpoint does not check the person's access inside its own transaction, and that the recovery endpoints check it outside any transaction; the plan fixes both for the page as well. Fourteen decisions (S7-1 to S7-14) await approval; nothing is built, no schema change is needed, and the work stays unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.221 — DIY step commands, 6c written:** an owner-run script for a throwaway Postgres (real handlers and service; what a command writes measured by database triggers; the in-transaction role and policy checks against real rows; the share locks with real concurrent transactions; two concurrent confirmations), eight read-only queries (what Ask would offer, out-of-order progress, and invariants such as a finished step without a ledger row or actor) and a rollout note. It also records that the shared step update now refuses a viewer or a removed member with a 403 for the project page too. Written and load-checked only: none of the database assertions or queries has been run, and the lock behavior is unproven on real Postgres.

**Revision 1.220 — DIY step commands, 6a/6b built (uncommitted):** from the DIY project guide in Ask, a contributor or owner can mark the current step done, or skip it when it is optional and has no safety note, after a confirmation; the receipt says it is the person's own report and nothing is verified. The shared step update now checks the person's edit access inside its own transaction for every caller (the project page too), and Ask's narrower rules (guide not withdrawn, this is still the first unfinished step, skip allowed) are re-checked inside that transaction after the project is locked, with the template's governance rows share-locked; the earlier checks and the confirmation's change detector (now covering every step, the project version and the guide's governance state) are early answers only. The `diy` skill moves from read-only to write effects for projects that pass the reviewed-guide gate (superseding the FRD v1.58 note). The audience filter now also hides skip and reopen actions from viewers, as defense in depth only. Focus lands on the guide heading when the guide refreshes in place, and the position is a polite status. Verified by tests against a database-free fake and jest only: not run on real Postgres or in a browser, and unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.219 — DIY step commands, step 6 plan approved with corrections:** the plan now requires that the canonical step update verify the person's edit access inside the same transaction that changes the step (for the project page as well as Ask), that Ask's narrower rules (project open, guide still reviewed and not withdrawn, this is still the first unfinished step, skipping still allowed) are re-checked inside that transaction rather than only before it, and that the confirmation's change detector covers every step, the project version and the guide's governance state. The earlier checks remain as early, friendly answers only. Tracing also recorded how the guide's declared actions, the confirmation and the registries fit. Nothing is built; the step stays unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (O7).

**Revision 1.218 — DIY step commands, step 6 plan (draft):** a plan for letting a household member who can edit the property mark the current step of a DIY project done, or skip it when it is optional and has no safety note, from the project guide in Ask, through a confirmation and the same version-checked, attributed step change the project page uses. What is recorded is the person's own report ("marked done by you"); nothing is verified, and nothing else changes (no project completion, task, note or home event). The plan reuses the confirmation pattern of the guided-journey step skip, keeps the safety note directly above the action and repeats it in the confirmation, refreshes the guide in place, and moves focus to the new step. Tracing found that the viewer filter hides actions only when their text starts with a fixed list of verbs and "skip" is not on it, so the plan closes that two ways. The step needs no schema change and, like step 5, is unreachable for real projects until a first template is published (decision O7). Twelve decisions (S6-1 to S6-12) await approval; nothing is built. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_STEP_COMMANDS_PLAN.md`.

**Revision 1.217 — DIY project guide, slice 5c (owner-run database script, queries and runbook):** a script for a throwaway Postgres was written for the owner to run, covering the guide against real rows: a project copied by the real project-creation code from a really published template version is guided, the step-by-step progress follows the real step changes, every refusal and the withdrawn and superseded cases on real template versions, and a measurement by database triggers that opening a guide writes nothing. Four read-only queries and a rollout runbook were added. As decided at review, no database was provisioned: the script's checks of syntax, safety guards and module loading were run, but **its database assertions and the queries have never been executed**, which the runbook says before anything else. Step 5 is complete as code. **It establishes a governed capability and delivers no usable production guide until a named content owner authors and publishes the first template (decision O7); until then every project answers with a refusal and a link to the project page.** See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_PLAN.md` §15 and `docs/operations/DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_ROLLOUT.md`.

**Revision 1.216 — DIY project guide, slice 5b (frontend):** the guide card now shows a stepped guide when the answer carries an outline: the progress as a sentence ("Step 3 of 4, 1 done, 1 skipped", with the time it was read), a list of every step with its state in words (Done, Skipped, You are here, Not started, Optional) and the current step marked for assistive technology, and the step's tip behind a "Show tip" button. Nothing is computed or corrected by the card, the list is not interactive, and a card without an outline looks exactly as before. Verified with component tests and mutation checks (10 of 11 caught; the survivor is a redundancy the icon library already provides) and `next build`; no browser has rendered it. **This still delivers no usable production guide until a named content owner authors and publishes the first template (decision O7).** See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_PLAN.md` §14.

**Revision 1.215 — DIY project guide, slice 5a (backend):** a project row in the DIY projects list can now open a read-only guide to that project in Ask (a new launch-only operation): where the homeowner is ("Step 2 of 6, 1 done", counted by position, with skipped steps named separately and never called done), the current step with its safety note in a caution block directly above it, and an outline of every step, all read fresh and never written. The guide refuses, with a link to the project page, any project whose steps are not exactly those of an approved, verified, eligible template version (checked step by step, in order, field by field), says when a guide was withdrawn or a corrected version exists, and calls only a project with an AI guide AI-written. It adds only two optional fields to the existing guide block, keeps the DIY skill read-only, and needs no schema change. Verified with unit tests, a write spy, 16 mutation checks, the registry and governance suites and the full Ask suite (whose existing failures are unchanged from a clean checkout); not run in a browser or against Postgres, and the frontend is slice 5b. **This establishes a governed capability and delivers no usable production guide until a named content owner authors and publishes the first template (decision O7): until then every project answers with a refusal and a link to the project page.** See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_PLAN.md` §13.

**Revision 1.214 — DIY project guide plan, revision 2 and the registration trace (slice 5a-0):** after review the step 5 plan separates the refusal for an AI-written plan from the refusal for a project whose origin is not recorded (only the first is called AI-written), requires the guide to match a project's steps to the reviewed template version exactly (every step id one-to-one and in order, and every instruction field equal) before it will call them reviewed, and defines progress precisely (the one-based position in the outline; only completed steps count as done; skipped steps are counted and named separately). The browser scenario was dropped from this slice and the real-database check is an owner-run gate; both stay unverified. A read-only trace found a direct precedent for a launch-only read of one record (the guided-journey continuation), which shows the new project guide needs no new entity allow-list, and listed the exact files and the test counts that will change. **This step establishes a governed capability and delivers no usable production guide until a named content owner authors and publishes the first template (decision O7): until then every project answers with a refusal and a link to the project page.** Nothing is built. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_PLAN.md` §11 and §12.

**Revision 1.213 — DIY project guide, step 5 plan (draft):** a plan for a read-only, step-by-step guide to a DIY project in Ask: opened from a project row, showing where the homeowner is ("Step 2 of 6, 1 done"), the current step with its safety note always visible, and an outline of every step, all read fresh from the project each time and never written. It refuses, with a link to the project page, any project whose steps were not taken from a reviewed template version (AI-written plans, projects started before reviewed versions existed, content that is no longer eligible), tells the person when a guide was withdrawn or a corrected version exists, and adds only two optional fields (progress and outline) to the existing guide block. It needs no schema change. The plan states plainly that, until a first production template is authored (still waiting for a named content owner) and a project is started from it, every project will answer with a refusal. Thirteen decisions (S5-1 to S5-13) await approval; nothing is built. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_PROJECT_GUIDE_PLAN.md`.

**Revision 1.212 — DIY reverse reconciliation, slice 4c (real governed completion and worker job on real Postgres):** the whole path was run for real on a throwaway Postgres: completing a task with a linked DIY project writes the task completion and the request in one transaction (a refused request rolls the task completion back with it), the existing seasonal sync, counter, recurring roll-forward and radar request are unchanged, and the real worker applies each rule (a pro-completed task hires the project out; a DIY-completed task completes it normally or closes it by the linked task with steps untouched and no home event; an unknown mode changes nothing and the page asks the person to review). A repeated completion date on a recurring task no longer collides, several projects on one task are handled independently with their outcomes recorded, a dead letter is recovered once without redoing finished projects, a deleted task ends as a recorded skip, and a task on another property is dead-lettered before anything is touched. Twenty checks passed repeatedly with and without the production worker stubs, and ten negative controls were caught. Seven read-only queries were added for the rollout, the first of which counts the open projects whose linked task was already completed without a recorded mode, which is the evidence for deciding the follow-up. Not run: the Docker and Raspberry Pi images, Redis and the poller, a crash between a task's status write and its side effects, and a browser. Step 4 is complete as code. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_TASK_RECONCILIATION_PLAN.md` §14.

**Revision 1.211 — DIY reverse reconciliation, slice 4b (disclosure, recovery, page):** a DIY project now tells the person what happened because its linked maintenance task was completed elsewhere: "updating this project" while the worker applies it, "review the project and confirm whether you completed the work or hired a professional" when the completion did not say who did the work (or the task no longer exists), "some updates could not be applied" with a **Finish updating** button for people who can edit when the update was dead-lettered, and "closed because your linked task was completed" or "...a pro completed your linked task" once closed. The read never writes, recovery re-queues only a dead letter and only the projects still unresolved, and a project closed from its task no longer shows as "recorded before tracking". The page checks again a bounded number of times while updating, with no promised time. Tested with service and page tests, mutation checks on both sides and `next build`; the real-Postgres run (4c) and a browser run are not done. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_TASK_RECONCILIATION_PLAN.md` §13.

**Revision 1.210 — DIY reverse reconciliation, slice 4a (request, worker handler, transitions):** completing a maintenance task that has an open DIY project linked to it now writes, in the same transaction, a request keyed by a unique id stored on the task (so a repeated completion date cannot collide). A worker applies the approved rules to each linked project: a pro-completed task hires the project out, a DIY-completed task completes it normally when its steps are done or closes it by the linked task when they are not (steps untouched, no home event), and an unknown completion mode changes nothing. A deleted task ends the request as a recorded skip; each project's outcome is saved so a retry only redoes what failed. Creating a project now checks that its linked task exists on the same property, and the backend refuses to start if either new event type is missing from the database. Verified with unit and producer tests, mutation checks and a production-style worker build; the real-Postgres run, the disclosure and the page are slices 4c and 4b. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_TASK_RECONCILIATION_PLAN.md` §12.

**Revision 1.209 — DIY reverse reconciliation plan, revision 2 (draft):** after review the plan keys each task completion's reconciliation request by a unique id generated inside the transaction and stored on the task (never by the completion date, which a caller can repeat), ends a deleted linked task as a typed skip that leaves projects unchanged and discloses "review", records a bounded outcome per project on the event so a retry or recovery only redoes what failed, and asks the person to "review the project and confirm whether you completed the work or hired a professional" instead of telling them to finish or stop it. The no-inference rule for an unknown completion mode was explicitly approved. Nothing is built. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_TASK_RECONCILIATION_PLAN.md`.

**Revision 1.208 — DIY reverse reconciliation, step 4 plan (draft):** a plan for making a DIY project follow its linked maintenance task when the task is completed somewhere else: an atomic request written with the task's completion, a worker that applies the approved rules (a pro-completed task hires the project out, a DIY-completed task completes it or closes it by the linked task, an unknown mode changes nothing), and a read-only disclosure for everything it cannot do. The reading found that only one completion path records who did the work, so most completions will be disclosed rather than applied, and that two raw writers complete tasks without any hook; the plan names both. Twelve decisions (S4-1 to S4-12) await approval; nothing is built. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_TASK_RECONCILIATION_PLAN.md`.

**Revision 1.207 — DIY completion outbox, slice 3c (real worker job against real Postgres):** the whole completion path was run for real on a throwaway Postgres: the completion's outbox row, the actual worker job claiming it, the home event, and the governed maintenance completion (recurring roll-forward, seasonal item and counter, radar request) with the incident untouched; duplicate delivery, a partial failure and its retry, a dead letter and its recovery, two workers racing, lease reclaim, a handler overlapping its own retry, a task on another property (dead-lettered before anything is created), a deleted task, and the actor leaving the household afterwards. Thirteen checks passed repeatedly, with and without the production worker stubs, and five negative controls were caught. The Docker and Raspberry Pi images, Redis and the poller, and a crash between a task's status write and its side effects were not run. Step 3 is complete as code; applying it follows `docs/operations/DIY_COMPLETION_OUTBOX_ROLLOUT.md`. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_COMPLETION_OUTBOX_PLAN.md` §15.

**Revision 1.206 — DIY completion outbox, slice 3b (disclosure, recovery, page):** a completed project now says what is happening to its follow-on records: "Recording your completion" (including while an automatic retry is under way, with no promised time), "Completion recorded", "Some records could not be updated" with a **Finish recording** button for people who can edit, or, for projects completed before tracking existed, "not verified here". Only a dead-lettered update can be re-queued; the same row is reset once, with who, when and how many times recorded on it. The read is proven not to write. The completion response no longer carries a home event id, the page polls a bounded number of times while recording, and an incident-linked completion says the incident was not changed. Tested with backend and page tests, mutation checks on both sides, a real-Postgres run and `next build`; not run in a browser. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_COMPLETION_OUTBOX_PLAN.md` §14.

**Revision 1.205 — DIY completion outbox, slice 3a (backend and worker):** completing a DIY project now verifies the actor's access inside its own transaction and writes one outbox event with a snapshot of what was completed; a worker handler creates the home event and completes the linked maintenance task through the governed path (the maintenance write is split so the public method still checks access), after a preflight that dead-letters a task on another property before anything is created. The inline effects and the raw incident writes are removed, so a DIY completion no longer resolves incidents. The backend refuses to start if the database lacks the new event type. Tested with handler, transaction, worker-gate and production-stub checks, a built-worker smoke test and a real-Postgres run, with mutation checks. The page and the recovery route are slice 3b, so this must not ship alone. A new exhaustive dispatch test found a pre-existing unhandled event type, `FOLLOW_UP_DUE` (claim follow-ups), left unchanged for a product decision. See `docs/architecture/ASK_COZY_DIY_COMPLETION_OUTBOX_PLAN.md` §13.

**Revision 1.204 — DIY completion outbox, slice 3a-0 (measurements):** no product code changed.
