A chip never counts records the result did not load unless the server declares the true total.

**IW-PRES-014 — Shelves.** Grouped records whose groups have a natural order (Overdue / This month / Later; Open / In progress / Done) render as horizontal shelves. Each shelf has:
- a heading and a count ("Showing 3 of 8" when partial);
- previous/next buttons that disable at either end, and snap scrolling;
- a "See all" end card when the section is partial.

Each card shows a status stripe, the title, one timing fact and one cost or amount fact. Tapping a card opens its detail in place (a drawer on desktop, a bottom sheet on phone) with the item's declared actions. A shelf holds up to about 12 cards. Beyond that, "See all" opens the full section in the sheet.

**IW-PRES-015 — Card deck.** A bounded set of items that each need a homeowner decision (inspection findings, radar alerts, walkthrough issues) may render as a deck. The deck has:
- one card at a time, with its position ("2 of 5") shown;
- the item's declared actions as buttons (at most three);
- swipe right and left mapped to two of those actions, plus arrow keys;
- **Back** to show the previous card again, **Skip for now** to move on without sending anything, and an end summary with counts.

A sent action is a real request, so the deck cannot undo it; Back only shows the card again, marked with what was already sent. *(Revised in v1.73: the prototype's Undo implied the deck could reverse a saved change.)*

**Batch confirmation (decided September 24, 2026; v1.75).** Sending each decision on its own would add a confirmation at the bottom of the conversation and scroll away from the deck after every card. So a deck may declare a batch: decisions made with the batch's actions are only recorded on the card, and at the end the homeowner reviews them and sends them together, which returns **one** confirmation for all of them. Nothing is written before that confirmation. Actions outside the batch (for example Mark resolved, which asks how the finding was fixed) keep their own confirmation. On confirm, each record is re-checked exactly as a single confirmation would be; a record that changed while the confirmation was open is left untouched and named, and the receipt says what happened to each record, because the writes are not one transaction. A deck is offered only when every item carries declared actions, and swiping is never the only way to act. Each decision is a registered item action under its usual rules: a consequential action still shows its confirmation card, and the deck never batches writes silently. The list view stays available.

**IW-PRES-016 — Comparison strip.** Two to four alternatives use the existing `COMPARISON` block. Examples: quotes, coverage options, upgrade scenarios, acting versus doing nothing, sell/hold/rent, refinance scenarios. Each card carries:
- the price with a relative bar;
- server-declared badges, each with its basis and policy code;
- the key attributes and the option's own actions.

A Cards/Table switch shows the same options as rows, marking the leading value in each row. No overall winner is shown unless a declared policy says so. **Contract change:** the schema allows one `badge` per option, but the prototype showed options leading on different things ("Lowest price", "Soonest start", "Longest warranty"). Add an additive `badges` array (at most 3) and keep `badge` for existing producers.

**Price bars and leading values (v1.76).** An option may declare its price as a number (`amount`: value and currency); bars are drawn only when every option declares one in the same currency, each as its share of the highest. A table cell is marked as leading only where the server set `leading` on that attribute, following a declared badge policy; the renderer never picks a leader itself.

**Quote review decisions (September 24, 2026; v1.76).** (1) Two to four quotes still in play render as the strip; one quote, or five and more, keep the table with every quote. (2) The only badge is **Lowest price**, and only when the workspace is comparable, among its comparison-ready quotes, in one currency, without a tie. Warranty and start date get no badge because they are stored as free text. (3) Quotes the homeowner rejected are left out of the strip, and the description says how many and points to the comparison page.

**IW-PRES-017 — Timeline.** Dated records render on a horizontal track. Examples: home events, claims, warranty and policy expiries, capital windows. The track has year ticks and a Today marker. Each category has a colour plus a text legend and a filter chip. Previous/next buttons step through events, and the selected event's detail shows below the track. Recorded date precision is kept: a month-precision event is placed and labelled as a month. On phone the track scrolls sideways, and a condensed vertical list is the fallback. **Contract change:** the `TIMELINE` block lacks category, date precision and item actions. Add them as optional fields.

**Home Timeline decisions (September 24, 2026; v1.77).** (1) The twelve event types map to five colour categories: Work done (repair, maintenance, improvement, verified resolution), Inspections, Claims, Purchases and value (purchase, value update), and Records and notes (document, note, milestone, other). The exact type still shows in the event's detail. (2) Events with an unknown date are not placed on the track; they are listed under it as "Date unknown". A range sits at its start date, with the full range in its detail. A Timeline/List switch shows the same events as a vertical list, and the choice is kept with the result. **Contract (additive):** `TIMELINE` items may carry `meta` (up to 6 short facts), and a block may hold up to 100 items (was 50), since the Home Timeline answer reads up to 80 events.

**IW-PRES-018 — Lifespan bars.** Items with an age and a typical life range (appliances, systems, roof) render one bar per item. Each bar shows the typical-range band and an age marker. A status label ("Past typical range", "Plan ahead", "Plenty of life") comes from the declared range. Items are sorted by the share of their typical life used. The result states that it is an estimate from install years. An item with no age is not drawn. Instead, an inline capture asks for the missing year and saves it through the existing inventory correction path, with its usual confirmation. Only the calculated part is shown: Appliance Oracle's AI recommendations stay page-only (v1.70). **Contract change:** a new block type (for example `LIFESPAN`) with per-item age, range, status and actions.

**Appliance Oracle decisions (September 24, 2026; v1.78).** (1) Each bar's label and colour follow the Appliance Oracle's own risk level ("Critical · 64% failure risk"), not the typical range, so Ask never contradicts the Oracle page; the bar still shows where the age sits in the range. Critical is red, High and Medium amber, Low green. The Oracle calls an appliance Critical once it reaches its average life, which can still be inside its typical range. (2) The Oracle reads age from the inventory item's **purchase date**, so the inline capture asks for a purchase date ("Add purchase date"), through the existing inventory correction and its confirmation. (3) An appliance bought this calendar year was skipped as if it had no date; the shared service now analyses it at age 0, which also changes the Oracle page. **Contract (additive):** `LIFESPAN` items may carry `meta` (up to 4 short facts), and the block may declare `missingAgeTitle`; without it the heading reads "No age recorded yet", no longer "No install year yet".

**IW-PRES-019 — Room map.** Records grouped by room render as a grid of room tiles. All recorded rooms are visible by default so the headline count matches the immediately visible collection; when more than one floor group exists, an “All rooms” default plus floor filters let the homeowner narrow the grid. Each tile shows its item count and an open-item badge, and tapping a room opens its items in place. The grid is a schematic ordering built from the stored `InventoryRoom.floorLevel` and a deterministic tile order. It is not stored geometry and must not imply sizes or positions. Rooms with no floor level go in an "Other" group.

**Rooms decisions (September 24, 2026; v1.79).** (1) A tile shows the room's recorded item count and, as its badge, the maintenance tasks linked to it that are still open (pending or in progress, as Maintenance counts them). (2) The room map is declared even when no floor level is recorded, which is most homes today because no page sets one: the rooms then sit in one "Rooms" group, and the list says "Floors aren't recorded yet; open a room to set its floor" (the last part only to someone who can change rooms, through Ask's existing "Change floor level" correction). Floor names follow that correction's convention: 0 is "Ground floor", 1 and up "Floor n", −1 "Basement", lower "Basement level n".

**IW-PRES-020 — Progress ring and stats.** Checklist-style readiness renders as a ring with its percent and the basis ("17 of 25 items done"). Up to three number tiles and the next three steps, each with its declared actions, follow. Examples: sale readiness, Digital Will readiness, property completeness, tax readiness, renovation permit readiness, buyer closing. The percent is the domain's own readiness figure (for example the seller-prep readiness calculation), never a ratio computed by the renderer when the domain weights items differently.

**Sale readiness figure (decided September 24, 2026; v1.80).** The sale case had no readiness figure: its service deliberately avoids self-reported completion percentages, the sale-case page shows none, and the only percent in the code belongs to the older seller-prep checklist that the sale case replaced. The homeowner chose to define one in the sale-case service (`saleReadinessFigure`), not in the renderer. It counts only the **must-address** items (material blockers, items that need verifying, professional decisions; presentation-category items excluded), and an item is settled when it is **resolved** (its source cleared) or **disclosed and waived**; pursuing is a commitment, not done. The basis reads "3 of 6 must-address items resolved or disclosed". With no must-address item there is no figure and no ring, because an absence of findings is not evidence of readiness. Only Ask shows it for now; the sale-case page does not.

**IW-PRES-021 — Folding and pinning.** Earlier results may fold into a one-line summary showing their headline, and any result may be pinned to a strip at the top of the conversation. Folding and pinning only change what is shown: they never re-run the request. A pinned result shows its freshness and goes stale like any other result (§15).

**IW-PRES-022 — The server declares the pattern.** The server declares an optional presentation hint on the block (for example `SHELVES` or `DECK`), together with the typed fields that pattern needs. The client picks the pattern only when all of these hold:
- the hint is present;
- the fields validate;
- the viewport and accessibility preferences allow the pattern.

Otherwise it renders the grouped list (IW-PRES-012). The renderer never infers a pattern from block ids or titles. Patterns are shared components, and each domain maps its data onto them instead of building its own list. The 13 domain-specific result lists are retired as their blocks move to shared patterns, keeping their inline detail, correction and action behaviour.

**Rules for every pattern** (from the prototype): horizontal strips have visible arrows and item counts; nothing moves by itself; reduced motion is respected; every swipe has a button equivalent; partial results say "Showing N of M"; details open in place and never navigate to a traditional page.

**First adopters.** Block ids come from the September 24 trace. Each slice re-traces its page, route and service before building.

| Pattern | First adopter | Later adopters |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shelves | Maintenance (`maintenance-groups`; **done in v1.74**), `home-actions-list` (**done in v1.82**), `seasonal-maintenance-items` (**done in v1.83**), `status-board-items`, `seller-prep-open-items`, `buyer-plan-actions` (**all done in v1.84**) | None left |
| Card deck | Inspection findings (`inspection-findings`; **done in v1.75**) | `home-event-radar-feed` (**done in v1.92**). `buyer-walkthrough-issues` and `buyer-negotiation-findings` **stay lists** (v1.92): they declare no item actions, and their decisions (resolving a walkthrough issue, a seller's response) have no Ask write operation, so a deck would have nothing to decide |
| Comparison strip | Quote review (`quote-review-table`; **done in v1.76**) | `sell-hold-rent-comparison` (**done in v1.85**), `coverage-comparison-options` (**done in v1.86**), `refinance-scenario-table` (**done in v1.87**), `home-upgrade-options` (**done in v1.89**) |
| Timeline | Home Timeline (`home-timeline-events`; **done in v1.77**) | `capital-timeline-table` (**done in v1.90**); `property-recent-events`, `inventory-history` and `property-warranties` **stay lists** (v1.90): short, with a live record detail and corrections in their own components, so a track adds little |
| Lifespan bars | Appliance Oracle calculated part (`appliance-oracle-items`; **done in v1.78**) | Inventory ages (`property-inventory`) **stays a list** (v1.93): the property summary's inventory read carries no purchase date or typical life, and adding them would run the Oracle's lifespan calculation inside a general summary; the Appliance Oracle answer is the lifespan view |
| Room map | Rooms (`property-rooms`; **done in v1.79**) | `inventory-results` grouped by room **stays a list** (v1.93): its items are inventory records, not rooms, so a room map would draw tiles that are not rooms; the room name is already in each item's line, and grouping would change the lookup's order and its 50-item limit for little gain |
| Progress ring | Seller prep readiness (`seller-prep-progress`; **done in v1.80**) | `property-completeness` and `buyer-closing-day-blockers` (**done in v1.91**); `renovation-readiness-items` (blocking items satisfied or acknowledged, of all blocking items) and `digital-will-items` (handoff requirements met, of three), both **done in v1.94** on the definitions recommended in the September 24 trace. **Not built:** `tax-readiness-gaps` (gaps with no recorded total, so no figure) |

**Delivery order.**
1. Additive contract fields and hints, the answer-first header, and the seven shared components with their grouped-list fallback, tested by component tests. No producer changes yet, so nothing the homeowner sees changes.
2. One slice per pattern for its first adopter. Each slice has a backend slice test, component tests, and a Playwright scenario at desktop and phone widths.
3. The later adopters. A domain-specific list is retired once all of its blocks have moved.

### 11.11 Calm conversational answers (decided September 25, 2026; FRD v1.111)

**Decision.** Ask Cozy is becoming the primary way homeowners use ContractToCozy. Live review on September 25 found the shell and answers heavier than IW-SHELL-002 and IW-SHELL-008 already require: three stacked headers, a per-answer card with its own header and a version trail before the answer, nested bordered cards, two overlapping retry buttons on an error, warning-styled disclaimers on ordinary results, helper copy under the composer, and internal vocabulary ("Main", "Fold", "server pages", a session status of "unavailable"). The Ask display lab (sample data) is the visual target: one headline sentence, one support line, count chips, a bare artifact, and follow-up chips docked above a one-line composer. This section makes that target testable. It adds no new data and changes no authorization, freshness, provenance, confirmation, or audit rule; it changes what is shown by default and what sits behind one tap.

**Non-goals.** No LLM is added; headlines and support lines are composed deterministically from the operation's own counts and blocks (the LLM-last-resort rule stays in force). No provenance or freshness requirement is weakened. Traditional pages are unchanged.

**IW-CALM-001 — Answer anatomy.** A Cozy response renders, in order: (1) a one-sentence headline that states the answer; (2) at most one supporting line; (3) count or status chips when the operation has them; (4) at most one primary artifact (shelves, table, deck, comparison, timeline, form); (5) follow-up chips. An operation that cannot supply a headline falls back to its existing title. A headline is composed from typed counts and never from free text.

**IW-CALM-002 — No answer container.** A response is not wrapped in a bordered card, and nothing inside it nests a bordered container inside another. The only bordered elements inside an answer are individual item cards and interactive controls. Tone (caution, urgent) is carried by the headline, chips, and item edge color, not by tinting a container.

**IW-CALM-003 — Response chrome behind one control.** Refresh, Pin, Fold, the version trail ("originally answered"), the updated time, and response-level feedback live behind a single overflow control that is always reachable by keyboard and touch and appears on hover or focus on pointer devices. The updated time lives in that menu; nothing about freshness is shown inline unless the result is stale or partial, and that is said by the revalidation notice, not by a timestamp line. The original response stays reachable; it is not removed.

**IW-CALM-004 — Errors and empty states.** An unavailable or failed result is one plain sentence and one primary retry action that re-reads current records. Two retry controls with overlapping meaning are not allowed. No home record is changed by a read error, and the sentence says so only when that is not obvious.

**Retry continuity clarification (v1.156).** Retry is an execution action, not a suggested conversational question. The client sends the failed execution id and an idempotency key; the server re-authorizes the request and replays its persisted question, property, resolved operation, and original launch context against current records. The retry creates an auditable successor linked to the failed execution and collapses the failed result in the transcript. Labels such as “Ask this question again” must never be submitted as literal homeowner questions, and retry-like strings are excluded from follow-up chips whenever the response already exposes its retry action.

**IW-CALM-005 — Limitations as footnotes.** An informational scope or limitation statement (a producer's `BOUNDARY` block of severity INFO with no actions) renders as a small muted footnote, without a warning icon or tinted card. The producer decides whether the statement applies; the client does not hide or reword it. Cautions and emergencies keep their warning treatment because the homeowner may need to act on them.

**IW-CALM-006 — Composer and follow-ups.** The composer is a single auto-growing input with send and voice controls. Persistent helper lines are removed; "Shift+Enter for a new line" shows only while the input is focused and already multiline. Follow-up chips (at most four, from the latest answer's declared suggested questions, minus questions already asked and a retry the answer already offers) dock directly above the footer composer and scroll sideways on a narrow screen. Choosing a chip asks it. The row is empty while an answer is pending, and answers no longer show their own inline chips.

**IW-CALM-007 — One Ask header.** The Ask surface adds one slim header: the Cozy avatar and the title "Ask Cozy". It adds no subtitle and no second title band; the selected home is already shown by the application header. Refresh state ("Partially refreshed", "Refreshing…", "Some updates delayed") becomes a small colored dot whose label is announced and shown on hover or focus, and it still opens the affected-capabilities detail. The delete action stays reachable as an icon.

**IW-CALM-008 — History rail.** The rail contains New conversation, the quiet Explore with Cozy group, search, grouped history, and archive. Discovery and history are visually separated, neither carries explanatory copy, and the rail collapses per IW-SHELL-008. Session status labels use plain words ("In progress", "Needs a retry", "Could not finish", "Not available"); a raw status name is never shown.

**IW-CALM-009 — Plain vocabulary.** Homeowner-facing copy must not expose internal concepts: session or branch names, page-size or server-paging notes, "fold", or raw status enums. Each removed string gets a plain replacement or is omitted.

**IW-CALM-010 — One item card.** All list adopters use one shared item card: urgency edge color, icon, title, one meta line, and an optional trailing tag (cost, DIY). Titles wrap to two lines then truncate; meta never clips. Carousel arrows appear on pointer devices only; touch devices swipe. Existing per-domain detail sheets and actions are unchanged.

**IW-CALM-011 — Numbers agree.** The headline, support line, and chips derive from one count source per result and must not appear to contradict each other. Records excluded by default (for example completed or cancelled work) are described once as excluded, never mixed into the "match" count.

**IW-CALM-012 — Phone first, on by default, reversible.** Every slice is designed and checked at phone width first. The calm presentation is the default for every user (decided September 25, 2026). A homeowner can return to the previous presentation with `?calm=0` in the address, which is remembered on that browser (`?calm=1` turns it back on), and a build-time switch (`NEXT_PUBLIC_ASK_CALM_ANSWERS=false`) turns it off for everyone if a release needs to. Until a domain adopts the headline anatomy, it keeps its own list layout inside the calm shell.

**Two levels of adoption.** The calm *shell* (IW-CALM-002 to 005, 008, 009: no answer frame, one overflow menu, plain state blocks, footnotes, plain wording) applies to every answer while the setting is on. The calm *anatomy* (IW-CALM-001, 010, 011: headline, supporting line, bare artifact, shared item card) applies per domain as each adopts it, so no domain is half-converted (IW-CALM-012).

**Delivery slices.** (A) Maintenance answer anatomy and shared item card. (B) Response chrome, errors, footnotes, and plain vocabulary across all answers. (C) Composer, follow-up row, single header, and rail. (D) Landing state strip and default state (decision pending). (E) Remaining domains adopt the anatomy in the §21 order.

**Acceptance.** For each adopted answer: the headline is the first content in the response; no bordered container is nested inside another in the response; all response controls are reachable through one overflow control by keyboard; an error shows exactly one retry; an informational limit renders as a footnote; counts agree across headline, support line, and chips; and the result is usable at 360px width without horizontal page scroll.

**Landing (slice D, decided by default September 25, 2026 pending product review).** With the setting on, the landing is the composer, a one-line greeting, a state strip, at most four starter questions as chips, the capability explorer link, and any pending work. The strip is one sentence about the home from the Concierge Home payload Ask already loads (counts of items needing attention now and to plan soon, never counting suppressed, completed, stale, unavailable, or watch-only items), count chips that each open the matching answer, an important-changes chip, a chip to continue the open decision, at most one urgent item, and plain notes when a source is unavailable. It never says nothing needs attention while priorities are unavailable. It replaces the "Popular ways" cards and the "For your attention" card. Weather is not a separate strip element: a critical weather signal reaches the strip only through the ranked priorities and important changes.

**Open decisions.** (1) Whether the serif headline is kept for every answer or only summary answers (today: every summary, in the display serif). (2) Whether the history rail is expanded or collapsed by default on wide screens (today: unchanged, expanded). (3) Whether a dedicated weather chip is wanted once the Concierge payload carries a weather flag.

### 11.12 Conversational UI principles (decided September 25, 2026; FRD v1.112)

**Decision.** Ask Cozy is the primary interface, so it is judged as a conversation, not as a dashboard that happens to accept typed input. A live review on September 25 (after the calm shell shipped) found two answers that do not read as conversation: a *Maintenance completion details* form drawn as a large tinted card with a legend, a required-field asterisk, two paragraphs of policy text and seven full-width choice buttons; and a *superseded* answer that reopened as the previous presentation, complete with its own bordered summary card, list card and filter row. This section fixes the principles those two fail, so every later slice is checked against them. It builds on §11.5–11.7 and §11.11 and changes no authorization, confirmation, provenance or audit rule.

**IW-CONV-001 — Message first.** An assistant turn is a message; a card, list, table or form is an attachment to it. Every turn opens with a sentence-level statement (the headline of §11.11) before any artifact, and nothing in a turn is a full-width document-style panel.

**IW-CONV-002 — One artifact, one primary action.** A turn carries at most one primary artifact and at most one primary action. Other actions are secondary text controls or follow-up chips.

**IW-CONV-003 — Progressive disclosure.** A turn shows the least that answers the question. Detail opens on demand (expand in place, side panel on wide screens, bottom sheet on narrow). Superseded, refreshed and long content collapses to a one-line stub, never a second full copy.

**IW-CONV-004 — Ask, do not present forms.** Capturing information is a sequence of short questions from Cozy, one at a time. Each question is a message with quick replies (chips) for a bounded set of options and a text or picker input otherwise, a visible *Skip* when the field is optional, and *Change* on any earlier answer. The answers so far read back as one line. Nothing is written until Cozy shows a plain review message ("Mark *Chimney cleaning* complete, no cost recorded?") and the homeowner confirms it. A multi-field form card in the transcript is allowed only when the data is irreducibly tabular, and then it opens in a sheet, not inline. Every existing capture schema, validation, confirmation version and authorization rule is unchanged; only the presentation and the order of asking change.

**IW-CONV-005 — Quick replies.** A bounded choice (about six or fewer) is offered as chips. A chosen chip is echoed as the homeowner's own message so the transcript reads as a dialogue and survives a reload.

**IW-CONV-006 — Visible work.** While an answer is being prepared, the turn shows a plain status line naming what is being checked ("Checking your maintenance records…") in the place the answer will appear, without moving the layout, plus the Stop control. A multi-source answer may name each step as it completes.

**IW-CONV-007 — Reversible by default.** Where a domain allows it, a completed write shows a one-line receipt with *Undo* for a short time instead of relying on heavy pre-confirmation. Actions that cannot be undone keep an explicit confirmation.

**IW-CONV-008 — Continuity.** Editing a question replaces its answer with the new one and leaves a stub for the old; it does not stack duplicates. Titles are generated, and the history rail shows plain states.

**IW-CONV-009 — Provenance without noise.** Sources, assumptions and freshness sit behind one quiet affordance per answer (a "Sources" chip that opens the panel or sheet). In the calm shell the chip shares one meta row with the Helpful and Not helpful buttons and the corrections menu (implemented, slice I), shows `aria-expanded`, and reads "Context open" while its panel is open. A caveat that changes what the homeowner should do is one line in the answer. Banners and warning cards are reserved for action the homeowner must take.

**IW-CONV-010 — Plain voice.** Copy is second person and plain: no session, branch, server, enum, or system nouns; errors say what happened and what to do in one or two sentences.

**IW-CONV-011 — One composer.** A single persistent composer with send, voice and (where supported) attach. Enter sends, Shift+Enter adds a line, the draft persists per conversation, and a failed send keeps the text.

**IW-CONV-012 — Calm motion and stable layout.** Motion is short and respects reduced-motion. Arriving content does not push what the homeowner is reading; the answer's space is reserved (IW-CONV-006).

**IW-CONV-013 — Phone first.** The composer is anchored above the safe area, follow-up chips scroll sideways, detail opens as a bottom sheet, tap targets are at least 44px, and nothing scrolls the page sideways.

**IW-CONV-014 — Accessible by construction.** Focus moves to the new answer's heading, status changes are announced in a live region, chips and menu items are named buttons, and no state is conveyed by color alone.

**IW-CONV-015 — Proactive, not intrusive.** A signal Cozy noticed (weather, a due date, a price change) appears as a message in the thread or as a chip on the landing, is dismissible, and is never modal.

**IW-CONV-016 — Density.** A turn is as short as its answer. Chrome that repeats what is already on screen (a timestamp under an answer that was just refreshed, a second "open" action next to the list's own, a per-tool paragraph the tool row already implies, an empty-state box in the history rail) is removed rather than restyled. On a wide screen the application header is the only title band; the refresh-status dot moves into the history rail. A supporting artifact (the "What comes next" tool list) is offered collapsed when it follows the main artifact, and is one row per tool when it is the main artifact.

**Audit of the September 25 screenshots against the principles.**

| Observed | Principle | Required change |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Completion capture is a blue-tinted card: eyebrow, H2, three paragraphs, fieldset legend with "*", seven full-width buttons, "Actual cost" field below the fold | 001, 002, 004, 005, 010 | One question per message ("Which task did you complete?" with the open tasks as chips, most recent first, "Something else" opens a picker), then "Any actual cost? (optional)" with a numeric input and *Skip*, then a review message with *Confirm* and *Change*. Policy text becomes one line inside the review. |
| A superseded answer reopens as a framed summary card, list card, filter row and paging note, with a focus ring on its summary bar | 003, 008, 012 | A one-line stub ("Earlier version · Show") that opens the older view in the same calm presentation, in reduced emphasis, without a frame. |
| Five to six tool cards after a Maintenance answer, each with a description, a "Full tool" sentence, a readiness line, a boundary paragraph and two buttons; large empty areas; a "Refreshed" line and a duplicate "Open maintenance" button | 002, 003, 016 | Related tools collapsed under one line; a tool is one row (name, one line, Explore, open-page icon), with a short note only when it is not ready or opens the full page; the timestamp and the duplicate action are removed. |
| An unexplained orange dot in the header | 009, 014 | The dot carries a visible plain label on wide screens and a tap target that explains it. |

**Delivery slices.** (F) Superseded stub and header status label. (G) Conversational capture: the completion flow first, then the other capture schemas. (H) Visible work status and reserved answer space. (I) Sources chip. (J) Undo receipts for low-risk writes. Each slice is reviewed against the screenshots above and at phone width before the next.

## 12. Desktop experience

**IW-DESK-001:** The full Ask page supports a conversation column and an expandable contextual workspace when structured work benefits from additional width.

**IW-DESK-002:** Opening inline detail must not replace or erase the conversation. The homeowner can see the originating result or return to it without rerunning the query.

**IW-DESK-003:** Dense comparisons, tables, document previews, and longer forms may expand within the Ask route. They must not require a domain-page transition solely because they need space.

**IW-DESK-004:** The floating panel may promote the user to the full Ask workspace when space is insufficient. That promotion is within Ask Cozy, carries the current session/workspace state, and is not a domain-page handoff.

**IW-DESK-005:** The composer remains reachable while inspecting non-modal content. Focus order follows conversation, current workspace, and composer semantics rather than visual column position alone.

**IW-DESK-006:** On sufficiently wide screens, the Ask conversation rail remains visible while the active conversation is open. The active item, New conversation, search, and recent/pinned groups are reachable without returning to the landing surface.

**IW-DESK-007:** When both application and conversation navigation are present, the shell preserves useful conversation width through compact/collapsible rails. It does not solve width pressure by navigating structured results to traditional domain pages.

**IW-DESK-008:** The optional contextual information panel opens alongside the conversation only when enough width remains; otherwise it overlays or replaces the workspace level with a clear in-Ask back action.

**IW-DESK-009:** Rail collapse state, active conversation, and optional context-panel state survive ordinary navigation and refresh where safe without overriding an explicit user preference.

## 13. Mobile experience

**IW-MOB-001:** Ask Cozy uses a full-height experience that responds to the visual viewport and device safe areas.

**IW-MOB-002:** Inline detail and workflows use nested full-screen views, sheets, or stacked panels under the Ask shell. They do not open unrelated dashboard pages by default.

**IW-MOB-003:** The in-Ask back action returns to the exact prior result, filter, selection, scroll position, and draft.

**IW-MOB-004:** The on-screen keyboard cannot cover the active field, validation message, primary action, or composer.

**IW-MOB-005:** Tables transform to labeled cards or horizontal sections without losing column meaning, units, source, or comparison relationships.

**IW-MOB-006:** Primary controls meet existing touch-target conventions. Repeated row controls include the target in their accessible name.

**IW-MOB-007:** Functional parity is required. A workflow cannot be desktop-only merely because its desktop layout uses a side workspace.

**IW-MOB-008:** Recent conversations are available from an Ask header control that opens a full-height drawer or nested view with New conversation, search, active-state indication, property labels, and session actions.

**IW-MOB-009:** Selecting a conversation closes the history drawer, restores that conversation, and moves focus to the conversation heading or restored position. Closing without selection returns focus to the history trigger.

**IW-MOB-010:** Application navigation, conversation history, contextual information, and the active workflow must not create stacked sheets with ambiguous back behavior. Every mobile layer has one clear title, close/back action, and restoration destination.

## 14. Result identity, state, and continuity

The existing distinction between original response, current view, and current authoritative data remains mandatory.

**IW-STATE-001:** Every interactive result has stable identity tied to session, execution, property, query, and canonical entities.

**IW-STATE-002:** Filtering, sorting, paging, expanding, selecting, and opening inline detail update the current view without creating duplicate live surfaces.

**IW-STATE-003:** A new substantive question creates a new response. A declared refinement updates its target result while retaining the homeowner message and a concise acknowledgement.

**IW-STATE-004:** Draft input survives recoverable failure, workspace close/reopen, mobile keyboard changes, and promotion from panel to full Ask workspace.

**IW-STATE-005:** Reloading or resuming the same session restores supported workspace state without converting it into a property fact or indefinite transcript archive.

**IW-STATE-006:** Switching property cannot retarget an existing result, proposal, workspace, or draft. Historical content remains visibly scoped to its original property.

**IW-STATE-007:** Out-of-order requests cannot overwrite newer property, result, filter, proposal, or workspace state.

**IW-STATE-008:** Optional traditional/external round trips restore the source context and trigger appropriate revalidation.

## 15. Freshness and reconciliation

**IW-FRESH-001:** Revalidate on explicit refresh, known local mutation, return from an optional edit, access change, and before preparing or executing a write.

**IW-FRESH-002:** If the target changed after review, invalidate the proposal and show the current state. Never substitute a similar entity.

**IW-FRESH-003:** Successful writes reconcile every visible result whose membership, status, totals, next actions, or dependent state may have changed, or mark it stale with a clear recovery action.

**IW-FRESH-004:** If a write succeeds but reconciliation fails, retain a truthful success receipt and offer read-only recovery. Never invite repeating the mutation.

**IW-FRESH-005:** Changes made through traditional pages are reflected in Ask Cozy after return/refresh; changes made through Ask Cozy are reflected in traditional pages because both use canonical data.

## 16. Structured input and editable proposals

**IW-INPUT-001:** Ask may collect information conversationally or through validated structured controls. A visible supported control must not force the homeowner to retype equivalent text.

**IW-INPUT-002:** Required, optional, sensitive, applicability, calculation, scenario, preference, and workflow fields remain distinguishable.

**IW-INPUT-003:** Validation is field-specific, retains entered values after recoverable failure, and never silently coerces invalid or ambiguous consequential input.

**IW-INPUT-004:** Editing a proposal creates a newly validated proposal version and invalidates prior consent.

**IW-INPUT-005:** Inline forms must use the same domain validation and canonical writer used by the corresponding traditional experience. UI parity does not authorize duplicated validation logic.

## 17. Confirmation, execution, and receipts

**IW-CONF-001:** Material writes use the existing Ask confirmation and receipt path unless an authoritative domain requirement explicitly permits direct execution.

**IW-CONF-002:** Confirmation names the exact property, target, operation, material inputs, consequences, and expiration/version state.

**IW-CONF-003:** Confirmation rechecks access, operation availability, target ownership, current version, and required context.

**IW-CONF-004:** Double submission, retry after lost response, and concurrent edits produce at most one authorized domain effect.

**IW-CONF-005:** Canceling a pending proposal does not imply reversal of an already-completed domain operation.

**IW-CONF-006:** A receipt distinguishes completed, already completed by this execution, changed elsewhere, rejected, failed, and unknown outcome.

**IW-CONF-007:** When a domain supports correction or reversal, Ask invokes that declared domain behavior. It must not invent a generic undo action.

## 18. Ask-native navigation and transition compatibility

**IW-TRAD-001:** Ask Cozy is the target homeowner interaction shell. Its discovery cues, starters, results, workflows, confirmations, and receipts remain inside Ask.

**IW-TRAD-002:** Explore with Cozy must not use an existing traditional route as its destination or expose the retired module hierarchy as a menu.

**IW-TRAD-003:** A capability advertised by Explore with Cozy must have an authorized Ask-native entry and an honest incomplete or unavailable state. A traditional-page link is not a substitute for missing Ask coverage.

**IW-TRAD-004:** Direct traditional routes may remain reachable during retirement for transition compatibility, bookmarks, administrative needs, or capability gaps, but their existence does not make them part of Ask navigation.

**IW-TRAD-005:** Transition work must not fork canonical records, permissions, validation, confirmation, audit, or business behavior between Ask and any surface that remains temporarily reachable.

**IW-TRAD-006:** Removal sequencing, redirects, and external deep-link compatibility are governed by the retirement plan. They must not block delivery of Ask-native discovery, and Ask telemetry must distinguish an Ask-native starter from a transition-route visit.

## 19. Domain rollout requirements

Before a domain is classified as inline-complete, its coverage matrix must record:

| Field | Required evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Operations | Every user-visible operation and its interaction class |
| Canonical owner | Service/model responsible for each read and write |
| Inline views | Result, entity, collection, workflow, and proposal components required |
| Targeting | Property/entity/workflow identity and ambiguity behavior |
| Inputs | Schemas, validation, current values, and sensitivity |
| Writes | Role floor, confirmation, idempotency, freshness, and receipt |
| Reconciliation | Source and dependent results affected |
| Ask-native entry | Supported topic, starter, result, workflow, and honest incomplete boundary |
| Transition compatibility | Any temporarily retained route, bookmark, or external deep-link behavior |
| Exceptions | External/admin/bulk boundary and rationale |
| Responsive proof | Desktop, narrow viewport, keyboard, and assistive technology |
| Verification | Static, unit, component, database, browser, and live-provider levels actually executed |

No domain is inline-complete merely because its backend handler exists, it returns a block, or it provides an `href`.

Capability-card coverage is registry-derived, not a hand-maintained list of screenshot examples. For each capability and each Ask entry surface, record its eligibility/readiness policy, current card action, canonical owner, normal homeowner outcome and constituent operations, typed inline launch target, required context, secondary traditional destination, permitted exception or incomplete boundary, and desktop/mobile verification. A registry addition or newly rendered launch surface without this classification fails the drift check.

## 20. Maintenance flagship requirements

Maintenance is the first end-to-end reference for this FRD.

**IW-MAINT-001:** Query, filter, sort, page/load more, refresh, and clear filters remain within the same stable Ask result.

**IW-MAINT-002:** Clicking a task title or row opens inline task detail. It does not navigate to `/dashboard/maintenance`.

**IW-MAINT-003:** Inline detail shows available canonical title, description, status, priority, due/completion dates, recurrence, scope, cost, source, freshness, evidence, and permitted actions without inventing missing values.

**IW-MAINT-004:** Complete, reschedule, explain, create, and supported correction flows execute inline through exact task identity, canonical services, confirmation, receipt, and reconciliation.

**IW-MAINT-005:** “View all” expands into an inline collection browser with full-scope server semantics. Backend truncation is disclosed and recoverable.

**IW-MAINT-006:** “Create maintenance task” uses the existing inline capture and confirmation operation. Opening Maintenance Setup is an optional secondary choice, not the primary path.

**IW-MAINT-007:** “Open Maintenance” remains available as a secondary explicit action and preserves property, representable filters, selected task, session/execution/result, and return position.

**IW-MAINT-008:** Changes made in either surface reconcile from canonical data. A failed refresh never turns a successful write into a failed or repeatable mutation.

**IW-MAINT-009:** Viewer, stale, deleted, access-lost, partial, empty, and unavailable states remain distinct and safe.

## 21. Cross-domain rollout order

The rollout should prove reusable interaction patterns before expanding surface area:

1. **Foundation and maintenance flagship:** action contract, workspace stack, component registry, responsive shell, and complete maintenance journey.
2. **Property records:** property summary, rooms, inventory, documents, warranties, and timeline/history.
3. **Operational work:** Home Actions, seasonal work, deadlines, and Buyer Plan tasks/lifecycle.
4. **Financial and decision experiences:** refinance, ownership costs, savings/benefits, reserves, tax readiness, sell/hold/rent, and scenario comparison.
5. **Projects and assets:** repair/replace, HVAC, quote comparison, seller preparation, renovations, permits, and inspections.
6. **Protection:** coverage, incidents, claims, evidence, transitions, and unknown-outcome recovery.
7. **Attention and proactive work:** attention aggregation, dismissal/reminder policies, monitors, and proactive continuations.
8. **Exception refinement:** external providers, booking/payment/account-linking, and approved admin/bulk boundaries.

**Implemented clarification (October 2, 2026).** A focused Home Action that withholds a material action until an exact inventory record is reviewed now routes its **Review [item] details** control through `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` with the canonical `INVENTORY_ITEM` id; it no longer reopens the desktop Inventory drawer or depends on matching the homeowner-facing label. Provider search and booking remain an external-boundary exception: a provider-search destination is presented as the quiet secondary **Continue to provider search** handoff, not as a primary **Schedule Service** action that could imply scheduling happens inside Ask. The shared Ask link wrapper preserves the Ask return context, while the canonical provider-search URL preserves the property, service category, work category, item and originating Home Action key.

Phases may overlap only when shared contracts do not conflict. Existing functionality remains available throughout; no big-bang cutover is required.

## 22. Accessibility requirements

**IW-A11Y-001:** Every control has an accessible name that includes the target where repeated controls would otherwise be ambiguous.

**IW-A11Y-002:** Workspace open/close, nested back, confirmation completion, reconciliation, error, and access redaction move focus to a logical location and announce the material change.

**IW-A11Y-003:** New proactive content does not steal focus.

**IW-A11Y-004:** Comparisons and transformed tables preserve header/value relationships, units, source, and status.

**IW-A11Y-005:** All actions and fields are usable with keyboard only. Focus is trapped only for genuinely modal interactions.

**IW-A11Y-006:** Color, iconography, and visual position are never the sole carriers of priority, status, selection, or validation meaning.

**IW-A11Y-007:** Reduced-motion and screen-reader behavior follows existing application conventions and applicable standards.

## 23. Performance and perceived responsiveness

**IW-PERF-001:** Opening already-returned detail should not require refetch solely to render known data; freshness-sensitive actions still revalidate.

**IW-PERF-002:** Preserve prior content during refresh and show scoped progress without blocking unrelated conversation controls.

**IW-PERF-003:** Long operations expose current status, cancellation only where safe, and resumable pending work where supported.

**IW-PERF-004:** Panel-to-page promotion and mobile nested navigation preserve session, draft, workspace, and scroll state.

**IW-PERF-005:** Establish measured baselines for first response, inline workspace open, refinement, proposal preparation, confirmation, and reconciliation before setting numeric pass thresholds. Do not invent thresholds without evidence.

## 24. Analytics and privacy

Instrumentation should reuse existing Ask execution, analytics, receipt, and domain-event mechanisms.

At minimum, measure:

- journey started and completed;
- typed message versus direct control;
- inline entity/collection/workflow opened;
- proposal prepared, edited, confirmed, canceled, expired, or conflicted;
- mutation outcome and reconciliation outcome;
- implicit navigation attempts, which are defects for inline-complete journeys;
- explicit traditional and external transitions;
- return and context restoration;
- clarification count, confirmation count, recoverable errors, and repeated context requests;
- lost property/entity/result/workflow context;
- desktop/mobile/narrow-width completion; and
- next action shown, invoked, suppressed, or absent; and
- discovery topics and starters visible, opened, selected, started, completed, and abandoned, and explorer search interactions, as bounded identifiers and bucketed counts under IW-SHELL-019 and kept apart from the next-action measures above.

Analytics must not include transcript text, document contents, credentials, sensitive proposal values, or unrestricted identifiers beyond existing approved conventions.

Usage analytics inform future product decisions but do not authorize removal of traditional navigation under this FRD.

## 25. Interaction quality harness

Maintain golden journeys for at least:

- maintenance query through completed/rescheduled task;
- property/inventory record inspection and correction;
- conversational record capture;
- refinance/scenario decision;
- sell/hold/rent continuation;
- Buyer Plan task action;
- claim or incident transition;
- read-only attention insight; and
- optional traditional-page round trip.

For each journey, review:

- turns and controls to outcome;
- implicit navigation count;
- information hierarchy and progressive disclosure;
- conversation rhythm and workspace transitions;
- repeated or lost context;
- mutation feedback and receipt clarity;
- empty, partial, stale, access-lost, failure, and unknown-outcome states;
- desktop, narrow viewport, keyboard-only, and assistive-technology behavior; and
- whether a traditional/external transition was truly optional or an approved exception.

A functionally correct journey fails quality review when it introduces unnecessary messages, forms, pages, confirmations, repeated context, or module rediscovery. Required domain confirmation is never classified as unnecessary merely to reduce steps.

## 26. Acceptance scenarios

| ID | Given / when | Required observable outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IW-A01 | Click a maintenance task title in Ask | Inline detail opens; route remains Ask; source result and selection remain available |
| IW-A02 | Choose “Open Maintenance” explicitly | Maintenance page opens with supported context and a safe return to the exact Ask result |
| IW-A03 | Open and close inline detail | Return to the same filter, page, selection, scroll, and conversation state |
| IW-A04 | Promote the floating panel to full Ask workspace | Session, draft, result, workspace, and focus context survive |
| IW-A05 | Perform the same supported journey on mobile | Same outcome is reachable through mobile-appropriate layout without domain-page navigation |
| IW-A06 | Filter or paginate a collection | Same result workspace updates from full-scope server data without duplicate live surfaces |
| IW-A07 | Create a maintenance task | Inputs, review, confirmation, canonical write, receipt, and list reconciliation occur inline |
| IW-A08 | Complete or reschedule a task | Exact target, current version, confirmation, one domain effect, receipt, and authoritative reconciliation |
| IW-A09 | Edit a proposal | Old consent/version is invalid; corrected input is retained and requires renewed confirmation |
| IW-A10 | Another session changes the target | Stale write is blocked; current state is shown; no substitute target is chosen |
| IW-A11 | Write succeeds but refresh fails | Success receipt remains; read retry is offered; mutation is not repeated |
| IW-A12 | Access is revoked while detail is open | Inaccessible data is redacted and actions removed without leaking prior usable controls |
| IW-A13 | A user opens a traditional page directly | Existing direct workflow remains usable without entering Ask |
| IW-A14 | A traditional page changes a record and returns | Ask restores context and revalidates/reconciles affected results |
| IW-A15 | An external provider flow is required | Boundary and destination are disclosed; saved state and return/resume behavior are clear |
| IW-A16 | A raw/unclassified navigation action is added | Static/contract validation fails before the control can be treated as delivered |
| IW-A17 | The network fails after structured input | Entered values remain available and supported retry does not duplicate a write |
| IW-A18 | Keyboard user completes the last visible action | Outcome is announced and focus moves to the receipt or next logical control |
| IW-A19 | Property changes during an in-flight request | Old response cannot appear under or mutate the new property context |
| IW-A20 | No useful next action exists | Journey ends cleanly without a forced link or suggestion |
| IW-A21 | A desktop-only dense comparison is opened on mobile | Labeled responsive representation preserves meaning and supported actions |
| IW-A22 | User voluntarily prefers traditional navigation | Existing sidebar/routes remain available and no preference is punished or blocked |
| IW-A23 | Ask returns three meaningfully comparable options | A bounded comparison-card view highlights declared differences and keeps each option actionable inline |
| IW-A24 | Ask returns many homogeneous records with sortable attributes | A table or compact list is selected instead of oversized cards; total/partial scope remains clear |
| IW-A25 | Viewport changes or the user switches Cards/Table/List | Identity, values, actions, selection, filters, source, freshness, and scroll context are preserved without a new Ask execution |
| IW-A26 | A comparison strip overflows horizontally | Visible controls, keyboard access, item position/count, and a non-carousel path make every option reachable |
| IW-A27 | Preferred presentation type is unavailable or incompatible | A safe registered fallback renders meaningful content and still-valid actions without exposing raw payloads |
| IW-A28 | A “Recommended,” “Lowest cost,” or similar badge appears | The label follows declared comparison policy, has an explainable basis, and is not conveyed by color or placement alone |
| IW-A29 | Open an existing conversation on desktop | Conversation rail remains available, active conversation is identified, and the composer/result state restores without returning to the landing page |
| IW-A30 | Search conversation history across authorized homes | Results match permitted indexed content, show property identity, and reveal nothing from inaccessible properties |
| IW-A31 | Open a conversation belonging to another authorized property | Original property context is clearly restored; the conversation is not retargeted to the previously selected property |
| IW-A32 | Refresh, deep-link to, or use browser back/forward between Ask sessions | The URL-addressed authorized session and supported state restore consistently without relying only on session storage |
| IW-A33 | Start a new conversation from the rail | Prior conversation remains intact, a new session opens, and focus moves to an empty composer |
| IW-A34 | Rename a conversation and later receive another response | User title remains unchanged and is reflected in the rail/search results |
| IW-A35 | Pin, unpin, archive, and restore a conversation | Navigation grouping changes correctly without changing canonical home data, authorization, or result freshness |
| IW-A36 | Delete a conversation | Target and consequences are confirmed; the conversation is removed while canonical artifacts created through it remain intact |
| IW-A37 | Open a pending conversation from the **Needs you** history group | Exact pending workflow and required next input restore; no new mutation or duplicate execution occurs |
| IW-A38 | Switch conversations while the prior request is in flight | Late response cannot enter the newly active conversation; outgoing draft/state are preserved according to policy |
| IW-A39 | Open conversation history on mobile | Accessible drawer/nested view exposes equivalent history actions and returns focus/context correctly on select or close |
| IW-A40 | Open sources/evidence on narrow and wide viewports | Wide view uses the contextual panel when space permits; narrow view uses an in-Ask sheet/view; claim mapping and conversation context remain intact |
| IW-A41 | Select any ready capability card from discovery, related tools, or next actions | Its declared inline operation/workflow opens in the same Ask session and selected home; `/dashboard/ask` remains the route, source result and draft remain available, and no full-page navigation occurs unless the homeowner selects a separately labeled traditional action |
| IW-A42 | Select Home Capital Timeline or Home Event Radar as reference cases | Capital Timeline opens the canonical timeline journey rather than only a reserve-plan summary; Radar opens its canonical feed/detail journey rather than only an intelligence-envelope answer. The required in-tool controls and normal outcome work inline or the card truthfully discloses its incomplete boundary |
| IW-A43 | A capability is unavailable, needs context, or is not yet inline-complete | No misleading primary inline claim or unsupported control appears; required context is collected inline where supported, while any permitted handoff identifies the destination, reason, saved state, and return path |
| IW-A44 | A registered capability or Ask recommendation surface is added or changed | Registry-to-coverage validation identifies any missing classification; desktop/mobile, keyboard, access-loss, stale-data, and optional traditional round-trip checks cover the resulting card and its supported inline journey |

**Implementation note — October 2, 2026.** The maintenance-task workflow keeps its update receipt and create capture in Ask: the receipt ends without a redundant “Open task” desktop handoff, and the multi-field create capture uses a compact responsive grid while preserving the same validation, confirmation, and canonical write. The coverage-gap summary likewise ends or continues through inline correction instead of presenting a redundant desktop CTA.

## 27. Delivery phases

**IW-CONV-017 — The composer leads; context says what is behind a number (FRD v1.113, September 26, 2026).** On the calm landing the composer is the focus (three rows tall, larger type, a teal edge and soft shadow). Below it: at most two "needs you" lines, each a count plus the top item behind it (for example "1 to plan soon — Schedule HVAC service", or the newest important change's summary), then at most three starter questions and "More ideas". A bare count chip with no context is not allowed. Unfinished work from an earlier session is one quiet line ("Unfinished · <question> — Dismiss / Continue"), not a titled card. Dismiss is idempotent from this snapshot UI: if retention or another client removed the execution after the list loaded, `ASK_EXECUTION_NOT_FOUND` removes the stale line locally and refreshes history; other cancellation failures keep the line and surface the error.

**IW-CONV-017A — Capital replacement actions remain readable and in context (October 1, 2026).** A focused Home Action produced from a Capital Timeline replacement window routes its exact inventory item into `REPLACEMENT_GUIDANCE`; its Plan CTA does not reopen the desktop timeline. Expanded planning details use a semantic table of category, field and value sourced from the action's structured fact groups. The inline funding-plan allocation table is already the Reserve Fund read, so it does not repeat an "Open Reserve Fund" footer link immediately beneath itself; the summary-level action may remain as the jump to that inline section.

**IW-CONV-018 — Capture is asked, not laid out (v1.113; limit restored to three in v1.114).** Every capture whose fields are all scalar (choice, yes/no, number, short text, time), standard sensitivity and workflow input, with at most three fields, is asked one question at a time. v1.113 raised the limit to seven so maintenance-task creation was a conversation; that was reverted on September 26, 2026 because seven sequential questions is a slower form, not a conversation. Longer groups stay one form until grouped/adaptive capture exists (related fields collected together, later questions shown only when earlier answers require them; ACUI-007 in `ASK_COZY_CONVERSATIONAL_UI_GAP_AUDIT.md`). Captures with date-precision or relational inputs still use the form.

**IW-CONV-019 — The launch opens with the home's state, and every entry can say why (v1.115, September 26, 2026; ACUI-001 and ACUI-002 of `ASK_COZY_CONVERSATIONAL_UI_GAP_AUDIT.md`).** On the calm landing, the selected home's name sits above a headline built from the same Concierge Home payload as the entries (`buildConciergeStateStrip().opening`): "N things need your attention now", "Nothing urgent. N to plan soon", "N important changes to review", or "Nothing needs your attention right now" only when no source is unavailable. When the state is unknown or a source is unavailable and nothing else can be said, the generic "How can I help with your home?" stays. The composer placeholder names the home. Each of the two entries has a collapsed "Why this appeared" control (`aria-expanded`) listing up to four plain sentences derived only from governed fields: ranking priority, `deadlineAt`, mapped `comparativeReasonCodes` (tie-break codes are not shown), `confidenceLabel`, change materiality and dates, and decision lifecycle and update date. If no sentence can be derived the control is not shown; the browser never invents a reason. No backend contract changed. Opening or closing it does not touch the composer or the launch.

**IW-CONV-020 — The history rail steps back on a fresh landing (v1.116, September 26, 2026; ACUI-006).** In the calm shell on desktop, the conversation history rail is a narrow strip with a labeled "History" control while the landing is fresh, so the composer and unfinished work lead. With a conversation active it is open. "History" opens it; "Hide history" collapses it (and clears any search text). An explicit choice is remembered in the browser (`askHistoryRail`) and wins over the default; a search in progress always keeps the rail open. After a toggle, focus moves to the counterpart control (`aria-expanded` reflects the state). Outside the calm shell, and on mobile (the existing sheet), nothing changes. Auto-collapsing on "New conversation" and a recent-artifacts row were considered and not built.

**IW-CONV-021 — Review, confirm and receipt read as stages (v1.117, September 26, 2026; ACUI-005).** In the calm shell: the confirmation card opens with the plain line "Review · nothing is saved until you confirm" (the consent box and confirm button are the Confirm step; no stepper). A completed action renders as a plain receipt: a "Receipt" label, the past-tense title, what changed, and one primary continuation. The producer's first declared action is the primary one and any others stay quiet; when the receipt declares none and the turn has an output artifact with a link (for example a created maintenance task), that record link is the continuation, so the created record is never reachable only through the context panel. A pending, cancelled or expired action is never labelled "Receipt" or given a success mark; it shows its own plain status. An action whose outcome is unknown shows "Outcome not yet known" with "Check status" only, which reconciles and never applies the action twice. A completion time is not shown because the receipt block carries no governed time field; producers that have one already list it in the details (for example "Completed"). The non-calm shell is unchanged.

**IW-CONV-022 — The trust line sits under the answer, and one step is dominant (v1.118, September 26, 2026; ACUI-003).** In the calm shell, sources and context are one plain line directly under the core answer and above any capture, review or handoff card (it moved out of the feedback row): "Based on N sources, latest Sep 18, 2026 · N assumptions · N limitations", built only from the response's own evidence, assumption and limitation blocks; a source with no readable date is skipped, and with no sources it reads "Response context". It opens the same panel as before. In a Maintenance answer, the first workflow action the answer declares (for example "Create a task") is the one filled button; page links such as "View all in Maintenance" stay quiet text; with no workflow action nothing is filled. Block order for Maintenance still comes from its handler (summary, list, then evidence); no client-side reordering was added because the handler already emits the required order. This choice of primary step is a product default and can be changed by declaring a different first workflow action in the handler.

**IW-CONV-023 — The composer offers a photo or document only for one known record (v1.119, September 26, 2026; ACUI-004).** A typed affordance model (`features/ask/composerAffordances.ts`) describes extra composer inputs: capability, a label that names the purpose ("Add a photo or document", "Add the warranty document"), accepted types and size, the resolved record, and the effect. The only affordance today is evidence: it appears beside the message box only when the latest settled result (ANSWERED or COMPLETED, no open clarification, capture or confirmation) holds exactly one distinct record of a type evidence can attach to (home event, inventory item, warranty) with actions declared for this requester, the same rule the record lists use. A result listing several records offers none; no target is guessed. The button reuses `AttachEvidenceControl` (same type, size, upload and property checks) and, after upload, sends the same `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` request as the inline control, so the file still goes through the review-and-confirm step and nothing is attached by uploading. Maintenance tasks are not offered: the evidence operation does not support them, and adding that support is a backend decision. Voice input is unchanged.

**IW-CONV-024 — Maintenance conversational slice: integrated acceptance (v1.120, September 26, 2026; ACUI-008).** `e2e/ask/maintenanceJourney.spec.ts` (9 Playwright scenarios, calm shell, fixture-backed) protects the slice as one journey: (1) the launch names the home, leads with its state, and each entry's "Why this appeared" opens in place without disturbing typed text; (2) the maintenance answer, its filter refinement (original question kept, earlier version stubbed, one live primary step), the trust line under the answer, and the one dominant step; (3) review names its stage, consent gates Confirm, an unknown outcome shows only "Check status" (no second execution invitation) and then the receipt; (4) the composer evidence affordance for each supported type (inventory item, home event, warranty), its purpose-named label, the exact request sent, and that upload attaches nothing; (5) unfinished work first, history collapsed by default, expanded on request, remembered across reload, and Dismiss; (6) the same at 390px with no sideways scroll and the existing history sheet; (7) keyboard operation and `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` of the explanation, the live status region, accessible names, no running animation once settled under reduced motion, and a failed request keeping the typed question with Try again. Not covered here, and covered elsewhere or not at all: editing a proposed value and the consent reset (existing corrections e2e), and any live-backend behavior.

**IW-CONV-025 — Inventory adopts the calm answer (I-1, v1.121, September 26, 2026).** An inventory list (block `inventory-results`) is now a calm adopter. The producer (`inventoryCalmCopy` in `inventory.handler.ts`) declares the summary's headline, an optional support line and answer chips from counts only: "12 items recorded, 5 with missing details.", "N records are missing details." for an incomplete-records question, "N items have a recorded end-of-life date in the next three years." for a lifecycle question. "None missing the details Ask checks" is said only when nothing in the result is missing and never claims completeness; a truncated result says how many are shown. A single-item result keeps its own title and body. "Add an item" (when the requester can add) is the one dominant step; "Open home inventory" moves onto the list as quiet text (id `open-inventory-list`, hidden outside the calm answer so the previous shell still shows one link). The disambiguation list ("Which inventory item do you mean?") is a question, not an adopter. Filtering and refinement are I-2.

**Implemented clarification (October 3, 2026).** When a visible inventory result contains incomplete records, contributor-and-up users receive the follow-up **Add or update missing details**. It resolves deterministically to the incomplete inventory collection, where canonical item identity is selected before any write. Item detail offers only missing fields supported by `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` (brand, model, serial number and purchase date) and keeps the existing review-and-confirm write path. A missing document uses the existing evidence attachment control. Coverage evidence remains a disclosed Home Inventory boundary; the suggestion is not shown to viewers and is not emitted when no matching record is incomplete.

**IW-CONV-026 — Inventory filters are a governed refinement (I-2, v1.122, September 26, 2026).** An inventory collection result (a list of two or more records that is not a single-item lookup, history or disambiguation) now carries a `viewState` (stable `resultId`, `revision`, `statusFilter` in ALL/INCOMPLETE/LIFECYCLE, and the category key in `domainScopePhrase`) and declares filter chips in two independent groups: status (All items, Missing details, Near end of life) and category (All categories, HVAC, Appliances, Roof and exterior), plus "Clear filters" whenever any filter is applied. A chip is a fresh authoritative backend query over the whole inventory (never a client filter of a truncated result); it keeps the result identity, increments the revision, replaces only the dimension it names, and the earlier result collapses to the "Earlier version" stub with its question kept. A chip's wording is a complete specification on its own and begins with a phrase the follow-up resolver already treats as a filter continuation (`isFilterContinuationMessage`, asserted for every chip). A refined result stays a list even with one row, so the way back never disappears, and a filter that matches nothing still continues the result with an empty list, "No items match these filters." and every chip. The list title follows the words of the current question so the answer-trust contract keeps matching; the headline states the real scope (for example "1 HVAC record is missing details."). The prior view is continued only when its source execution was an inventory lookup, never another domain's. No new operation, route or storage: `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` was already filter-continuable. The calm answer shows the two groups and the clear action as quiet text; the previous shell keeps its single row of chips.

**IW-CONV-027 — Inventory certified as the second calm domain (I-3, v1.123, September 26, 2026).** `e2e/ask/inventoryJourney.spec.ts` (9 Playwright scenarios, calm shell, fixture-backed, desktop and 390px) protects the Inventory answer as one journey: the direct answer with counted headline and chips, one dominant step ("Add an item") and one quiet "Open home inventory" link, and the trust line under the answer; governed filters (each chip continues the source execution by id, replaces one dimension, keeps the original question, collapses the earlier answer to its stub, and "Clear filters" resets both); inline item detail without navigation and a correction through edit, consent reset, confirmation and receipt; the composer evidence affordance only for a single-record result, with the exact request sent and nothing attached by upload; the phone layout (no sideways scroll, chips at least the WCAG 2.2 AA 24px target, primary step in view); keyboard operation, `aria-pressed`, named filter groups, the live status region and reduced motion; and recovery (a failed first request keeps the typed question and Try again works; a failed refinement leaves the current answer on screen and the same chip works on retry). Not covered: creating an item (a seven-field form by the three-field rule, exercised by the existing add-item e2e), and any live-backend behavior. The Warranties domain is next and reuses this interaction system.

**IW-CONV-027A — Inventory table and correction-follow-up continuity (October 3, 2026).** Multi-item `inventory-results` render as a responsive semantic table with Item, Room/category, Brand/model, and Condition columns; narrow screens retain table semantics while presenting each row as labeled stacked fields. Item names remain buttons that open the canonical inline detail, so tabular density does not reintroduce Desktop UI navigation. Generated correction suggestions must name both the inventory domain and the exact item (for example, `Correct the install date of inventory item "Microwave"`). This wording is part of the deterministic `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` grammar and lets the handler resolve exactly one canonical item; the UI must not emit the earlier unsupported `Correct the install date of Microwave` wording.

**IW-CONV-028 — Warranties: a deterministic WARRANTY_LOOKUP answer (W-1, v1.124, September 26, 2026).** Warranty questions had no typed answer: "Show my warranties" and "What does my HVAC warranty cover?" went to GROUNDED_GUIDANCE, "Which warranties expire soon?" to COVERAGE_GAPS. `WARRANTY_LOOKUP` (adapter `warranty.lookup`, skill `warranties`, VIEWER floor, `RECORD_QUERY`, deterministic) now owns them. It reads the same property-scoped rows as `GET /properties/:propertyId/warranties` (any household member, including a viewer) and derives each warranty's status exactly as the Warranties page does: the governed `evaluateCoverageRecord` for a future start or dates that need review, then calendar days to expiry with the page's own **60-day** window ("Expires within 60 days"; COVERAGE_GAPS keeps its separate 90-day risk-review horizon, and the bare word "soon" is never shown without the number). The calm summary declares exact counts (active, expiring within 60 days, expired, dates that need review, starting later), rows read expiring first, then dates that need review, and the recorded coverage text is shown exactly as recorded (or "No coverage details recorded."), never interpreted. Inline detail, owner-authorised corrections (only the requester's own warranty declares them) and evidence attach reuse the existing renderer and operations. A quiet "Open Warranties" link and an INFO boundary say: "This reports the warranty information recorded in your Home Record. It does not determine whether a repair is covered or file a claim." No claim action is exposed; a policy number and cost are never read; an empty record and an unrecorded item are said to be unrecorded, never uncovered. Routing: `WARRANTY_LOOKUP` owns show/list, still-active, expiring or expired, "what does my X warranty cover", "do I have a warranty for X", "how long is the warranty" and "under warranty"; COVERAGE_GAPS keeps missing protection and gaps, CLAIM_FILE and CLAIM_TRANSITION keep claims, WARRANTY_CORRECT and CAPTURE_WARRANTY_CONFIRM keep corrections and adding, and HOME_DEADLINE_MONITOR keeps reminders. Registered in the same commit as its audience policy, trust boundary and action whitelist, coverage-matrix entry, semantic records, calibration fixture 095, skill package and adapter. W-2 (viewState filters) and W-3 (journey) follow.

**IW-CONV-029 — Warranty filters are a governed refinement (W-2, v1.125, September 26, 2026).** A warranty collection result (any question that is not about one named item) now carries a `viewState` (stable `resultId`, `revision`, `statusFilter` in ALL/ACTIVE/EXPIRING/EXPIRED/NEEDS_REVIEW, the category key in `domainScopePhrase`) and declares filter chips in two independent groups: status (All, Active, Expires within 60 days, Expired, and "Dates need review" only when some warranty has unreadable dates) and category (All categories plus only the categories this home actually has), and "Clear filters" whenever a filter is applied. A chip is a fresh authoritative query over every recorded warranty (never a client filter of a truncated result); it keeps the result identity, increments the revision, replaces only the dimension it names, and the earlier result collapses to the "Earlier version" stub with its question kept. Chip wording is a complete specification on its own and begins with a phrase the follow-up resolver treats as a filter continuation (asserted for every chip, with the resolver's own output). A refined result stays a list even at one row, and a filter that matches nothing still continues the result with every chip and "No warranties match these filters." The prior view is continued only when its source was a warranty lookup, and only a filter phrase refines: an ordinary question that names a category ("Tell me about my roof warranty") is answered on its own even when a source result exists. That guard (`containsFilterContinuation`) was added to Inventory's refinement too, which had the same gap. `WARRANTY_LOOKUP` joins `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` in the filter-continuable operations; no new route or storage.
