**IW-CONV-030 — Warranties certified as the third calm domain (W-3, v1.126, September 26, 2026).** `e2e/ask/warrantyJourney.spec.ts` (14 Playwright scenarios, calm shell, fixture-backed, desktop and 390px) protects the Warranties answer as one journey: a list question with exact counts (never the bare word "soon"), one dominant step ("Add a warranty"), one quiet Warranties link that carries Ask's return path, recorded coverage text as written and a missing one said to be missing, no coverage decision anywhere in the answer, the recorded-information boundary, rows needing attention marked, and a trust line under the answer; governed filters (each chip continues the source execution by id, replaces one dimension, keeps the original question, collapses the earlier answer to its stub, an empty match keeps the way back, and "Clear filters" resets both); unreadable dates said so and reachable by their own filter; an exact-warranty question with a purpose-named evidence attach whose file still goes through review; inline detail without navigation and an owner's correction through edit, consent reset, confirmation and receipt; a viewer with no write, correction or attach control anywhere; a warranty removed after the answer; access loss redacting the whole result; an empty record; the phone layout (no sideways scroll, chips at least the WCAG 2.2 AA 24px target); keyboard operation, `aria-pressed`, named filter groups, the live status region and reduced motion; and recovery from a failed first request and a failed refinement. The three journey specs' reduced-motion checks now poll until finite transitions settle instead of sampling once (one failed under full-suite load). Not covered: adding a warranty (an existing e2e covers that flow), and any live-backend behavior. The claims journey is separately governed and not exposed here.

**IW-CONV-031 — Claims: the incident and claim status answer adopts the calm anatomy (C-1, v1.127, September 26, 2026).** The incident and claim status read (`INCIDENT_CLAIM_STATUS`, VIEWER floor, block `incident-claim-list`) is now a calm adopter. The producer (`claimsCalmCopy` in `claims.handler.ts`) declares a one-sentence headline and answer chips from counts only ("1 active incident and 1 open claim."; "No open claims."), names closed and resolved records once as "also on file", and says when the page is full. It never says anything about approval, coverage or eligibility, and a BOUNDARY block (allowed for this operation from now on, with its trust whitelist entry) states: "This shows the incident and claim records in your Home Record. It does not decide whether a claim will be approved or covered. Filing a claim or changing its status happens only when you ask and confirm." The calm answer has **no filled step**: nothing here starts a claim, and a related answer (for example a warranty) never offers one. The two record pages ride on the list as quiet text links (`open-incidents-list`, `open-claims-list`), drawn by the calm answer only, because the calm summary carries no actions and the previous shell keeps its summary links. Claim rows keep exactly the status-transition actions they declared (contributor and up; a viewer gets read-only rows), still filtered by the live claim status in the detail and still behind CLAIM_TRANSITION's confirmation, authorization and recovery contracts. Filing (`CLAIM_FILE`) and transitions are unchanged. C-2 (authoritative filters) and C-3 (journey) follow.

**IW-CONV-032 — Claim status filters are a governed refinement (C-2, v1.128, September 26, 2026).** The incident and claim status read now carries a `viewState` (stable `resultId`, `revision`, the state in `statusFilter` as ALL/OPEN/CLOSED, the scope in `domainScopePhrase` as BOTH/CLAIMS/INCIDENTS) and declares filter chips in two independent groups: scope (Incidents and claims, Claims, Incidents) and state (Open and closed, Open, Closed), plus "Clear filters" whenever one is applied. The read itself was made authoritative: it previously took the 20 most recent rows of each kind and bucketed them in memory, so counts and buckets were wrong past 20. Every bucket the question asks about (active incidents, resolved incidents, open claims, closed claims) is now read with its own status filter and its own exact count, a bucket a filter excludes is never read, and a longer list says it is showing the most recent records. A chip keeps the result identity, increments the revision, replaces only the dimension it names, and the earlier result collapses to the "Earlier version" stub with its question kept; a filter that matches nothing still continues the result with every chip. Chip wording is a complete specification on its own and begins with a filter-continuation phrase (asserted for every chip, with the follow-up resolver's own output); a typed follow-up joined to the prior question refines too. The prior view is continued only when its source was a claim status read. Reading changes nothing: filing (`CLAIM_FILE`) and status changes (`CLAIM_TRANSITION`) are untouched, and no claim action is added to any answer.

**IW-CONV-033 — Claims certified as the fourth calm domain (C-3, v1.129, September 26, 2026).** `e2e/ask/claimsJourney.spec.ts` (16 Playwright scenarios, calm shell, fixture-backed, desktop and 390px) protects the incident and claim status answer as one journey, and it is deliberately read-only: the answer says what is open, offers no filled step and nothing that files or starts a claim, keeps the two record pages as quiet links, states that it does not decide approval or coverage, and carries a trust line under the answer; governed scope and state filters (each chip continues the source by id, replaces one dimension, keeps the question, and clears); an exact-claim question; a claim opening inline without navigating with its actions following the live status (a draft may start, submit or close, never approve or deny); a status change through review, consent and confirmation to a receipt with Cancel distinct from Confirm; an unknown outcome reconciled by a status check rather than a second execution invitation; a confirmation the requester is no longer authorised to make being refused by withdrawing the review (the existing access-loss contract) with nothing changed; a viewer with the same answer and detail and no status control; a claim removed after the answer; access loss redacting the whole result; an empty record; the phone layout; keyboard operation, `aria-pressed`, named filter groups, the live status region and reduced motion; and recovery from a failed first request and a failed refinement. The warranty journey now also asserts that a warranty answer offers no button or link that mentions a claim, so related domains never lend each other actions. Not covered here: filing a claim (its own existing capture, review and confirmation flow) and any live-backend behavior. Open finding, not changed: the claim detail formats its date fields in the browser's local zone, and `incidentAt` is a user-entered date stored at UTC midnight, so it can read a day early in the Americas (the warranty detail had the same defect and was fixed); whether each claim date is a calendar day or an instant is a product call.

**IW-CONV-034 — The landing leads with the composer (v1.130, September 26, 2026; design review of the production landing).** The calm landing used too much vertical space before the primary interaction and gave the headline more weight than the composer. It now reads, top to bottom: one summary line, at most two attention cards, the composer, and one row of suggestions. The stacked date, address and subhead are removed: the address is in the property selector and is named by the composer's placeholder ("Ask anything about <home>…"), so it is not repeated in the content (this supersedes the ACUI-001 address line above the headline). The summary is one line built from the same counts as the entries, "Nothing urgent · 4 to plan soon · 3 changes to review" (or "2 to do now · 1 change to review"; "Nothing urgent" only when no source is unavailable, "Nothing needs your attention right now" when everything is quiet, and the generic prompt when the state is unknown), at 26px on a phone and 32px semibold on desktop. The content area is up to 1140px wide and the cards and the composer share that full width. The composer is 120px tall with more internal padding (it was a compact field of at most 76px). The cards are quieter (lighter tints, less padding and height) and separated from the summary by more space instead of more copy. The suggestions never wrap: they stay on one row and scroll sideways when they do not fit, in a slightly smaller type. The prototype-parity e2e assertions now require the composer to be 112 to 128px, the same width as the cards, the summary to be one line above them, and the suggestions to share one row; the two prototype screenshots were regenerated for this layout.

**IW-CONV-035 — One content width across the calm Ask pages (v1.131, September 26, 2026; production review).** The landing was 1140px while the conversation thread, the docked composer, the follow-up row and the unfinished-work list were capped at `max-w-3xl` (768px), so the page changed width the moment a question was asked. In the calm shell all four now share the landing's `max-w-[1140px]`; the classic presentation keeps its own widths. Asserted by `calm.spec.ts` ("calm width"), which compares the landing, the thread article and the docked composer boxes. Full Ask e2e 200/200.

**IW-CONV-036 — Home Event Radar adopts the calm anatomy (R-1, v1.132, September 26, 2026).** The monitored-event feed (`home-event-radar-feed`) is a calm adopter. The producer declares one counted sentence from the feed's own exact total ("2 events are happening now.", "Nothing is happening now; 1 upcoming.", or the filtered count such as "2 upcoming weather events."), a supporting line naming the top-ranked event, and answer chips (high priority, happening now, upcoming, recently ended, saved). A lifecycle or priority breakdown is stated only when the page holds the whole result; a truncated page says "Showing the N most important of M" and shows no partial tally. Sections now keep the feed's own order (lifecycle, then priority) instead of alphabetical order, so the first item of the first section is the top-ranked event. The calm answer offers one dominant step, "Review: <top event>", which opens the event's canonical detail inline (no navigation); the timing, source and dismissed filters are quiet text groups; the page link and notification settings keep their existing actions. A complete-coverage feed ends with the `radar-feed-boundary` footnote (not an emergency alert service; does not confirm nothing else is happening); a degraded feed keeps its own coverage boundary instead. Save, dismiss, restore, mark done, feedback, task planning and notification settings are unchanged. Filters still re-send self-contained messages; governed refinement with view-state continuity is R-2. Tests: `radarCalmAnswer.test.js`, `calmRadarAnswer.test.tsx`; the five existing Radar e2e journeys pass.

**IW-CONV-037 — Radar filters are a governed refinement (R-2, v1.133, September 26, 2026).** The feed's filters use the same continuity model as Maintenance, Buyer Deadlines, Inventory, Warranties and Claims. Three independent dimensions ride in the result's view state: timing in `statusFilter` (`ALL` or a lifecycle), source in `domainScopePhrase` (`ALL` or a family), dismissed events in `dateScopePhrase` (`INCLUDE_DISMISSED` or null). A declared chip is a fresh authoritative read of the feed with the resulting filters, never the filtered subset of an earlier page, and replaces exactly one dimension: "Now show events at any time", "Only show events happening now / upcoming events / recently ended events", "Now show events from all sources", "Only show <family> events", "Now show events including dismissed ones", "Now show events without dismissed ones", and "Now show all events with no filters" (offered only while a filter is applied). Each begins with a phrase `FILTER_CONTINUATION_PATTERN` accepts; `HOME_EVENT_RADAR_FEED` joins `FILTER_CONTINUABLE_OPERATIONS`, so a chip sets `continuesExecutionId` and the earlier result collapses to an "Earlier version" stub. The prior view state is loaded by source `operationId` (another domain's state is never continued), an ordinary question is not a refinement, and paging keeps the filters the source result recorded (the cursor is bound to them). Source chips are offered only for sources that have events under the other filters (read authoritatively, one small read per source), plus the applied one, so narrowing never hides the way to another source. A filter that matches nothing still continues the result and keeps every chip. Tests: `radarRefinementRuntime.test.js` (11) and the frontend `calmRadarAnswer.test.tsx`. Writes are untouched.

**IW-CONV-038 — Home Event Radar certified as the fifth calm domain (R-3, v1.134, September 26, 2026).** One acceptance journey (`radarJourney.spec.ts`, 23 scenarios, desktop and phone) over a producer-shaped fixture (`radarJourneyFixtures.ts`): the counted answer with one filled step and quiet page links; inline event detail without navigation and with focus returned to the step; timing, source and dismissed-events refinement (each chip continues the source, replaces one dimension, keeps the question, clears); a filter that matches nothing; direct per-user writes (save, dismiss, restore) with no confirmation; confirmed writes (mark done, feedback, planning, notification settings) through review, consent and receipt, with an unknown outcome reconciled by a status check and a refused confirmation redacting that result; viewer permissions; a removed event; access loss; partial coverage; an empty record; phone layout, keyboard and reduced-motion accessibility; recovery of a failed first request and a failed refinement. Two product changes came out of it: inside an adopted calm answer the card deck's swipe-right action (Save) is quiet, so the Review step is the answer's one filled control, and closing the event detail returns focus to that step. Full Ask e2e 223/223. Known and left as is: the planning journey starts at its review step here (its capture form is covered by `ask.spec.ts`, v1.41); the card declares item actions for the state recorded when the answer was made, so after a Save the same card does not offer "Remove from saved" until the feed is asked again.

**IW-CONV-039 — Documents adopts the calm anatomy (D-1, v1.135, September 26, 2026).** The document lookup (`document-lookup-groups`) is a calm adopter. The producer declares one counted sentence ("12 documents on file."), a supporting line naming the most recent document and, when a type is truncated, that the page holds the full record, and answer chips for the recorded verification status ("3 not yet verified" for UNVERIFIED and PENDING, "1 rejected", "8 verified"), all from the recorded rows with no model and no document contents. A `document-lookup-boundary` footnote states that this is what is recorded (type, date added, verification status) and that Ask has not read or interpreted the documents. The answer has no filled step: uploading and verifying happen on the Documents page and Ask offers no action for them, so the page is a quiet text link (`open-documents-list`, drawn by the calm answer only). The previous shell is unchanged. Bug fixed on the way: `DOCUMENT_LOOKUP` declared no action ids, so the trust validator removed the "Open Documents" link from the answer and from its empty state; both are now declared. Refinement (by type and verification status) is D-2 and the journey D-3. Home Record (`PROPERTY_SUMMARY`) is a separate slice. Tests: `documentsCalmAnswer.test.js`, `calmDocumentAnswer.test.tsx`; full chunked Ask suite 1309/1310 (1 pre-existing skip), full Ask e2e 223/223.

**IW-CONV-040 — Ask reads the canonical document inventory (v1.136, September 26, 2026; Home Records replaces legacy Documents).** Decision: Home Records (`PropertyRecord`) is the canonical store for a home's documents; the legacy `Document` table is retired in slices, with no dual-write and no historical migration (there are no real users or production data). `propertyDocuments/propertyDocumentInventory.service.ts` is the one canonical read: Home Records authoritative, the legacy vault projected only when a caller asks for it by name (`includeLegacy`) and every legacy row marked `transitional`, removed with the legacy branch once no domain needs it. The two stores do not share a status vocabulary, so a row carries only what its own store records (a Home Record: needs review, expiry, lifecycle, sensitivity, visibility; a legacy document: verification status), never a fabricated mapping. `DOCUMENT_LOOKUP` reads this interface with the caller's role, so record-level visibility applies: an owner-only record is not returned to a contributor. The calm answer's chips state those own-store facts ("2 need review", "1 expired", "3 not yet verified"), a legacy count is disclosed ("8 are still in the older Documents vault"), kinds share one vocabulary and one section whichever store holds them, and the page link goes to Home Records. A Home Record row opens an inline, read-only detail read through the record route (`PropertyRecordAskDetail`; a record no longer visible reads as "no longer available", a property access denial redacts the result); a legacy row keeps the document detail. Remaining slices: Property Summary (S3), the Documents page, dashboard links, general uploads and upload-driven Timeline events (S4), the legacy consumers (S5+), then the legacy branch and model. Documents D-2 and D-3 build on this read. Tests: `propertyDocumentInventory.test.js`, `documentsCalmAnswer.test.js`, `propertyRecordAskDetail.test.tsx`.

**IW-CONV-041 — Property Summary reads the canonical document inventory (S3, v1.137, September 26, 2026).** The property record overview's `documents` section (used by the Property Summary answer and the property page) reads `listPropertyDocuments` with the caller's role, including the transitional legacy vault and legacy documents attached to an inventory item, instead of querying the legacy table itself. Each count states a fact the row's own store records, with no invented mapping: `verifiedCount` is the legacy verification status; `needsReviewCount` is Home Records awaiting review plus legacy documents that are not verified (unchanged for legacy-only data); every Home Record counts as linked. The section gains `homeRecordCount` and `legacyCount`, and `items` and `latest` carry the canonical row shape (`title`, `addedAt`, `source`, `transitional`, own-store status). In the Property Summary answer, each row opens the inline detail of its own store (Home Record through the record route, legacy through the document route), only a legacy row links to the older vault, and the page link is Home Records. Lesson recorded: frontend `tsc --noEmit` passed while `next build` failed on the renamed fields, because stale `.next/dev/types` syntax errors suppress semantic checking; a real build is the frontend check. Tests: `propertyRecordOverviewDocuments.test.js`, the updated Property Summary governance test.

**IW-CONV-042 — General documents move to Home Records; an upload is not a Timeline event (S4, v1.138, September 26, 2026).** The property Documents route now only redirects to Home Records (carrying `?action=upload` and `?backTo=`), Home Records opens its upload for `?action=upload`, the `/dashboard/documents` route map resolves there, and every explicit link that built a property-scoped legacy Documents URL now builds a Home Records URL (a guardrail test fails if one returns). The 962-line legacy Documents page client is removed. Ask no longer links a transitional legacy row to a page (the legacy workspace is retired; its inline detail still works) and its document fallback link is Home Records. The two legacy upload writers no longer create an "Uploaded document" Timeline event and the auto-generator's upload hook is removed: a Timeline moment comes from a reviewed fact, never from the act of uploading (guardrail tests). Domain uploaders (buyer plan centers, Negotiation Shield, quote comparison, warranty and insurance pages, project proof, onboarding, property photo) still write legacy storage until their reader and writer convert together (S5). Known consequence: buyer-plan links to the property `/documents` route now land on Home Records, which does not list those buyer uploads until that domain converts. Left in place, unused: `HomeEventsAutoGen.promoteDocumentEventToSemantic`. Pre-existing failures unrelated to this work (also failing on a clean HEAD): `coveragePolicyTermProvenance` and `phase8CleanupGuard` (backend), and `priority-route-visual-contract`, `propertyContextForm`, `cta/contracts` (frontend). Tests: `homeRecordsDocumentsEntryPoints.test.ts`, `uploadIsNotATimelineEvent.test.js`, the updated route-map test. Full chunked Ask 1311/1312 (1 pre-existing skip), Ask e2e 223/223, real `next build` passes.

**IW-CONV-043 — General document readers read the canonical inventory (S5a, v1.139, September 26, 2026).** The readers that only ask "how many documents does this home have" or "is there an inspection report" now read `countPropertyDocuments` and `latestPropertyDocumentOfKind` (Home Records active records plus the transitional legacy vault; a linked count uses a Home Records entity link or the legacy link columns): the daily pulse, the digital-twin quality signal, the home score (three sites) and home actions (total), and the capability recommendation's inspection-report signal. They are property-level signals, so they count every active Home Record regardless of the caller's role and never return a row; soft-deleted legacy rows are excluded. Home actions' "verified documents" count stays legacy-only on purpose: "verified" is a legacy status and what it means for a Home Record is a product decision (recorded in the plan, §17). The remaining consumers, grouped by what must convert together, and the decision that blocks the verified-document readers, are in the plan's §17. Tests: `propertyDocumentInventory.test.js` (counts and latest-of-kind), `generalDocumentReadersUseCanonicalInventory.test.js`. The same 8 backend tests around these services fail on a clean HEAD before and after this slice.

**IW-CONV-044 — Buyer workflows with plain document ids move to Home Records (S5b-1, v1.140, September 26, 2026).** Title and escrow, insurance, walkthrough and contract now store Home Record ids: their uploads go to Home Records (`uploadRecordForWorkflow`), their services resolve ids through the canonical inventory with the caller's role (`resolvePropertyDocuments`, `assertPropertyDocumentsExist`) and no longer touch the legacy table, and the insurance binder is an idempotent Home Records entity link written in the policy-binding transaction. The finding that reshaped the slice: about 25 models hold foreign keys to `documents`, which cannot hold a Home Record id, so foreign-key workflows (closing day, loan estimate, closing disclosure, reinspection proof, task completion, milestones) need a schema retarget the user applies with `prisma db push` (S5b-2, in the plan §17.1). The buyer evidence panel lists Home Records read-only and keeps Verify and Reject for legacy rows; the verified counts stay legacy-only pending the "verified" decision. Tests: `buyerWorkflowDocumentsAreHomeRecords.test.js`, the resolver tests in `propertyDocumentInventory.test.js`, and the updated title/escrow, insurance and walkthrough governance tests; buyer-related tests show no failures beyond the same ones on a clean HEAD.

**IW-CONV-045 — Price radar and onboarding evidence move to Home Records; the rest of S5c is foreign-key-bound (S5c, v1.141, September 27, 2026).** The Service Price Radar's linked document is resolved as a Home Record with the caller's role (its picker lists Home Records), and onboarding activation evidence is uploaded to Home Records and checked as a Home Record the caller added; the buyer entry-context document count reads the canonical inventory. Claims, hazard evidence, coverage-comparison sources and Ask's recent-document context hold or feed foreign keys to the legacy `documents` table (a Home Record id cannot be stored there), and Ask's evidence attach writes the legacy link columns the inventory and warranty screens read; each is pinned by a test with its reason, and the plan (§17.2) regroups the remaining work as one schema retarget of about 25 `Document` foreign keys to `PropertyRecord`, applied by the user with `prisma db push`, followed by the writers domain by domain. Tests: `s5cWorkflowDocumentsAreHomeRecords.test.js`; related backend tests show no failures beyond the same ones on a clean HEAD.

**IW-CONV-046 — Maintenance uses a compact priority stack (v1.142, September 27, 2026; production review).** In the calm Maintenance answer, the shelves/list switch and sparse full-width task rows are replaced by one responsive priority stack. Tasks remain grouped and counted, but render as compact three-column tiles on wide desktop screens, two columns on intermediate screens, and one column on narrow screens. This avoids stretching a small amount of task information across an under-filled row while preserving readable card widths as the workspace narrows. Each tile shows the urgency edge, title, timing and at most one secondary fact; its only visible control is **Review**. Review opens the existing canonical detail in the in-Ask sheet, where description, full record fields and permitted actions remain available. The first six tasks in a group render initially and additional tasks disclose in batches of six; authoritative server paging remains available and labelled. This is presentation-only: filter refinement, exact identity, live-record revalidation, authorization, confirmation, receipts, recovery, and the secondary Maintenance-page action are unchanged. The classic `?calm=0` presentation retains the existing shelves/list choice as the reversible fallback.

**IW-CONV-047 — Home Actions uses one ranked artifact and reviews inline (v1.143, September 27, 2026; production review).** The calm `HOME_ACTIONS` answer adopts the calm anatomy. Its headline, support line and count chips come from the same governed feed buckets. The `PRIORITY_LIST` is the answer's single artifact; the duplicate `home-actions-list` shelves are omitted only in calm mode, superseding v1.82's duplicate-display decision there, while the classic `?calm=0` presentation remains unchanged. Ranked items use the same responsive one/two/three-column card rhythm as other calm collections. The primary **Review in Ask** control sends a `CONVERSATION_CONTINUE` to `HOME_ACTIONS` with the exact `HOME_ACTION` id and source execution, causing the server to re-read the current governed feed and build focused guidance. It does not navigate to a traditional page. Existing page hrefs remain server-owned compatibility data and may appear later only as an explicit secondary traditional-workspace choice. Unavailable items remain non-actionable and show their safe next state. Ranking, authorization, freshness, evidence, usefulness feedback and the reversible classic view are unchanged.

**October 1 continuation correction:** Focused Home Action guidance's suggested follow-up, “What else needs my attention?”, routes deterministically back to `HOME_ACTIONS`; “else” no longer drops the prompt into generic clarification. Item-specific `/inventory/items/:itemId/coverage` Home Action CTAs route to `COVERAGE_GAPS` as an in-Ask workflow with the canonical `INVENTORY_ITEM` identity, and that handler scopes its authorized coverage records to the launched item. The traditional URL remains compatibility data, not the primary interaction.

**October 2 focused-action and suggestion correction:** A focused Sale Prep Home Action's **Review sale-prep item** control now continues to the canonical `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST` inside Ask instead of opening the Sale Case desktop tool. A focused Home Record fact review (including Water Heater age/condition) uses its already-rendered inline capture and omits the redundant Resolution Center CTA. The exact suggested prompts **Which gaps have the largest exposure?** and **What should I prioritize first?** are deterministic continuations: the former accepts only the typed `COVERAGE_GAPS` response envelope, while the latter routes directly to `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST`; neither is allowed to degrade into presentation-version fallback or generic answer-relevance clarification.

**October 2 duplicate-surface correction:** The `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST` result itself no longer displays **Open sale readiness checklist**, because the progress, items, details, and supported decisions are already present in the conversation. Focused Operational Work in `REPORTED_COMPLETE` state likewise omits its desktop review CTA: Ask displays the reported-completion state and recorded details inline, but does not imply that it can perform a verification workflow that is not registered. The existing inline **View details** disclosure remains available.

**October 2 seller-prep continuation-loop correction:** This supersedes the seller-prep treatment of **What should I prioritize first?** above. The completed `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST` answer already contains the domain-ranked next steps and live item decisions, so it emits neither **What should I prioritize first?** nor **Open seller prep** as follow-ups; both were paraphrases that reopened the same checklist. The generic question **What should I prioritize first?** now routes to the governed `HOME_ACTIONS` priority feed. Only sale-specific language (for example, **Check my sale readiness**) routes directly to `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST`.

**October 2 Property Record capture receipt correction:** `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE` is an inline-complete journey. After a confirmed answer, its receipt no longer offers **Open property record**, and the area-progress block no longer carries the same desktop escape hatch. When more supported questions remain, **Continue with [area]** starts the next exact `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE` step inside Ask; when none remain, the receipt and completeness summary are the closure. The canonical write, idempotency receipt, freshness reconciliation, and property-level completeness suggestion are unchanged.

### Phase 0 — Authority and coverage

- Adopt this FRD and add precedence notes to conflicting documents.
- Build an executable operation/action coverage matrix.
- Join the canonical capability registry to every `CAPABILITY_LIST` producer and other Ask launch surface; classify all registered capabilities and their constituent homeowner actions, including workflow-only/contextual entries.
- Classify every user-visible action as inline, optional traditional, approved external, admin/bulk exception, or not yet inline-complete.
- Add drift checks for unclassified operations, cards, surfaces, and controls.

**Exit:** no user-visible operation or action is unclassified; conflicts with domain requirements are recorded rather than guessed.

### Phase 1 — Shared workspace foundation

- Define backend and frontend action schemas for the §10 interaction types.
- Replace the `CAPABILITY_LIST` card's href-only primary contract with typed, source-bound inline launch metadata and an explicitly separate optional traditional destination; route all Ask card producers through that contract.
- Build the workspace stack and state restoration contract.
- Refactor presentation rendering toward a component registry.
- Implement the deterministic adaptive-presentation resolver and §11.6 selection matrix with view-preserving transformations.
- Build the §11.7–11.9 conversational shell, desktop history rail, mobile history drawer, addressable session navigation, and contextual information panel.
- Extend recent-session contracts from the current property-scoped five-session/seven-day baseline to authorized pagination, search, grouping, title, pin, archive, and per-session deletion semantics.
- Build shared entity, collection, form, comparison, timeline, document, receipt, and error primitives.
- Preserve existing confirmation and canonical write paths.

**Exit:** a synthetic reference flow demonstrates inline open, edit, confirm, reconcile, adaptive presentation, persistent history navigation, URL restoration, responsive layout, optional traditional transition, and state restoration.

Subsequent domain phases complete each registry-covered tool's canonical in-tool actions before calling it inline-complete. Begin with Home Capital Timeline and Home Event Radar as contrasting financial-planning and monitored-signal reference cases; then work through the remaining registry inventory by domain ownership and the §19 matrix, without treating a typed card launch alone as completion. Preserve direct traditional routes throughout.

### Phase 2 — Maintenance flagship

- Implement IW-MAINT-001–009.
- Remove implicit task-title navigation inside Ask while preserving explicit “Open Maintenance.”
- Add desktop, mobile, keyboard, stale, access-loss, failed-refresh, and round-trip coverage.

**Exit:** IW-A01–A12, IW-A14, and applicable existing maintenance scenarios pass at the verification level claimed.

### Phase 3 — Property records

- Deliver inline property summary, rooms, inventory, documents, warranties, timeline, and supported corrections.
- Reuse canonical record and evidence services.

**Exit:** ordinary inspect/capture/correct journeys complete inline; traditional record pages remain available.

**Status (updated September 22, 2026): NOT complete**, but entity write/correction coverage is now closed — every entity is either delivered inline (Inventory item, Home event including evidence attach, Warranty, Room name/type/floor, Property Summary's completeness capture) or recorded as a §9.4 exception (Document metadata edits, Household member role/removal, inline deletion, the PROPERTY_SUMMARY single-question path, Room sortOrder/profile/heroImage — PR-EXC-01/02/04/05/06); "adding a fact" is closed as already-covered by existing paths, not a new exception. Phase 3 remains NOT complete for reasons unrelated to entity coverage — browser acceptance, adaptive presentation, and the capability-card audit are all still open (see their own status-table rows). See the "Property records — remaining write/correction gap" row for the exact current wording.

### Phase 4 — Operational work and Buyer

- Deliver Home Actions, seasonal work, deadlines, and Buyer Plan collections/actions.
- Reuse exact identity, confirmation, and reconciliation grammar proven by maintenance.

**Exit:** task and lifecycle journeys complete inline without undeclared mutation or required page handoff.

### Phase 5 — Financial and persistent decisions

- Deliver inline refinance, costs, savings, reserves, taxes, scenarios, and sell/hold/rent continuation.
- Keep scenario assumptions distinct from canonical facts.

**Exit:** read, compare, assumption-edit, confirmation, continuation, and optional full-workspace journeys pass.

### Phase 6 — Projects and protection

- Deliver repair/replace, HVAC, quotes, renovation, permits, inspection, coverage, incidents, and claims.
- Prove evidence privacy, legal lifecycle, exact targeting, stale transitions, and unknown outcomes.

**Exit:** applicable project/protection scenarios and cross-domain gates pass.

### Phase 7 — Attention, proactive work, and exceptions

- Deliver read-only attention first, then controls whose duration/scope/persistence policies are explicit.
- Refine approved external and admin/bulk boundaries.

**Exit:** every exposed control has a typed outcome; exceptions are narrow, disclosed, and tested.

## 28. Cross-domain acceptance gates

A domain phase cannot be marked complete until all applicable gates pass:

| Gate | Required proof |
| --- | --- |
| Inline completion | Normal journey completes in Ask without implicit domain-page navigation |
| User choice | Existing direct/traditional navigation remains available |
| Identity | Exact property/result/workflow/entity targeting; ambiguity clarifies |
| Read integrity | Full-scope semantics or explicit partial/truncation disclosure |
| State continuity | Conversation, workspace, filter, selection, draft, scroll, and focus behave correctly |
| Freshness | Refresh and pre-write revalidation; out-of-order responses rejected |
| Proposal integrity | Typed validation, edit versioning, and consent reset |
| Write safety | Canonical service, authorization, idempotency, and truthful outcomes |
| Reconciliation | Source/dependent results update or become honestly stale |
| Traditional round trip | Context-preserving optional transition and safe return |
| Responsive parity | Desktop and mobile reach the same supported outcome |
| Presentation fitness | The chosen card/table/list/comparison/detail mode fits the task and preserves semantics through view changes |
| Conversation navigation | Active/recent/pinned/archived sessions, search, URL restoration, property scope, and pending states behave correctly |
| Shell continuity | Conversation canvas, composer, rails, contextual information, browser navigation, and responsive layers preserve state and focus |
| Accessibility | Keyboard, names, focus, announcements, and responsive semantics |
| Privacy | Access loss redacts; URLs/analytics exclude sensitive content |
| Next action | Relevant bounded action or intentionally none |
| Quality | No unexplained unnecessary turns, pages, forms, or context loss |

## 29. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Required mitigation |
| --- | --- |
| Ask becomes a second domain application | Reuse canonical services, schemas, and policies; no duplicated business logic |
| Renderer becomes unmaintainable | Component/action registry with exhaustiveness and contract tests |
| Adaptive presentation becomes unpredictable | Deterministic registered resolver, bounded server hints, selection matrix, safe fallback, and view-mode tests |
| ChatGPT reference becomes visual imitation | Treat it as an interaction benchmark; retain ContractToCozy identity, terminology, trust, and domain governance |
| Two persistent left rails reduce usable space | Compact application rail, collapsible conversation rail, viewport-aware exclusivity, and minimum canvas width |
| Cross-property history leaks context | Current-access revalidation, explicit property labels/scope filters, privacy-safe indexing, and redaction |
| Session switching corrupts state | URL-addressed identity, request tokens, per-session drafts/views, and stale-response rejection |
| History management deletes domain data | Separate conversation lifecycle from canonical artifacts and disclose consequences before deletion |
| Inline UI overwhelms conversation | Progressive disclosure and contextual workspace layer |
| Mobile experience becomes a compressed desktop layout | Mobile-native nested views with functional parity |
| “Zero friction” weakens safety | Preserve required confirmation, clarification, consent, and revalidation |
| Traditional users feel displaced | Preserve navigation/routes and explicit secondary actions |
| Two surfaces drift | Shared domain APIs/contracts plus parity and reconciliation tests |
| Raw links reappear | Coverage matrix and static drift checks |
| External transitions are misclassified as convenience | Approved-exception rationale and quality review |
| Metrics encourage removing safeguards | Treat metrics as diagnostic; domain safety requirements take precedence |

## 30. Definition of done

The program is complete when:

- every user-visible Ask operation and action is classified in the executable coverage matrix;
- every delivered homeowner domain supports its normal journey inline;
- no ordinary record click or primary action implicitly navigates away from Ask;
- every rendered control has a tested typed dispatch outcome;
- presentation selection and responsive transformations satisfy IW-PRES-001–022 without losing semantics or actions;
- the conversational shell and history experience satisfy IW-SHELL-001–013, IW-HIST-001–018, and applicable desktop/mobile requirements;
- recent conversations remain reachable during an active chat with search, property-safe restoration, URL identity, lifecycle controls, and responsive parity;
- desktop and mobile provide equivalent supported outcomes;
- canonical authorization, validation, confirmation, idempotency, and reconciliation remain intact;
- optional traditional navigation remains available and context-preserving;
- approved external/admin/bulk exceptions are narrow, documented, and tested;
- flagship golden journeys have recorded quality baselines and resolved unexplained regressions;
- accessibility, privacy, failure, stale-state, and unknown-outcome requirements pass at the level claimed; and
- documentation distinguishes requirements, static inspection, automated verification, browser verification, and live-provider verification.

Completion does not authorize progressive removal of traditional pages or navigation. Any future removal requires a separate FRD or approved amendment supported by actual usage and outcome evidence.

## Appendix C — Implementation status

Static implementation status as of September 19, 2026:

**September 20 capability-card foundation update:** The shared `CAPABILITY_LIST` contract now carries a server-declared inline entry read and an explicit incomplete boundary. Discovery, related-capability, and next-action producers use one registry-checked classification. Maintenance, Documents, and Home Records cards offer a typed, source-execution-bound “Explore in Ask” read; their full tool journeys are still incomplete. Other available cards no longer make their whole surface a navigation link: they disclose the missing inline boundary and offer a separately labeled traditional-page choice. This starts IW-CAP-002/003 but does not satisfy IW-CAP-004 or the full 48-capability audit. Block/result identity, further domain operation mapping, exact per-capability boundaries, and browser verification remain open.

**September 20 inline-results update:** Generic `GROUPED_LIST` results with five or more visible records now offer Auto, List, and Cards controls. Auto retains the deterministic density rule; an explicit choice stays with the exact session/property/result/block and survives rehydration without issuing a new Ask execution. The same records, filter controls, status, actions, and disclosed counts render in either view. Smaller lists keep the automatic card view. The response context panel also adds an in-Ask browser-history level: Back closes it, Forward restores the still-authorized response context, and explicit Close returns to the prior level. Focused presentation/state/component tests, TypeScript, and lint passed; browser acceptance coverage was added but not run. This extends IW-PRES-001/008 and §11.2 only for these surfaces. Broader adaptive rules and proposal/workflow workspace levels remain open.

Opening detail adds an in-Ask browser-history entry; browser back closes that level and restores focus to its exact trigger, while explicit close returns to the preceding entry without leaving the Ask route or result. | History data contract | Selected-home pagination and title/homeowner-question search plus a bounded all-home scope implemented in code; focused contract/component tests and static verification passed | The authorized recent-session endpoint returns up to 20 conversations at a time with a validated opaque keyset cursor, ordered by activity and bounded by configured raw-conversation retention, session expiry, current user, and current property access. The desktop rail and mobile drawer append older pages and preserve loaded rows on a page-fetch failure. A separate authenticated POST searches retained session titles or original homeowner questions with the query in the request body, not a URL; matching questions must belong to a currently accessible home and be within execution retention and expiry. No question text or snippet is returned with search results, copied into analytics, or stored in a new index. This is a bounded relation-filtered search, **not** the indexed content-search capability required by IW-HIST-004, and generated answer content is not searched. The rail offers “This home” and “All homes”; the latter enumerates current ownership/household membership, includes only property-scoped sessions whose stored executions all belong to accessible homes, and displays each property's label. Selected-home listing now excludes legacy sessions whose stored executions have a different or missing property, preventing their titles from being attributed to the wrong home. Cross-home selection rechecks session access and reloads Ask under that home's URL context instead of silently retargeting the active canvas. All-home history still excludes unscoped/deleted-property sessions until their provenance is distinguishable, and still permits legacy sessions spanning multiple *authorized* homes. A single-property session write invariant, privacy-safe indexed search, and browser acceptance execution remain required before IW-HIST-002–018 or Phase 1 can be claimed complete (lifecycle controls were added in v1.71; see that row). |

| Session lifecycle controls (IW-HIST-003 pinned group, IW-HIST-009–012, IW-HIST-014; September 24, 2026, FRD v1.71) | Implemented in code. The full backend `tests/ask` suite (1102 tests, 1101 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped) and all Ask frontend jest suites (28 suites, 203 tests) pass; the full mocked `e2e/ask` Playwright suite (`npm run test:ask:e2e`, production build, desktop Chromium, never a real backend) passed 80 of 80. **Requires `npx prisma db push`** for three new nullable `AskSession` columns and one index before this runs on a real database; not run against a real backend | **Schema (edit only; the user applies it):** `AskSession.titleSetByUserAt`, `pinnedAt`, `archivedAt` (all nullable) and `@@index([userId, archivedAt, pinnedAt])`. **Backend:** `PATCH /api/ask/sessions/:sessionId` takes exactly one of `{ title }` (1–120 characters, trimmed; blank is rejected, not a reset), `{ pinned }` or `{ archived }`. It only finds the user's own, still-retained conversation, and for a property-scoped one also rechecks current home access; anything else is a plain 404 with no write (IW-HIST-006). A rename stores `titleSetByUserAt`; automatic titling only ever sets the title when the session is created, so a homeowner title is never overwritten (IW-HIST-009). Pinning only sets or clears `pinnedAt` (IW-HIST-010). Archiving sets `archivedAt` and clears the pin, so a restored conversation returns to the ordinary recent list (IW-HIST-011). None of these touches retention (`expiresAt`), `lastActiveAt`, executions or any home record. `GET /api/ask/sessions/recent` now leaves archived and pinned conversations out of the paginated list and returns the pinned group once, with the first recent page (not with later pages, search or the archived view). `?view=archived` lists archived ones with the same retention, access and pagination bounds. Search spans every unarchived conversation, pinned or not. Each summary carries `pinned`, `archived` and `titleSetByUser`. The existing `DELETE` already removes the conversation, its executions and their feedback and nothing else (IW-HIST-012). **Frontend:** each rail row has a separate "Conversation actions for …" menu trigger, so opening the menu never opens the conversation. The menu (Radix: pointer, keyboard and touch) offers Rename, Pin/Unpin, Archive/Restore and Delete (IW-HIST-014). Rename edits inline: Enter saves, Escape cancels, and blank cannot be saved. Delete first shows an inline confirmation naming the conversation and its home and saying that home records, tasks, documents and decisions made through Ask stay; focus starts on "Keep it". Deleting the open conversation starts a new one, as clearing it does. Pinned conversations show first under "Pinned" with a pin mark. "Archived conversations" at the foot of the rail opens the archived view, which has its own empty state and "Back to recent" and hides search and Pin. A failed change leaves the row as it was and says "Nothing was changed". The open conversation keeps its pinned, archived and homeowner-title state from the loaded rows. **E2E selectors:** the rail's conversation selectors in `e2e/ask` are now anchored to the start of the button name, because each row's menu trigger also names the conversation. **Still open:** archived conversations are not searchable; there is no bulk history-deletion privacy control; indexed content search (IW-HIST-004) and the rest of IW-HIST-002–018 browser acceptance remain as recorded in the rows above. |

Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask` and `src/features/ask`. New backend test `tests/ask/askDisplayPatternContract.test.js` (10 tests) and frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/displayPatterns.test.tsx` (17 tests), mutation-checked twelve ways (deck without actions, Cards choice leaving the list, swipe action missing on some items, month-precision date getting a day, lifespan order, a derived instead of declared status label, Skip sending an action, partial-shelf count, pattern dispatch, percent computed from steps, the typical-range check, the viewer action filter on PROGRESS), each caught. Full chunked `tests/ask` 1112 tests, 1111 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; all Ask jest suites 29 suites, 220 tests; mocked `npm run test:ask:e2e` 80 of 80 (production build, desktop Chromium, never a real backend). Nothing was run against a real backend. | **Contract (all additive and optional; existing blocks parse exactly as before):** `SUMMARY.chips` (up to 4, tone); grouped-list items gain `tone`, `timingLabel`, `amountLabel`, `floorLevel`, `countLabel`, `badgeLabel`; `GROUPED_LIST.presentation` is one of `SHELVES`, `DECK` (with optional `swipeRightActionId` / `swipeLeftActionId`) or `ROOM_MAP`; comparison options gain `badges` (up to 3) beside the legacy `badge`; `TIMELINE` items gain `category`, `datePrecision`, `entityType` and item `actions`; new blocks `LIFESPAN` (per-item age, typical range with min ≤ max, server-declared status and label, plus `missingAge` items with their actions) and `PROGRESS` (percent 0–100, required basis, up to 3 metrics, up to 3 next steps, block actions). The audience filter removes change actions from `PROGRESS` for viewers like other blocks; the answer checker reads only Ask-written copy on the new blocks (never appliance or step names), as it already does for grouped lists; evidence claims may target `LIFESPAN` items. **Frontend:** `features/ask/displayPatterns.ts` checks that a block's data fits its declared pattern and otherwise returns the ordinary list (a deck needs actions on every item and at most 30 items; a swipe id counts only if every item declares it; a timeline track needs two or more readable dates). Shared components in `components/ask/patterns/`: shelves with previous/next buttons, honest counts and a See all end card; card deck; room map by stored floor level; lifespan bars; progress ring; timeline track with category filters and stepping. The timeline track now renders every `TIMELINE` block with readable dates and falls back to the old vertical list otherwise. Item details open in a Radix dialog: a right-hand drawer on wide screens, a bottom sheet on narrow ones. A patterned list offers a List switch and "Show as …" back; the plain list's own List/Cards choice keeps the plain list. **Found while testing:** the first shelf card was a button whose role was overridden to list item, so screen readers would not have announced it as a button; each card is now a button inside its own list item. **Not in step 1:** folding and pinning (IW-PRES-021); any producer emitting the new fields (step 2, one first adopter per pattern); retiring the domain-specific lists (step 3); swipe gestures are covered by keyboard tests only, not a touch device. |
| Display pattern library, step 2: Maintenance shelves (IW-PRES-013, IW-PRES-014; September 24, 2026, FRD v1.74) | **Implemented in code.** Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask`. New backend test `tests/ask/maintenanceShelves.test.js` (6 tests; it runs the real `MAINTENANCE_STATUS` handler against a stubbed task service and household lookup, and passes its output through the answer checker with the source evidence the orchestrator attaches) and frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/maintenanceShelves.test.tsx` (7 tests). Mutation-checked twelve ways, eleven caught; the twelfth (removing the shelf card's own "select this task") changed nothing because opening a detail already selects the task, so that line was removed as redundant. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop drawer; phone bottom sheet with sideways shelves and no page-wide horizontal scroll), both confirmed to fail against a rebuild with the layout forced to List. Full chunked `tests/ask` 1118 tests, 1117 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; all Ask jest suites 30 suites, 227 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 82 of 82. Not run against a real backend | **Server (`maintenanceResult`):** the maintenance list declares `presentation: { pattern: 'SHELVES' }`. The unfiltered open view is now split by timing into Overdue / Due in the next 30 days / Later / No due date (`maintenanceOpenTimingGroups`), each its own section with its own server paging; empty groups are left out, and with no open tasks the single "Pending and in progress" section stays. **This also changes the plain list**, which now shows the same four groups instead of one. Filtered views (Overdue, Due soon, a date range) keep their single section. Every record carries `tone` (overdue critical, due within 30 days caution), `timingLabel` ("Was due …", "Due …", "Done …", "Cancelled …", "No due date") and `amountLabel` ("Est. $25", "Spent $140") from the same fields as its meta line (`maintenanceShelfFacts`). The summary gains three answer chips (overdue, due in 30 days, open), from the same counts as its body, and only when records match. **Frontend:** `MaintenanceResultList` has a Shelves layout next to its List layout and keeps one set of behaviour for both: filters, block actions, the live-record detail (re-read on open, Complete and Reschedule hidden when the live task is no longer open, a distinct "Task no longer exists", access loss), selection for follow-up questions, and browser Back. In Shelves the detail opens in the shared drawer or bottom sheet; cards carry the markers Back-button focus and scroll-to-selected look for. A shelf shows at most 12 cards; a longer group ends with "See all N in the list", which switches to the list and its server pages. A Shelves/List switch appears only when the server declared shelves, and the choice is kept with the result. The declared pattern still decides the layout; the block id only picks Maintenance's own component. **Not done:** `home-actions-list` and the other shelves adopters; any browser run against a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 2: inspection findings card deck with batch confirmation (IW-PRES-015; September 24, 2026, FRD v1.75) | **Implemented in code.** Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask` and `src/features/ask`. New backend test `tests/ask/inspectionFindingDeck.test.js` (10 tests; the real `INSPECTION_FINDINGS` and `INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE` propose and confirm handlers against a fake prisma and stubbed hub service, plus the answer checker on the batch proposal) and frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/inspectionFindingDeck.test.tsx` (6 tests); the step-1 deck test's wording for a skipped card was corrected. Mutation-checked fourteen ways, all caught. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: Details sheet, decisions, one request, one confirmation; phone: card fits, bottom sheet), the desktop one confirmed to fail against a rebuild with batch collection disabled. Full chunked `tests/ask` 1128 tests, 1127 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; all Ask jest suites 31 suites, 233 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 84 of 84. Not run against a real backend | **Decision:** the homeowner chose to confirm once at the end (§11.10 IW-PRES-015 batch confirmation). **Contract (additive):** the `DECK` presentation may declare `batch` (`operationId`, `entityType`, up to 3 `actionIds`, and the request `message`); a request's `launchContext.batchDecisions` carries up to 30 `{ entityId, actionId }`. **Server:** the findings answer declares the deck with a batch of Accept as work and Dismiss (swipe right accepts, left dismisses), but only when the viewer can act. Each finding now declares only the actions its recorded state allows (accept needs an open finding not already accepted; dismiss and resolve need one not already closed), where before every finding carried all three and only the inline detail filtered them. Cards carry a severity badge and tone (Safety critical, Major caution), the inspection date and the estimated cost range. `INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE` gained a batch mode rather than a new operation, keeping its contributor floor, audience policy and live checks: a batch proposal re-reads the findings, keeps the last decision per finding, leaves out and names any finding that is gone, closed, not in an allowed state or given a non-batch action, writes nothing, and returns one confirmation (counts per action) with a review list; if nothing applies it returns BLOCKED and changes nothing. Confirming re-reads each finding, counts an already-applied change as done, leaves a changed or missing finding untouched, catches a failure per finding, refuses a malformed or resolve-carrying batch, and returns a receipt naming each finding's outcome, then refreshes the source list once. It refuses with a conflict if nothing could be applied. **Frontend:** `CardDeckView` has a batch mode: batch actions are recorded on the card ("Your choice: … (not sent yet)"), other actions are sent at once and marked "…", and the end screen lists the decisions by action, the ones sent on their own and the skipped ones, with **Review and confirm N changes** (sent once, then disabled) and Back to the cards. A refreshed result starts a fresh deck. `InspectionFindingResultList` has a Deck layout ("One at a time" / "List" switch) whose Details button opens the live finding in the drawer or bottom sheet, read-only there. A deck that declares a batch falls back to the plain list wherever the batch sender is not available. **Not done:** the other deck adopters (`home-event-radar-feed`, buyer walkthrough and negotiation findings); swipe is covered by keyboard and pointer code only, not a real touch device; nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 2: quote review comparison strip (IW-PRES-016; September 24, 2026, FRD v1.76) | **Implemented in code.** Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask` and `src/features/ask`. New backend test `tests/ask/quoteReviewStrip.test.js` (6 tests; the pure helper, the real `QUOTE_COMPARISON_REVIEW` handler against a fake prisma and stubbed workspace reads, the answer checker, and answer relevance) and frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/quoteReviewStrip.test.tsx` (4 tests); the existing comparison resolver test was updated for the new Table choice. Mutation-checked eighteen ways, all caught. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: strip, badge and its basis, price bars, Table view with one declared lead, one request; phone: cards within the screen, strip-only choices hidden, the table scrolling inside its own box with no page-wide horizontal scroll), both confirmed to fail against a rebuild with the Table choice removed. Full chunked `tests/ask` 1134 tests, 1133 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; all Ask jest suites 32 suites, 237 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 86 of 86. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions:** see §11.10 IW-PRES-016 "Quote review decisions". **Contract (additive):** comparison options may declare `amount` (`value` ≥ 0, three-letter `currency`); attributes may declare `leading`. **Server (`quoteReviewComparison`):** two to four non-rejected quotes become a `COMPARISON` block keeping the id `quote-review-table`; each card shows Price, Readiness (Comparison ready is positive, other stages a caution), Scope ("Scope not confirmed" is a caution), Warranty (the recorded warranty term or "Not recorded"), Freshness (the comparison page's rule: expired, no quote date, or over 90 days old is a caution) and Missing facts (up to three named). Cards carry no actions; accepting a quote stays on the comparison page. The summary, comparison controls, freshness evidence and boundary are unchanged. `COMPARISON` is now allowed for `QUOTE_COMPARISON_REVIEW` in both the operation registry and the quote-comparison skill manifest; the skill policy intersects the two, so without the manifest entry the strip would have been silently dropped (caught by the skill-registry tripwire). The answer checker reads only Ask's own copy on comparison blocks (titles, attribute labels, badges), not provider names. **Found while testing:** answer relevance already turned the quote review into a clarification ("Which home information should I check…") with the old table too (`INSUFFICIENT_RELEVANCE_SIGNAL`), because provider names and prices share few words with the operation's examples. Fixed like Maintenance and Home Actions with a typed answer-contract match (`askQuoteReviewIntent.ts`): only the review's own blocks, led by its own summary. **Frontend:** `ComparisonStripBlock` draws price bars from declared amounts, marks a declared leading value with "Leads", and has a Table view (options as columns, attributes as rows, "Not listed" where an option lacks one) next to Auto / Card strip / Show all, or Cards / Table for two options; the choice is kept with the result. On a phone only Show all (or Cards) and Table are offered. **Not done:** the other comparison adopters (`coverage-comparison-options`, `home-upgrade-options`, `do-nothing-items`, `sell-hold-rent-comparison`, `refinance-scenario-table`); nothing on a real backend. |

This predates v1.77 and needs a decision on the summary wording or on how the checker treats quoted record values. **Not done:** other timeline adopters (`property-recent-events`, `inventory-history`, warranty expiries, `capital-timeline-table`); nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 2: Appliance Oracle lifespan bars (IW-PRES-018; September 24, 2026, FRD v1.78) | **Implemented in code.** Backend and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask` and `src/features/ask`. `tests/ask/applianceOracleBudgetCapabilitySlice.test.js` rewritten for the bars and extended (13 tests: the real handler and the real Oracle service with its model calls counted, the age-0 fix, the status mapping, a legacy report, "Add purchase date" driving the real `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` propose handler to a date confirmation for that appliance, the answer checker with answer relevance on, and an internal code leaking into the heading), and new frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/applianceLifespan.test.tsx` (3 tests).
