| Clear appliance-age and room-map questions do not fall into answer-relevance clarification (IW-PRES-018/019; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.144) | **Implemented in code.** | “How old are my appliances?” and equivalent whole-inventory age wording route deterministically to `APPLIANCE_FAILURE_RISK`, the canonical lifespan view, rather than generic guidance. A valid `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` response is certified by its adapter-owned `property-summary` and `property-summary-evidence` blocks, so record-value-heavy room maps do not depend on lexical similarity and “Show me my home by room” remains answered. Focused tests exercise routing and the semantic-enabled production trust path. The clarification UI is unchanged; it remains the recovery surface for genuine uncertainty. |
| Home Change density and room-focused Property Summary (IW-PRES-019/IW-CONV-047; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.145) | **Implemented in code; room-focused response refined by v1.149.** | The conversational Home Change cluster uses three columns at the desktop breakpoint (two on smaller screens, one on phones). A direct room request such as “Show me my home by room” does not lead with core property facts, inventory, household, warranties, documents, completeness, or timeline blocks, and it does not surface an unrelated Property Context capture. The original room summary, Rooms link and record-freshness treatment from this slice are superseded by the denser v1.149 response; the room tiles retain their inline detail behavior. An ordinary Property Summary remains unchanged. |
| Shared compact Ask item-card foundation (IW-CALM-010/IW-CONV-046/047; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.146) | **Implemented for the first three adopters.** | A shared `CompactAskCard` adopts the Inventory card’s proportions and icon treatment for Ask without copying its dashboard density: 40px semantic icon tile, two-line title, one optional status badge, up to two short supporting lines, one metadata line, and the existing primary action/detail behavior. It preserves the urgency edge required by IW-CALM-010 and uses the repository icon resolver rather than domain-local icon drawings. Home Change, calm Home Actions, and calm Maintenance use the primitive in a responsive one/two/three-column grid. Their canonical identity, ranking, feedback, inline detail, authorization, and action dispatch are unchanged. Specialized comparison, room-map, timeline, lifespan and progress artifacts are not forced into this card. |
| Ask Home continuity cards (IW-CONV-034 and ACUI-006; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.147; wording aligned with v1.152 on September 28; contract hardened October 2) | **Implemented in code.** | Beneath the landing composer and its single-row suggestions, Ask Home shows two compact continuity cards: **Decisions to make** and **Active major moment**. They consume the same `getHomeContinuityProjection` as Unified Home, including its canonical Home Action decision selection, exclusion of the dashboard-projected attention actions, active-project precedence, and guidance-journey eligibility. Ask does not issue a second Unified Home request and does not reinterpret its conversation-continuation `decisions` as dashboard decisions. A decision opens its exact canonical Home Action as a focused Ask turn with the action id preserved; it no longer ejects the homeowner to the traditional desktop surface. The Ask question and category are required response fields, and the Ask frontend always dispatches the canonical `HOME_ACTION` identity through `HOME_ACTIONS`; there is no missing-field fallback to the traditional href. The projection retains that traditional href for non-Ask continuity consumers. The major moment preserves its canonical destination and states its type, stage, blocker when present, and next milestone. Successful empty sources render explicit quiet states; a failed continuity source renders unavailable copy and never implies that no work exists. The two priority section cards, composer, suggestions and continuity cards retain their IW-CONV-034 hierarchy. Recent artifacts remain out of scope under ACUI-006 because no canonical property-scoped artifact contract exists. |
| Home Change canonical cards and inline review (IW-CALM-010/IW-CONV-034/046; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.148) | **Implemented in code.** | Home Change collapses rows by canonical Home Action or Home Event identity before applying the ten-card display limit. The first row in the governed feed order is retained (materiality first, then newest); rows without a canonical entity fall back to their source type and source entity identity. Display titles are never used as identity, so distinct actions with the same name remain distinct. Home Change alone uses the dense `CompactAskCard` treatment and omits the redundant generic “Updated” line. The primary **Review in Ask** action starts the exact `HOME_ACTIONS` or `HOME_TIMELINE_EVENTS` workflow with canonical entity context and remains inside Ask; **Full record** is a quiet secondary link to the traditional destination. |
| Home Change live-action reconciliation (IW-CALM-010/IW-CONV-047; September 28, 2026, FRD v1.150) | **Implemented in code.** | Before rendering, Home Change reconciles ledger rows with the current governed Home Action feed. A Property Change's Operational Work Item UUID is translated to the actual surfaced Home Action id; event-backed and work-backed rows that resolve to the same feed `canonicalKey` collapse before the ten-card limit. Operational Work changes absent from the live feed are omitted, preventing expired forecast preparation and other inactive work from retaining urgent styling or a dead review control. A Home Event without a live action remains reviewable through its exact Timeline identity. **Review in Ask** is emitted for a Home Action only after this live match and still revalidates on continuation as a race-condition safeguard. Title-only deduplication remains prohibited. This refines v1.148's definition of canonical identity and action eligibility. |
| Focused Rooms answer density and inline contents (IW-PRES-019/IW-CONV-034/047; September 27, 2026, FRD v1.149) | **Implemented in code.** | A direct room request returns only a compact count-first summary and a focused room grid. It omits the duplicate Rooms heading and description, the Map/List switch, top and bottom Open Rooms links, record-freshness block, generic follow-up suggestions, and the automatic cross-skill “Suggested next step.” Each tile has a room icon, item count, chevron and interactive states. Opening it stays in Ask and re-fetches the canonical room detail, including a bounded first 20 inventory items with truthful truncation. Contributors can start **Add an item** or the room’s declared correction actions inline; **Add a room** is the only collection action. The traditional room page remains available only as a quiet **Open full room record** escape hatch inside the opened detail. Ordinary Property Summary retains its fuller room block and navigation choices. |
| Ask Home dashboard-parity priority cards (IW-CONV-017/019/034; September 28, 2026, FRD v1.152) | **Implemented in code.** | Ask Home and Unified Home consume one server-produced compact projection of the governed Home Action feed. That projection preserves canonical feed order, collapses multiple regulated-coverage correction actions into the same single entry used by Home, partitions raw priorities (`NOW`/`SOON` for **What needs attention**, `PLAN`/`CONSIDER` for **Plan ahead**), and independently caps each partition at three entries. Consumer-category downgrades for unavailable recommendations must not move an action between dashboard sections. Both landing cards remain present with truthful quiet states when their partition is empty; if the source is unavailable, neither is manufactured. Opening either card returns only those same projected entries, not an unfiltered eight-item priority list. Calm action cards omit ranking ordinals, confidence labels, comparative-reason prose, and policy terminology; those governance fields remain in the contract for audit use. Focused action guidance uses one compact next-step card with useful facts and no Auto/List/Cards control, repeated title sections, or technical Source/Execution facts. Recent changes and open-decision continuation remain secondary suggestion pills and cannot displace either priority card. This supersedes v1.130's dynamic ordering and v1.151's client-side reconstruction from consumer priority categories. |
| First-party landing prompts preserve operation identity (September 28, 2026, FRD v1.155) | **Implemented in code.** | A prompt generated by Ask's governed landing state is not treated as ambiguous free text when the product already knows its owning operation. **What needs attention** and **Plan ahead** carry `HOME_ACTIONS`; the important-changes prompt carries `HOME_CHANGE_SUMMARY`; a focused priority carries `HOME_ACTIONS` alongside its entity context. The existing `launchContext.operationId` remains a routing hint only: the server validates it against the operation registry and all authorization, audience, target, trust and confirmation checks still run. The exact Plan-ahead sentence also routes to `HOME_ACTIONS` when typed manually, so keyboard entry and a generated prompt have the same outcome. |
| Home Action focused-guidance CTAs leave Ask for a traditional dashboard page — Groups A, B (health-factor checklist, recall/inspection-finding review, resolution-center capture, recall confirm/dismiss/resolve writes), and C fully implemented; Group D repair/replace slice implemented, financial/weather journey continuation remains open (reported via screenshot, "See age-related checklist"; September 29, 2026, FRD v1.156; slices FRD v1.157-v1.163) | **Group A implemented in code (FRD v1.157).** `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s `resolveGroupAAskRouting` matches `action.primaryCta.href` (by parsed pathname/query, not by label) against the nine Group A destinations below and, on a match, replaces the sole `PRIMARY` action with the same `interactionType: 'START_WORKFLOW'` pattern `warranties.handler.ts`/`homeEventRadar.handler.ts` already use for their own declared actions — `{ message, operationId, style: 'PRIMARY' }`, no `href` — so `ExecutionCard.tsx`'s existing `dispatchBlockAction`/`ActionLink` wiring (already generic across every `GROUPED_LIST` block's top-level `actions`, confirmed by reading `GroupedListBlock.tsx`'s `focused-home-action-guidance` branch) keeps the homeowner in Ask with no frontend change required. The forced `operationId` bypasses NLU routing entirely (`createAskExecution.ts`'s `declaredItemActionOperationId`), so the `message` text only needs to (a) read as a sensible homeowner question and (b) satisfy the one operation that still keyword-branches internally: `MAINTENANCE_STATUS`'s seasonal-checklist branch requires a seasonal keyword in the message (`maintenance.handler.ts:105`), so the seasonal route's message is "What is on my seasonal checklist?" rather than a generic phrasing. Verified each of the other seven operations' handlers for the same risk (message-content branching that a generic phrasing could silently mis-route) before wiring them — none has one, except `PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_READINESS`, which is a known, accepted limitation: `propertyTaxAppealReadinessResult` infers the appeal `ground` (assessed value / tax class / exemption) from message keywords and defaults to `ASSESSED_VALUE` when none match (`capitalPlanning.handler.ts:155`), and the Home Action feed does not expose the appeal case's actual ground to `askFocusedGuidance.ts`, so a generic message can show the wrong ground's readiness view for a `TAX_CLASS`/`EXEMPTION` appeal. Not fixed in this pass; would need either exposing the ground on the `HomeAction` record or extending the operation to accept an explicit ground parameter. Covered by `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js`'s two new tests (all nine Group A destinations route to `START_WORKFLOW` with the matching `operationId` and no `href`; the savings-benefits in-progress `actionId` case and unmapped destinations are confirmed to still navigate, proving Groups B/C/D are untouched). Groups B/C/D remain as originally classified below and still navigate; `askInteractionCoverageMatrix.ts`'s `HOME_ACTIONS` `handoff` note has not yet been updated to reflect the Group A change — still open.

**Group B re-classification and health-factor checklist slice implemented (FRD v1.158).** Before implementing, read the actual traditional pages behind three Group B destinations rather than trusting the original audit's "genuine drawer/inline-capture candidate" grouping, which turned out to be three different implementation shapes, not one: (1) the reported health-factor checklist page (`focus/health/[factor]/page.tsx`) is a ~1,800-line client component computing a fully deterministic, static checklist from property fields (`yearBuilt`, `hvacInstallYear`, `waterHeaterInstallYear`, `roofReplacementYear`) — no database record, no Property Context capture contract at all; (2) recall review (`RecallAlertsClient.tsx`) and inspection-finding review (`inspection-hub/[reportId]/page.tsx`) are real DB-backed lists with mutate actions (confirm/dismiss/resolve/edit) — structurally a small new Ask operation, same shape as `HOME_EVENT_RADAR_FEED`/`HOME_EVENT_RADAR_STATE`, not a drawer; (3) resolution-center's "confirm details" (`ResolutionCenterClient.tsx:665`) genuinely is `PropertyContextCapturePanel`-shaped, matching the original Sheet/capture idea. Implemented (1) only, since it fixes the exact reported bug with no new UI surface: `apps/backend/src/services/ask/healthFactorChecklist.ts` ports `getAgeTier`/`getAgeChecklistItems` (Property Age factor) and `getSystemKind`/`getSystemAgeTier`/`getSystemChecklistItems` (HVAC/Water Heater/Roof Age factors — Electrical Panel Age is NOT ported, since `calculateHealthScore()` never emits that factor, so it is unreachable from any Home Action card) verbatim from the frontend page's pure content functions. This is a deliberate, flagged content duplication: frontend and backend are separate npm projects with no shared package, so wording drifts if the frontend page's copy changes without a matching update here. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s `buildFocusedHomeActionGuidance` takes a new optional `propertyFacts` parameter; `homeActions.handler.ts` fetches the four year fields via one `prisma.property.findUnique` call, gated on `isHealthFactorFocusHref` (only for a health-insight card's focus/health route, not on every focused-guidance turn), and passes them through. When a checklist resolves, it renders as a new `checklist` section on the existing `focused-home-action-guidance` `GROUPED_LIST` block, and the original navigation CTA is demoted from `PRIMARY` to `SECONDARY` (content now answers inline; the traditional page stays a supported escape hatch, matching this FRD's own scope statement that traditional navigation remains "a fully supported user choice"). Required a small, additive frontend change (not the drawer/Sheet infrastructure Group B originally implied): that block already has its own special-cased renderer in `GroupedListBlock.tsx` (hard-coded to look up `next-step`/`why-it-matters`/`known-details` by section id), which needed one more `.find()` for the new `checklist` section id, following the exact same pattern as the existing `known-details` rendering — confirmed by testing that a renderer keyed to fixed section ids silently drops any id it doesn't know, which is exactly the failure mode this change had to avoid. Covered by `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js` (age and HVAC-age checklists render with the correct section and demoted CTA; missing underlying year data and callers that pass no `propertyFacts` at all both fall back to the original unaffected behavior) and a new `focusedHomeActionChecklist.test.tsx` (the renderer actually surfaces the checklist section's content, guarding the exact silent-drop risk above). Backend `tsc --noEmit` and frontend `next build` both clean. The remaining Group B destinations — (2) and (3) above — and Groups C/D are unimplemented, still navigate exactly as originally classified, and now have a clearer, corrected implementation shape each rather than one shared "drawer" plan.

**Group C implemented (FRD v1.159).** Unlike Group A/B, Group C's fix is not to change the destination — the audit's own conclusion was "navigation is fundamentally correct" — it is to stop presenting an out-of-Ask navigation as the bare `PRIMARY` action when the SUMMARY/GROUPED_LIST content above it is the actual answer. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s new `isGroupCWholeToolDestination` demotes the CTA's `style` from `PRIMARY` to `SECONDARY` (href and label unchanged — this is a style-only fix, no rerouting) for: the Risk Premium Optimizer mitigation plan (identified by `action.lineageId.startsWith('mitigation-plan:')`, since its href is a per-item DIY/PROVIDER/CARRIER handoff link with no fixed pattern to match on, unlike every other Group C/D destination); a Renovation Case (`/dashboard/properties/{id}/renovations/{caseId}`); the Sale Case tool (`/tools/sale-case`); Capital Timeline (`/tools/capital-timeline`); and Savings & Benefits' in-progress resume case (`/tools/savings-benefits` with `actionId`, the exact case Group A explicitly excluded). One exclusion, not in the original audit text but a real correctness concern found while implementing: a `SAFETY_EMERGENCY` action whose href happens to match the renovations pattern is NOT demoted — its visual urgency should not be reduced just because its destination also falls in Group C. The "seasonal (full tool)" item the original audit listed under Group C turned out to have no actual separate href in the codebase (grepped) — the one real seasonal `primaryCta` site is the Group A seasonal-checklist case, already handled there and, since it stays fully inline via `START_WORKFLOW`, in a strictly better way than "honest secondary navigation" would have been. Covered by `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js` (all five real Group C destinations demoted with href/label unchanged; the safety-emergency exclusion; an unmapped destination confirmed unaffected). Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; no frontend change needed — `ActionLink` already renders a `SECONDARY` href-based action correctly, exercised elsewhere (e.g. `warranties.handler.ts`'s `openPage` action). Group D and Group B's remaining two destinations are still unimplemented.

**Group D repair/replace decision slice implemented (FRD v1.160).** Reading the two named Group D destinations first (per the now-established practice from Group B/C) found they were not equally scoped. The financial/weather guided-journey continuation genuinely has no covering operation — `GUIDANCE_JOURNEYS_LIST` only lists every journey with a still-external `href` per item, not a specific journey's current-step content — and building one is real new-operation work (new handler, registry entry, semantic corpus, audience/trust policy, certification fixture, per the existing "New-operation checklist" practice), not attempted here. The "HVAC-style decision-comparison review" destination, by contrast, already has a fully-capable existing operation: `REPLACEMENT_GUIDANCE` (`inventory.replacement`), which for a non-HVAC item runs the same `replaceRepairService.runItemAnalysis` the traditional page shows, and for an HVAC item delegates internally to `hvacDecisionStartResult` — the durable HVAC Decision Platform read that continues an existing `DecisionThread` or offers to start one, which is the exact "start or resume the tracked HVAC decision" content this Home Action's own copy (`homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts`'s `loadRepairReplaceDecisionActions`) already promises. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s new `resolveGroupDReplacementGuidanceRouting` identifies this Home Action by `lineageId` prefix (`repair-replace:` or `appliance-repair-replace:`, per `specialistAdmissionRegistry.ts`), not by href — the producer's href varies (a bare item page, or a `guidance-overview?journeyId=...` URL) depending on whether an active guided journey exists for the item, but the underlying decision content read is identical either way, so both branches route the same. The item id comes from `action.presentation.subject.id` (`kind: 'INVENTORY_ITEM'`, already set by the producer), passed through as `entityType: 'INVENTORY_ITEM'`/`entityId` on the `START_WORKFLOW` action (the same `launchContext.entityId` mechanism `focusedOperationForLaunchContext` already uses elsewhere), which the operation's own registration (`envelope.launchContext?.entityType === 'INVENTORY_ITEM' ? envelope.launchContext.entityId : null`) already reads — deterministic item resolution, bypassing the operation's own message-based alias matching entirely (matching Group A's finding that a forced `operationId` bypasses NLU routing). Covered by `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js` (HVAC and appliance items both route correctly regardless of which href branch fired; a guidance journey with no `INVENTORY_ITEM` subject — the financial/weather case — is confirmed NOT routed and still navigates). Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; no frontend change needed. Group B's remaining two destinations and the financial/weather guidance-journey continuation are still unimplemented.

**Group B recall-review and inspection-finding-review implemented (FRD v1.161).** Reading both destinations before implementing (same practice as Group C/D) found they were not equally scoped, contrary to the original audit's single "genuine drawer/inline candidate, no existing operation covers it" label for both. Inspection-finding review turned out to already have a fully-built operation: `INSPECTION_FINDINGS`/`INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE` (`inspection.handler.ts`) already reads open confirmed findings into a `GROUPED_LIST` with declared, confirm-gated `accept`/`dismiss`/`resolve` item actions (including a deck/batch presentation) — only routing from the Home Action CTA was missing, a Group-A-style fix, not new-operation work. Recall review had no covering operation at all — `recalls` never appears anywhere in `askOperationRegistry.ts` before this change — so a new read-only `RECALL_REVIEW` operation (`recallReview.handler.ts`, adapter `recalls.review`) was built: lists open/needs-confirmation `RecallMatch` rows (`recalls.service.ts`'s existing `listPropertyRecallMatches`) as a `GROUPED_LIST` with severity/status/remedy per match, an EMPTY_STATE when there are none, and a BOUNDARY disclosing the data is regulator-sourced, not a safety inspection of the home. Confirm/dismiss/resolve mutations for recalls are a deliberately deferred follow-up: unlike the read side, a write would need a new branch in the shared `workflowConfirm.handler.ts` confirmation dispatcher (the same generic mechanism `INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE`'s own writes already run through) rather than a self-contained addition, mirroring how `HOME_EVENT_RADAR_FEED` (read) shipped before its `STATE`/`MARK_DONE`/`FEEDBACK` write siblings. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s new `resolveGroupBRecordReviewRouting` identifies a recall Home Action by `lineageId` prefix (`recall:`, from `loadRecallActions` — its href is bare `/recalls` with no per-match id, so routing to the list loses no specificity the traditional page itself had at this entry point) and an inspection-finding Home Action by href pathname (`/inspection-hub/{reportId or open-items}`, matching both branches `loadInspectionFindingActions` can produce), routing each to `START_WORKFLOW` with no entity (both operations are plain property-scoped reads). Registering `RECALL_REVIEW` required the full "new-operation checklist" this codebase enforces by type and test, not just `tsc`: an `AskOperationId` union entry, an `ASK_OPERATION_DEFINITIONS` entry, a deterministic routing-cascade pattern, five `askOperationSemanticPackages.ts` map entries (job/positive/hard-negative/answer-positive/answer-negative), an `askTrustCertificationCorpus.ts` certified-answer entry, and a full `askInteractionCoverageMatrix.ts` entry (that file's `Record<AskOperationId, ...>` type fails `tsc` on a missing entry by itself, but its own runtime drift-check and two hardcoded operation-count assertions in `tests/ask/askGovernance.test.js`/`tests/ask/askInteractionCoverageMatrix.test.js` do not, and needed updating by hand — caught by running those suites, not by `tsc`). Covered by new cases in `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js` (recall and both inspection-finding href variants route correctly, with no entity and no href on the resulting action). `tsc --noEmit` clean; the full targeted governance/trust-architecture/interaction-coverage-matrix/focused-guidance suites pass (79 tests). No frontend change needed. Group D's financial/weather guidance-journey continuation and Group B's resolution-center capture cases (and the deferred recall-mutation follow-up above) remain unimplemented. **Original audit (FRD v1.156), Bug:** `askFocusedGuidance.ts:70-76`'s `buildFocusedHomeActionGuidance` takes `action.primaryCta.href` completely unmodified from the governed Home Action feed and renders it as the sole, `style: 'PRIMARY'` action on the `focused-home-action-guidance` block — for the reported case, `homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts:2322/2393` sets that href to `/dashboard/properties/${propertyId}/focus/health/${factorSlug}`, a traditional dashboard route, so the click navigates the whole browser out of Ask. **Scope: systemic, not isolated.** Counted 30 `primaryCta` construction sites across `homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts`/`homeActions.service.ts` (3 are pass-throughs that relabel/decorate an existing CTA, leaving 27 real destinations); nearly all are `/dashboard/...` routes, because the Home Action feed is genuinely shared with the traditional dashboard (same feed FRD v1.152 already documents Ask Home consuming). `askInteractionCoverageMatrix.ts`'s existing `HOME_ACTIONS` `handoff` note does not mention this gap — it is undocumented, not a previously-accepted tradeoff. **Classification (verified per-site, not assumed from labels):** *Group A — an existing certified Ask operation already covers the destination; route there instead of navigating* — `/dashboard/warranties` → `WARRANTY_LOOKUP`; coverage-intelligence gaps/comparison stages → `COVERAGE_GAPS`/`COVERAGE_COMPARISON_STATUS`; `/dashboard/home-event-radar` → `HOME_EVENT_RADAR_FEED`; sell-hold-rent tool → `SELL_HOLD_RENT_ANALYSIS`; mortgage-refinance-radar tool → `REFINANCE_ANALYSIS`; savings-benefits (no `actionId`) → `SAVINGS_OPPORTUNITIES`; property-tax appeal stage → `PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_READINESS`; **seasonal checklist** (`/dashboard/seasonal`) → `MAINTENANCE_STATUS` (traced to its own `buildSeasonalMaintenanceResult` branch reading the same `SeasonalChecklistContext` — not `MAINTENANCE_FORECAST`, an earlier guess that turned out wrong on verification); coverage-renewal action's Warranty case (`loadCoverageRenewalActions`) → `WARRANTY_LOOKUP`. *Group B — genuine drawer/inline candidate: bounded content tied to this one answer, no existing operation covers it* — the reported health-factor checklist; recall review (`/recalls`); inspection-finding review (`/inspection-hub/{reportId}?findingId=`); coverage-renewal's Insurance case (confirmed no Ask operation covers insurance policies at all — grepped the full registry); resolution-center "confirm details" (see below). *Group C — whole-feature/stateful tools where navigation is fundamentally correct* — seasonal (if a caller wants the full tool, not just the checklist), Risk Premium Optimizer plan, Renovation Case, Sale Case, Capital Timeline, Savings & Benefits' in-progress (has-`actionId`) resume case. *Group D — guided/decision journeys needing real Ask-operation wiring, not a drawer* — the financial/weather guidance-journey continuation and HVAC-style decision-comparison review, both stateful multi-step flows with their own decision contracts. **Two corrections made during verification, not assumed:** the ownership-cost-change action (`enrichedDestination.href`, line ~4318) was first guessed as Group A (`OWNERSHIP_COSTS`) but is wrong — `ownershipCostsResult` (`savingsOwnership.handler.ts:235`) only reads `ownershipCostReadModelService.getCurrent` (a current-snapshot lens: `CASH_OUTFLOW`/`OPERATING_EXPENSE`), never the comparable-period change data the Home Action producer surfaces (whose own fallback href is the *different* `?view=changes`); this moves to Group B/C pending either new work or an `OWNERSHIP_COSTS` lens extension. The duplicate-expense action (`/dashboard/expenses`) is not a UI-pattern question at all: no Ask operation touches expenses (grepped, zero matches), and the underlying task is a *write* (delete/merge a duplicate record) Ask cannot do today regardless of surface — blocked on a missing capability, not a routing or drawer decision. **State-sync design constraint, verified positively:** the generic risk in "a drawer form must instantly trigger a chat confirmation" only exists if the drawer's content submits through a separate API from Ask's own. Confirmed `@/components/ui/sheet` is a plain, content-agnostic Radix Dialog wrapper (not hardwired to the existing "Response context" panel's data shape), and `CaptureCards.tsx`'s capture/confirmation components already take a clean `{ executionId, onCompleted, onAccessLost }` contract decoupled from where they render — so a new dedicated Sheet driving those same components would get "confirm closes the drawer and posts a receipt to the thread" for free, the same mechanism inline capture already has, with no new event-bus/pub-sub needed. Best evidence this pattern is sound: `ResolutionCenterClient.tsx` already renders `PropertyContextCapturePanel` (`surface="drawer"`) — a component driven by the *same* `featureKey`/`operationKey`/`operationInput` Property Context Platform contract Ask's own inline capture (`evaluateFeatureContext`/`PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE`) already speaks — inside a Sheet, today, in production code. This corrects an earlier, too-pessimistic guess that resolution-center's flow would need a rebuild; the appliance-purchase-date edit (`/edit?focus=appliances`) does still need one, confirmed as a large, separate react-hook-form property-edit page with its own bulk submit, not wired to any capture contract. **Status at time of original audit:** no code changed; audit and classification only, recorded per the user's explicit "review, don't implement" instruction. **Superseded by the Group A implementation (FRD v1.157), the Group B health-factor checklist slice + re-classification (FRD v1.158), the Group C style-demotion fix (FRD v1.159), the Group D repair/replace decision slice (FRD v1.160), the Group B recall-review/inspection-finding-review slice (FRD v1.161), and the Group B resolution-center capture slice (FRD v1.162) below.** Remaining recommended sequencing: a recall confirm/dismiss/resolve write operation (needs a `workflowConfirm.handler.ts` branch); the financial/weather guidance-journey continuation still needs a new operation reading a specific `GuidanceJourney`'s current-step state. |

**Group B resolution-center capture slice implemented (FRD v1.162).** The last of the three Group B shapes found by the v1.158 re-classification: resolution-center's "confirm details" CTA (`homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts`'s `home-digital-twin-fact-review` producer — one producer covering HVAC/water-heater/roof/appliance lifecycle facts alike, distinguished only by `componentLabel`/`operationInput`, so this single slice covers the appliance-purchase-date case too, not a separate one) is `kind: 'CORRECT_FACT'` with a `propertyContextFeature` ref (`CAPITAL_TIMELINE`/`RUN_TIMELINE`, scoped to the specific inventory item), the exact same contract `ResolutionCenterClient.tsx`'s `PropertyContextCapturePanel` already reads. Rather than porting that Sheet/drawer into Ask, this reuses Ask's own generic chat-native inline-capture mechanism instead — the same `captureRequests`/`AskCaptureRequest` contract `HOME_SAVINGS`/`OWNERSHIP_COSTS`/`CAPITAL_RESERVE_PLAN` already drive from `evaluateFeatureContext` — so the missing fact is asked and answered as a capture card in the conversation, with **no new frontend surface**: `ExecutionCard.tsx` already renders `execution.captureRequests` generically via `InlineCaptureCard`, keyed by `requirementId`, with no operationId allowlist. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s new `isPropertyContextCaptureAction` mirrors `canCaptureResolutionInline`'s own test (`CORRECT_FACT` kind + a `propertyContextFeature` ref); `homeActions.handler.ts`'s focused branch evaluates that specific feature (in parallel with the existing health-checklist property-facts fetch) and, when a household-role-eligible active requirement exists, builds an `AskCaptureRequest` via the existing `askCaptureRequest()` helper (already used by `capitalPlanning.handler.ts`) and demotes the CTA to `SECONDARY` (the resolution-center page stays an optional escape hatch). **The one real plumbing risk, found and fixed, not assumed away:** `askCapture.ts`'s shared `submitAskCapture` dispatcher has a single `HOME_ACTIONS`-operationId branch already used by the *unfocused* Home Actions list's own `HOME_ACTIONS`/`VIEW_FEED` capture — hardcoding that featureKey/operationKey there would have written a focused item's answer to the wrong Property Context feature scope, and the branch's post-capture recompute call passed no `launchContext` at all, which would have silently replaced the focused single-action card with the unfocused list once capture succeeded. Fixed by having `buildFocusedHomeActionGuidance` store the action's own `propertyContextFeature` and `focusedHomeActionId` in the execution's `parameters` (the same stored-discriminator pattern `capitalPlanning.handler.ts`'s `phase5CaptureFeature` already established for `CAPITAL_RESERVE_PLAN`/`PROPERTY_TAX_APPEAL_READINESS` sharing one operationId across two features); the `HOME_ACTIONS` branch reads that discriminator to capture against the correct feature when present (falling back to the unchanged `HOME_ACTIONS`/`VIEW_FEED` behavior otherwise) and passes `launchContext: { entityType: 'HOME_ACTION', actionId }` on recompute so the same focused card re-renders, now without the capture request, exactly the way `home-actions.feed`'s own registration derives `focusedActionId` for the original turn. Covered by new cases in `tests/ask/askFocusedGuidance.test.js` (`isPropertyContextCaptureAction`'s predicate; the captureRequest/parameters/CTA-demotion shape with and without an active capture). Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted `askFocusedGuidance`/`askGovernance`/`askInteractionCoverageMatrix`/Home-Actions-handler suites pass (62 tests) with no registry drift (still 86 operations — no new `AskOperationId`, since this reuses the existing `HOME_ACTIONS` capture path rather than adding one). No frontend change. Not yet live/browser-verified (no local database — verified by code reading and targeted tests only). Group D's financial/weather guidance-journey continuation and the deferred recall-mutation follow-up (FRD v1.161) remain the two open items in this arc — see the recall-mutation slice immediately below, which closes the second. |

**Group B recall-mutation follow-up implemented (FRD v1.163), closing the last open item this whole arc's own re-classifications named.** A new confirmation-required `RECALL_MATCH_UPDATE` operation, paired with the existing read-only `RECALL_REVIEW` (FRD v1.161) the same way `INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE` pairs with `INSPECTION_FINDINGS` — the closest existing precedent, read and mirrored rather than designed from scratch. The traditional `RecallMatchCard.tsx`/`ResolveRecallModal.tsx` (read before implementing, not assumed) already call canonical `recallsService.ts` writes — `confirmRecallMatch`/`dismissRecallMatch`/`resolveRecallMatch` — so this slice is pure Ask-side propose/confirm wiring to existing writes, not new write logic. `askFocusedGuidance.ts`'s `isPropertyContextCaptureAction`-style live-state gate (`recallMatchActionAllowed`, mirroring `inspectionFindingActionAllowed`) reuses `listPropertyRecallMatches`'s own already-computed `applicability.status` field rather than re-deriving identity-confirmation logic. Confirm-time write dispatch (`confirmRecallMatchUpdate`) and an edit-in-place handler for the Resolve confirmation's two fields (`editRecallMatchResolveConfirmation`, mirroring `editInspectionFindingResolveConfirmation` minus the cost field — `resolveRecallMatch` has none) both live in the shared `workflowConfirm.handler.ts` dispatcher the original Group B note said this would need. Registered as a full, skill-less domain command (following the `CAPTURE_FACT_CONFIRM` precedent — a confirmation-required operation needs no skill package; `askConfirm.ts`/`askDomainCommandRegistry.ts`/`confirmCapabilityHandlerRegistry.ts` all gate on the skill being optional), so no new skill manifest was built. New `AskOperationId` required the same full checklist FRD v1.161's `RECALL_REVIEW` slice already established (union entry, `ASK_OPERATION_DEFINITIONS`, routing-cascade pattern, five `askOperationSemanticPackages.ts` entries, `askTrustCertificationCorpus.ts` certification fixture appended at the end + certified-answer entry, `askInteractionCoverageMatrix.ts` entry, `askAnswerTrustPolicy.ts` action-id allowlist, `ASK_MUTATION_IMPACT_MAP` sibling declaration) plus the domain-command-specific pieces (`ASK_DOMAIN_COMMAND_REGISTRY` entry, confirm-capability + edit-confirmation registrations) — five hardcoded operation/command-count assertions across `askGovernance.test.js`/`askInteractionCoverageMatrix.test.js`/`capabilityHandlerRegistry.test.js`/`confirmCapabilityHandlerRegistry.test.js` needed bumping by hand, caught by running those suites, not `tsc`. **Two real bugs found and fixed while implementing, neither assumed away:** (1) the shared `HOME_ACTIONS`-style stored-discriminator risk did NOT apply here (this operation has its own dedicated `askCapture.ts`-independent dispatch, unlike the resolution-center capture slice immediately above), but a parallel risk DID: `askCapture.ts` was not touched, so no fix was needed there, but `confirmRecallMatchUpdate`'s own re-fetch had to independently derive a per-match `contextVersion` (`recallMatchVersion`, sha256 of id+status+updatedAt, mirroring `inspectionFindingVersion`'s exact shape) since `listPropertyRecallMatches` computes `applicability` fresh on every call with no stored version concept, the same documented freshness asymmetry `INSPECTION_FINDINGS`/`INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE` already have. (2) Adding the new operation's calibration evidence (two new frozen `askRoutingCalibrationEvidence.ts` rows, re-derived from the live retriever per that file's own established convention) shifted the GLOBAL rawScore→confidence calibration curve enough to flip an unrelated, already-borderline message ("Does our water heater still have a warranty on file?", `WARRANTY_LOOKUP` 0.6579 vs `CLAIM_FILE` 0.6168 raw scores, only 0.041 apart) from confidently routing to `WARRANTY_LOOKUP` via semantic fallback to falling through to `GROUNDED_GUIDANCE` — a real regression against this project's explicit "LLM is a last resort, never widen the fallback" standard, caught only by the full `test:ask:chunked` sweep, not by any targeted test. Root-caused (not just patched around): `warrantyLookupPattern`'s own deterministic regex already matched this exact message, but a separate exclusion pattern's bare `file` token (meant to exclude "file a claim" phrasing) was also matching the completely unrelated, common "warranty **on file**" phrasing, silently forcing this message through the fragile semantic fallback on `main` even before this change — fixed by requiring `file`/`filing` to be followed by an article/possessive (`file a`, `filing my`, etc.), the same technique the same pattern's own `record a` alternative already used one entry over. This is a genuine, unrelated latent bug this change's recalibration exposed, not something introduced by the recall work itself (confirmed by reproducing the identical raw retrieval scores on unmodified `main`). Covered by a new `tests/ask/recallMatchCapabilitySlice.test.js` (7 tests: live-state gating for all three actions including the identity-confirmed/unconfirmed transition, each canonical write called with the right arguments, stale-contextVersion rejection with the already-applied bypass, item-action role gating, the answer-trust whitelist, and every canned action message). `tsc --noEmit` clean; full chunked `tests/ask` 1351 of 1352 pass (1 pre-existing skip), 0 regressions. No frontend change needed — `ExecutionCard.tsx`/`GroupedListBlock.tsx` already render declared `MUTATE_RECORD` item actions on a `GROUPED_LIST` generically (the exact same mechanism `INSPECTION_FINDINGS`' own item actions already exercise). Not yet live/browser-verified (no local database). **This closes the last open item from the original Home Action focused-guidance CTA audit (FRD v1.156/gap audit §17) — the financial/weather guidance-journey continuation (Group D) is the sole remaining gap, and it was never part of Group B's own scope.** |

**Focused-guidance redundant CTA and irrelevant handoff removed (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.164).** Reported live: after the health-factor checklist rendered inline (v1.158), the answer still carried a "See age-related checklist" link back out to the traditional page it had just replaced, and a generic "Suggested next step: Understand maintenance status" card (the static `HOME_ACTIONS` → `MAINTENANCE_STATUS` entry in `skillHandoff.ts`, which ignores which action is focused). `buildFocusedHomeActionGuidance` now emits `actions: []` when the checklist is inline (Group C whole-tool and feature-capture cases keep their secondary link; the no-checklist case keeps its primary link) and sets `suppressSkillHandoff: true` on all focused guidance. No booking operation exists in Ask, so the suggested step is deliberately not faked as "Book a general home inspection"; the recommended step is already the first section of the answer. Covered by `askFocusedGuidance.test.js` and `focusedHomeActionChecklist.test.tsx`. Not browser-verified.

**Skill-handoff relevance audit: write-side handoffs wired, per-result relevance gate, buyer suppression (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.165).** An audit of all nine `SKILL_HANDOFF_DEFINITIONS` (executed: every goal's follow-up text routes to its intended target; code-traced: which result modes reach the resolver) found five COMPLETED-only handoffs (`CLAIM_FILE`, `CLAIM_TRANSITION`, `INSPECTION_FINDING_UPDATE`, `DOCUMENT_PROMOTION_CONFIRM`, `BUYER_LIFECYCLE_UPDATE`) could never fire: confirmed writes never passed through `executeOperation`'s finalize, and `askConfirm.ts` persisted `result.skillHandoff ?? null` with nothing setting it. Now a shared `resolveAskSkillHandoff` (executeOperation.ts) serves both paths, and `askConfirm.ts` resolves it before validating/persisting the confirmed result. A new optional `isRelevant` predicate on a definition gates on the concrete outcome so the follow-up matches what happened: inspection handoff only after accept (dismiss/resolve create no work), document handoff only after `DOCUMENT_PROMOTION_CONFIRMED`, buyer-lifecycle handoff only after resume/date change (not pause/cancel). The pre-close buyer branch of `HOME_ACTIONS` now sets `suppressSkillHandoff` (the homeowner maintenance follow-up does not fit an unowned home). Verification: `skillHandoff.test.js` (source-level wiring test plus behavioural gates), `askBuyerPlanPresentation.test.js`, startup validators return no issues, 196/196 across confirm/governance suites; the confirm path was not exercised end-to-end against a real database. Open (audit scope 3): move the follow-up decision from a static per-operation table to a per-result decision owned by the handler.

**Skill-handoff scope 3, slices 1-2: handler follow-up nominations and display labels (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.166; not yet adopted by any handler).** `AskOperationResult.followUp` is a new optional field with three modes: `undefined` keeps today's static-registry behaviour byte-for-byte, `null` declines any follow-up, and a nomination `{ goal, reasonCodes?, label? }` selects a transition that must already be allowlisted in `SKILL_HANDOFF_DEFINITIONS` for that source operation and still passes the status, policy, health, and context-reference checks. A failed nomination yields no handoff and never falls back to the static entry (which would reintroduce the irrelevant follow-up); a nomination bypasses `isRelevant`, since the handler already made that call. `validateFollowUpNomination` is exported so a handler test can assert its nominations are acceptable. The response contract gains `skillHandoff.suggestedLabel` (nullable, default null, max 80, so executions persisted earlier still parse). Frontend: `features/ask/skillHandoff.ts` derives the click prompt and the button label; the label is display-only and the message sent stays the slug-derived prompt, because those phrasings are verified to route deterministically. Verification: `skillHandoff.test.js` (all modes, allowlist rejection, malformed input, contract default), frontend `skillHandoff.test.ts` (including a guard that the click sends the prompt, not the label), 100/100 targeted backend tests, `next build`, startup validators clean. Two pre-existing failures in `askWorkspaceSplitGuardrails.test.ts` (AskWorkspace.tsx 459 lines vs 450 ceiling; unlisted AskShellHeader.tsx) reproduce without this change. Next: slice 3 migrates HOME_ACTIONS and PROPERTY_SUMMARY to handler nominations; slice 4 the write-side operations and removal of `isRelevant`/`suppressSkillHandoff`.

**Skill-handoff scope 3, slice 3: content-driven Home Actions follow-up, Property Summary declines, repeat/duplicate guard (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.167).** Decided after a review of the desktop Home surface (its shortcut tiles are count/reason-driven and appear only when there is something to review) and of the two independent next-step mechanisms in one response (the static handoff card and the capability recommender's "What comes next"). (1) `HOME_ACTIONS` (`homeActionsFollowUp.ts`) nominates `understand-maintenance-status` only when the canonical feed, counted BEFORE presentation capping, holds at least one `MAINTENANCE`-source action classified NOW or SOON ("actionable", not "urgent"; SOON is deliberately not urgent), with a label stating the reason ("See your maintenance schedule (N due now or soon)"); it declines when every displayed action is already maintenance. The rule is structural, never message-text based. The all-property answer declines explicitly (several feeds are never merged). Focused guidance and the pre-close buyer branch already suppress. (2) `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` now sets `followUp: null` on every result: the generic maintenance follow-up was unrelated to completing the record and duplicated the capture card in completeness mode. (3) `resolveAskSkillHandoff` now takes `recentCompletedOperationIds` (the same last-5-completed-turns query that already fed the recommender) and excludes any target operation that was just answered this session or whose capability is already in the response's `CAPABILITY_LIST`; the exclusion applies to every handoff that goes through the read path. Verification: new `homeActionsFollowUp.test.js` (rule table, cap-independence, dedupe against a real `CAPABILITY_LIST` block, handler-wiring pins), plus 102 targeted and 37 Home Actions/Property Summary tests, typecheck and startup validators clean. Not exercised against a real database or in a browser. Deliberately not done yet: click/completion telemetry (needs frontend attribution and a bounded-label metric design; only SUGGESTED is recorded today) and migrating the write-side handoffs to outcome-owned nominations.

**Skill-handoff acceptance telemetry (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.168).** Until now only `SUGGESTED` was recorded on `ask_skill_handoffs_total`, so there was no way to tell whether any follow-up was ever used. The handoff click now sends `launchContext.handoffFromExecutionId` (new, telemetry-only, deliberately not `sourceExecutionId`, which drives in-place refresh and request keys). `createAskExecution` resolves it through `askHandoffTelemetry.ts`: the source execution must belong to the requesting user and carry a stored handoff that matches a registered `SKILL_HANDOFF_DEFINITIONS` transition for its source operation; every metric label is derived from that stored data, never from the client, so a forged id can neither inject labels nor count a handoff that was never offered. Outcomes, all with the same three bounded labels (`source_skill`, `target_skill`, `outcome`; execution ids never become labels): `OPENED` when a new execution is created from the card (after the duplicate-`clientRequestId` early return, so a replay cannot double-count; also written as a `SKILL_HANDOFF_OPENED` execution event carrying the source execution id for audit), `COMPLETED` when that execution reaches ANSWERED/COMPLETED/READY_WITH_LIMITATIONS and routed to the operation the handoff intended, and `MISROUTED` when it completed but routed elsewhere (a regression signal for the assumption that the goal-derived prompt routes deterministically to its target). There is deliberately no `DISMISSED`: the card has no dismiss control and "not clicked" is not a dismissal. Known limits: only the first pass is observed, so a launched execution that needs a clarification, capture or confirmation and finishes on a later turn counts as OPENED but neither COMPLETED nor MISROUTED; telemetry failures are swallowed and never fail the answer. No schema change (`launchContextJson` already persists the field). Verification: new `askHandoffTelemetry.test.js` (attribution scoping and forgery cases, label boundedness against the real counter, completion classification table, contract, creation-wiring order), frontend guard tests, 118 targeted backend tests, `next build`, typecheck and startup validators clean. The creation path was not exercised against a real database (the `*.db.test.js` integration suites need one) and nothing was checked in a browser. Open: migrate the write-side handoffs to outcome-owned nominations (step 5).

**Refresh keeps focus; accepted-work CTA moves into Ask (Group E) (September 29, 2026, FRD v1.169).** Two live reports. (1) A focused Home Action answer ("Install storm windows or check weatherstripping") rendered correctly, then a refresh replaced it with the unfocused "5 Home Actions are ready to review" feed. Cause (code-traced, then unit-tested): `refreshAskExecutionAfterConflict` re-ran the execution with `launchContext: { surface: 'ASK_REFRESH', sourceExecutionId }` only, dropping the `entityType`/`entityId`/`actionId` the focused branch keys on. It is a bug class, not a Home Actions quirk: about 15 handlers read `launchContext.entityId` (including `REPLACEMENT_GUIDANCE`, which Group D routes to). New `refreshLaunchContext` restores only the focus fields (`entityType`, `entityId`, `actionId`, `decisionThreadId`, `workItemId`, `journeyId`) from the stored `launchContextJson`; one-shot mutation hints (`operationId`, `documentId`, `batchDecisions`), the stale `contextVersion`, `returnTo` and `capabilityId` are deliberately not carried, since declared add/attach handlers gate on `surface !== 'ASK_REFRESH'` plus `operationId` and a refresh must never re-issue them. The retry path already reuses the full stored context and was not affected. (2) "Seal driveway cracks before freezing" still carried an "Open work" CTA navigating to `home-operations?focusWorkItemId=...&openManage=1`. **This exposes a gap in the original 27-site classification above: its per-producer trace covered `homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts`, but accepted Operational Work is built by a different producer, `appendAcceptedOperationalWork` in `homeActions.service.ts`, which was never traced.** Group E: for `presentation.variant === 'ACCEPTED_WORK'` the CTA becomes declared `START_WORKFLOW` actions routed to the existing confirmation-gated `OPERATIONAL_WORK_UPDATE` with `entityType: 'WORK_ITEM'` and the item id (the handler resolves the exact item from `launchContext.entityId`). What is offered mirrors the card's own governed `feedbackControls`: "Mark complete" only when `COMPLETE` is present (completion-eligible maintenance work), "Snooze reminders" when `SNOOZE` is. Messages omit the title on purpose: the handler infers the verb with a loose `complete|done|finished` pattern that a title such as "Finish the deck" would confuse. `REPORTED_COMPLETE` keeps its "Review completion" navigation (verification is not an Ask operation), and a viewer or a caller that does not say keeps the link (fail closed; the operation needs CONTRIBUTOR). Trade-off: the drawer's advanced options are no longer one click from this answer; the Work tab remains in the Ask navigation. Verification: `refreshLaunchContext.test.js`, new accepted-work cases in `askFocusedGuidance.test.js`, the new blocks parse against the real `AskPresentationBlockSchema`, 258 targeted tests, typecheck and startup validators clean. Not exercised against a real database or in a browser: the refresh fix in particular has only been run as a unit, so confirm by opening a focused Home Action and refreshing it. Open: other producers may share the untraced-producer gap; a sweep of every `primaryCta` producer outside `homeActionSourcePromotion.service.ts` has not been done.

**Focused Home Action progressive disclosure and inline snooze choices (October 1, 2026, FRD v1.172).** A targeted card-action audit confirmed the bespoke record cards already open their supported detail views inside Ask, and accepted Operational Work already routes Snooze through the confirmation-gated `OPERATIONAL_WORK_UPDATE`; the remaining inconsistency was the focused Home Action renderer itself. It showed secondary facts permanently and sent Snooze immediately with the handler's implicit two-week default. The focused card now keeps its recommended next step visible, moves rationale and known details behind an inline “View details” disclosure, and opens “Snooze reminders” as an inline choice of tomorrow, one week, two weeks, or one month. Selecting a duration starts the existing exact-work-item Ask workflow and still requires its normal confirmation; it never navigates to Home Actions or mutates from the browser. The backend parser now recognizes those four declared intervals deterministically, with the previous two-week default retained for free-text requests that name no interval. Only one secondary panel is open at a time. Verification: focused-card Jest coverage for disclosure, exclusivity, exact action dispatch, and checklist retention; a pure backend interval test; frontend and backend typechecks. No database or browser environment was used.

**Accepted-work missing-detail continuation (October 5, 2026, FRD v1.176).** A focused accepted-work answer must not tell a homeowner to add missing information while offering only work-management controls. Whenever its recommendation is degraded, the answer makes **Add missing details** the primary action. It names the missing details when the canonical work record supplies them; when an older or incomplete work record has low/null confidence but an empty `missingContext`, the action remains available and the governed correction flow asks which detail to update instead of dead-ending. Inventory-backed work starts the exact `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` flow for the recorded item. Accepted `GUIDANCE` work whose persisted subject was flattened to `PROPERTY` recovers the inventory identity from its canonical `GuidanceJourney.inventoryItemId`. If no typed asset can be recovered, Ask starts an inventory-scoped correction using the work item's display label; the correction handler may resolve one exact property-scoped item or ask which equipment item is meant, but it must never substitute unrestricted `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` completeness, which can select an unrelated area such as Location. Completion and snooze remain available as secondary actions where policy allows. Inventory correction declares `HOME_ACTIONS` as a reconciliation sibling, so after the governed write the originating focused action and other visible Home Action results are recomputed from the current record. Viewers receive the missing-detail explanation but no write action and are told that an owner or contributor must update the record.

**Reported-completion closure inside Ask (October 2, 2026, FRD v1.173).** Removing the desktop “Review completion” handoff prevented an unwanted navigation but left `REPORTED_COMPLETE` work with no meaningful decision. Focused accepted-work guidance now offers two confirmation-gated actions in Ask: “Confirm completion” records the homeowner's observed result as verified attestation evidence, transitions the canonical work item to `VERIFIED`, and records the Operational Work outcome; “Still needs attention” transitions it to `REOPENED`. Confirmation is offered only for `LOW_CONSEQUENCE` and `MATERIAL_FINANCIAL`, where the completion-evidence policy accepts explicit homeowner attestation (and the latter also requires the observed result). `REGULATED_COVERAGE` and `SAFETY_EMERGENCY` continue to require qualifying domain/document evidence and expose only the safe reopen decision. Viewers remain read-only. Both proposal and confirmation recheck the exact item, current state, evidence tier, context version, and authorization; neither completion decision links back to Desktop UI.

**Card whitespace balance and responsive answer width (October 1, 2026, FRD v1.173).** A complete review of the registered Ask presentation blocks and bespoke result lists established one shared density policy. Ordinary prose, guidance, task, and record answers use a centered readable measure capped at 920 px. Artifacts whose meaning depends on side-by-side scanning—tables, timelines, comparisons, scenario comparisons, shelves, decks, and room maps—may use the full 1140 px workspace. Compact cards and the two landing continuity cards are content-driven rather than padded to a fixed minimum height; CSS grid may still equalize cards within a populated row. Action regions render only when at least one supported action is visible, use a compact 12 px vertical / 16 px horizontal boundary, and never reserve a blank footer after filtering. Two fixed-height exceptions remain intentional: the one-at-a-time card deck keeps a stable swipe surface, and shelf-card titles keep a short aligned title measure for horizontal comparison. These rules change presentation only; they do not alter block selection, data, authorization, confirmation, or navigation contracts. Regression coverage checks standard and wide classification, content-driven compact cards, and suppression of filtered-empty action footers. No browser or runtime environment was used for this pass.

**Artifact-led width and age-checklist table refinement (October 1, 2026, FRD v1.174).** Production review showed that v1.173 constrained the whole answer turn when only its prose needed a readable measure. The standard turn is therefore restored to a 1080 px cap, its calm headline/supporting copy stays capped at 800 px, and multi-item Home Action priority lists join the 1140 px wide-artifact class. Compact-card controls follow their content rather than being forced to the bottom of a stretched card. The focused Home Action detail facts grid is count-aware (one fact spans the row; two use two columns; three or more may use three), and the disclosure has one control whose label changes between “View details” and “Hide details.” An age-related checklist is repeated columnar information, not a collection of independent cards: it renders as one semantic table on wider screens with System, Current condition, Recommended action and Priority columns, while the same rows stack with visible field labels on narrow screens. The server keeps the already-available condition, action and urgency as separate GROUPED_LIST item fields; the browser does not parse prose. Authorization, ranking, evidence and action behavior are unchanged.

**Focused checklist contract hardening and stable composer anchoring (October 1, 2026, FRD v1.175).** Live review found a focused age-checklist request ending in the generic retry state and the persistent composer intermittently stopping near the middle of a tall viewport. The grouped-list item contract now declares `condition` explicitly (separate from the recommended action and short metadata), the age-checklist producer emits that field, and contract/component coverage verifies the complete producer-to-table shape. The Ask route now owns one dynamic viewport (`100dvh`) because its dashboard top bar is intentionally absent; it no longer subtracts the unrelated 72 px dashboard-header allowance. The transcript remains the only scrolling flex region, and the composer is a non-scrolling `shrink-0` footer rather than a sticky child whose containing-block height can change during conversation restoration. Mobile bottom-navigation and safe-area padding remain unchanged. These changes do not weaken retry behavior, source validation, authorization, or confirmation safeguards. Validation is static and component/unit based; no live backend or database environment was available for reproducing the reported server exception.

**Outer-scroll containment correction (October 1, 2026, FRD v1.176).** A second deployed screenshot showed that fixing the Ask child alone was insufficient: `AppShell` still used `min-h-screen`, the mobile body-padding effect still applied to Ask, and result restoration used `scrollIntoView`, which is allowed to scroll every ancestor including the document. The Ask route now locks `AppShell` itself to one `100dvh` viewport, propagates `min-h-0` through the shell, removes route-external body padding, and gives the conversation pane an explicit internal scroll boundary. Loading, newly answered turns, and restored result positions now scroll that pane directly; they do not move the document or hide the Ask header. The footer remains a non-scrolling flex sibling at the bottom of the viewport, above the existing mobile bottom-navigation allowance.
| Documents D-2/D-3: governed verification-status and type filters, the sixth calm domain to certify (ASK_COZY_CONVERSATIONAL_UI_GAP_AUDIT.md §15; September 28, 2026, FRD v1.153) | **Backend implemented and verified; frontend renderer already generic; acceptance journey written, not run green.** `documents.handler.ts` adds `resolveDocumentRefinement`/`documentFocus`/`buildDocumentViewState`/`documentFilterChips`/`loadDocumentViewState`, the same governed-`viewState`-continuity model as Warranties/Inventory/Buyer Deadlines. `DOCUMENT_LOOKUP` added to `FILTER_CONTINUABLE_OPERATIONS` (askFollowUpContext.ts) so a declared chip is recognized as a filter continuation and forced back to the same operation. 10 new backend runtime tests (`documentsRefinementRuntime.test.js`) against the real registered handler: fresh-query chip set, status/type chip replace-one-dimension semantics, all/all-types/no-filters clearing, no-match-keeps-chips, free-text initial filtering, foreign-view-state isolation, and every declared chip round-tripped through the real follow-up resolver. Existing D-1 tests (`documentLookup.test.js`, `documentsCalmAnswer.test.js`) pass unchanged (filtering is applied in-memory after the same unfiltered `listPropertyDocuments` read, so the canonical-inventory call signature does not change). Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; full chunked `tests/ask` 1328 of 1329 pass, 1 pre-existing skip. **Frontend:** `DocumentResultList.tsx` already rendered `block.filters` generically (built ahead of D-2, unused while D-1 shipped `filters: []`), so no frontend code change was needed — verified by reading the component, not assumed. A Playwright acceptance journey (`documentsJourney.spec.ts`, 9 scenarios: list, filter replace/clear/no-match, inline detail for both a Home Record and a transitional legacy row, removed-row detail, empty state, 390px, keyboard/live-region, and two failure-recovery cases) was written mirroring the certified Warranties/Claims journeys, but a local build/serve of the acceptance fixture route did not render (composer never became interactive against either `next start` or a manually-assembled standalone server) — reproduced identically against the already-certified, unmodified `warrantyJourney.spec.ts` in the same local rebuild, so this is a pre-existing local environment gap, not a regression from this change. Not run against a real backend, and this revision's Playwright scenarios are unconfirmed pending that environment fix. `askInteractionCoverageMatrix.ts`'s DOCUMENT_LOOKUP handoff note updated to mark the `filters:[]`/REC-001 gap fixed. |
| Property Summary P-1/P-2: the two already-focused sub-answers calm-adopted, and the vague-overview branch redesigned as a conversational synthesis, not an aggregate rendering (ASK_COZY_CONVERSATIONAL_UI_GAP_AUDIT.md §16; September 28, 2026, FRD v1.154) | **Implemented in code.** **P-1** (`isCalmAdopter`, frontend): the room-focused and completeness-focused sub-answers are calm-adopted, gated by the absence of the core-facts `TABLE` block the (now-removed) vague-overview dump used to be the only branch to declare — this also gates the new P-2 synthesis answer, identified by its own `property-summary` SUMMARY id when neither the room nor completeness GROUPED_LIST is present. **P-2** (`propertySummary.handler.ts`): the vague-overview branch ("Tell me about my home") no longer builds the core-facts `TABLE` or embeds copies of Inventory/Household/Warranties/Documents/Timeline — Inventory, Warranties and Documents already have their own dedicated, filterable, calm-certified answers (`INVENTORY_LOOKUP`/`WARRANTY_LOOKUP`/`DOCUMENT_LOOKUP`); Timeline is owned by `HOME_TIMELINE_EVENTS`/`HOME_CHANGE_SUMMARY`; Household has no dedicated read operation yet, so it is dropped without an invented redirect. The answer is now one or two grounded prose sentences from recorded property facts only (`propertyOverviewFactsSentence`, omitting any unrecorded field rather than printing "Not recorded"), followed by exactly one priority-ordered status observation (`propertyOverviewStatusObservation`: an actionable completeness issue, worded as a plain reassurance, never an alarm — a recent material change is deliberately deferred, since it would need `HOME_CHANGE_SUMMARY`'s own live-action reconciliation, not just its materiality filter, to avoid mentioning a change that reconciliation would have dropped as stale), and two or three contextual follow-up suggestions (`propertyOverviewSuggestions`) rather than a catalog of every domain. No generic "Open property record" action; no `captureRequests` (reserved for the explicit completeness question, unchanged). Explicit room and completeness questions are unaffected. **Tests:** `propertySummaryOverview.test.js` (new: the three pure functions plus the real handler's vague-overview shape, complete and no-facts-recorded cases, and confirming the explicit completeness path is untouched); `askGovernance.test.js`'s five Property-Summary producer tests rewritten to assert the removed producers no longer exist in source (the shared `propertyRecordOverview.service.ts` and frontend dispatch assertions they also carried are unchanged, since that service and `HouseholdResultList`/`WarrantyResultList`/`DocumentResultList` remain fully alive through the traditional property page and their own certified operations); `roomMap.test.js` updated to trigger through the explicit focused-room question (rooms are no longer reachable from the vague overview); `inventoryItemCorrect.test.js`/`homeEventCorrect.test.js`/`warrantyCorrect.test.js` producer-count assertions updated for the one fewer embedded copy each. Full chunked backend `tests/ask` 1334 of 1335 pass (1 pre-existing skip); backend and frontend `tsc`/`next build` clean; frontend Ask jest suite unaffected (2 pre-existing, unrelated `askWorkspaceSplitGuardrails` failures predate this change). Not run against a real backend or browser. |

New backend test `tests/ask/sellerPrepProgress.test.js` (5 tests: the figure, the real `SELLER_PREP_CHECKLIST` handler against a stubbed sale case and household lookup, viewers, no ring without must-address items, the answer checker with answer relevance on, and the relevance fix) and frontend test `components/ask/__tests__/sellerPrepProgress.test.tsx` (1 test); the existing seller-prep tests pass unchanged. Also found, not fixed: `tests/unit/propertySaleCase.test.js` and two related unit files fail 25 of 56 tests on `main` before this change as well (a fake prisma without a model the service now reads). **Frontend:** `ProgressBlock` shows each next step's link ("Open"). **Not done:** the other progress adopters (`digital-will-items`, `property-completeness`, `tax-readiness-gaps`, `renovation-readiness-items`, `buyer-closing-day-blockers`); showing the figure on the sale-case page; nothing on a real backend. |

Tiles are Met, Missing, Entries; the steps are the unmet requirements in the plan's own words; no entry or contact detail is repeated. `PROGRESS` is allowed for `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS` and `HOME_DIGITAL_WILL` in the registry and the renovation and home digital will skill manifests and evaluations. **Frontend:** none. **Not done:** `tax-readiness-gaps`; nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 3: folding and pinning results (IW-PRES-021; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.95) | **Implemented in code (frontend only).** Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask`, `src/features/ask` and `e2e/ask`. New test `features/ask/__tests__/conversationView.test.ts` (5 tests: per-session storage, hostile or unreadable values, toggling and reconciliation, the headline and its cut, what can fold). Mutation-checked five ways in the unit code (id limit, the settled-result rule, reconciliation, the headline cut after a test was added for it) plus one against a rebuild (the folded body no longer hidden), all caught. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: fold to the summary headline, pin to the strip with its freshness, jump to the result, show and unpin, and no further request sent; phone: the strip and a folded result within the screen). Ask jest 39 suites, 260 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 126 of 126. Backend untouched. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions (recommended approach):** **Fold** collapses a result's body to its headline (the summary title, else the first block title, else the question, never generated) and when it was updated, with a Fold / Show button; the body is hidden, not removed, so its view state and any open detail come back as they were. Only a settled result can fold: one waiting on a confirmation, a question, a missing detail or a property choice stays open. **Pin** puts the result on a "Pinned" strip above the conversation showing its headline and when it was last updated, with a jump to the result (focusing its heading) and an unpin button; the result itself keeps its own Refresh, and goes stale like any other (§15). Neither re-runs anything. The state is the execution ids only, per session, in session storage (bounded to 100, ignoring anything unreadable), and a result that leaves the conversation (deleted, or redacted after access was lost) is dropped from both. **Frontend:** `conversationView.ts` (state helpers), `useConversationView.ts`, `PinnedResultsStrip.tsx`, and the header buttons and folded row in `ExecutionCard`. **Not done:** folding all earlier results at once; keyboard shortcuts; nothing on a real backend. |
| Composer: stop, try again and change-and-resend (P0; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.96) | **Implemented in code (frontend only).** Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask`, `src/features/ask` and `e2e/ask`. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: a held request stopped, the question back in the composer with the notice, the late answer discarded; a failed request back in the composer and sent again with Try again; an earlier question brought back, focused, and the earlier answer kept; phone: the Stop button and the banner within the screen). Mutation-checked two ways against rebuilds (no invalidation of the stopped request, so its late answer lands; the Send and Stop buttons sharing one element), both caught. Ask jest 39 suites, 260 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 128 of 128. Backend untouched. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions (recommended approach):** **Stop** replaces Send while a question is being answered. A request cannot be recalled once sent, so Stop only stops waiting: the question returns to the composer (and its draft), a notice says the answer may still appear in the history if the request had already reached Ask, and the late answer is discarded here (its token is made stale, so it never appears and never clears a newer request's loading state). **Try again** is a button on the composer's error banner that sends the restored question; a failed request already put the question back. **Change and resend** is a button under a result's question that puts that question back in the composer, focused, to change and send as a new question; the earlier answer is kept, and the history is never rewritten. **Found while building:** Send and Stop shared one button element, so the click that pressed Stop also submitted the form once the button turned back into Send; they now have their own keys. The button is named "Change and resend this question" so it does not collide with the existing Edit controls. **Not done:** cancelling the server-side request (no endpoint exists for a request that has no execution yet); measuring clarification and unmatched rates (backend analytics work); nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 3: the bespoke domain lists are kept (IW-PRES-012; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.97) | **Decision only; no code changed.** | **Decision (recommended approach taken):** the step 3 delivery note said a domain-specific list is retired once all of its blocks have moved to a shared pattern. The bespoke lists (maintenance, seller prep, inspection findings, radar, rooms, inventory, warranties, household, documents, home events, reserve allocations, buyer tasks, claims) are **not retired**: each one holds what a shared pattern cannot, a live re-read of the canonical record, the corrections and decisions for it, and access-loss handling, and the shared patterns now render through them as layouts (shelves, deck, map) rather than replacing them. They are the domain component; the patterns are how they lay their items out. **Revisit** only if a shared detail mechanism (a generic entity-detail contract) is built, which would be a separate design. |
