Pilot: `askFormatting.ts` and `handlers/statusBoard.handler.ts` moved out with the orchestrator re-exporting their names; backend typecheck clean; chunked `tests/ask` 1,210 tests, 1,209 pass, 1 skipped, unchanged. The full `test:chunked` shows failures in `tests/unit` that do not import any changed file; not compared with a clean checkout | No product behaviour changed. Recommended next: phase 1 of the plan, in slices of four to six self-contained read handlers. |
| `askHandlerSupport.ts` split by subject, and an import-graph guardrail (architecture; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.110) | **Done.** The 1,253-line shared bucket became ten modules under `services/ask/support/`: `capture`, `propertyContext`, `executionState`, `answerGuards`, `capabilityDiscovery` (with its `capability.discovery` registration), `commandInputs`, `homeEventCorrection`, `outcomes`, `domainHelpers` (each under 260 lines) and `roomMap`. Code moved unchanged. `roomMapFacts`, which sat in the Home Timeline handler and was used by the Property Summary handler, now lives in `support/roomMap.ts`. `askHandlerSupport.ts` is now only `export * from './support/...'`, so no handler or lifecycle import changed, and the orchestrator facade still exports every name it did before (checked by loading it against the earlier list). New `tests/ask/askImportGraphGuardrails.test.js` builds the value-import graph over `handlers/`, `execution/`, `support/`, `askFormatting.ts`, `askHandlerSupport.ts` and the orchestrator, and fails on a cycle (naming it), on a support module importing a handler, lifecycle file, the facade or the orchestrator, on a handler importing the orchestrator or a lifecycle file other than `executeOperation`, on the facade holding code or missing a module, and on a support module over 400 lines; type-only imports are ignored because they cannot cause a load-time cycle. Mutation-checked: a support cycle, a handler cycle (`homeRecordWrites` and `miscHandlers`), a support module importing the facade, a handler importing a lifecycle file, facade code, a missing facade line and an oversized module are each caught, and a type-only import is not flagged. The source-text helper now reads `support/` too. Today's handler-to-handler edges (for example `miscHandlers` to `homeRecordWrites`) are an acyclic graph and stay allowed. Verified: typecheck, and `test:ask:chunked` 1,228 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped. |
| Calm conversational answers (§11.11, September 25, 2026, FRD v1.111) | **Slices A to E implemented in code and ON BY DEFAULT for everyone (September 25, 2026); `?calm=0` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASK_CALM_ANSWERS=false` revert.** Some per-domain list titles and detail flows still use each domain's own layout. | **A** (d81ca7d1) Maintenance headline anatomy. **B** (9aa7ab72) calm shell for every answer. **C** one slim header with a status dot and icon-only delete, no composer helper copy (multiline hint only while focused), history rail without brand block or explanatory footer, follow-up chips docked above the composer (`followUpSuggestions`, `FollowUpRow`), inline chips removed from calm answers. **D** landing: greeting, state strip (`buildConciergeStateStrip`, `CalmLanding`), starter chips, explorer link; the "Popular ways" and "For your attention" cards are not shown. **E** every summary uses the headline anatomy (producer headline when declared, else title as headline and body as ordinary text, thin rule for caution or critical tone), and every domain list loses its outer frame (`FRAMELESS_LISTS`, CSS only; section dividers stay). Verified: frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean; 56 Jest suites / 390 tests in `components/ask` and `features/ask`; **Ask Playwright suite 139 of 139 passed on a fresh `ASK_ACCEPTANCE_FIXTURE=1` build with calm as the default**: every older spec pins the previous presentation through `installAskContext`, and 4 calm specs use the default (landing, answer, domain list, and `?calm=0` being remembered); 9 new helper tests and 6 new chrome component tests. Not verified at phone width for the calm setting, on the live app, or with real data. **Visual pass (after live review the same day):** the global `h1`–`h6` rule (Poppins, 20 to 36px, bottom margin) was making every list title in an answer look like a page heading, so headings inside the Ask surface now inherit the body face and size (`.ask-calm`, scoped, in `globals.css`); the serif is Fraunces 500 at 22 to 30px and appears only on the greeting and the answer headline; each list's own title is visually hidden but stays for assistive technology; the landing shows count chips with a colored dot instead of repeating them as a sentence, labels the one urgent item "Top priority", drops any starter that repeats a chip or that item, scrolls chips sideways on a phone, and uses a warm page background; a calm answer moves focus to its own heading rather than its first button. Checked by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 and 390 px. Not done: a bare item card per domain, per-domain headline sentences beyond Maintenance, and dropping each list's own title. |
| Conversational UI principles (§11.12, September 25–26, 2026, FRD v1.113) | **Requirements written; slices F, H and the first part of G (the completion flow) implemented in code.** Slice I (sources chip in the feedback row) implemented; slice J (undo receipts) and the remaining capture forms were evaluated and deliberately not built (see the J and G-remainder notes below). | IW-CONV-001–015 and an audit of two live screenshots (a form-card capture flow and a superseded answer). **F:** in the calm shell a superseded answer is a one-line "Earlier version of this answer · show" stub with no frame that opens the same calm presentation in reduced emphasis; the header status dot shows its plain label beside it on wide screens. **Density pass (same day, after live review of the deployed calm UI):** "What comes next" is collapsed after a main artifact and one row per tool otherwise; the per-answer timestamp line and the summary's duplicate action are gone; on wide screens the second title band is hidden, the status dot sits beside New conversation in the rail (an accessible heading remains), the rail shows nothing when there is no history, the landing's top priority is one line, the hero composer is one row, and turn spacing is tighter. Verified by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 px, frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 58 suites / 400 tests, Ask Playwright suite 139 of 139 plus 6 calm specs. Not verified on the live app or at phone width for this pass. **G (completion flow):** with the calm shell on, a plain standard-sensitivity workflow capture (`canAskConversationally`: a group of choice, yes/no, number, short-text or time fields, classification WORKFLOW_INPUT) is asked one question at a time (`ConversationalCapture`): the question, then a chip per option for a bounded choice (a picker above 8), a number field with Continue for a value, Skip when optional, earlier answers as "so far" chips that can be changed, and the same single `submitAskCapture` request once every question is answered or skipped. Nothing submits by itself when the card opens already answered (a restored draft or a value the server rejected); it shows the answers and a Continue button. The capture's plain lead-in summary is not drawn above the question, and the review that follows is the existing confirmation restyled as a message (no tinted card, no eyebrow). Backend: `StructuredCaptureField.prompt` (plain question), and the Maintenance completion capture now asks *Which task did you complete?* then *Was there an actual cost?*; the project follow-up outcome question is asked only when the chosen task is a project follow-up (the handler already asks again with the outcome required). No schema, validation, confirmation version, consent or authorization rule changed; property-context forms and every other capture keep the existing form. Verified: backend `tsc` clean, 219 targeted Ask tests (4 new), frontend `tsc` clean, 60 suites / 413 tests (13 new), Ask Playwright suite 145 of 145 on a fresh fixture build including 4 new completion specs, screenshots of the three steps at 1440 and 390 px. Not verified on the live app or against a real backend. **Cleanup pass (after the deployed slice G review):** the refresh-status label no longer sits beside New conversation in the rail (it made the button wrap; the rail shows the dot only); the landing is the greeting, the composer and one row of pills (state chips with a dot, starter questions, then a dashed "More ideas" pill that opens the capability explorer), with no Top priority row and no separate link; sources are one small chip ("N sources") that opens the same context panel instead of a boxed card; the three correction links are one menu beside the thumbs; maintenance filters and the Shelves/List switch are quiet text controls, shelf cards are a fixed size with a one-line meta row, and the answer keeps View all and Create a task while the generic Open and Setup links are dropped; a capture is asked conversationally only when it has three or fewer questions (creating a task, six fields, stays one form), and after a rejected save a Continue button returns so the person is never left without a next step. Verified by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 and 390 px, frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 60 suites / 418 tests, Ask Playwright suite 145 of 145. Not verified on the live app. **H (visible work):** with the calm shell on, a question is on screen the moment it is sent (`PendingTurn`): the homeowner's message, the Cozy avatar row, a status line, and two quiet placeholder bars in the answer's place (no motion under reduced-motion). The status names an area only from the question's own words (`pendingStatusLabel`: maintenance, coverage and warranty, inventory, costs and finances, weather and alerts, documents; otherwise "Checking your home record…"), never as a claim about server progress. After 6 seconds it says "Still working on this…"; after 15 seconds it says the request is taking longer than usual and can be stopped. The first question no longer leaves the landing in place with no sign of work (the previous behavior shows nothing until the answer arrives): the landing gives way to the conversation and the footer composer, whose Stop control returns the question to the input. The answer replaces the placeholder in the same position (the bubble does not move; checked in a browser). Verified: frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 62 suites / 425 tests (7 new), Ask Playwright suite 148 of 148 including 3 new pending specs, screenshots of the pending and answered states. Not built: naming each source as it completes (needs server progress events). Not verified on the live app. **I (sources chip):** in the calm shell the `ResponseContextSummary` chip is no longer its own line under the blocks; `ExecutionFeedback` takes it as `leading`, so the chip, the two rating buttons and the corrections menu are one row. Verified: frontend `tsc` clean, Ask jest 61 suites / 424 tests (1 new: chip shares a row with both rating buttons, `aria-expanded` false then true, text "Context open"), Ask Playwright suite 148 of 148 on a fresh fixture build, screenshots at 1440 (closed and open) and 390 px. Not verified on the live app. **J (undo receipts, IW-CONV-007) — evaluated, not built (decision, code-traced, nothing executed):** the only reversal that exists is `MAINTENANCE_UPDATE` action `REOPEN`, which sets the task to PENDING behind a confirmation. It is not a faithful undo of a completion: completing a task also writes `lastCompletedDate`, `actualCost`, `completionMetadata` and, for a recurring task, advances `nextDueDate` (`buildStatusUpdateData`); reopening reverses none of these (only the seasonal-checklist counter and Radar reconciliation are reversed), and a project follow-up completion additionally updates the `ProjectRecord`, may create a remediation task and has emitted analytics. A one-tap "Undo" would therefore leave a recurring task pending with next cycle's due date. A correct undo needs a new write operation that snapshots and restores the pre-completion fields (scoped to non-recurring, non-project tasks), with its own compare-and-swap and registry/governance coverage; that is a product-and-backend decision, so no receipt was added and completion keeps its explicit confirmation. **G remainder — evaluated, no change:** every remaining capture is excluded for a reason that would need a rule change rather than presentation: more than three questions (task creation, inventory, claim filing, Radar), or `APPROXIMATE_DATE` / relational (`RELATIONAL_SELECT_CREATE`, `RELATIONAL_UPDATE`) inputs whose precision and create-new semantics a chat step would have to re-implement. Captures within the existing gate (including the deadline and maintenance-date captures, which are short-text dates) already use the conversational flow. **v1.113 landing and task capture (after live review of the deployed app):** IW-CONV-017 and IW-CONV-018 above. Reverses the earlier three-question limit (creating a task is now conversational). Verified: frontend and backend `tsc` clean, Ask jest 424/424 (three tests updated for the new landing and limit), Ask Playwright suite 148/148 on a fresh fixture build (calm landing spec updated), screenshots of the landing at 1440 and 390 and of the first task question at 1440. Not verified: later task questions in a browser, the live app, or a real backend. |

| Adaptive presentation runtime | Foundations implemented for `TABLE` and bounded `COMPARISON`; component registry implemented for all 27 block types; focused component/state/contract tests and static verification passed | Existing schema-validated `TABLE` blocks use a dedicated renderer selected by the validated type discriminator, with a deterministic responsive default and lossless Auto/Cards/Table view choice. Bounded two-to-four option `COMPARISON` blocks now use stacked cards on narrow screens and an accessible card strip on wider screens, with visible previous/next controls, position context, a persisted non-carousel Show all path, and option-scoped inline actions. Comparison badges require a server-declared label, homeowner-facing basis, and machine-inspectable policy code; the client does not invent winners. **Added 2026-09-18:** the §11.3 component registry itself is now built — `BlockView`'s former ~460-line if-chain across all 27 `AskPresentationBlock` types is a typed `Record` (`components/ask/blocks/registry.tsx`) that fails to compile if a type is left unregistered, with each type's rendering split into its own component file and a preserved honest fallback for a runtime-unrecognized type. This closes the "one monolithic renderer" risk (§29) but is a dispatch-mechanism refactor, not new adaptive-presentation intelligence. **Updated September 22, 2026:** grouped-list density, evidence-density, and a reduced-motion accessibility preference are now implemented (see the "Deterministic adaptive presentation" row, which also records Timeline and Form as investigated and closed, not gaps); a deterministic multi-mode selection *policy* spanning every block type together — as opposed to each type's own independent resolver, which is what exists today — still remains required before IW-PRES-001–012 can be claimed complete. Browser acceptance scenarios are present but were not executed in this environment. |
| Maintenance task workspace (October 2, 2026; self-contained compact revision October 3) | Workspace, governed-edit, cross-page navigation, task-local guidance, canonical snooze, compact desktop presentation, and in-workspace action completion implemented; focused component/unit tests and static verification passed | Selecting a task from any Maintenance list, shelf, or calm priority card opens a compact, centered task card on desktop and a near-full-screen modal on narrow screens, replacing both the narrow right drawer and the list-only inline expansion. Desktop width is capped at 50rem, height is content-driven with a `100dvh - 6rem` ceiling, and only the record body scrolls when necessary; it must not resemble a second full-page application. The workspace re-fetches the canonical task, shows its description, status, priority, dates, recurrence, category, linked system, costs, source and freshness, and keeps the existing governed actions in a sticky footer. Previous/next controls follow section order and continue across server pages: an edge navigation stores a bounded result-view intent, requests the adjacent page through the existing Ask collection continuation, then opens the first/last canonical task after the page revision arrives. The position remains explicitly labelled “in this view” because filters can change the wider collection. The Ask composer remains outside and inaccessible behind the modal: there is no second free-form chatbox inside this workspace. **Self-contained actions:** Complete, Reschedule, Snooze/Resume reminders, Remove/Cancel and governed detail edits create the same versioned Ask execution as before, but its review, editable fields, consent, confirmation and errors render only inside the workspace. Proposal and confirmation turns are not appended to the visible transcript. After confirmation, the workspace refreshes the canonical task and publishes only the completed receipt (for example, “Maintenance task updated” with task, action, status and due date) into conversation history; closing the popup therefore reveals the final outcome rather than a duplicate actionable review card. “Why is this important?” opens the task's local, record-grounded rationale instead of launching a separate chat turn. **Governed editing:** when the result declares a permitted `MAINTENANCE_TASK_UPDATE` mutation, Edit details exposes due date, priority, recurrence and service category inside the workspace and submits the exact task identity through the existing versioned Ask confirmation flow; it never writes directly from the browser. The update input validates recurrence invariants, the proposal explicitly displays the new recurrence/category, and confirmation writes through the canonical service's existing compare-and-swap/idempotency path. Read-only/viewer results do not invent the edit control. **Task-local guidance:** three disclosures answer why the task exists, when to act, and what cost is recorded from the canonical task itself; they remain inside the workspace, start no new Ask turn, and never invent rationale or costs. **Maintenance snooze contract:** `PropertyMaintenanceTask.snoozedUntil` suppresses only reminder delivery until an explicit date. Snoozing does not change due date, status, visibility, recurrence, or ownership; it is confirmation-gated through `MAINTENANCE_TASK_UPDATE`, versioned and idempotent like other task edits. Rescheduling or completing clears the snooze so a new occurrence cannot inherit stale suppression; the reminder worker includes expired snoozes and excludes active ones. The workspace offers one week and Resume reminders. A Prisma migration is intentionally not included; it must be generated by the repository owner. **Still pending:** governed linked-record edits remain read-only because neither the current Ask command contract nor the desktop task editor defines selectable linked-record changes, validation, or unlink semantics. Live browser/mobile verification remains pending because this repository has no test environment; mocked acceptance coverage and static checks are the available boundary. Governed remove/cancel is already present when declared by the result. Unsupported linked-record controls must not be mocked in the UI merely to match a visual reference. |
| Maintenance inline task detail | Superseded by the task-workspace first slice above | The canonical re-fetch, status-aware action filtering, deleted/unverifiable states, authorization-loss redaction, result-view identity, and focus restoration from the earlier drawer implementation are retained in the task workspace. The explicit traditional “Open Maintenance” collection action remains separate. Broader maintenance entry points remain required before IW-MAINT-001–009 can be claimed complete. |
| Maintenance canonical-state safety | Implemented for inline task detail; focused component tests and static verification passed | Opening detail revalidates the canonical task before presenting mutation controls. A completed or otherwise terminal task loses stale mutation actions while conversational actions remain available; a deleted or unverifiable task is identified distinctly and its stale actions are removed until result reconciliation; and a `401`/`403` response invokes the existing property-level access-loss redaction path so private result content and all commands disappear together. A browser acceptance scenario covers access loss without leaving Ask, but was not executed in this environment. |

| Property records: Documents inline detail (Phase 3) | Implemented in code; component/contract tests and static verification passed | Selecting a document title in the `document-lookup-groups` result (`DOCUMENT_LOOKUP`'s own output) now keeps the Ask route and opens an authorization-checked canonical document detail surface inline (`DocumentResultList`/`DocumentDetail`), the fourth bespoke `GROUPED_LIST` exception. The detail exposes type, verification status, verified-at date, MIME type, formatted file size, created date, description, and last-updated date, sourced only from a fresh canonical re-fetch through a new, purpose-built `GET /api/documents/property/:propertyId/:documentId` endpoint. This endpoint deliberately does not reuse the existing `GET /api/documents/:id` route, which enforces `requireDocumentOwnership` (a CONTRIBUTOR floor for a document the requester did not personally upload) — stricter than `DOCUMENT_LOOKUP`'s own VIEWER floor, which would have made every VIEWER-role household member's inline detail click 404 on documents someone else added. The new route instead uses `propertyAuthMiddleware`, matching `DOCUMENT_LOOKUP`'s existing floor exactly. Read-only: no item `actions` are declared, since no per-item document mutation operation is registered for Ask -- this slice satisfies OPEN_INLINE_ENTITY, not MUTATE_RECORD. Canonical-state safety follows the same item-not-found-vs-access-loss error-code distinction pattern established for Inventory and HomeEvent detail (`DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND` routes to a not-found message; any other 404/401 routes to the shared access-loss redaction path). The explicit traditional "Open Documents" action remains available. Browser acceptance scenarios are present but were not executed in this environment. |

That block is removed; `property-inventory` and `property-household` are now dedicated, bounded (50-record) collections in the same style as `property-rooms`/`property-documents`. `property-inventory` carries the exact canonical `INVENTORY_ITEM` identity and is routed through the existing `InventoryResultList`/`InventoryItemDetail` pair (a third block id added to that component's dispatch set, alongside `inventory-results` and `inventory-entity-selection`) — no new backend route was needed since `getInventoryItem` already re-fetches canonically per item. `property-household` carries a new `HOUSEHOLD_MEMBER` identity through a new `HouseholdResultList`/`HouseholdMemberDetail` pair (the fifth bespoke `GROUPED_LIST` exception); its canonical re-fetch reuses the existing authorized `listHouseholdMembers` list endpoint (there is no single-member GET route) and finds the matching id in the response, so a member who left the household surfaces as a data-absence "no longer a household member" state rather than an HTTP 404 — there is no shared-status ambiguity to resolve here the way Inventory/Document detail must, since this endpoint has no per-member not-found response at all. `getPropertyRecordOverview`'s inventory section gained an `items` array (from data already being queried) and its household section changed from a `groupBy` count query to a `findMany` of full member rows (role, primary-owner flag, joined date, display name, user identity), with `roles`/`totalCount` now derived from those rows instead of a separate aggregate query; both changes are additive to the DTO and the existing traditional Property Record Overview page's own count-only usages were verified unaffected. | Property records: Warranties inline collection/detail (Phase 3) | Implemented in code; focused contract/component tests, TypeScript, and ESLint verified | Warranties previously had no Ask surface at all — `capture.warranty.confirm` remains an explicit `captureNotDirectlyRoutableResult` stub, and nothing in Property Summary listed a homeowner's warranties. `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` now emits a dedicated, expiry-ordered `property-warranties` collection (the sixth bespoke `GROUPED_LIST` exception) from a new `warranties` section added to `getPropertyRecordOverview` (`prisma.warranty.findMany({ where: { propertyId } })`, additive to the DTO). Like Household, there is no single-warranty GET, so a removed warranty surfaces as a data-absence "Warranty no longer exists" state rather than an HTTP 404. Read-only: no item `actions` are declared, since no per-item warranty mutation operation is registered for Ask — this slice satisfies OPEN_INLINE_ENTITY, not MUTATE_RECORD; renewing, purchasing, or otherwise writing a warranty remains the unbuilt `capture.warranty.confirm` journey. | Property records — remaining write/correction gap (Phase 3) | **Entity coverage complete as of September 22, 2026** — every entity below is now either delivered inline or recorded as a §9.4 exception. Per entity: **Inventory item** — twelve fields correctable inline: installed / purchased / last-serviced date, condition, brand, model, serial number, purchase cost, replacement cost, notes, room and category (see the "Property records: Inventory date corrections", "Property records: Inventory detail corrections and richer confirmation fields" and "Property records: Inventory room and category" rows); warranty and insurance-policy links remain on the traditional Inventory page. **Home event (timeline)** — title, date, summary, amount, type and importance corrections implemented (see the "Property records: Timeline event corrections" and "Property records: Event and warranty detail corrections" rows); visibility is now correctable inline (see the "Property records: Timeline event visibility" row); warranty and claim links remain uncorrectable inline; room and inventory item links are now correctable inline (see the "Property records: Timeline event links" row). **Warranty** — provider, expiry date, start date, coverage type, policy number, cost and coverage-details corrections implemented, **owner-only** (see the "Property records: Warranty corrections" row); owner-only is a deliberate rule (PR-RULE-03, §9.4); adding a warranty is now available inline ("Property records: Add a warranty"); renewal and deletion are not inline (PR-EXC-04). **Document** — `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` attaches evidence only. The traditional Documents surface exposes no metadata-edit route at all (only delete/restore), so there is no canonical correction writer to reach parity with; decided September 21, 2026: this is an approved exception (PR-EXC-01, §9.4), not an open gap. **Room** — rename, type and floor level implemented (see the "Property records: Room rename" and "Property records: Room type and floor level" rows); sort order, profile and hero image are an approved exception (PR-EXC-06, §9.4) — none has any traditional UI to reach parity with. **Household member** — decided September 21, 2026: role/removal is an approved exception, invitations remain the only inline household write (PR-EXC-02, §9.4). The four `capture.*.confirm` handlers were originally registered as `captureNotDirectlyRoutableResult` stubs (`askOrchestrator.service.ts`) with no detail surface able to start them; three of the four now have a declared entry point (`capture.event.confirm` via "Add a timeline event", `capture.warranty.confirm` via "Add a warranty", `capture.evidence.confirm` via "Attach evidence") — only `capture.fact.confirm` still has none, and that is closed below, not open. Phase 3 must not be claimed complete until each entity is either delivered inline (typed action, confirmation, canonical writer, receipt, reconciliation) or recorded as a documented §9 exception — **as of September 22, 2026 this condition is met for every entity** (§9.4 records documents, household role/removal, deletion, the PROPERTY_SUMMARY single-question path, and Room sortOrder/profile/heroImage: PR-EXC-01/02/04/05/06). This closes entity coverage specifically; it does **not** by itself mean Phase 3 is complete (see the Phase 3 status line and this table's other still-open rows — browser acceptance, adaptive presentation, the capability-card audit, and more). **Closed September 22, 2026:** (1) the pre-existing `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` single-question direct-write gap is now an approved exception (PR-EXC-05, §9.4), not an open gap; (2) `capture.evidence.confirm` is no longer conversational-extraction-only — a homeowner can now attach an uploaded file as evidence directly from a timeline event's inline detail (see the "Property records: Evidence upload" row); (3) **"adding facts inline" is closed as already-covered, not built as a new slice** — re-examination found `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE` (shipped) already re-asks about every fact in `UNKNOWN`, `CONFLICTED`, or `STALE` state across all seven areas (not only fully-missing ones), and `capture.fact.confirm`'s conversational-extraction path already lets a homeowner state any fact spontaneously and confirm it; together these are the complete "adding/updating a fact" surface, since facts (unlike warranties/events/rooms/items) are never created or deleted, only known or unknown. The one thing genuinely uncovered — correcting a fact that is already known, uncontested, and not stale, which today never surfaces as a requirement and has no UI listing individual known fact values to correct — is a distinct, much larger capability (a new browsable "facts" surface across ~7 areas, not a small extension) and was deliberately NOT scoped as a slice; it is not tracked as a Phase 3 gap unless a future decision adds it; (4) **Room `sortOrder`/`profile`/`heroImage` closed as an approved exception (PR-EXC-06, §9.4)**, not built — none has any traditional frontend surface today, so there was no existing journey to inline. |
| Property records: Evidence upload (Phase 3, add slice, implemented September 22, 2026) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 95 runtime tests in the correction-handler file (10 new for this slice, mutation-checked against two independent mutations: dropping the PRIVATE-creator-only scoping, and disabling the new USER_ADD confirm branch — both caught), whole `tests/ask` directory run (count reported once the run completes), frontend jest (`homeEventResultContinuity.test.tsx`) 10/10; not run against a real backend or database | Reuses the existing `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` operation/adapter/registries unchanged (no new operation id, no registry entries) — only a new USER_ADD branch alongside the pre-existing extraction-sibling branch, matching the design approved the same day. **Byte upload:** a new purpose-built route, `POST /api/documents/property/:propertyId/evidence-upload` (contributor floor via `propertyAuthMiddleware` + a `ROLE_RANK` check, `uploadRateLimiter`, the same `validateDocumentUpload` magic-byte/content-threat check `/analyze` uses), stores via the existing `uploadDocumentBuffer` and creates a `Document` row with no AI analysis and no Magic Scan quota consumed — deliberately not `/analyze`. **Attach:** a new bespoke `AttachEvidenceControl` on `HomeEventDetail` (shown whenever the event has any declared correction action — the same "requester can manage this event" signal `homeEventCorrectionItemActions` already encodes, not a new capability flag) opens a native file picker, uploads immediately via a new `api.uploadAskEvidence` client method, then dispatches `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` with the resulting `documentId` and the event's `entityId` through the existing item-action pipe, widened with an optional 6th `documentId` parameter (`onItemAction`/`dispatchItemAction`/`AskCapabilityPrompt['context']`/`CreateAskExecutionPayload.launchContext`, and the matching backend `CreateAskExecutionRequestSchema.launchContext.documentId` field) — every other item action across every entity type passes `undefined` for it unchanged. **Propose** (`evidenceAttachResult`, new): declared-action-only guard (operationId, surface ≠ `ASK_REFRESH`, exact canned message, `entityType === 'HOME_EVENT'`, `entityId`, `documentId` all present) before falling back to the original not-directly-routable boundary; re-verifies the event (same PRIVATE-creator-only scoping as `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT`/`HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY`) and the document (property-scoped) against live data, never trusting `launchContext`; builds a `NEEDS_CONFIRMATION` card directly, one-shot like `HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY`, no separate form step since picking/uploading the file already happened client-side. **Confirm** (`confirmCaptureEvidence`, extended): a new `parameters.captureOrigin === USER_ADD_ORIGIN` branch reads `eventId` straight from the execution's own stored parameters and skips the pre-existing extraction path's sibling-execution wait entirely (a homeowner attaching evidence to an *existing, already-confirmed* event has no sibling EVENT candidate to wait on, unlike extraction's paired EVENT+EVIDENCE candidates); writes through the same `homeEventsServiceForCapture.attachDocument` (an upsert, naturally idempotent on retry). The pre-existing extraction-sibling branch is untouched and covered by a regression test. **Boundaries:** one file per attach action; same JPEG/PNG/WEBP/PDF/10MB validation as `/analyze` (both client- and server-side); no detach/replace inline (stays within PR-EXC-01's reasoning); no multi-file batch; an upload that completes but is never confirmed leaves an orphan `Document` row scoped to the property but linked to no event — an already-accepted characteristic shared with `/analyze`'s existing behaviour, not a new one. **Browser coverage added September 22, 2026:** two scenarios now exist and pass in `ask.spec.ts` — one uses `setInputFiles` on the underlying (visually hidden) file input, confirms the upload endpoint is called before Ask ever sees a `documentId`, asserts the exact `launchContext` (entity + `documentId`, never a raw file) reaches `POST /api/ask/executions`, and confirms a zero-editable-field review card (mutation-checked: severing the `documentId` pass-through in `AskWorkspace.tsx`'s `dispatchItemAction` was confirmed to fail this exact assertion against a rebuilt production bundle); the other picks an unsupported file type and asserts the client-side rejection happens before the upload endpoint is ever called. This closes the "evidence needs a file upload" item under the Property records remaining-gap row (below) — `capture.evidence.confirm` is no longer conversational-extraction-only. |
| Property records: Inventory date corrections (Phase 3, first write slice) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, governance/registry tests updated, focused frontend component tests passed; not executed in a browser or against a live DB | New `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `inventory.item-correct` adapter, registered in the operation/command/audience/entity/trust/semantic/skill/coverage registries under the `property-record` skill, which is now autonomy 2 with WRITE effect; Concierge Home is not granted it). The inline inventory detail (`InventoryItemDetail`) shows up to three declared `MUTATE_RECORD` item actions — Correct install date / purchase date / last serviced date — only when the server declares them, which it does only for contributor-and-up on all three inventory producers (`inventory-results`, `inventory-entity-selection`, `property-inventory`). Each dispatches through the normal Ask path with exact `INVENTORY_ITEM` identity via `launchContext`. Propose builds a review card with the current value and a DATE editable field, and performs no write; the existing edit-confirmation path (`editInventoryItemCorrectConfirmation`, version-and-status guarded) saves the corrected date; confirm rechecks a `sha256(id:updatedAt)` freshness version, writes through the canonical `inventoryService.updateItem` (same validation as the Inventory page), and reconciles `INVENTORY_LOOKUP`/`PROPERTY_SUMMARY` via `ASK_MUTATION_IMPACT_MAP`. A retry of an already-applied execution is recognised by value equality and is not reported as a conflict. **Boundaries:** `updateItem` has no compare-and-swap, so a write landing between the confirm-time freshness check and the update is not detected; if no date is supplied and none is recorded the field starts empty and confirm rejects until a date is entered; clearing a date is not offered; no item-level rollback beyond a further correction. Room, document, warranty, household-member and timeline-event writes remain open. No browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. |
| Property records: Timeline event corrections (Phase 3, write slice 2) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, governance/registry tests updated, focused frontend tests passed; not executed in a browser or against a live DB | New `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `home-event.correct` adapter, same registries and `property-record` skill as the inventory slice; not granted to Concierge Home). The inline event detail (`HomeEventDetail`) shows *Correct title* / *Correct date* item actions only when the server declares them (contributor-and-up, on `inventory-history` and `property-recent-events`). Propose lists only current, non-deleted events the requester may see (PRIVATE events only to their creator), writes nothing, and builds a review card with the current value and an editable field; the existing version-and-status-guarded edit path saves the value; confirm rechecks `sha256(id:revision)` freshness and visibility and writes through `HomeEventsService.updateHomeEvent`. **Identity:** `updateHomeEvent` supersedes the row and creates a replacement with a **new id**, so the receipt/artifact carries the replacement id, source lists refresh through `ASK_MUTATION_IMPACT_MAP` (`INVENTORY_LOOKUP`, `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`), and an inline detail opened on the old id resolves to "Event no longer exists" until the refreshed list is used. Retries are guarded by the same `ask-correction:{executionId}` key as `confirmCaptureEvent` (pre-check plus `HOME_EVENT_NOT_FOUND`/`P2002` winner recovery). A date correction records `EXACT_DATE` precision (disclosed on the card); RANGE-precision events are declined for date correction. **Boundaries:** summary, amount, type, importance, visibility, links and clearing values are not correctable inline; the traditional timeline's `correctionReason` prompt is replaced by a fixed "Corrected through Ask after homeowner confirmation" reason; no browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. Room, warranty, document and household-member writes remain open. |
| Property records: Inventory detail corrections and richer confirmation fields (Phase 3, slice 5) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 30 runtime tests, routing guard and full `tests/ask` gate, frontend jest 82/82 and Ask Playwright 50/50; not run against a real backend or database | **Confirmation contract:** the editable-field types grow from `DATE`/`TEXT` to `DATE`/`TEXT`/`TEXTAREA`/`SELECT`/`MONEY` (a `SELECT` carries its `options`; a `MONEY` value is dollars with up to two decimals). The values stay strings, each operation's edit handler owns field validation, the API edge's per-edit limit rose from 500 to 2000 characters (long notes), and the item-action limit on a list row rose from 3 to 12. The card renders a dropdown, a `$` amount input and a multi-line box, and shows a `SELECT` value by its label. **Inventory corrections generalised** from three date fields to ten: the dates, `condition` (New/Good/Fair/Poor/Unknown), `brand` and `model` (≤80), `serialNo` (≤120), `purchaseCostCents` and `replacementCostCents` (entered in dollars, written as integer cents, ≤ $10,000,000), and `notes` (≤2000). The fields written are `brand`/`model`/`serialNo`, the ones the traditional form edits (the model also carries a parallel `manufacturer`/`modelNumber`/`serialNumber` set that Ask does not write). Blank values are rejected, so nothing can be cleared inline. The inline detail shows up to three corrections as buttons and folds a longer list behind one *Correct a detail* disclosure. Values compare in canonical form (850 equals 850.00) for the already-applied retry check, and the receipt shows money as `$1,200.00` and a condition by its label. **Shipped gap fixed:** the traditional item `PATCH` controller (not the service) marks five analyses stale — coverage, item coverage, replace-or-repair, risk premium and do-nothing — and the date correction shipped earlier called only the service, so a correction silently left those analyses stale; every inventory correction that writes now repeats all five, and none is called when the value was already applied. **Routing:** the new fields extend the explicit correction-verb pattern, which requires the word *inventory* or *appliance* (not a bare *item* or *system*) so *"edit the notes on this checklist item"* is not captured; `tests/ask/correctionRouting.test.js` now keeps the routing guard permanently (35 read questions that must not reach a correction command, 18 correction phrasings that must). **Boundaries:** room, category, links, `manufacturer`/`modelNumber`/`serialNumber`, UPC/SKU and currency are not correctable inline; no clearing of values; no browser scenario against a live backend. The same field types are not yet used by the event, warranty or room corrections. |
| Property records: Event and warranty detail corrections (Phase 3, slice 6) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 39 runtime tests, routing guard and full `tests/ask` gate, frontend jest 82/82 and Ask Playwright 52/52; not run against a real backend or database | The richer confirmation fields (dropdown, money, multi-line) now serve the event and warranty corrections as well as inventory. **Timeline event:** summary (≤500), amount (dollars, written as a number; currency unchanged), type and importance join title and date. The type list omits `VERIFIED_RESOLUTION`, which the system creates when a guided plan completes; an event that already has that type cannot have its type changed (its other fields stay correctable), and a date-range event still cannot have its date corrected — both are declined at propose time with a clear reason and re-checked at confirm. The confirmation now states that a correction records a new revision and that an evidence-verified event returns to pending confirmation until verified again (that downgrade already happened; it was not disclosed). **Warranty (owner-only, unchanged):** start date, coverage type, policy number, cost and coverage details join provider and expiry date; the two dates are kept in order (an expiry before the start, or a start on or after the expiry, is refused); cost is written as a number and compared in canonical form (450 equals 450.00). Every write is a narrowed single-field patch through the existing writers. **Shared code:** validation, canonical form and display for the five field kinds live in one module (`askCorrectionFields.ts`) used by inventory, event and warranty, replacing three inline copies; the frontend's correction buttons and *Correct a detail* disclosure are one component (`CorrectionActions`) used by the inventory, event and warranty details. **Routing:** the explicit correction-verb patterns extend to the new fields and stay verb-only; the permanent routing guard now covers 45 read questions that must not reach a correction command and 27 correction phrasings that must. **Boundaries:** event visibility and links, warranty linked item and category-specific fields, and clearing any value are not correctable inline; room type and floor level still are not (the operation is a rename); no browser scenario against a live backend. |
| Property records: Add a timeline event and a room (Phase 3, add slices 2 and 3) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 53 runtime tests, routing guard, governance tripwires, frontend jest 84/84 and Ask Playwright 54/54; not run against a real backend or database | **Add a timeline event:** a contributor-and-up *Add a timeline event* `START_WORKFLOW` action on the Property Summary events list pins `CAPTURE_EVENT_CONFIRM`, with the same guard as the warranty add (only the declared action starts the form; an `ASK_REFRESH` re-run of a pending extraction-created confirmation and a bare message keep the not-routable boundary). The extraction edit path deliberately excludes the date, so an added event has its own form (`CAPTURE_EVENT_ADD`: title, type, date, details, amount, provider). Submission validates (title 3–140; type from the list without the system-created `VERIFIED_RESOLUTION`; date `YYYY-MM-DD`, **not in the future** because a timeline records what happened, with one day of tolerance for timezone skew; amount ≥ 0; unknown fields refused) and builds the review card with the same parameters extraction produces (`datePrecision` `EXACT_DATE`, `attribution` `FIRSTHAND`, the existing capture channel), so the existing `confirmCaptureEvent` / `createHomeEvent` writer handles it unchanged, keyed on the execution id. The confirmation says *"You entered these details"* rather than the extraction copy, the form stays available so the entry can be changed, and each resubmission raises the confirmation version so a stale card can never match. The events block now also appears on a home with no confirmed events, so a contributor still has the entry point. **Add a room:** a new `ROOM_CREATE` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `room.create` adapter under the `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home) reached only from a declared *Add a room* action on the rooms list, with no message pattern and no fuzzy retrieval (it is in `ASK_INTERNAL_OPERATION_IDS`). The form asks for a type, a **required name** (the service would otherwise derive a default from the type, which could silently collide) and an optional floor level (−5 to 50). Propose checks the name against the live rooms and, if it is used, returns the form again with what was typed and never offers the confirmation; confirm re-checks, creates through `inventoryService.createRoom`, and repeats the three stale-analysis markers the traditional `POST` controller calls (coverage, risk premium, do-nothing). `createRoom` has no idempotency key, so a lease-reclaim retry is recognised by an existing same-named room created at or after the execution began (reported as *Room already added*, no second write); a same-named room from before the execution, or a lost race on the unique name, is refused with a clear message. **Boundaries:** an added event cannot be linked to a room or inventory item inline and is always an exact date (no month/year/range precision); a room's profile, sort order and hero image are not settable; inventory items are added through their own slice (see the "Property records: Add an inventory item" row); no browser scenario against a live backend. |
| Property records: Add an inventory item (Phase 3, add slice 4; free-text refinement October 3, 2026) | Implemented in code; static and focused validation recorded in the implementation handoff; not run against a real backend or database | A contributor-and-up *Add an item* `START_WORKFLOW` action on the Property Summary inventory collection and inventory lookup list, plus a narrow deterministic command beginning with **add**, **record**, or **create**, selects `INVENTORY_ITEM_CREATE` (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `inventory.create` adapter under the `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home). The operation remains in `ASK_INTERNAL_OPERATION_IDS`, so fuzzy semantic retrieval cannot select this write. Free text such as “add microwave to kitchen” pre-fills a meaningful item name, a conservative category inference, and a room only when the normalized room phrase exactly matches one of the property's canonical rooms. Generic nouns such as “junk”, “stuff”, or “item” are not accepted as an item identity: the form asks for the specific item while retaining a valid room. An unknown room such as “dirty” produces a targeted recorded-room choice and retains any meaningful parsed item; it is never guessed or created. An `ASK_REFRESH` re-run and unrelated text remain non-routable. The form collects **name** (required, ≤120), **category** (14 values), **room** (the property's recorded rooms, up to 50, or explicit *No room (whole-home)*) and optional brand and model (≤80). Propose and confirm enforce the writer's rules: room required for Appliance, Furniture, Electronics and Other; water heater is plumbing; only one record per major appliance. Confirm writes through `inventoryService.createItem` and repeats the three traditional stale-analysis markers. The form context version hashes canonical room ids, so room-set drift causes a context conflict. The receipt names the resolved room and offers item-specific follow-ups for purchase date, condition, and brand/model. **Boundaries:** purchase date, costs, condition and notes remain corrections after creation through `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT`; serial number, warranty/insurance links, tags, barcode fields, documents and photos remain on the traditional Inventory page. |
| Property records: Property Summary completeness rows (Phase 3, add slice 5) | Implemented in code; backend and frontend TypeScript clean, 13 new runtime tests for the handlers (eight mutation-checked) and 8 new evaluator/contract tests (three mutation-checked), whole `tests/ask` directory 837 tests / 836 pass / 0 fail / 1 skipped, frontend jest (`src/components/ask` + `src/features/ask`) 114/114, Ask Playwright 57/57 (two new scenarios; the skip control is mutation-checked); not run against a real backend or database | Each incomplete area on the Property Summary (Core, Location, Structure, Exterior, Responsibility, Systems, Safety) has a contributor-and-up *Fill in missing details* item action that starts the new `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `property-context.area-capture` adapter under the `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home; non-routable, with one fixed message per area and the row id as the area). The Rooms and Inventory rows reuse the existing *Add a room* / *Add an item* actions; rows for read-only areas (risk, financial, coverage and so on) and viewers keep the link, which now carries the area's page anchor. **Contract:** a new versioned Property Context contract, `PROPERTY_RECORD_SUMMARY:CAPTURE_AREA` 1.0, whose enhancement entries select on the `scope` operation input and reuse each fact's existing capture definition (structured profiles first, because one answer then fills several facts); `VIEW_SUMMARY` and its receipts are unchanged. A registry test fails if a writable fact in the seven areas is missing from it. **Confirmation (IW-CONF-001):** every answer follows form → review card → confirm → receipt; the form is a proposal and writes nothing. The card names the property, each fact and value, and every area affected; the write is `captureFeatureContext` (version-checked, idempotent on execution + requirement + review version), and the receipt names every fact and area actually written and reports *Already saved* on a retry. **Skip:** *Skip for now*, and an answer that is "not sure" for everything, save nothing and are kept in the execution's server-controlled parameters (`skipFactKeys`); a contract-scoped evaluator change honours them only to choose the next question. A skipped fact is never treated as known, never bypasses capture validation (a submitted answer must still be the live evaluated question), and never comes from the client. A refresh re-asks with the execution's own stored skips; a fresh start from a row begins with none; the receipt's *Continue* carries the skips of that same workflow. The same evaluator change leaves out facts that do not apply to the property (for example a private fence for a townhouse). **End state:** never "all done": *No more questions in this session*, the remaining completeness from the same calculation the row uses, how many details were skipped or marked not sure, and the read-only or derived details that need another surface (county, map location, climate region, derived roof age, installed types) by name. **Boundaries:** per-answer confirmation only (batching is a candidate if the flow proves cumbersome); no persisted "not now"; read-only facts cannot be filled here, so an area such as Location may not reach 100% from Ask alone. **Resolved September 22, 2026 (was an open gap):** the pre-existing single-question capture on the Property Summary (`PROPERTY_SUMMARY`, contract `VIEW_SUMMARY`) is still a direct write without a review and confirmation card. This is now a recorded approved exception (PR-EXC-05, §9.4) rather than an open gap: `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE`, built in this same slice, already puts the seven fillable completeness areas on the full confirmation path, so widening the older single-question path to match would duplicate that coverage. No code changed. |
| Property records: Warranty corrections (Phase 3, write slice 3) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, governance/registry tests updated, focused frontend tests added; not executed in a browser or against a live DB | New `WARRANTY_CORRECT` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `warranty.correct` adapter, same registries and `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home). Whether household members may edit each other's warranties is a product/authorization decision this slice deliberately does not make. **Narrowed write:** the traditional `PATCH` route spreads its entire request body into the update; Ask writes only the one confirmed field. The edit-confirmation handler signature now also receives the acting `userId` (needed for the ownership check). Freshness is `sha256(id:updatedAt)` rechecked at confirm, an already-applied value is recognised on retry, and `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` is reconciled. **Boundaries:** no compare-and-swap in `updateWarranty` (a write between the freshness check and the update is not detected); other fields (category, policy number, cost, start date, coverage details, linked item), renewal and deletion are not correctable inline; no browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. Room and household-member writes remain open; Document correction is blocked on the absence of any canonical writer. |

The room id is stable across a rename, so an open inline detail stays valid; `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` and `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` (item rows show the room name) are reconciled. Freshness is `sha256(id:updatedAt)` at confirm, with the same already-applied retry recognition; no compare-and-swap. **Boundaries:** rename only; deleting a room, changing its type and clearing anything are not offered; no browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. Household-member writes remain open; Document correction remains blocked on the absence of any canonical writer. |

The operation id, adapter, registries, skill binding and routing are kept, so nothing already registered moved; the proposal's `field` says which one, and a proposal stored before this change (no `field`) still confirms as a rename. **Type** is a dropdown of the ten room types (the same set the add form uses). **Floor level** is a whole number from −5 to 50 (0 is the ground floor, a negative number is below ground); it is trimmed and written in canonical form ("01" is 1), and it must be a plain integer. Each writes through `inventoryService.updateRoom` with a patch narrowed to that one field, then repeats the three stale-analysis markers the traditional `PATCH` controller calls; freshness is the room's update time as for rename, and an already-applied value is reported as *Already corrected* with no second write. The room detail offers *Rename room*, *Change room type* and *Change floor level* (three, so they stay inline). Routing: the two new canned messages and free phrasings such as "change the floor level of the guest room" reach the command through an extended explicit-verb pattern; the word "rename" always means the name (so a room called "Floor 2 office" is not misread), and read questions such as "what floor is the bedroom on?" or "what type of room is the office?" never reach it (added to the routing guard). **Boundaries:** the floor level cannot be cleared inline (the edit API does not accept an empty value), and sort order, profile and hero image stay on the traditional Rooms page. |
| Property records: Timeline event visibility (Phase 3, write slice 7) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 11 new runtime tests in the correction-handler file (5 mutation-checked) plus a new source-shape test file (2 more mutations caught there), routing guard extended, whole `tests/ask` directory 858 tests / 857 pass / 0 fail / 1 skipped, Ask Playwright full suite 60/60 (one new scenario; no frontend source change needed); not run against a real backend or database | A new `HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `home-event.visibility` adapter under the `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home) changes who can see a `HomeEvent` (Private / Household / Resale pack) through the existing `setVisibility` writer. Moving between Household and Resale pack needs no special permission. **Consent copy:** choosing Resale pack shows a consent line naming buyers and listing agents explicitly (Resale pack feeds resale and buyer briefs); every other choice uses the plain consent line. Unlike every other field on this operation, a raw id would mean nothing to a homeowner, so these two are never message-extracted from free text — the confirmation card always pre-selects the event's current link (or "No room"/"No item") and the dropdown is the only way to change it. Writes through the same `updateHomeEvent` supersede as every other field; `updateHomeEvent` itself already validates a room/inventory-item id belongs to the property, and Ask's own value check re-verifies the same property scoping before offering or accepting a value, so a stale, deleted, or cross-property id is refused with a clear message rather than silently accepted or silently rejected by the writer with a generic error. The dropdown's option list, and the id-existence check, are both re-fetched fresh at propose and at edit time, never trusted from an earlier response. **Boundaries:** claim, warranty and expense links, and the parent-event link, remain out of scope (recorded, not a gap — choosing a sensible parent event needs different UI, and the other three links reach into domains this slice did not review). | Property records: Inventory room and category (Phase 3, write slice 9) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 9 new runtime tests plus 2 new source-shape tests in the correction-handler files (7 mutation-checked), routing guard extended, whole `tests/ask` directory 874 tests / 873 pass / 0 fail / 1 skipped, Ask Playwright full suite 62/62 (one new scenario); not run against a real backend or database | `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` gains two more correctable fields: **room** (a dropdown of the property's own rooms, capped at 50, plus an explicit "No room" to unlink) and **category** (a dropdown of all 14 categories). Writes through the same `inventoryService.updateItem` every other field uses. `updateItem` itself already enforces two combined-state rules whenever category or room changes (a room is required for Appliance, Furniture, Electronics and Other categories; a water-heater-named item cannot take the Appliance category) — Ask pre-checks the same rules (reusing the identical `ROOM_REQUIRED_CATEGORIES` set and water-heater name check the create and correct-room-type slices already use) before offering or accepting a value, so a doomed confirmation is never shown, and re-checks them again at confirm and at edit against live data. | Property records: Add a warranty (Phase 3, first add slice) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 6 runtime tests + governance suites green, frontend jest and one browser scenario passing; not run against a real backend or database | A contributor-and-up **"Add a warranty"** `START_WORKFLOW` action on the Property Summary warranties list pins `CAPTURE_WARRANTY_CONFIRM`. The previously-unrouted propose handler now returns an empty capture form **only** for that declared action (`launchContext.operationId` is the operation, the surface is not `ASK_REFRESH`, and the canned message matches); every other call — a refresh re-run of a pending extraction-created confirmation, a message that merely names the operation — still returns the original not-directly-routable boundary, so a pending candidate can never be replaced by an empty form. The form reuses the existing `CAPTURE_WARRANTY_EDIT` capture key, so submitting goes through the existing edit-before-confirm path (same validation, ISO-datetime normalisation and parameter shape the confirm handler already reads) and confirm writes through the existing `captureWarranty` writer keyed on the execution id. A `captureOrigin: USER_ADD` parameter switches the confirmation copy to *"You entered these details"*, because the extraction path's *"Cozy noticed you mentioned…"* would be false for a typed entry; the extraction path's copy is unchanged. The form is `WORKFLOW_INPUT`, so its button reads *Continue to review*, not *Save*. The warranties list now renders workflow actions through the shared `ActionLink` (as the maintenance list does). **Ownership caveat:** `captureWarranty` assigns the new warranty to the *property's* homeowner profile, so a contributor who adds a warranty cannot later correct it under the owner-only rule (PR-RULE-03); this is inherited behaviour of the existing writer, not new. **Boundaries:** events, facts and evidence cannot yet be added inline; no inventory-item or room add; no browser scenario against a live backend. |
| Routing safety for the correction commands (found and fixed September 21, 2026) | Fixed; full `tests/ask` directory green (783 tests, 782 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped) | **A real regression was introduced by the earlier correction slices and is fixed here.** The semantic router scores by lexical, phrase and concept similarity, and a hard negative can only offset a positive when its evidence exceeds the positive's by 0.05, which is impossible when the positive is already saturated. The four correction operations' examples therefore attracted ordinary read questions: *"When was my water heater last serviced?"* routed to `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` at 0.997 confidence, and *"What is the warranty expiration date for the roof?"* routed to `WARRANTY_CORRECT`. Fix: `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT`, `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT`, `WARRANTY_CORRECT` and `ROOM_RENAME` are now in `ASK_INTERNAL_OPERATION_IDS`, so fuzzy retrieval never considers them; they are reached only by an explicit correction-verb pattern or by the declared item action a homeowner clicked (which pins the operation). A 34-message battery (24 read questions that must not reach them, 10 correction phrasings that must) passes with no problems. Trade-off: a correction phrased with no correction verb (e.g. *"that date on my dishwasher record is wrong"*) is no longer recognised and falls back to ordinary guidance. **Process finding:** the earlier slices were pushed after running only subsets of `tests/ask`; the full directory then showed six failing tests on `main` (an adapter-count tripwire, three skill-catalog/health expectations, two routing-calibration tests, one caused by my routing fixtures shifting index-derived fixture ids). All are fixed; the legitimate expectation changes (the ASK skill catalog now lists the four commands, the "all adapters disabled" health case must disable them too, adapter count 67→71) preserve each test's original intent. The direct-answer certification objective (pass rate ≥ 0.95) tolerates at most four failing operations out of 81 and already had three unrelated failures (`BUYER_PLAN_STATUS`, `BUYER_TASK_CREATE`, `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS`), which remain and are not part of this work. Also corrected: the answer-trust policy whitelisted only `open-property-record` for `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`, so every Property Summary reported a failed action-applicability check (the block-level links and the new Add action survived only because that repair does not strip `GROUPED_LIST` actions); the real action ids are now whitelisted and a synthetic Property Summary passes trust cleanly. |
| Maintenance inline create workflow | Implemented in code; contract test, focused UI test, TypeScript, ESLint, and production build passed | “Create maintenance task” and “Create a task” are typed response actions that start the existing `MAINTENANCE_TASK_CREATE` capture, review, confirmation, canonical write, and receipt inside Ask Cozy. The source maintenance result is carried through the workflow and refreshed after success; refresh failure is disclosed without inviting a duplicate write. Maintenance Setup and the canonical Maintenance task page remain secondary traditional-navigation choices. A browser acceptance scenario is present but was not executed in this environment. |
| Maintenance inline collection pagination | Implemented in code; focused backend/UI tests and static verification passed | Truncated maintenance sections expose Previous/Next server-page controls inside the same stable Ask result. Paging carries the exact effective query and per-section offset in durable view state, re-queries the full canonical task set, preserves true counts, replaces the prior live result rather than duplicating it, and keeps “View all in Maintenance” as an explicit secondary traditional choice. A browser acceptance scenario is present but was not executed in this environment. |
| Contextual information panel | Foundations implemented for `EVIDENCE`, `ASSUMPTIONS`, `LIMITATION`, exact claim-to-source mapping, two authoritative `OUTPUT_ARTIFACTS` producers, and one authoritative `RELATED_RECORDS` producer; focused contract/component tests and static verification passed | Current response context collapses into one execution-scoped control rather than separate evidence, assumption, artifact, and relationship cards. It opens beside the conversation as a right contextual panel when sufficiently wide and as a dismissible sheet on constrained/mobile layouts, preserving the Ask route, conversation, and composer. Ownership-cost evidence carries server-declared mappings to exact table row identities; dangling references are rejected. Confirmed maintenance-task creation declares its exact canonical task output. Confirmed quote-comparison creation now declares the exact canonical comparison workspace returned by `getOrCreateQuoteComparisonWorkspace`, including whether the record was created or an existing open workspace was reused, its type-specific status and original creation time, and its property-scoped destination. The schema prevents maintenance-only lifecycle values from being applied to quote workspaces and vice versa. Confirmed conversational evidence attachment declares the exact canonical document-to-home-event relationship returned by the authorized, idempotent `HomeEventDocument` write; the client does not infer these artifacts or relationships from labels, URLs, or neighboring content. Completion, reuse, no-provider-selection, and attachment outcomes remain visible in the conversation, while optional canonical navigation is progressively disclosed in context and property-checked by the trust pipeline. Open-panel identity remains bounded per session/property, and archived or superseded responses retain inline disclosures. Broader governed related-record and output-artifact producer coverage remains required before IW-SHELL-006 can be claimed complete. Browser acceptance scenarios are present but were not executed in this environment. |
| Property records: correction browser acceptance (Phase 3) | Two scenarios written and **executed** in desktop Chromium against the mocked Ask acceptance API (`e2e/ask`, `playwright.ask.config.ts`); mobile, the other correction slices and any live-backend run are not covered | (1) A contributor opens a Property Summary timeline event's inline detail, clicks *Correct title*, and the request carries the exact `HOME_EVENT` identity via `launchContext`; the confirmation card's new **TEXT** editable field is edited and saved as a new confirmation version, consent is given, and the confirm request and the completion receipt stay in the conversation without leaving Ask. (2) A result whose items declare no correction actions (the shape a VIEWER receives) renders no correction control. **What this proves and does not:** it verifies the frontend against fixture responses — action rendering, identity dispatch, the TEXT input, the edit/confirm request bodies — not the server's role gating, freshness checks, supersede semantics or writes, which the browser scenarios do not cover. **Backend runtime coverage (added September 21, 2026):** `tests/ask/correctionHandlersRuntime.test.js` executes the *real registered confirm handlers* for inventory, event, warranty and room correction (15 tests) with `prisma` and the canonical writers replaced by recording fakes (the fake `prisma` throws on any model or method a test did not declare; no database is touched). It asserts the actual behaviour: narrowed single-field write patches; the warranty owner check (a CONTRIBUTOR who did not add the warranty is refused with `ASK_PERMISSION_REQUIRED`); stale-version, deleted-record, blank/short-value, expiry-before-start and room-name-clash rejections that perform no write; already-applied retries that do not write again; the timeline event's `ask-correction:{executionId}` idempotency key, replay to the earlier replacement, replacement id on the receipt, `EXACT_DATE` precision on a date correction, RANGE-precision decline, and PRIVATE-event visibility (creator only); and that a room rename repeats the controller's three stale-analysis markers. Two deliberate breakages of the real handlers (removing the warranty owner check and the room name-clash check) were each caught by exactly the matching test. **Still not covered at runtime:** the propose handlers, the edit-confirmation handlers, the real writers (`updateItem`, `updateRoom`, `updateWarranty`, `updateHomeEvent` are stubbed, so their own behaviour and transactions are unverified), and everything against a real database. The first run found two defects in the scenarios themselves (an over-broad selector that matched the response-level *Correct home information* feedback control, and a mocked edit response using a different session id than the page, which the card rejected); neither was an application defect. **Suite repair (September 21, 2026):** verifying showed the full `e2e/ask` suite was already red — 17 of 39 desktop scenarios failed on the original files. It was repaired and both projects now pass (**44 passed / 0 failed**: 39 desktop, 5 mobile, run against a fresh production build). The failures were: (a) **two real application defects the mocks had been hiding** — `MaintenanceResultList` rendered only `href` actions, so the *Create a task* `START_WORKFLOW` action the backend emits for contributors was silently dropped from the maintenance list (fixed by routing non-href actions through the shared `ActionLink`), and the mobile response-context sheet drew the panel's own text *Close* button on top of the sheet's built-in X, two overlapping close controls (fixed with `showCloseButton={false}` inside the sheet; the desktop side panel keeps its button); and (b) **fixture/assertion drift** — responses hardcoding a session id the page no longer uses (now adopted from the create request), a `GROUPED_LIST` fixture missing the `filters` field the real backend always emits (which crashed the renderer), links that now carry a `?backTo=` return context, `getByRole` name matching being a case-insensitive substring, sessionStorage keys that embed the generated session id, a comparison layout control that now defaults to *Auto*, the current access-loss copy, and a mocked recent-session title that no longer matched the restored conversation's question. Scope limits still apply: the scenarios run against a mocked Ask API, so they verify the frontend only. **Correction coverage (added the same day):** browser scenarios now exist and pass for inventory install-date correction (the **DATE** editable field), owner warranty expiry-date correction (DATE) and room rename (**TEXT**), alongside the timeline-event title scenario — each opens the record's inline detail from the Property Summary, clicks the declared action, asserts the exact entity identity in `launchContext`, edits the confirmation field, asserts the exact edit and confirm request bodies (`{ value }`, version 1 then 2, `consentConfirmed`), and sees the receipt without leaving Ask (full suite: 47 passed / 0 failed across desktop and mobile; the identity assertion was confirmed to fail when deliberately broken). Still without browser scenarios: the *non-owner* warranty limitation, the propose-time name-conflict and RANGE-precision declines, freshness/stale-confirm conflicts, and any run against the real backend. Inventory, warranty and room correction flows and the DATE editable path have no browser scenario yet. |

Built as a genuine platform capability, not a one-off: `AdaptiveTableBlock.tsx` gained an optional `rowDetail` prop (`{ activeRowId, onRowClick, renderDetail }`) that both its `DataTable` and `LabeledCards` renderers honor — when present, each row's first column becomes a `<button>` (the same `data-ask-detail-trigger`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` convention `GROUPED_LIST`'s own detail rows use) and the returned detail node mounts below the table/cards. A new `TableBlock.tsx` dispatcher (registered in `registry.tsx` in place of the previous inline arrow function, mirroring `GroupedListBlock.tsx`'s own structure) routes by block id — `capital-timeline-table` opts into the capability via a new `CapitalWindowDetail` component; every other `TABLE` block keeps today's plain, non-interactive rendering, so nothing changes for `ownership-costs` or any other existing `TABLE` producer. `CapitalWindowDetail` follows the exact `ReserveAllocationDetail` shape (open/close/history, loading/not-found/access-lost states, focus management) and the same list-scan exception: `capitalTimelineApi.getLatestTimeline` (already existing and already typed — no new API client method needed) has no per-item GET, only the whole analysis, so a window removed by a later timeline re-run is a data-absence "no longer exists" state, not an HTTP 404. The two confirmed writes do refresh it through `reconcileAskExecutionSideEffects`. A done event shows only feedback, plus a note to change it in Home Event Radar. **Not in this slice:** task create-or-link (`/radar/events/:matchId/actions/:actionCode/task`) and notification preferences (both built in v1.41; see the next row), severity/impact/confidence/attention filters (not surfaced on the traditional page either), and browser coverage of the two confirmed card flows. The form has every control on the traditional "Radar notifications" card: on/off, categories, channels, minimum severity and impact, delivery timing, quiet hours, the extreme-safety override and timezone. It is prefilled from the current settings or the defaults and validated by the traditional PUT route's own `updateRadarNotificationPreferencesBodySchema`. The review names which settings change. The context version is the stored row's identity plus its `updatedAt`, checked at submission and again at confirm. Role floor: CONTRIBUTOR, **stricter than the traditional route**, which has no floor, because domain commands have no VIEWER floor. A viewer can still change their settings on the Home Event Radar page. **Platform additions:** a `TIME` (24-hour `HH:mm`) scalar capture field type (`CaptureFieldControl`, both apps' type unions, and property-context capture validation). The form kept next to a review card gets a new `requirementId` for each entry. The inline capture card is keyed by that id and holds one idempotency key per mount, so reusing the id would turn a changed resubmission into an `ASK_CAPTURE_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. An idempotent replay of either radar form returns the stored execution instead of re-routing the canned message. **Found, not fixed (pre-existing, outside this slice):** "Add a room" and "Add an item" keep their form under a constant `requirementId`, so they appear to have the same resubmission conflict and the same replay re-route. This was not verified in a browser. **Not in this slice:** browser coverage of the notification-settings flow and of the Mark done and Feedback confirmation cards. Unlinking a task is not offered, because the traditional page has no unlink. |

A row action for a task that had since closed could therefore have proposed completing a *different* task (the confirmation would name it, but nothing stopped a quick confirm). It now takes the launched task id (`launchContext.entityType` `BUYER_TASK`); if that task is no longer open it returns `BUYER_TASK_NO_LONGER_OPEN` and never falls back. Free-text completion without a launched task is unchanged. **Built:** blocking-task rows of `buyer-deadlines-list` carry identity and a declared "Mark complete" (`BUYER_TASK_COMPLETE`) for contributors and up; the row is built by an exported `buyerDeadlineTaskRow`. The list renders through a new `BuyerTaskResultList`, the twelfth bespoke `GROUPED_LIST` exception, which keeps the lane filter chips. A task opens inline from `GET /home-buyer-tasks/properties/:id/tasks/:taskId`, the Buyer Plan's own task read: status, priority, due date, phase, whether it blocks closing, estimated cost, notes. That route answers a missing task and a denied property with the same uncoded 404; the service's messages tell them apart ("Task not found…" is only reached after the access check), so a removed task reads as removed and a denial redacts. "Mark complete" shows only while the **live** task is pending, in progress or blocked and applies to the purchase; the completion carries the source list for in-place refresh. Milestone rows stay links to the Buyer Plan. The `buyer-closing` card now launches inline ("What is due before closing?", test-proven to route to `BUYER_DEADLINES`). **Not in this cut (stays on the Buyer Plan, linked from the detail):** reopen and restore, "not needed after close", assignment, rescheduling (Ask's `BUYER_TASK_UPDATE` can reschedule and assign by free text, but it too matches by text and was not given launched-id targeting here), task creation, milestones, and the other 13 readiness reads. **Found, not fixed:** `buyerTaskUpdateResult` has the same text-match-then-only-open-task fallback as complete had; it needs the same launched-id targeting before any update row action is declared. |
