It is a pure read: the lookups only fill in-memory caches, and the page's room recommendations (generate, save, dismiss, add to home) and plant or zone edits stay on the page. **Source honesty (service change):** every weather signal reads false when its source does not answer, so an outage looked the same as calm weather. `getOutlook` now also returns `sourceStatus` (`OK`, `UNAVAILABLE` or `NO_LOCATION` for weather, air quality, drought and hardiness). The change is additive and the page ignores it. The answer: a summary of care changes due now or soon, plant and garden-zone counts and the hardiness zone, which says "No weather-driven care changes are recommended" only when the weather source answered and otherwise says they could not be checked; a limitation naming each unavailable source (air quality, drought and weather, with "no map location for this home" when there are no coordinates) and saying that does not mean nothing is needed; a limitation when outdoor care is not covered, with the reason from the page's own applicability policy (no private outdoor space, association or landlord responsible, or unknown); care changes grouped do now, do soon and routine, each with the location, triggers, adjusted check cadence, guidance and placement warning; garden zone plans; and a boundary that this is general care guidance, not a plant diagnosis. Plant notes are never included. A home with no plants or zones gets "No plants or garden zones tracked yet", never an all-clear. The page has no care-tab or per-plant deep link (the Care tab only opens for a weather-context or Environment Report launch), so every row links to the page. **Page gap found, not fixed here:** when the weather source is down, the page's own Care tab shows "No weather-driven care changes are recommended right now." It can now read `sourceStatus` to say weather could not be checked. **Routing:** a deterministic pattern (plant advisor, plant care, my or our plants, watering my plants, garden zones, my garden) with an exclusion for choosing new plants ("Which plants should I add to my garden?", "Recommend plants for my garden") and power plants. Card launch: "Show my plant care outlook". **Calibration:** plant phrasing scores low in the retriever, and "plant" contains "plan", so plant text scores close to the Home Continuity Plan. Every candidate fixture with a weak competitor pushed "Show my inspecion findings" to clarification (the v1.53 lesson again). Fixture 080 ("Do my house plants need anything with this weather?", 0.506 vs `HOME_DIGITAL_WILL` 0.678; the deterministic pattern routes it) was chosen by sweeping pairs against every Skill routing case. It keeps every case resolved and moves "Show my inspecion findings" from 0.4322 to 0.4330. No existing calibration row changed. The certified answer first failed relevance (it named a room, which matched the operation's own room-recommendation hard negative), so an answer positive in forecast-care wording was added. The full relevance pass rate went from 0.9592 to 0.9694. **Follow-up:** a `?tab=care` deep link; the page's outage wording; logging watering from Ask. |

The pages ignore both fields. **Page gaps found, not fixed here:** Oracle silently leaves out appliances with no recorded age, and with none left the page says "No Appliance Data"; Budget silently assumes a 10-year-old home when the year built is missing; both services throw plain errors for non-owners, which the pages show as 500s. "Which appliances are likely to fail soon?" used to reach `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` through its broad "which … appliances" pattern and now reaches this operation. Budget: budget planner; maintenance, upkeep or repair budget; budget forecast; "how much should/do/will we budget/set aside/save … for maintenance/upkeep/repairs". Four competitor rows were re-derived because the new operations share intrinsic words with them: 005 and 021 to `APPLIANCE_FAILURE_RISK` ("equipment", "appliance"), 008 to `MAINTENANCE_BUDGET_FORECAST` ("budget"), and 087 to `MAINTENANCE_BUDGET_FORECAST`, a tie far below its expected score. Expected scores are unchanged. Fixtures 093 ("Which of our appliances are closest to failing?", 0.270 vs `HOME_STATUS_BOARD` 0.262) and 094 ("What does the budget planner say we will spend on maintenance?", 0.728 vs `OWNERSHIP_COSTS` 0.584) were the only swept candidates that kept every Skill routing case resolved; "Show my inspecion findings" is at 0.4481. The v1.68 Do-Nothing certified answer was reworded after the Budget operation's "upkeep" wording started outscoring it; the full relevance pass rate is 0.9732, and the three failures left were already failing (renovation readiness, buyer plan status, buyer task create). **Follow-up:** the pages' left-out appliances, assumed age and non-owner 500s; per-appliance AI picks and budget tips from Ask (option B, not chosen). |
| Owner-only records: product decision (September 24, 2026, FRD v1.69) | Documentation only; no code changed. | **Decided:** Service Price Radar checks (v1.60), Price Finalization records (v1.67) and Do-Nothing Simulator runs and scenarios (v1.68) stay homeowner-only by default. Their services admit only the primary homeowner profile even though the routes admit any household member; that is now the intended behaviour, and Ask's OWNER floor with a plain "only the home's primary owner" answer for others is final. The earlier open questions on these three are closed. |
| Runtime verification | **Executed and updated September 22, 2026** — the full `e2e/ask` Playwright suite (mocked Ask acceptance API, never a real backend/DB) was run in this session, not just claimed to exist; household, inventory not-found/access-loss/disambiguation, evidence-upload, both capability-card-audit reference journeys (Home Capital Timeline, Home Event Radar), then the TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability added across the day. **Every Property Records browser-coverage gap named in earlier versions of this row is now closed; both capability-card audit reference-journey slices and the TABLE-block platform capability are also now covered.** | `npx playwright test --config=playwright.ask.config.ts` (both `desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium` projects, against a fresh production build) — **80 passed, 0 failed** on the last re-run for FRD v1.46 (72 in `ask.spec.ts` + 5 in `ask.mobile.spec.ts` + 3 in `decisionThreadAccessibility.spec.ts` = 80, cross-checked against the run's own reported total, not just its exit code; ten new scenarios added across six rounds, each mutation-checked against a real rebuild — see the "Property Summary inventory and household inline collection/detail", "Inventory inline detail", "Evidence upload", "Home Capital Timeline reference journey", "Home Event Radar reference journey", and "TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability" rows). This covers, by actual test title (not paraphrase): the full-window Ask shell and desktop conversation sidebar/rail (history paging, search, all-home switching, new/restored conversations, draft continuity), the mobile drawer, adaptive table view switching, bounded-comparison strip/show-all switching on desktop and mobile, desktop/mobile response-context (sources) panels, authoritative maintenance and quote-workspace output-artifact context, the document-to-home-event related-record surface, inline maintenance task detail/access-loss redaction/creation/paging, inline Property Summary timeline-event/room/document/**household** detail (including the household-specific "no longer a member" data-absence state), inline inventory item detail's **item-not-found-vs-access-loss distinction** and the **inventory-entity-selection disambiguation list's** inline detail, **inline evidence upload** (out-of-band file upload via `setInputFiles`, exact `launchContext` identity, a zero-editable-field confirmation, and client-side file-type rejection), **inline Home Capital Timeline reserve-allocation detail**, **inline Home Event Radar monitored-event detail** (the capability-card audit's two reference-journey slices), and **inline Home Capital Timeline `TABLE`-row (capital window) detail** (the TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability), the accessibility checks for `DECISION_PROGRESS`/`WHY_NOW`/`SCENARIO_COMPARISON`, and every correction/add/completeness-capture scenario listed in the "Property records: correction browser acceptance" row. **Confirmed still NOT covered by any scenario in the current spec files** (grepped, not assumed): nothing left within Property Records; Home Event Radar notification settings and the two confirmed write cards, Mark done and Feedback. Task create-or-link is now covered; see the "Home Event Radar task create-or-link and notification settings" row; Home Capital Timeline assumption-set editing and contribution adjustment (no traditional-page control exists to mirror; the horizon re-run is covered, see its own row); the other 44 non-reference-journey capabilities in the audit; more broadly, no scenario anywhere in this suite runs against a real backend or database, and adaptive presentation's own remaining scope (a unified cross-block-type selection policy) is its own separate row, untouched by this work. **What this run does and does not prove:** it verifies the frontend against fixture responses on a fresh build — rendering, dispatch, request bodies — exactly as the other browser-acceptance rows already state; it proves nothing about the server's real role gating, freshness checks, or writes. |

## Appendix C.1 — Ownership-cost and savings action audit (FRD v1.177)

The October 2 audit of the `OWNERSHIP_COSTS`, `SAVINGS_OPPORTUNITIES`, and capital-plan follow-up surfaces distinguishes an answer already complete in Ask from a workflow that truly exists only in a traditional tool:

- A populated ownership-cost answer must not repeat that same review as a desktop-navigation CTA. Its recorded and missing categories default to a tabular presentation, including a single recorded category; a homeowner's explicit multi-row view choice remains authoritative.
- **Superseded by Revision 1.256:** missing ownership-cost categories are actionable rows whose controls launch the registered Ask operation that owns the underlying record (for example, **Review property tax** launches property-tax review). A missing row must not use an unscoped desktop link or imply that a loosely related destination corrected the category. If the canonical ownership-cost snapshot cannot load at all, the secondary “Open Ownership Costs tool” handoff remains an explicit availability fallback.
- A populated savings answer must not show redundant “Open Savings and Benefits” or “Review all opportunities” navigation. Related property-tax, coverage, and refinance decisions use their registered Ask operations. A never-analyzed savings record may retain a clearly labeled secondary tool handoff because Ask does not run that tool's initial analysis.
- Capital-plan repair-or-replace suggestions must identify the canonical item by name. The ambiguous “oldest system” wording is not emitted because it cannot provide a deterministic entity identity to the repair-or-replace operation.

This is code-path and contract validation, not live browser certification. The focused ownership and savings contract suites and the adaptive-table component suite pass. The backend build failure found during validation was a stale projection type: `getHomeContinuityProjection` selects decisions directly from the canonical ranked Home Action feed but declared them as `HomeAction[]`, erasing ranking, deduplication, work-item, decision-lineage, and fatigue metadata before Ask Concierge called helpers that require `RankedHomeAction`. Its return contract now preserves `RankedHomeAction[]`; no cast or synthesized ranking data is used. No production or database environment was started.

## Appendix C.2 — Home-summary and inventory conversation corrections (October 2, 2026)

- A vague Property Summary keeps its concise status observation but presents the recorded core facts as a two-column table. Only canonical values that exist are included; missing values are omitted rather than rendered as “Not recorded”. This supersedes the prose-only presentation choice recorded in v1.154 without reintroducing embedded inventory, document, household, or timeline collections.
- The `INVENTORY_ITEM_CREATE_INPUTS` capture uses the compact two-column workflow layout: name and category retain full width, while room, brand, and model use the available conversational-card width instead of separate full-width panels. The confirmation boundary and captured fields are unchanged.
- An inventory list item legitimately exposes thirteen governed correction actions. The response contract now permits up to sixteen bounded item actions so the complete declared correction set remains schema-valid when an execution is persisted and restored. This fixes the generic “saved response needs to be refreshed” fallback for inventory-detail queries without dropping a correction path.
- Room creation no longer offers an `Open Rooms` traditional-page CTA in its form, review, permission boundary, or completion receipt. The receipt instead offers `Show my rooms`; that exact phrase is a deterministic `PROPERTY_SUMMARY` route and returns the focused inline room map through the canonical typed-answer contract rather than falling into semantic clarification.

## Appendix C.3 — Home Habit Coach review and actions inside Ask (October 5, 2026)

- **Review inside Ask.** Each habit row is now a `HOME_HABIT` item with a **Review** action (`HOME_HABITS`, launched with the habit's entity id) instead of a link to the desktop Home Habit Coach page, which had no per-habit deep link, so every row opened the same generic page. The review is the Ask counterpart of the maintenance task review: why the habit was suggested, how often, how long, difficulty, area, suggested or next-due date, last done, the coach's tip, and recent activity.
- **Actions.** The review offers only what the service will accept for the habit's live status: **Add to my routine** (adopt; creates a recurring maintenance task), **Mark done**, **Snooze for a week**, **Skip for now**, **Stop suggesting**. They are `HOME_HABIT_UPDATE`: a confirmation-gated, contributor-floor, non-routable command (reached only from these declared actions; ASK_REFRESH and free-text messages never start one), skill-less like `RECALL_MATCH_UPDATE`. It calls the same `HomeHabitCoachService` methods as the page's controller and emits the same `HOME_HABIT_COACH` analytics event (the controller has no other side effect). An adopted habit offers nothing, because the service gives its schedule and completion to the maintenance task. A block carries at most three actions (response schema), so the first three sit under the habit and the rest, with **Back to my habits**, in a second block.
- **No desktop link.** The summary no longer offers **Open Home Habit Coach** when habits are listed; the answer already shows them. Only the empty state keeps it, because generating habits is not an Ask action.
- **Wording.** A suggestion the homeowner never adopted is "Suggested for <date>" and the summary counts "past their suggested date"; only an adopted routine task is "Overdue since".
- **Duplicates.** The same habit appeared twice because two catalogs with differently-keyed twins were seeded into one database (`seedHabitTemplates.ts` and `reference-data-bootstrap.pgadmin.sql`) and the generator de-duplicates by template id only. The bootstrap now carries the same 16-template catalog, the three SQL-only twins (`safety_smoke_co_test`, `exterior_gutter_visual_check`, `general_monthly_home_walkthrough`) are retired by both seed paths, and `prisma/habit-catalog-dedupe.pgadmin.sql` cleans up existing habits (dismisses a twin habit where the property already has the canonical one, moves it onto the canonical template where it does not, keeps the oldest of a same-template pair, never touches habits adopted into a routine). The SQL was run against a throwaway local Postgres with the real table definitions (not a real database); a unique constraint on property plus template is not added, so two simultaneous generate calls could still race.
- **Status.** Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean. New `tests/ask/homeHabitReviewWrites.test.js` (13 tests, including the answer-trust allowlist and the three-action block cap). Not live-verified in a browser.

## Appendix C.4 — Seasonal home-care answer as a conversational plan (October 5, 2026)

Reference: the product mock for "What should I do to get ready for next season?". Applies to `SEASONAL_HOME_CARE` (the general, climate-region answer behind the "this season" and "next season" starters).

- **Layout.** A conversational intro with a season icon (season, climate-region basis including when the national default was used, how many things to focus on, which to do soon); **Do these soon** (critical tasks) and **Can wait** as numbered cards with a one-line reason, priority and DIY / "usually a pro" chips, and an expandable **How to do it** (the task template's own facts: what to do, time, typical cost, who does it, when). An **About this recommendation** card states that these are general tasks, not an assessment of this home. A **What would you like to do next?** card offers up to three inline steps (see below). Renderers: `SeasonalPlanResultList`, `SeasonalIntroCard`, `SeasonalAboutCard`, `SeasonalNextSteps`, keyed by block id (the calm shell's one-action-per-summary rule does not apply to this card). New additive fields: grouped-list `detail` (item) and `caption` (section).
- **Next steps, all inside Ask.** **Add these to my tasks** (`SEASONAL_CHECKLIST_SETUP`, below), **Walk me through the first task** (`SEASONAL_HOME_CARE` launched with a `SEASONAL_TASK` entity: that task's facts, then Next task / Back to the tasks), and **Update home details** (the home-record completeness check, `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`). When the season's checklist already exists, the first becomes **Show my winter checklist** (`MAINTENANCE_STATUS`). Nothing on the answer links to the desktop seasonal page.
- **`SEASONAL_CHECKLIST_SETUP`.** Declared-action only, owner-only, confirmation-gated. It does not add the four general tasks shown: it runs `SeasonalChecklistService.generateSeasonalChecklist`, the generator the Seasonal Checklist page and the worker use, which evaluates every template against Property Context and links each applicable task to Maintenance. The confirmation therefore previews the real result with the same applicability code (`previewSeasonalChecklist`, read-only, extracted from the generator so they cannot disagree), says it can differ from the general list, and counts tasks held back until more home details are known. Nothing to add yet offers **Update home details**; an already-set-up checklist is reported without a second write. The page's controller adds only the seasonal-checklist analytics event, which Ask repeats. Correction mode `REVERSE` (remove a task or dismiss the checklist on the Seasonal Checklist page).
- **Existing checklist answer.** Converted to the same layout in Appendix C.5.
- **Found, not fixed.** The seasonal template catalog has the same two-catalog duplication the habit catalog had: `reference-data-bootstrap.pgadmin.sql` carries 8 templates, 6 of them differently-keyed twins of templates in the 44-template canonical catalog (`src/data/seasonalTaskTemplates.json`), so a database seeded from both shows near-duplicate checklist tasks. **Resolved October 6, 2026 (FRD v1.179): Appendix C.8.**
- **Process.** `HOME_HABIT_UPDATE` (Appendix C.3) shipped without its `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_OPERATIONS` entry and would have crash-looped the boot-time governance check; fixed in 3e1c6205 and guarded by `tests/ask/startupRegistryValidation.test.js`. `SEASONAL_CHECKLIST_SETUP` is listed from the start. The Playwright acceptance fixture lacked `homeContinuity` (the landing reads it), so every Ask e2e spec hit the error boundary; added.
- **Status.** Backend `tsc --noEmit` and all 26 startup validators clean; new `seasonalChecklistSetup.test.js` (8), additions to `seasonalHomeCareOperation.test.js`, frontend `seasonalPlan.test.tsx` (5) and a Playwright spec (desktop and 390px) against a fixture generated from the real builder. Not verified against a real backend or database.

## Appendix C.5 — The home's own seasonal checklist answer in the plan layout (October 5, 2026)

- **Scope.** `MAINTENANCE_STATUS`'s seasonal view (`askSeasonalMaintenance.ts`, "What seasonal tasks are pending?") now uses the layout of Appendix C.4 and the same renderers. Block ids and titles, the trust contract and routing are unchanged; the new block is `seasonal-maintenance-next`.
- **Layout.** One checklist: **Do these soon** (pending critical tasks), **Can wait** (other pending or snoozed), then **Completed** and **Dismissed** when the view includes them; several checklists keep one group per season and year, so tasks are never merged. Cards are numbered across groups with priority, timing/status and "In Maintenance" chips. Each opens to its facts: what to do, timing, status, the linked maintenance task's status, and, when the task key is in the seasonal catalog, the reason and time/cost/who-does-it facts (otherwise the checklist item's own description).
- **No desktop links.** The summary's "Open <season> checklist", every card's link to the desktop page, and the unavailable state's "Open Seasonal Care" are gone. The unavailable state offers **Try again**. The raw suggestion strings are replaced by inline actions: switch to pending, completed or dismissed (never the view already shown) and **Show my maintenance tasks** (`MAINTENANCE_STATUS`), all allowlisted.
- **Still open.** Completing, snoozing and dismissing a checklist item still has no Ask operation (a task linked to Maintenance is completed there); this answer is read-only, as before.
- **Status.** `askSeasonalMaintenance.test.js` (14), the e2e fixture regenerated from the real builder, the two seasonal e2e specs rewritten, and the acceptance fixture's shell routes moved into `installAskApi` so the Ask e2e specs load. Not verified against a real backend.

- **List.** `HOME_HABITS` now uses the layout of Appendix C.4: an intro with an icon, **Start with these** (habits past their suggested date), **Up next**, **In your maintenance routine** and **Snoozed**, numbered across groups. Each card shows cadence, difficulty, time and date chips, its own **Review** button (the inline review, `CONVERSATION_CONTINUE`) and a separate toggle that opens its facts. A next-steps card offers **Review the first habit** and **Show my maintenance tasks**. The raw suggestion strings are gone; the empty state still carries its one page link.

## Appendix C.7 — Task and habit walkthroughs as one guide card (October 5, 2026)

- **Problem.** "Walk me through <task>" and "Review <habit>" returned an intro, then a generic grouped list that gave every fact its own large card, then separate cards for actions: a long, mostly empty answer that did not read as one conversational turn.
- **New block `TASK_GUIDE`.** One card, after the product reference: breadcrumb ("Winter prep › Task 1 of 4"), an icon, the title and a one-line summary, key facts as chips (priority, time, cost, DIY or pro; for a habit cadence, time, difficulty, status), an optional tip, a **What to do** panel with its facts, short explanatory rows (**Why this is on your list**, **How personalized is this?**, **About these habits**; a row can carry one of the card's actions, such as **Update home details**), recent activity, and an action row (up to six). Every string is the producer's recorded content; the renderer adds none.
- **What is not shown, and why.** The reference's step-by-step stepper, photo and "I found it" flow have no source content: a seasonal task template records one description, time, cost and timing, and a habit template one description and a tip. Nothing was invented. If step content is authored later it fits this block as new fields.
- **Seasonal walkthrough.** `SEASONAL_HOME_CARE` launched with a `SEASONAL_TASK` entity returns one guide card: **Next winter task** (filled), **Add these to my tasks** (owner, no checklist yet) or **Show my winter checklist** (checklist exists), **Back to the winter tasks**, and **Update home details** in its row. "Mark as complete" and "Skip this task" from the reference are not offered: a general task has no record to complete or skip.
- **Habit review.** One guide card: the tip as **Before you start**, **What it involves**, recent activity, and the habit actions (add to routine, mark done, snooze, skip, stop suggesting, back). The fact sheet component remains for the seasonal-checklist-style lists.
- **Boot-time guard.** The habit-coach skill manifest also had to declare the new block (`allowedResultBlocks`); missing, the startup validator would have crash-looped the backend. `startupRegistryValidation.test.js` now runs every validator `index.ts` runs at startup (read from `index.ts`), not two.
- **Status.** Backend `tsc --noEmit` and all 26 startup validators clean; `taskGuide.test.tsx`, updated habit and seasonal backend tests, Playwright specs against fixtures generated from the real builders. Not verified against a real backend.

## Appendix C.8 — Seasonal template catalog clean-up (October 6, 2026)

- **Problem.** `reference-data-bootstrap.pgadmin.sql` carried 8 seasonal templates, 6 of them differently-keyed twins of canonical ones, and the 44-template catalog (`src/data/seasonalTaskTemplates.json`, loaded by `prisma/seasonalTasks.seed.ts` and `prisma/seasonalTaskTemplates.pgadmin.seed.sql`) reached a database by two other paths. A database seeded from both generated near-duplicate checklist tasks, because generation de-duplicates by template id only.
- **Catalog decision (product).** `WINTER_SMOKE_CO_TEST` has no canonical winter counterpart (the catalog tests detectors in spring and CO in fall), so retiring it would remove winter's only CRITICAL detector test. It is **promoted** into the JSON, keeping its key and content: the catalog is now 45 templates, and the Ask winter general-task list grows from 4 to 5. The other five twins are **retired**: `FALL_WINTERIZE_OUTDOOR_FAUCETS` (direct match for `FALL_DRAIN_OUTDOOR_FAUCETS`), `SUMMER_HVAC_FILTER_CHECK` (conditional match for `SUMMER_AC_FILTER_CHANGE`), `FALL_HEATING_SYSTEM_SERVICE` (conditional match for `FALL_FURNACE_INSPECTION`), and `SUMMER_EXTERIOR_DRAINAGE_CHECK` and `WINTER_FREEZE_READINESS` (no counterpart; retired only).
- **Why two mappings are only conditional.** The AC filter template needs a cooling system, skips VERY_COLD and is CRITICAL; the twin applied everywhere and was RECOMMENDED. The furnace template needs a furnace on record; the twin was generic heating service. Applicability comes from Property Context, which SQL cannot evaluate, so the clean-up never moves an item onto a conditional canonical template.
- **Seed paths.** `reference-data-bootstrap.pgadmin.sql` now carries the same 45 rows as the JSON (including the asset columns), the pgAdmin seed gained the 45th row, and **every** path retires the five twin keys: both SQL files with an `UPDATE ... SET is_active = false`, the TypeScript seed from the shared list `src/data/retiredSeasonalTaskKeys.json`. Template rows are kept because checklist items reference them. `tests/unit/seasonalCatalogRetirement.test.js` fails if a path carries a twin, drifts from the JSON, or stops retiring a key.
- **Existing data: `prisma/seasonal-catalog-dedupe.pgadmin.sql` (idempotent, nothing deleted, completed history untouched).** For each open twin item (RECOMMENDED, ADDED, SNOOZED) of a mapped twin: with the canonical item already on the same checklist, the item becomes DISMISSED (`SeasonalTaskStatus` has no CANCELLED) and a still-PENDING linked maintenance task becomes CANCELLED, as `dismissTask` does; CRITICAL twin items, which the UI refuses to dismiss, are dismissed here. A direct mapping with no canonical item moves the item onto the canonical template (template id, task key, title, description) and an open task gets the same wording. Conditional mappings with no canonical item are kept and reported. A duplicate whose task is IN_PROGRESS or NEEDS_REVIEW, a completed task, and a canonical template not valid for the checklist's climate are kept and reported. Touched checklists have `total_tasks`, `tasks_added` and COMPLETED/IN_PROGRESS status recomputed with the generator's and `syncSeasonalChecklistStatus`'s rules. `excluded_task_keys` gets the canonical key added beside a mapped twin key, so an excluded task does not reappear. The final result reports retired templates, moved items, dismissed duplicates, cancelled tasks, migrated exclusions, each kept category, remaining duplicates and unresolved excluded keys.
- **Not done, deliberately.** A cancelled task's Home Operations work item is not closed by SQL (a state-machine transition the application owns); the report counts those tasks so they can be closed from the app. An item kept on a retired conditional twin can still get a canonical item from a later regeneration if the canonical template becomes applicable; no compatibility layer was added for that. Frontend `taskGuide.test.tsx` keeps its own fixture ("Task 1 of 4").
- **Status (code-traced and executed on a throwaway local Postgres 15, never the real database).** Schema pushed from a copy of `schema.prisma` without the two PostGIS columns and their GiST indexes (PostGIS for PostgreSQL 15 is not installed locally). The pre-change bootstrap plus the new seed produced 50 templates; five fixture checklists covered repoint (with an exclusion), duplicate dismissal of a CRITICAL item with a PENDING task, a climate mismatch, a duplicate with an IN_PROGRESS task next to a completed twin item, and a checklist that became COMPLETED once its duplicate was dismissed. Results matched expectations, a second run changed nothing, and re-running the bootstrap and pgAdmin seed left 45 active templates; the TypeScript seed retired a re-activated twin. Backend tests: new retirement test (4), `referenceDataBootstrap`, `startupRegistryValidation` and the five seasonal Ask test files, 53/53 (`seasonalHomeCareEmptyHome` updated from "Task 1 of 4" to 5). Full backend suite, `npm run typecheck` and the real database were not run.

Four rendered through the generic grouped-list cards and are now converted to the plan layout of Appendix C.4 (numbered cards, urgent group tinted, facts that open under the card): the **hiring guide** (`hiring-guide-items`, "Before you hire"), the two **home-basics guides** (`home-basics-items`, safety basics and monthly routine), the **renovation readiness checklist** (`renovation-readiness-items`) and the **property-tax appeal readiness list** (`tax-readiness-gaps`).

The guides are numbered in order and neutral. Renovation: **Blocking** is the urgent group and **Other open items** the calm one, numbering runs across both, each card says **Blocks starting** or **Open** (plus **Acknowledged**), and the long chips (next action, evidence required) became the facts under the card (**Next step**, **Evidence needed**). Tax: open gaps are urgent cards marked **Still needed**; a ready case lists **Confirmed** evidence with its type. Item `status` and `href` are unchanged for the trust contract; the plan cards do not navigate, and the readiness ring and summary keep their links.
- **Deliberately not converted, and why.** The plan cards are read-only with at most one action per card and no per-item decision flow. **Seller prep checklist** (`SellerPrepItemResultList`) and **buyer plan tasks** (`BuyerTaskResultList`) carry item decisions and completion with their own confirmations; **maintenance task lists** (`MaintenanceResultList`) are shelves with filters, completion and rescheduling; the **claim checklist** is a section of the claim detail; the **Home Event Radar**, **inspection findings** and **HOA** lists are records, not checklists. Converting them needs a product decision on whether the plan card should carry several actions per item.
- **Status.** Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; new `checklistPlanLayout.test.js` (2, real renovation handler with stubbed reads) and the existing renovation, hiring and home-basics tests plus `startupRegistryValidation`, all passing; frontend `checklistPlanLayout.test.tsx` (6) with the seasonal and habits plan tests, 15/15. The tax list was changed by code trace only (its handler needs the appeal service and Property Context, not stubbed). Not verified in a browser or against a real backend; frontend `next build` not run.

## Appendix C.10 — Home record summary as a facts grid (October 6, 2026)

- **Problem.** The home overview's "Home record summary" was a two-column table with an Auto/Cards/Table switch and a record count. With three recorded facts it left most of the card empty. Bedrooms and bathrooms were already emitted when recorded, but this home had neither, and several loaded fields never reached the table.
- **Facts.** `propertyOverviewFactRows` now emits, in a fixed order and only when recorded: Home type, Year built, Living area, Bedrooms, Bathrooms, **Occupancy**, **Occupants** (owner only), **Heating**, **Cooling**, **Roof**. An UNKNOWN enum is absent. HVAC and Central/Window AC have their own labels instead of title-casing. Bathrooms print as "2.5" or "2". **Occupant count is shown only to an owner** (`access.role === OWNER`; a contributor or viewer never receives it); occupancy status is shown to everyone with access. Lot size is deliberately not shown: `Property.lotSize` has no recorded unit.
- **Gaps.** No empty tiles and no "Not recorded" values. One plain sentence is appended to the table description when bedrooms, bathrooms or occupancy are unrecorded (`propertyOverviewMissingLine`: "Not recorded yet: bedrooms, bathrooms."). It names only those three, since a missing cooling or roof type can be true of the home.
- **Layout.** The block stays a `TABLE` (no new block type, so no registry or skill-manifest change). `AdaptiveTableBlock` renders ids in `FACT_GRID_TABLE_IDS` (`property-summary-facts`) as a grid of label-over-value tiles: three across from the `lg` breakpoint, two from `sm`, one on a phone, with no view switch and no record count. Any other table is unchanged.
- **Status.** Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean; `propertySummaryOverview.test.js` 12/12 (5 new: order and labels, owner-only occupants through the real handler as owner and viewer, the missing line, the answer checker passing with it); new frontend `factGridTable.test.tsx` (2); all Ask jest 80 of 82 suites pass, the two failures (`displayPatterns`, `maintenanceShelves`) were failing before this change. Not verified in a browser, at 390 px width, or against a real backend; frontend `next build` not run.

## Appendix C.11 — Conversational presentation policy (approved October 6, 2026; delivery phase 1 implemented, phases 2–4 pending)

### C.11.1 Outcome and governing sequence

Ask Cozy must read as an intelligent homeowner advisor using structured UI only where structure improves comprehension or action. The governing sequence is:

```text
Conversation → Judgment → Evidence → Action → Continuation
```

It must not routinely become `Conversation → generic component → navigation`. A structured result must still read as an answer from Cozy. In the first meaningful viewport, the homeowner should normally understand the direct answer or judgment, what matters most, and the safest useful next step.

### C.11.2 Semantic composition modes

The following terms classify the job a response is doing. They are not, by themselves, new persisted response enums, presentation-block types, renderer families, or permission to bypass the existing operation, block, action, confirmation, or history contracts.

| Mode | Required conversational behavior | Expected reuse |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **ANSWER** | Answer the question first, then provide only the explanation and evidence needed to support it. | `SUMMARY`, optional evidence/boundary, contextual continuation |
| **RECOMMEND** | State Cozy's judgment, group or order choices by meaningful priority, explain the leading priority, disclose personalization, and offer a justified next step. | `SUMMARY`, `PRIORITY_LIST` or `GROUPED_LIST`, disclosure/boundary, declared actions |
| **GUIDE** | Help with one task in the current turn. A guide must not masquerade as a stepper when only a single recorded instruction exists. | Existing `TASK_GUIDE`; a later stateful guide contract only after C.11.8 decisions |
| **ASSESS** | Ask at most one currently high-value question whose answer materially changes guidance, risk interpretation, readiness, or useful home context. | Clarification/capture plus existing confirmation mechanisms |
| **EXPLAIN** | Give a plain-language explanation, why it matters, optional deeper evidence, and a relevant continuation. | `SUMMARY`, `WHY_NOW`, evidence/boundary |
| **COMPARE** | Emphasize meaningful differences, tradeoffs, and a governed recommendation when evidence permits one. | Existing comparison blocks and lossless table/card choices |
| **ACT** | Prepare, review, confirm, execute, and reconcile a registered operation without presenting a recommendation as a completed write. | Existing typed actions, capture, confirmation, receipt, and reconciliation paths |

An operation may compose more than one mode over successive turns. Implementations should first compose existing registered blocks. A new block or persisted mode is justified only when the existing contract cannot express required semantics losslessly and the history/fallback behavior is defined.

### C.11.3 Ownership boundary

**IW-CONV-PRES-001 — Server-owned judgment.** The operation/producer owns the direct answer, semantic mode when one must be declared, ordering, priority groups and reasons, safety classification, recommendation basis, personalization disclosure, limitations, and available actions. The client must not infer business priority, urgency, safety, confidence, or home-specific judgment from prose, titles, block ids, colors, item counts, or incidental order.

**IW-CONV-PRES-002 — Client-owned lossless presentation.** The client owns registered rendering, responsive transformation, local expansion/collapse, focus and keyboard behavior, and useful lossless view preference. These changes must not create an Ask execution, change business meaning, or mutate canonical data.

**IW-CONV-PRES-003 — Historical integrity.** Persisted executions retain their original conversational response. Local presentation state and current underlying record state remain distinct. Reopening history may disclose that live data changed or offer refresh/reconciliation, but must not silently rewrite the original answer into a materially different one.

### C.11.4 Answer anatomy, density, and cards

The default response hierarchy is: direct answer or Cozy judgment; decision-driving support; one visually dominant action when justified; contextual continuation; compact personalization/trust disclosure; and progressively disclosed evidence and secondary detail. Decision-driving support may be concise reasoning, a recorded fact, a personalization basis, or formal mapped evidence; not every answer requires an `EVIDENCE` block. This is a hierarchy, not an inflexible block sequence. Emergency guidance, clarification, balanced comparisons, and confirmation choices may require a different order or legitimately have no single primary action.

Cards represent objects, entities, bounded actions, or meaningful interactive units—for example a quote, contractor, policy, appointment, property, document, maintenance event, workflow, or comparison option. Ordinary sentences and individual attributes such as time, cost, priority, and DIY suitability do not each receive a card. Compact metadata should read together, for example `High priority · about 15 min · $15–$40 · DIY`.

Broad answers must not render every available attribute merely because it exists. Secondary recommendations, evidence, assumptions, provenance, and procedural detail use disclosure controls such as Why, How, Show details, or Show remaining items. Disclosure controls are UI state unless their action genuinely requires another conversational execution.

### C.11.5 Recommendations and continuation

**IW-CONV-PRES-004 — Visible judgment.** A recommendation explains what matters most. Priority groups use domain-appropriate labels such as Do now, Do soon, Can wait, or Optional, and include a concise reason where the producer has a governed basis. A frontend renderer never invents the grouping or rationale.

**IW-CONV-PRES-005 — Recommendation is not commitment.** A recommended task, reminder, fact, appointment, or other action is not persisted until the homeowner invokes the registered operation and completes every existing authorization and confirmation requirement. Presentation must keep proposed and committed states visibly distinct.

**IW-CONV-PRES-006 — Contextual continuation.** Response-level follow-ups continue the current intent before generic discovery suggestions. Global starters may return when no meaningful continuation exists, the work is complete, or the homeowner starts a new conversation. The server-governed Suggested Next Action policy remains authoritative for eligibility and deduplication.

### C.11.6 Personalization, confidence, and safety

The response distinguishes recorded home facts, homeowner-confirmed facts, inferred context, location/climate context, general guidance, and unknown information in conversational language. It must not imply home-specific assessment from general catalog or climate guidance. Raw internal confidence scores, ranking diagnostics, or debug provenance are not homeowner-facing by default.

Confidence-like concepts remain separate: extraction confidence, evidence quality, recommendation certainty, personalization completeness, freshness, and confirmation state are not interchangeable. Producers translate them into governed homeowner-facing statements such as “based on your recorded gas service,” “general guidance for your climate,” or “this date needs verification.”

Routine safety advice and emergency instructions require different semantics and treatment. Existing `BOUNDARY` severity remains the reusable mechanism. Emergency instructions must be prominent, calm, accessible without color, and must not be buried inside an ordinary recommendation card. Conditional guidance—such as natural-gas checks—must respect known absence, known presence, and unknown status as three distinct states.

### C.11.7 Assessment and knowledge capture

ASSESS asks one high-value question at a time only when its answer changes current guidance or materially improves future home context. It must not turn a broad question into a questionnaire. A response used only to continue the current assessment remains conversation state; it does not become a canonical fact automatically.

Observed, inferred, homeowner-confirmed, proposed, and persisted states remain distinct. A material home-record write uses the existing governed capture/writer, authorization, confirmation, idempotency, provenance, freshness, and reconciliation path. No assessment renderer or guide may create a shadow home record or silently persist an inference.

### C.11.8 Stateful GUIDE is a separately gated capability

The existing `TASK_GUIDE` is a focused conversational presentation of recorded task or habit content. It is not a stateful walkthrough, and it must not display invented steps when the source supplies only one instruction. A true step-by-step GUIDE is not approved for implementation until its design resolves:

1. the canonical, author-reviewed source and versioning of steps, safety instructions, help branches, and optional media;
2. whether progress is execution-local, session-local, cross-session, or canonical domain workflow state;
3. pause, resume, previous, skip, abandonment, stale-source, and completion semantics;
4. whether completion affects a guide only, a maintenance task, a seasonal item, or another canonical record;
5. the proof policy for physical completion and the rule that homeowner wording alone must not falsely certify work;
6. how contextual help creates child conversation turns without advancing the step;
7. how facts discovered during the guide enter the existing proposal/confirmation path; and
8. history, access-loss, role, idempotency, reconciliation, mobile, and accessibility behavior.

Until those decisions are approved, improve `TASK_GUIDE` density, wording, action priority, and personalization transparency without adding simulated step state.

### C.11.9 Reference-scenario requirements

**Winter preparation.** Reuse the existing seasonal builder and plan layout. Lead with the season/climate judgment, show the governed Do soon/Can wait grouping, progressively disclose task facts, explain how personalized the recommendation is, and offer contextual actions. Do not create tasks until setup is explicitly confirmed.

**Furnace-filter guidance.** In the near-term slice, render the best recorded instruction as one compact `TASK_GUIDE`; remove any premature “next unrelated task” emphasis and prioritize help for the current task. A numbered multi-step walkthrough belongs only to the separately approved capability in C.11.8.

**Home-safety basics.** Lead with the few items that matter most and explain why. Reveal remaining items progressively. A later ASSESS slice may ask one conditional readiness question, but a conversational answer must not silently become a recorded safety fact. Known gas absence removes gas-specific recommendations; unknown status produces conditional language and an optional clarification. Emergency gas guidance uses an `EMERGENCY` boundary.

### C.11.10 Delivery phases and acceptance

1. **Presentation consistency:** audit the three reference scenarios against the current builders; refine answer/judgment ordering, density, disclosures, contextual actions, and inappropriate view controls using existing contracts.
2. **Reusable semantic composition:** add the smallest producer-side policy or fields necessary where current blocks cannot express the required semantics; do not add seven renderer systems.
3. **Stateful GUIDE:** proceed only after C.11.8 is decided and documented.
4. **ASSESS and safety capture:** add the one-question flow and confirmed knowledge capture after conversation-only versus canonical state is explicit.

For each implemented slice, tests must establish semantic producer output, no automatic persistence, contextual actions, authorization/confirmation preservation, stored-execution compatibility, stale/unknown behavior, role visibility, access-loss behavior, keyboard and screen-reader semantics, reduced motion where applicable, and desktop/mobile layout. Runtime/browser behavior is reported as unverified when no environment exists; static inspection is not execution proof.

## Appendix C.12 — Lightweight capability discovery: implementation status (October 10, 2026; FRD v1.254)

**Shipped in four pushed slices:** the discovery projection on Concierge Home (`1ad59c68`), the Ask shell presentation (`ff61885a`), owning-domain indicators (`7a8ea1a6`), and the reviewed explorer, search, and measurement (`6fb88f93`). The governing plan is `ASK_COZY_LIGHTWEIGHT_CAPABILITY_DISCOVERY_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.

**How to read the evidence.** *Executed* means a test or build was actually run and passed in this work. *Code-traced* means the behavior follows from code that was read but not run end to end. **Nothing here was live-verified:** no run against a real backend or database, no production build in the deployed environment, no manual browser or screen-reader pass.

| IW-SHELL-014 — three stable topics, focused starter view, no submit on topic selection | **Implemented.** | *Executed:* backend discovery suites 27/27 (`askExplorerRegistry`, `askDiscoveryTopics`, `askDiscoveryIndicators`); frontend Explore, explorer, and search suites 41/41 across four suites, including ten that mount the real `AskWorkspace` with only the API mocked (a topic click sends no request; a starter sends exactly one with its message, operation, and property); Ask Playwright 219 passed, including five new Explore specs on desktop and a phone. The server projects `discoveryTopics` on the existing `GET /api/ask/concierge-home`; a failure leaves the topics without starters and the rest of the shell loaded. |

Home care counts the uncapped dashboard attention section (NOW/SOON, a coverage group counted once) minus suppressed, completed, unavailable, stale, and watch-only items; DIY & Projects counts `PLANNING`/`IN_PROGRESS` projects through the service's own open-status set; My Home Record is Property Context completeness over the Property Summary scopes. A failing source omits only its own indicator. Zero is not rendered for the two counts; `0%` is. **Behavior decisions recorded.** The projection is loaded for the page session, not only the empty landing, and is kept for the selected home; returning to the landing still refetches. A starter refuses to send when the loaded overview belongs to a different property than the selected one. A starter below the caller's role floor is shown disabled with its reason; one that is disabled, runtime-unavailable, or hidden by audience policy is not shown. "Completed" means the request settled, not that the underlying task succeeded.

Remaining: host selectors in the "More ideas" dialog if wanted, and adopt the contract in routing and Suggested Next Actions, which was the point of making it reusable.

On the backend only the files relevant to this work were run, not the full suite (which has its own known failures in `docs/operations/BACKEND_TEST_SUITE_TRIAGE.md`); one routing-calibration test in those files fails the same way on a clean `HEAD`.

## Appendix D — Capability-card audit scope and implementation sequence

The source inventory is `ALL_TOOL_CAPABILITY_DEFINITIONS` in the canonical registry, not the two cards in the reported screenshot. The groups below total 48 registered capabilities as of September 19, 2026. These IDs define the exhaustive **audit population**, not a claim that each capability is shown simultaneously or that each full workflow has been independently verified. `quote-comparison` is workflow-only and must be reviewed at its applicable Ask entry point; the other 47 remain subject to actual availability/readiness policy. A `CATALOG_ONLY` recommendation mode does not waive inline requirements when its card is rendered in Ask.

| Definition group | Count | Capability IDs to classify |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Understand home | 7 | `documents`, `home-records`, `home-digital-will`, `home-risk-replay`, `home-timeline`, `property-brief`, `material-specs` |
| Maintain/prevent | 4 | `home-operations`, `maintenance`, `home-habit-coach`, `plant-advisor` |
| Protect/monitor | 8 | `appreciation`, `claims`, `energy`, `guidance-overview`, `home-event-radar`, `home-briefing`, `neighborhood-change-radar`, `visual-inspector` |
| Decide/compare | 7 | `do-nothing-simulator`, `home-digital-twin`, `negotiation-shield`, `price-finalization`, `quote-comparison`, `replace-repair`, `service-price-radar` |
| Plan/budget | 14 | `budget`, `buyer-closing`, `capital-timeline`, `diy`, `emergency`, `hoa-compliance`, `home-renovation-risk-advisor`, `inspection-hub`, `oracle`, `permits`, `project-tracker`, `reserve-fund`, `seller-prep`, `status-board` |
| Save/optimize | 8 | `break-even`, `coverage-intelligence`, `financing`, `mortgage-refinance-radar`, `ownership-costs`, `property-tax`, `savings-benefits`, `sell-hold-rent` |

Implementation sequence:

1. Generate the §19 coverage rows from this registry and trace all Ask producers/entry surfaces to the shared card contract. For each ID, enumerate the traditional page's normal outcome and all visible controls against canonical services and Ask operations; mark proven inline, missing, or a documented §9 exception. Do not infer completion from an existing answer, link, or recommendation mode.
2. Introduce a typed card primary action with source/property identity and a separate labeled `OPEN_TRADITIONAL` option. Dispatch through registered Ask interactions and retain the conversation/result/draft in the current route. Validate the registry-to-action mapping and reject unclassified new cards or broken targets.
3. Complete the two reference journeys before generalizing: Home Capital Timeline must expose canonical timeline browsing, selection, planning/refinement, and any supported consequential updates, beyond the existing `CAPITAL_RESERVE_PLAN` summary. Home Event Radar must expose its canonical feed, event detail, filters, and supported event/preference actions; do not substitute the broader `INTELLIGENCE_ENVELOPE_QUERY` as if it were the same workflow. Scope each operation against its domain contract before implementing controls or writes.
4. Apply the same contract to every other registry capability and Ask launch surface, completing normal outcomes domain by domain. Until a journey is complete, disclose its exact boundary and optional handoff under §9 rather than silently treating a page link as inline delivery. Preserve existing direct routes.
5. Verify contract/drift checks and focused static/component tests, then desktop, narrow/mobile, keyboard, authorization-loss, stale/partial/empty data, in-flight context change, and explicit traditional-return scenarios when a runnable environment is available. Record which levels actually ran; static inspection alone is not browser proof.
